# Plan: Phase 11, Connect (reusable KYC) and hardening

**Source:** `docs/PLAN.md` §2 (the "Connect / Reusable" row), §3 (data protection by design, hardening, the technology table), §6 Phase 11 and "Next release: iOS", §9 (risks) and §10 (program acceptance); `CLAUDE.md` rules 1–6; ADR 0002 (tiers and honesty), ADR 0004 (Kotlin Multiplatform), ADR 0005 (report honesty), and the ADRs the Phase 7–10 plans write (0006–0009); owner decision 1 after Workflow 6C (`docs/plans/phase-6.plan.md`, "Owner decisions after Workflow 6C"); and the Phase 7, 8, 9 and 10 plans, which Phase 11 builds on. Two research notes were taken on 2026-10-29 for this plan and are committed with it under `docs/research/`:
- `phase11-connect.md` (cited **connect**): the vendor's public sharing between organisations (connections, share tokens, peek, clone and hydrate, client-side redemption, events, scopes), what the law asks of a consent record (EDPB 05/2020 and 3/2019), the security of sharing in two deployment shapes, the open credential standards and why the wallet work waits, OpenVerify today, the gap tables R1–R36, the carried items K1–K12, five corrections to the repository's records, twelve owner questions and a fact-check (27 confirmed, 2 corrected: RFC 9901's salt length is a RECOMMENDED minimum, and the Token Status List draft's revision 21 is dated 2026-06-21);
- `phase11-hardening.md` (cited **hardening**): the carried items H1–H22, the security pieces the repository has today, a first-pass STRIDE model of 46 rows over 14 trust boundaries, ASVS 5.0.0 Level 2 by chapter with 13 day-one gaps (G-HSTS … G-REGEX), scanners, SBOMs, signing and provenance with each tool's licence and pinnable version, load testing (k6 fails rule 4), the Helm chart and production images, the documentation set, twenty owner questions with defaults, its gaps and a fact-check (33 confirmed, 1 corrected: RCW 19.375.020(1) lists notice, consent or a mechanism as alternatives; 2 could not verify in part; plus a correction to its request discipline).

The two notes number their carried items independently (connect K1–K12, hardening H1–H22); this plan cites them so. The Phase 9 notes (`phase9-behaviour.md` §4.8 and S15, `phase9-engineering.md`) were read for what Phase 9 hands over (SSO among it), and the Phase 10 notes (`phase10-android-behaviour.md` M38, `phase10-android-engineering.md`) for the Android side of Connect (no share token on any Android entry point; a `share-consent` renderer in `commonMain`). Downloads made during research stay out of git.

**Complexity:** Very large, and the last phase of this release. Seven Workflows of at most ten agents each:
- **11A, Connect (ten agents):** contracts and plumbing for connections, share policies, share tokens, consent records and the `share-consent` step; the pure share logic; then the connection and token API with peek, the redeem job (clone and hydrate, provenance, what a cloned pass counts for), consent records with attestation and withdrawal, the two clients' `share-consent` screens (web flow and Android `commonMain`), the console pages on Phase 9's shell and the workflow actions on Phase 8's engine; then integration.
- **11B, platform hardening (nine agents):** console sessions (idle timeout, ending sessions), the login limiter in Valkey, API-key pepper rotation and scope bounds, OIDC sign-in for the console, data-store TLS and a separate migration role with a locked migrate, `/ready` and worker health with the internal listener, per-tenant queue quotas, bounded operator patterns, the DSAR access export, and the two list-ingestion carries (the UN opt-in, conditional requests); then integration.
- **11C, supply chain and deployment (nine agents):** production images per process with digest-pinned bases; the per-push scanning jobs (dependencies, workflows, configuration, the PII-pattern scan) and the SAST baseline; SBOMs; the Helm chart, its security templates and its tests; the publishable web SDK packages (`@openverify/js`, `@openverify/react`, `apps/demo-web`); the release workflow that stages every artifact without publishing; then integration.
- **11D, performance (ten agents):** the HTTP load harness (vegeta and a small `httpx` driver of ours, replacing k6; Locust if the owner grants Q12 before 11D's Stage 1a); the matcher's write cost carried from Phase 6; C78.7 measured at base and head, and worked only where Workflow 6D leaves a budget open (conditional, sized from 6D's published calibration); then two calibration agents, one after the other, alone on the machine; two reviewers, a verifier and a fixer.
- **11E, the documentation set and the security records (eight agents):** the threat model with its parsing test, the ASVS 5.0.0 Level 2 and MASVS v2.1.0 checklists with their evidence tests, the cryptographic inventory, data classification and logging inventory, the self-hosting guide with a generated configuration reference, the compliance guide (not legal advice), the generated check catalog and API reference, the generated mapping from the source vendor's public API, the sharing guide, and the release checklist, its check and the generated release notes (C284); then integration.
- **11F, dynamic testing, review and fixes (nine agents):** a ZAP API and baseline scan against the e2e stack's images mode; a review of all of Phase 11 in four lenses, C284 included (built in 11E so it is reviewed); a verifier; three path-owned fixers, the last of which runs the gate.
- **11G, the owner-gated release artifacts (up to five agents):** image and chart publication with signatures and provenance, npm publication, CodeQL, and the records each owner answer changes (the toolchain release-age rule, weights in images, counsel's review, the vendor-named migration page, the UN opt-in); each part starts only once its owner decision is recorded (Q1–Q12), and each has a stated outcome if the owner declines.

**Why this split.** Connect is the only new product surface of the phase and every hardening artifact must cover it (its threat rows B13, its ASVS rows, its load scenario, its docs), so 11A builds it first and freezes its contracts in its own Stage 1a, as every earlier phase did. 11B closes the code-level gaps the threat model and ASVS checklist open on day one (hardening 4.3) and the items earlier plans carried to Phase 11; it is API and console work that needs nothing from 11A but must exist before the chart (its `/ready`, its internal listener, its migration role) and before the load test (its quotas). 11C packages what 11A and 11B built: production images, the chart, scanning and SBOMs, and the release workflow that stages them; it must follow them because a chart probes `/ready` and a scan reads the final dependency tree. 11D measures on 11C's production images, alone on the machine, as the Phase 6 calibrations did, and works the matcher's write cost and any C78.7 gap beside the load test (owner decision 1 after 6C). 11E writes the documents once the code they describe exists, generated where the repository holds the facts so they cannot drift. 11F tests the running stack and reviews everything. Every part that needs the owner's accounts, keys, money, a lawyer or an exception to a `CLAUDE.md` rule is in 11G, behind a switch, so 11A–11F build, test and stage the release without it (C289). Each Workflow is pushed once, after a full local validation (Rules).

**Stage 1b carries plumbing only**, as in Phases 6–10: migrations, repository and route stubs with frozen schemas, masking lists, queues and job stubs, shared hooks and configuration. Every algorithm stays with its owner behind a declared interface. Stage 2 starts as soon as the Stage 1a commits it needs land, beside Stage 1b, and each Stage 3 agent waits for its family's Stage 1b checkpoint and its named Stage 2 dependency. Each checkpoint is committed with every suite green, so a pause resumes from the last one.

**Phase 7, 8, 9 and 10 dependency.** Phase 11 starts only once `docs/PROGRESS.md` records Phase 10 done (CI-green, Phase 10 decision 72); until then the Phase 7, 8, 9 and 10 plans are the interfaces it relies on, and each is named where it is used. In short:
- **Phase 10:** the SDK's step registry, which takes a `share-consent` renderer without a format change (C194.1); the consent digests in `commonMain` (C200); the transport seam that carries a host's certificate pins (C191.1, C213.4, decision 38); dependency locking and verification for the scanners (C214.5); the `android-release.yml` staged repository (C217.3); the owner items P1–P5 and decision 50, which stay pending and are carried to the release checklist; the manual TalkBack pass and the real-document NFC read (decisions 47, 48), carried to the release gate;
- **Phase 9:** flow-spec v2's step union, which takes `share-consent` (C175.2); the server flow engine with `position`, the client view, screen submission and the session response (C151); the consent evidence and its digests (C162.9); the flow server with its per-request `frame-ancestors` and headers (C157); `@openverify/js` (C159); the console shell, settings pages, roles per environment and the role editor (C167, C168); MFA required for new organisations (C168); the console's script CSP (C170); the hand-over list C175.2 (SSO, the idle timeout, production images, the chart, npm publication, the B2 rows, the CSP rows, the internal route); C177's deferrals;
- **Phase 8:** the workflow engine's actions and triggers, its per-environment admission slots (C117.8 and its fairness rule: default 50 in-flight steps), the decision record (C128) and `docs/compliance/automated-decisions.md`; the code runner's isolated placement (P17);
- **Phase 7:** transactions (a hydrate destination and source), the signal registry, the strictly-necessary basis for session integrity (its line 612), the handoff threat note `docs/flow/handoff.md`;
- **Phase 6:** C88's deferral of Connect snapshots, decisions 10, 21 and 22 after 6B, and owner decision 1 after 6C (the C78.7 stopping rule and the write cost).

Each Stage 1a re-verifies every fact under "Starting point" and every Phase 7–10 interface it uses against the code it finds, and records drift as a "Workflow 11A (or 11B … 11G) Stage 1a amendment".

**Workflow 6D.** On 2026-10-29 the newest Phase 6 entry of `docs/PROGRESS.md` is Workflow 6C done, and Workflow 6D is still running in the same tree. `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6d.md` is not published. The newest evidence on `main` is TDD files, not 6D's calibration, so it is only interim:
- `6ded0d33`: 100,000 names at 63.2 ms (within 100 ms), and 2,000,000 names at 326.6 ms against 250 ms (missed by 76.6 ms, with its profile);
- `bd8adabd`: the re-screen at a median of 64.5 s against 60 s on a loaded machine, and 54.6 s at load 2.5.

C271's missed-budget branch (`matcher-c787-11d`) therefore looks likely (re-read at `a3b478aa`; verification round), but the C78.7 item stays planned as conditional (C271), sized only from 6D's published report: `contracts-11d` reads 6D's published calibration and PROGRESS entry first and sizes the work from them; if 6D met every budget, no optimisation agent starts, but the budgets are still measured at 11D's base and head, and the head's figure is the release's verdict (review H09).

**Review of this plan.** As for Phases 6–10, three adversarial reviews (privacy, security and law; honesty and product coverage; feasibility and fit with the code) read the first draft, and `docs/plans/phase-11-review-resolution.md` disposes of each of their 92 findings; this revision carries the fixes. A verification round then checked each fix against this text. It found 7 findings partly resolved and 16 new defects (V01–V16), and this revision carries those fixes too, recorded in the resolution's "Verification round" section. The decisions' "Review" subsection records the counts and the main changes.

## Summary

OpenVerify verifies people and businesses for one organisation at a time; nothing lets an organisation that has verified someone share that result with a partner, and the consent record it keeps names no recipient, no purpose and no text. The platform's security work exists piece by piece (envelope encryption with row-bound associated data, hashed tokens, Argon2id with TOTP and WebAuthn, an SSRF guard, gitleaks, pinned downloads), but there is no threat model, no ASVS checklist, no dependency, image or workflow scanning, no SBOM, no production image, no deployment beyond a development compose file, no HTTP load test, and the documentation PLAN §10 requires (self-hosting, compliance, the check catalog, the API reference, the threat model, the migration mapping) is unwritten. Phase 11 adds them, each at its tier (ADR 0002):
- **R** (real logic): connections, share policies, share tokens with peek, clone and hydrate, consent records on both sides with withdrawal, the `share-consent` step on the web and on Android, provenance on every cloned object; console idle timeouts, a shared login limiter, pepper rotation, OIDC sign-in, a locked migration under its own role, readiness probes, per-tenant queue quotas, the DSAR access export; production images, the Helm chart, scanning, SBOMs, the release workflow; the load harness; the generated documents and their tests.
- **H** (heuristic, never a result): nothing new. A cloned verification keeps its source's tier, stage and provenance; it is never re-labelled as a fresh pass.
- **A** (an adapter with a deterministic sandbox): nothing new. OIDC sign-in is tested against a local, in-process identity provider; real identity providers are the operator's.

**Goals** (each a contract below):
1. **Reusable KYC between organisations on one install** (C230–C244): the vendor's eight Connect paths, its token lifecycle and its events at parity except the `ready` prefetch (review H18), plus ours, labelled: a versioned share policy per connection accepted at activation, consent records on both sides naming the recipient with the exact text's digest and a stored copy, explicit single use and revocation, withdrawal, no token in any URL, and a cloned pass that never satisfies a required check unless the destination template opts in.
2. **The platform gaps closed in code** (C245–C256): every ASVS day-one gap that code can close (G-IDLE, G-LOGIN, G-TLS's warnings, G-REGEX, B7-E1's role split, B3-S1's pepper rotation), SSO (OIDC), the internal listener, per-tenant quotas, the DSAR access export, and the Phase 6 ingestion carries.
3. **A deployable, scanned, reproducible release** (C257–C267): production images per process, digest-pinned bases and checksummed tools, per-push scanning within a stated minute budget, SBOMs, a Helm chart with restricted Pod Security and default-deny network policies, tests on Kubernetes 1.35–1.37 and Helm 4.3.1 and 3.22.1, and a release workflow that stages everything and publishes nothing until the owner decides (Q1–Q4).
4. **Measured performance** (C268–C272): an HTTP load test with vegeta and a small driver of ours (k6 is AGPL and fails rule 4; Locust waits for Q12), the budgets of hardening 6.4 recorded and never gated in shared CI, the matcher's write cost reduced and measured, the C78.7 gap (if 6D leaves one) worked beside the load test, and `jit = off` for the application role measured before it is recommended.
5. **The complete documentation set** (C273–C282): the threat model and the ASVS and MASVS checklists as tested files, the security records, the self-hosting guide, the compliance guide (not legal advice), the generated check catalog and API reference, the generated migration mapping, the sharing guide, and every record correction (PLAN §3 and §6 lose k6).
6. **A release the owner can tag** (C283–C288 and the release checklist): DAST against the running stack, a review of the whole phase, every deferral of every plan placed, and a checklist that says what is true and what still waits for the owner.

What lands:
- **Connect** (C230–C244): `connect/connection` (`cxn_`), `connect/share-token` (`cnst_`), the share policy (`cxnp_`, ours) and the consent record (`cnsr_`, ours) with its stored texts; connections created by the source, activated by the destination only in the console by a user with a second factor, deactivated by either side, reactivated by the source to `pending`, never deleted; tokens of 60 s to 21 days (default 14 days), single use under a row lock, expired by the source, by deactivation or by withdrawal, sandbox to sandbox and production to production; peek with `{visibility, value}` behind its own scope; redemption as a 202 and a job, clone of inquiries and six verification types, hydrate of accounts, inquiries and transactions, field mappings and account linking; `provenance` on every cloned object; `accept-cloned-results` on the destination template, off by default; the `share-consent` step in flow-spec v2, rendered by the web flow and by the Android SDK in `commonMain`; client-side redemption only through an inquiry the destination's backend creates with `meta.share-token-id`; the vendor's four token events plus ours for expiry, failure, connections, consent and source redaction; seven scopes, two rate policies, one indistinguishable 404, audit entries on both sides; the console's Connect pages; three workflow actions and three triggers.
- **Platform hardening** (C245–C256): a 30-minute idle timeout beside the 12-hour absolute lifetime; sessions ended when a user is disabled, loses a role, resets MFA or changes a password; the login limiter's windows in Valkey, failing closed for login only; API-key pepper rotation with a previous pepper and lazy re-hashing; a clone or a new key never exceeding its creator's scopes; OIDC sign-in with PKCE, organisation-scoped providers and just-in-time membership off by default (SAML and SCIM in the next release); TLS settings for every data store with production warnings on plain text; a migration role that owns DDL and a runtime role that cannot create objects, with a session advisory lock around `db:migrate`; `/ready`, a worker heartbeat and `OPENVERIFY_INTERNAL_LISTEN`; per-environment admission caps for every queue that holds tenant work; every operator-supplied pattern run through Phase 9's linear-time matcher; a per-account access export; the UN list's opt-in with its acknowledgement; conditional requests on list syncs; HSTS documented and set by the chart; certificate pinning on Android as a documented transport recipe.
- **Supply chain and deployment** (C257–C267): `openverify/api`, `openverify/dashboard`, `openverify/flow`, `openverify/vision` and `openverify/code-runner` images, non-root, read-only, production dependencies only, bases pinned by digest; the compose `app` profile on them; osv-scanner, `pnpm audit` and `pnpm audit signatures`, pip-audit, zizmor, Trivy `config` and the PII-pattern scan per push, each installed as a checksummed binary, with an expiring allowlist and remediation time frames; ESLint with `eslint-plugin-security`, ruff's `S` rules and Android lint as the SAST baseline; Syft, cdxgen and the CycloneDX Gradle plugin for SBOMs; the chart under `deploy/helm/openverify/`; `release.yml`, which builds, scans, signs nothing and stages everything until 11G.
- **Performance** (C268–C272): `load/` (a uv project with an `httpx` driver; Locust 2.46.7 only under Q12) and vegeta v12.13.0; scenarios for reads, writes, uploads, logins, verifications, webhooks, screenings, the re-screen, fairness and a soak; the reports in `docs/calibration/load-phase11.md`; the incremental key-frequency rebuild, a measured re-key batch size and GIN pending-list handling; C78.7 if 6D leaves it open.
- **Docs and records** (C273–C282): `docs/security/` (threat model, ASVS and MASVS checklists, cryptographic inventory, data classification, logging inventory, vulnerability policy), `docs/self-hosting/`, `docs/compliance/`, `docs/checks/catalog.md`, `docs/api/README.md`, `docs/migration/`, `docs/connect/`; ADR 0010; PLAN, `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` and `persona-surface.md` corrected.
- **Owner-gated** (C262, C285, C280.6, C254.1, C289; Q1–Q12): publication of images, the chart, SBOMs and signatures to a registry; npm publication; the vendor's name in the migration page; the release-age correction for toolchain binaries; CodeQL; model weights inside images; counsel's review of the compliance guide and the SFace licence review; the UN list's opt-in.

ADR 0010, "Connect, release hardening and deployment", records six rules:
- **one install, one witness:** sharing moves data between tenants of one install, server-side; the install's operator is the trust anchor both sides rely on, the docs say so, and sharing between installs waits for a signed envelope in a later release;
- **no share without a recorded consent:** every token cites a consent record of the source environment that names its recipient, purpose and categories, holds the digest of the exact text shown (or is labelled attested and not witnessed), and has not been withdrawn; a redemption into a flow records the destination's own consent;
- **a copy is never a fresh pass:** a cloned verification keeps its source's statuses, checks, tiers and stages with `provenance`, and counts toward the destination's required checks only where the destination's template opts in;
- **nothing published without the owner:** images, charts, SBOMs, signatures, provenance and npm packages are built and staged by CI; publication waits for the owner's registry, identity and visibility decisions;
- **tools follow rule 4:** every scanner, load tester and chart tool is permissive, pinned and checksummed, installed as a binary or through a lockfile, never through a mutable third-party action (the Trivy compromise of 2026-03-19);
- **self-assessed, never certified:** the threat model and the ASVS and MASVS checklists are the project's own assessment with evidence paths, re-reviewed at every phase end; no certification is claimed.

## Starting point (interim, from the research notes and the code; re-taken at the Phase 11 base)

The research notes read the code at `91c83e02`; this plan re-read the facts below at `a63128c7` (Phase 6 being closed by Workflow 6D, Phases 7–10 planned and not built). Each row gives its source. The full inventory at the Phase 11 base (Phase 10 done) is `contracts-11a`'s first deliverable, written to `docs/research/phase11-inventory.md` with a `path:line` for every claim; `contracts-11b` to `contracts-11g` extend it. In short:

| Area | Exists | Does not exist |
|---|---|---|
| Tenancy | `organizations` (`org_`) with at most one `sandbox` and one `production` `environments` row each (`environments_organization_id_kind_key`), environment `settings` jsonb with the C16 retention keys; every resource environment-scoped (`packages/db/src/tables/tenancy.ts`) | No relation between two organisations; no organisation-level setting for sharing |
| Ids | `ID_PREFIXES` with 30 prefixes through Phase 6 (`org`, `env`, `key`, `usr`, `uss`, `inq`, `iqse`, `ver`, `acc`, `rep`, …), ULIDs (48-bit time, 80 random bits) (`packages/contracts/src/ids.ts:11-57`); Phases 7–10 add theirs | No `cxn`, `cnst`, `cxnp`, `cnsr` |
| API keys and scopes | 28 `API_SCOPES` and 8 `OPERATOR_PERMISSIONS` (`pii:view`, `users:*`, `roles:*`, `settings:*`, `environments:production`) (`packages/core/src/permissions.ts`); keys `ov_sbx_`/`ov_prod_` with 256 random bits, stored as HMAC-SHA-256 under a pepper of at least 32 bytes, scopes, expiry, up to 50 allowed IPs, `clone` and `expire` routes (`packages/db/src/api-keys.ts`, `apps/api/src/routes/server/api-keys.ts`); Phases 7–9 add scopes and the role editor | No `connect-*` scope; no pepper rotation (a pepper change invalidates every key); no test that a clone or a new key stays within the creator's scopes |
| Encryption at rest | Envelope encryption, a fresh 256-bit DEK per value under AES-256-GCM wrapped by the KEK (`OPENVERIFY_KEK`, retired keys in `OPENVERIFY_KEK_PREVIOUS`), 96-bit random IVs with the 2^32 budget documented, HKDF key ids (`packages/db/src/crypto.ts`); row-bound sealing `sealValue(column, rowId)` with AAD `<table>.<column>#<row id>` (`packages/db/src/sealed.ts`); `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` listing 39 columns with their binding, which drives `kek:rewrap` and the schema tests (`packages/db/src/encrypted-columns.ts`); blind index under per-environment subkeys (`blind-index.ts`) | The KEK and every secret are environment variables only (no `*_FILE` form, no KMS adapter); Phase 1 columns are column-bound, not row-bound (accepted, B7-T1) |
| Consent | One record per flow session in `inquiry_sessions.consent` (accepted time, `text_keys`, `optional_text_keys`, locale, sealed IP and user agent) (`packages/db/src/tables/inquiries.ts`); Phase 9 adds the text digests, copy source and catalog version (C162.9), Phase 10 the Android rows (C200) | No purpose, recipient, data categories, stored text, withdrawal or consent id a share could cite (connect 2.5) |
| Redaction and retention | Irreversible, idempotent redaction of accounts, inquiries, reports (Phase 6 `redact` actions name their actor) and transactions (Phase 7); the daily C16 pass: inquiries by template or environment retention, storage leftovers, API logs, deliveries, events, idempotency keys, user sessions and one-time links 30 days past expiry, reports, face templates at 365 days (`apps/api/src/services/retention.ts:1-25`) | No access or portability export (H14); nothing for shares |
| Audit | `audit_logs` append-only by a trigger that rejects every UPDATE, per environment, sealed IP and user agent, a `NULL` actor for the system (`packages/db/src/tables/audit-logs.ts:11`) | No shipping off-box (G-LOGINV) |
| Sessions and login | Console sessions `ovu_` of 12 h absolute (`USER_SESSION_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS`), flow sessions 24 h, one-time links 5 min–30 days, MFA tokens 5 min, invitations 7 days (`packages/contracts/src/tokens.ts:21-36`); Argon2id (19 MiB, t=2, p=1), TOTP, WebAuthn, recovery codes, `user:reset-mfa`; login limits 50 per address and 10 per email in 15 minutes **in process memory** (`apps/api/src/operator-auth/login-rate-limiter.ts`, "Phase 2 can move the counters to Valkey"); C4 login carries no organisation, so it checks every account with that email (`operator-auth.ts:283`, the Phase 1 carry) | No idle timeout (G-IDLE); no SSO; the login limit multiplies with replicas (G-LOGIN) |
| Rate limits | GCRA in Valkey per principal, policies `api` 300, `flow-session` 120, `flow-ip` 600, `one-time-link-redeem` 30, `reports` 600 a minute, failing open when Valkey is down (`packages/contracts/src/rate-limits.ts`, `apps/api/src/rate-limit/`; Phase 2 decision) | No `connect-*` policy; no per-tenant queue quota (H1) |
| Browser headers | Console: `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `nosniff`, a restrictive Permissions-Policy (`apps/dashboard/next.config.ts`); Phase 9 adds the nonce script CSP (C170). Flow: a build-time `<meta>` CSP (`apps/flow/src/security/security-meta.ts`); Phase 9's flow server sends `frame-ancestors`, Permissions-Policy and Referrer-Policy per request (C157) | No HSTS anywhere (expected from the TLS terminator, undocumented) |
| Outbound | One SSRF guard for webhooks and providers (vetted DNS answers, no redirects, 1 MiB cap, 10 s, private ranges refused in production) (`apps/api/src/webhooks/ssrf.ts`, `apps/api/src/http/index.ts`) | No egress policy as a second layer (a cluster concern) |
| Containers | `infra/docker-compose.yml`: postgres (pgvector pg17), redis (Valkey), objectstore (RustFS) with bucket init and versioning, mailpit, and the `app` profile's migrate, api, worker, vision, flow and dashboard; every port on loopback; vision isolated (internal network, uid 10001, read-only root, `cap_drop: ALL`, `no-new-privileges`, 4 GiB) with `infra/tests/vision-container.test.sh`; Postgres never logs bind parameters. `infra/docker/apps.Dockerfile` is one development image for every TypeScript process (`node:22.22.1-bookworm-slim` by tag, dev dependencies kept for `tsx`); `services/vision/Dockerfile` multi-stage (`ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:0.13.3`, `python:3.12-slim` by tag) | No production image, no digest pin, no resource limits on api, worker, flow, dashboard; one shared Valkey password, no TLS; no chart (`infra/` holds no Helm, PLAN §3 "Helm (later)") |
| Migrations and probes | `db:migrate` applies Drizzle migrations through `0021_watchlist_check_backoff.sql` and backfills event object ids with the KEK; one `DATABASE_URL` for migrations and the runtime; `/health` only (`apps/api/src/routes/system.ts:16`); SIGTERM drains the server and stops the worker | No advisory lock in `packages/db/src/migrate.ts` (two runs are not serialised); no migration role; no `/ready`; no worker health signal |
| Configuration | `apps/api/src/config.ts` reads 56 distinct `OPENVERIFY_*` variables (on 146 lines) and refuses placeholder secrets in production; `apps/api/src` as a whole reads 141 distinct names, among them every provider binding and secret composed at run time (`OPENVERIFY_PROVIDER_<NAME>_<MODE>`, `apps/api/src/providers/`), `OPENVERIFY_WORKER_CONCURRENCY` (`jobs/worker-config.ts:11`) and the seed variables; the dashboard reads 9, the flow 1, vision 3 (review F10, at `6cceece3`) | No generated configuration reference |
| CI | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, 13 jobs on a full run (`node`, `unit-api` ×2 shards, `unit-api-coverage`, `unit-dashboard`, `format`, `integration`, `infra`, `vision` with three logged, never gated benchmarks, `android`, `android-instrumented`, `e2e`, `secrets`), about 100 billed minutes; `permissions: contents: read`, every action pinned by commit, `persist-credentials: false`, actionlint inside `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`; Phase 10 adds `android-release.yml` (dispatch only) | No dependency audit, workflow lint (zizmor), container or configuration scan, SBOM, SAST beyond ESLint and ruff, DAST, provenance, PII-pattern scan |
| Secrets in git | gitleaks 8.30.1 as a binary pinned by SHA-256 twice (`infra/scripts/install-gitleaks.sh`), a fail-closed pre-commit hook (`.githooks/pre-commit`), a full-history scan in the `secrets` job | No PII-pattern rules (H15) |
| Downloads | `scripts/fetch_models.py`, `fetch_data.py`, `fetch_fixtures.py`, `fetch_trust_anchors.sh` pin URL, SHA-256, size and SPDX licence (the hyphenated `scripts/fetch-*` of `CLAUDE.md` rule 4 are these underscored files) | No fetch entry for the ASVS export, the scanners or the load tools |
| Performance | `pnpm --filter @openverify/watchlist benchmark:performance` (with `benchmark:quality`, `benchmark:render`, `benchmark:test`); the C78.7 gate in `apps/api/src/watchlists/` over `test-support/heavy-headed-list.ts` (6D moves the table into `packages/watchlist/src/testing/heavy-headed.ts`); budgets 100 ms p95 at 100,000 names, 250 ms at 2,000,000, the re-screen 60 s at 1,000 changed entries and 200,000 subjects; 6C measured 109.5 ms, 399.4 ms and 91.0 s (`docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md`); 6D's report not yet published | No HTTP load test, per-route budget or worker throughput figure; the write cost (an incremental key-frequency rebuild, 184 s a sync at 2,000,000 names; the re-key, 2,414 s; GIN pending lists) carried by decision 9 after 6C |
| Docs | `docs/api/openapi.json` (OpenAPI 3.1.0, 121 paths at `91c83e02`, generated from zod), `docs/checks/*.md` (9 families), `docs/reports/*.md` (10), `docs/research/` (27 files with `persona-surface.md` and the Phase 10 notes), `docs/calibration/` (5 reports), `docs/tdd/` (124 evidence files); Phases 7–10 add `docs/flow/handoff.md`, `docs/compliance/automated-decisions.md`, `docs/flows/`, `docs/android/`, `docs/accessibility/` | No `docs/security/`, `docs/self-hosting/`, `docs/compliance/` index, check catalog, migration mapping or sharing guide |
| Licences | `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` §1–§5 with the weak-copyleft tooling exceptions and the Excluded list (MinIO, Redis 7.4+, gitleaks-action, gradle/actions, PyMuPDF, poppler, Ghostscript, InsightFace packs, …); SFace's legal review open (line 291) | No row for any scanner, load tool or chart tool; k6, the Semgrep engine and rules and Opengrep not yet excluded |
| Records | ADRs 0001–0005 (Phases 7–10 write 0006–0009); PLAN §6 Phase 11 names "k6 load test" (line 448); PLAN §3 data protection lists "DSAR export" (line 155) and "env/KMS adapter" | No ADR for Connect or deployment |

Records Phase 11 corrects are handled in C282. Items earlier plans and the progress log carried to Phase 11, to "hardening" or to "a later phase" are connect K1–K12, hardening H1–H22 and this plan's own search of `docs/plans/*.md` and `docs/PROGRESS.md` (Phase 9 C175.2 and C177, Phase 10 C223 and decisions 38, 47, 48, 50, Phase 0 and 1 carries, Phase 2 line 849, Phase 4 decision 8); C288 places every one of them (decision 70).

## Patterns to mirror

| Category | Source | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| Contracts | `packages/contracts/src/*.ts`, `contracts/fixtures/api/` | Zod schemas as the source of truth, golden fixtures per resource and problem, kebab-case attributes, JSON:API-style documents (`data`, `attributes`, `relationships`), the OpenAPI document generated from the schemas (`openapi:write`) and checked unchanged in Validation |
| Errors | `apps/api/src/problem-details.ts`, `apps/api/src/errors.ts` and the problem fixtures | RFC 9457 `application/problem+json` with a stable `code`, no PII and no fraud detail; one generic 404 for every object the caller may not see |
| Routes | `apps/api/src/routes/server/*.ts`, `routes/flow/*.ts`, `routes/dashboard/*.ts` | Fastify plugins (`app.register`), one permission per route, the C12 idempotency plugin on `POST`/`PATCH`, environment-scoped repositories, events staged in the transaction |
| Data access | `packages/db/src/repositories/*.ts`, `sealed.ts`, `encrypted-columns.ts` | Repository functions over Drizzle; PII through `sealValue`/`sealJson` with row AAD and a row in `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` (and the rewrap list); one migration per Workflow with its `meta` entry; Testcontainers race tests for every lock |
| Jobs | `apps/api/src/jobs/*.ts`, `jobs/report-queues.ts` | BullMQ jobs carrying ids only, the worker re-reading Postgres; failures described by class and code (`describeJobError`); fenced claims and `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` sweeps |
| Tokens and one-time links | `packages/contracts/src/tokens.ts`, `apps/api/src/services/one-time-links.ts` | A prefix plus 256 CSPRNG bits, SHA-256 or HMAC at rest; single use under a row lock; one generic refusal |
| Logging | `apps/api/src/jobs/logger.ts`, pino with redaction paths | Ids, codes and durations only; no field value, body, token or name; a log test with positive controls (Phase 6 R003) |
| Shell tools in CI | `infra/scripts/install-gitleaks.sh`, `infra/tests/install-gitleaks.test.sh`, `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` | A pinned version, a SHA-256 checked twice (release checksums and an in-repo pin), an installer test, the workflow contract test asserting pins, permissions and steps |
| Fetch scripts | `scripts/fetch_models.py`, `models/manifest.json` | Standard library only, `python3 -I`, URL, SHA-256, size and SPDX licence pinned, the file written only after its digest matches, never committed |
| Generated documents | `docs/api/openapi.json` with `openapi:write` | A generator in the repository, its output committed, a test that fails when the committed file differs from a fresh run |
| Benchmarks | `packages/watchlist/scripts/benchmark-*.mjs`, `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md` | Committed method before the seed, three takes and the median, the load average beside every timing, the e2e lock, base beside head, budgets never moved to fit |
| Console | Phase 9's shell (`renderPage`, `DataTable`, settings pages) and the `OptionalSection` slots the Phase 7 and Phase 8 plans name, RSC payload tests | Server components through the BFF, `pii:view` masking proven by payload tests, every problem code in words |
| Flow steps | Phase 9 C147 (flow-spec v2), C151 (engine), C162.9 (consent digests); Phase 10 C194 (step registry), C200 | A step type in the v2 union with its client view, a server route per exit, the server deciding the position; the Android registry entry in `commonMain` with host tests |
| Workflow actions | Phase 8 C122 (the action registry), C118 (triggers), C120 (runs) | An action key with a zod input, a typed output in the scope, idempotent effects, an audit entry |
| Tests | Vitest colocated, Testcontainers, Playwright on the reusable stack with shards, JUnit 5 and Robolectric in `androidHostTest`, kotlin.test in `commonTest`, shell contract tests in `infra/tests/` | RED commit first; ≥ 80% per package and module; `docs/tdd/phase11-<agent>.tdd.md` |

## Locked contracts

C1–C229 stay locked except where a section below amends them. Stated amendments of earlier contracts:
- **C3 and Phase 9 C151 (the flow API):** the client view of the new `share-consent` step (C238.3) and its three exits, `POST /api/v1/flow/share-consents/:step-id/accept`, `/decline` and `/withdraw` (C237.6); the session response gains one member, `withdrawable-share-consents` (C237.6), and nothing else. The withdraw exit is the one flow action allowed on a step already passed, and it never moves the position: an amendment of Phase 9 C151.4 and C151.12 (verification V02).
- **Phase 9 C147 (flow-spec v2):** `share-consent` joins the step union without a format change (Phase 9 C175.2), with its configuration (C238.1) and the Connect publish checks of C238.2's hook (`share_consent_connection_invalid` with its warning `share_consent_connection_not_active`, `share_consent_after_completion`, `share_consent_after_cloned_step`, `share_consent_connect_disabled`, `cloned_results_without_binding`, `share_consent_withdrawal_contact_missing`, `share_consent_retention_missing`) and acknowledgements (`cloned-results`, `cloned-results-unbound`, `share-consent-default-text`; verification V09); `contracts/fixtures/flow-spec/` gains its golden documents.
- **Phase 10 C194 (the Android step registry):** a `share-consent` renderer; `android/openverify-sdk/flow-spec-members.json` lists its members (C238.6).
- **C10 and the inquiry create route:** `meta.share-token-id` on `POST /api/v1/inquiries` (C238.4).
- **C12 (rate limits, idempotency):** the policies `connect-redeem`, `connect-peek` (C240.3) and `account-export` (C253.7) join `RATE_LIMIT_POLICIES`; the C4 login limiter moves to Valkey as its own two-window limiter, outside `RATE_LIMIT_POLICIES` (C246; review F19).
- **C14 and C70.4 (events):** the `connect` subject family with names containing `/` (`connect/share-token.*`, `connect/connection.*`) and `consent-record.created`, `consent-record.declined` and `consent-record.withdrawn` (C239.1; verification V09); `eventSubject` maps every `connect/` head (C239.4); the existing `inquiry.*` payloads gain an optional `provenance` member, present on a cloned inquiry (C235.7).
- **C15 (permissions):** seven `connect-*` scopes and one operator permission (`connect-connections:activate`, C240.1), the connection writes and activation organisation-level in Phase 9's `PERMISSION_LEVEL` (C231.9); `accounts:export` (C253.1); `PERMISSION_INFO` labels each.
- **C16 (retention, redaction):** connect rows and consent records join the retention pass and the redaction cascade (C241); the environment settings `share-token-max-seconds` and `consent-proof-retention-days` (C232.3, C241.3).
- **C4 and C15 (operator auth):** the idle timeout, the session-ending events (C245), OIDC sign-in (C248) and an optional `organization` hint on login (C248.8).
- **C2 (API keys):** `OPENVERIFY_API_KEY_PEPPER_PREVIOUS` and lazy re-hashing (C247.1); the creator's-scope bound on create and clone (C247.3).
- **Phase 8 C117.8 and its fairness rule:** the per-environment admission cap is extended from workflow steps to every queue that holds tenant work (C251), with one shared slots table.
- **Phase 8 C122 (the action registry) and C118 (triggers):** `create_share_token`, `redeem_share_token`, `expire_share_token` and the triggers `connect/share-token.inbound-created`, `consent-record.withdrawn` and `connect/share-token.consent-withdrawn` (C243); Phase 8's condition evaluator treats a cloned check as absent unless counted (C235.7).
- **Phase 9 analytics:** cloned checks and verifications are excluded from pass rates and failure distributions (C235.7).
- **Phase 6 C73, C76 (ingestion):** the UN source's opt-in (C254.1) and conditional requests (C254.2), amending decision 21 after 6B and adding to decision 10.
- **Phase 5 C60.2 (vision isolation):** reproduced in the chart and asserted by a chart test (C264.4).
- **Phase 9 C157 (the flow server):** HSTS as an optional header behind `OPENVERIFY_HSTS_MAX_AGE` (the one variable for the API, the flow server and the console; review P28) for installs without a TLS terminator that sets it (C255.1); `/api/v1/internal/*` served on `OPENVERIFY_INTERNAL_LISTEN` when set (C250.3).
- **Phase 9 C177 and C175.2:** SSO is OIDC only in this release; SAML and SCIM move to the next release (C286).
- **PLAN §3 and §6 Phase 11:** vegeta and a driver of ours replace k6 (Locust under Q12); the chart, scanners and images are named; the KEK is read from an environment variable or a file, and a KMS adapter is the next release's (C282).

### C230. Connect resources, ids, tables and the share policy (connect R1, R2, R5)

1. **Ids** (connect R1): `cxn` (connection) and `cnst` (share token), the vendor's prefixes; `cxnp` (a share-policy version) and `cnsr` (a consent record), ours. All four join `ID_PREFIXES` and both prefix tables, with the existing prefix test. Consent texts are keyed by their SHA-256 and have no public id.
2. **The connection resource** (`connect/connection`; connect 1.2, R2, R6, R10): attributes `status` (`pending`, `active`, `inactive`; `archived` is in the enum for parity, never listed or retrieved directly, and set by nothing in this release, since OpenVerify deletes no organisation), `source-organization-id`, `destination-organization-id`, `created-at`, `updated-at` (the vendor's); ours: `source-organization-name` and `destination-organization-name` (each organisation's display name, NFC as Phase 10 C200.2 serves it), `activated-at`, `deactivated-at`, `deactivated-by` (`source` or `destination`), `share-policy` (the current version's body with its `version`), `accepted-policy-version` (the version the destination last accepted, or `null`), `agreement-reference` (C231.3). Relationship `creator` (`user` or `api-key`), shown to the source only. `destination-organization-name` is `null` in every answer to the source until the connection is first activated (review P09: a create is not a name oracle); the destination always sees the source's name, which it needs to decide. One connection per ordered pair of organisations; a self-connection (source equals destination) is allowed in both environment kinds for testing without a partner (connect R2).
3. **The share policy** (ours; connect R5, 2.3): a versioned body, frozen per version, validated by a zod schema:

   ```ts
   const sharePolicy = z.strictObject({
     purpose: z.literal('share.reuse-kyc'),                    // closed list, one value this release
     'source-types': z.array(z.enum(['inquiry', 'account', 'transaction', 'verification'])).min(1),
     verifications: z.strictObject({
       types: z.array(z.enum(['government-id', 'selfie', 'database', 'document', 'email-address', 'phone-number'])),
       'extracted-data': z.boolean(),                          // names, dates, numbers, addresses read from the evidence
       'check-results': z.boolean(),
       files: z.boolean(),                                     // document images and other uploads
     }),
     fields: z.strictObject({                                  // allowlists of field keys; no wildcard
       inquiry: z.array(fieldKey).max(200), account: z.array(fieldKey).max(200), transaction: z.array(fieldKey).max(200),
     }),
     biometric: z.boolean(),                                   // selfie images, ID portraits, liveness and face-match results
     peek: z.record(peekPath, z.enum(['full', 'presence'])),   // only paths the policy shares; default presence; at most 400
     'attested-consent': z.enum(['none', 'non-biometric', 'all']).default('non-biometric'), // review P03: which shares an attested source consent may authorise
   });
   ```

   Rules: `selfie` in `verifications.types` requires `biometric: true`; with `biometric: false`, government-ID portraits and every face-match and liveness check are left out of a share even when `check-results` and `files` are on; face templates are never shareable whatever the policy says (connect 2.2, decision 23); a `peek` path outside what the policy shares is refused; `attested-consent` (ours, review P03) says which source consents the destination accepts at activation: `none` (only `flow` records), `non-biometric` (the default: an `attested` record may authorise a share without `biometric`), `all`. Errors: 422 `share_policy_invalid` with JSON pointers.
4. **Data categories** (ours; connect 2.6): each share carries a closed set computed from the policy and the source: `identity-fields`, `contact-fields`, `other-fields` (by each field's type: email and phone types are contact, names, dates, addresses and identification numbers identity, anything else other), `document-images`, `biometric`, `check-results`. A consent covers a share only if its categories include every category the share carries (C232.1). **The map is code** (review F07): `contracts-11a` commits `DATA_CLASSES`, `SHARE_CATEGORIES` and `FIELD_TYPE_CLASS` in `packages/core/src/connect/categories.ts`, frozen, with a table test over every field type; `connect-logic` uses it, and C276.2's data classification is generated from it, never the other way round. **Fixed at creation** (review H04): the token row stores the categories computed at creation (`data_categories text[]`, `explicit_biometric`), and the redeem job copies nothing outside them (C234.2).
5. **Tables** (one migration, `00NN_phase11_connect.sql`, numbered at the Phase 11 base; review of `meta`):
   - `connect_connections (id, source_organization_id, destination_organization_id, status, creator_type, creator_id, current_policy_id, accepted_policy_id, agreement_reference sealed, activated_at, activated_by_user_id, deactivated_at, deactivated_by, created_at, updated_at)`, organisation-scoped (ours: the vendor's connection is between organisations, and each organisation has up to two environments), with a unique index on `(source_organization_id, destination_organization_id)`;
   - `connect_share_policies (id, connection_id, version, body jsonb, created_at, created_by_type, created_by_id)`, unique `(connection_id, version)`, never updated;
   - `connect_share_tokens (environment_id, id, connection_id, policy_id, environment_kind, destination_environment_id, source_type, source_id, consent_record_id, data_categories text[], explicit_biometric, peek_count, status, expiry_reason, failure_reason, redeem_mode, destination_type, destination_id, destination_consent_record_id, reserved_inquiry_id, redeem_summary jsonb, creator_type, creator_id, expires_at, pending_at, redeemed_at, expired_at, failed_at, created_at, updated_at)`, scoped to the source environment, with an index on `(destination_environment_id, status, created_at, id)` for inbound lists and a partial index on `expires_at` where `status = 'created'`;
   - `consent_records (environment_id, id, side, outcome, purpose, subject_type, subject_id, connection_id, share_token_id, recipient_organization_id, recipient_name, data_categories text[], explicit_biometric, text_locale, text_keys text[], text_sha256, biometric_text_sha256, attested_text_body sealed, method, session_id, evidence_ip sealed, evidence_user_agent sealed, evidence_reference sealed, accepted_at, attested_at, attested_by_type, attested_by_id, expires_at, withdrawn_at, withdrawn_via, withdrawal_reason, redacted_at, created_at)`;
   - `consent_texts (environment_id, sha256, locale, body sealed, created_at)`, primary key `(environment_id, sha256)`, insert-if-absent, never updated; `body` is sealed with the row AAD `consent_texts.body#<environment id>:<sha256>` (review P11). It holds only the server-composed `flow` texts; an `attested` text is the operator's own words and is stored on its record (`attested_text_body`), so the record's redaction deletes it;
   - **storage for provenance and reliance** (review F08), on existing tables, each column named by the inventory: `provenance jsonb` on `inquiries`, `verifications`, the check-result rows (or the verification's checks document, wherever Phase 3 keeps them) and `files`; `hydrated_fields jsonb` (keys and the token id) on `accounts`, `inquiries` and Phase 7's transactions; `relied_on_cloned` and `share_token_id` on Phase 8's decision record; `accept-cloned-results` inside the template version's definition document (Phase 9's publish schema), so it needs no column.

   Every sealed column uses row AAD and joins `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` and the rewrap list (the schema test's expected set changes in the same commit).
6. **Honesty:** the policy states what the platform will copy; it says nothing about what the destination does with a copy.

### C231. Connections: create, activate, deactivate, reactivate (connect R2–R4, R6, R27; 1.2)

1. **Create** (`POST /api/v1/connect/connections`, scope `connect-connections:write`, the source): body `data.attributes.destination-organization-id` (the vendor's) and `share-policy` (ours, required; decision 17). 201 with `status: pending` and policy version 1. Errors: 422 `destination_not_found` (no organisation with that id on this install, **or one that has not opted in to inbound connections**, byte-identical, C231.10); 409 `connection_exists` (the pair already has a connection, whatever its status). Event `connect/connection.created` in both organisations' environments (C239).
2. **List and retrieve** (`GET /connect/connections`, `GET /connect/connections/:id`, `connect-connections:read`): both directions; filters `status` and `destination-organization-id` (the vendor's), cursor pages; a third organisation gets the generic 404.
3. **Activate** (ours in mechanism, the vendor's rule; connect R3, R27; decisions 18, 26): only the destination, only in the console. The BFF route `POST /api/v1/dashboard/connect/connections/:id/activate` accepts a user session only (an API key gets the generic 404, since the route is not on the server API), needs the operator permission `connect-connections:activate` (built-in `admin` only, organisation-level, C231.9), and Phase 9's step-up (C168.1: `user_sessions.mfa_verified_at` within the last 10 minutes, else 403 `step_up_required`; for an SSO session, the OIDC re-authentication of C248.6; review P06). A user with no factor at all, on a password session, cannot activate (403 `step_up_required`), whatever the organisation's `require-mfa`. Body: `policy-version` (must equal the current version, else 409 `share_policy_changed`), `agreement-reference` (1–500 characters, required: the reference of the data-sharing agreement between the two organisations; sealed; the platform does not judge it; its help text asks for the agreement that also records the transfer safeguards, such as Chapter V's standard contractual clauses, where the destination is established outside the source's jurisdiction (C278.2), and says not to enter personal data; review P13) and `acknowledged: true`. `pending` → `active`, `accepted-policy-version` set. A user of the source organisation cannot activate (the generic 404), and a self-connection is activated by the same organisation's admin.
4. **Deactivate** (`POST /connect/connections/:id/deactivate`, `connect-connections:write`, either side; connect R4): `pending` or `active` → `inactive`; 409 `connection_not_active` when already inactive. **In the same transaction** (ours; the vendor says only that access stops): every `created` token on the connection becomes `expired` with `expiry-reason: connection_deactivated`, with its events. A `pending` redemption fails at commit with `failure-reason: connection_inactive` (C234.2), because deactivation takes the connection row `FOR UPDATE` and the redeem job holds it `FOR SHARE` while it commits.
5. **Reactivate** (`POST /connect/connections/:id/reactivate`, the source only): `inactive` → `pending`; the destination must activate again, which the docs call the destination's re-acceptance (connect 1.2).
6. **Change the policy** (ours; `POST /connect/connections/:id/share-policy`, `connect-connections:write`, the source): creates version n+1; an `active` connection returns to `pending` until the destination accepts the new version; a `created` token keeps the version it was created under and redeems under it once the connection is active again; nothing is widened for an existing token. Event `connect/connection.share-policy-changed`.
7. **No deletion:** no delete route exists; the record stays for audit (connect R4).
8. **Honesty:** activation records that a named destination user accepted a stated policy and cited an agreement; it is not the end user's consent, which C236 records per share.
9. **Organisation-level writes** (review P01): a connection carries both environment kinds' tokens, so `connect-connections:write` and `connect-connections:activate` are organisation-level in Phase 9's `PERMISSION_LEVEL` (C167.2: effective only through an assignment with `environment-ids: null`). Create, deactivate, reactivate and the policy change are refused from a sandbox API key (`ov_sbx_`; the generic 404, as for activation by key) and from a user without `environments:production`. The C240.2 matrix has a case for each.
10. **Opt-in** (review P09; decision 71): Connect is off unless the install allows it (`OPENVERIFY_CONNECT_ENABLED`, default `false` in the images and the chart, `true` in `seed:dev`, `seed:load` and the e2e stack; while `false` every Connect route answers the generic 404 and the `share-consent` step fails publish with `share_consent_connect_disabled`), and an organisation receives connections only after its admin turns on the organisation-level setting `connect-inbound` (Phase 9 `SETTING_LEVEL`, default off, audited). A create towards an organisation that has not opted in answers exactly C231.1's unknown-id 422. The compliance guide's `roles.md` says that a hosting operator, processor for both organisations, moves data between them only on both controllers' instructions (C278.1).

### C232. Share tokens: create, list, retrieve, expire (connect R7–R11, R13, R14, R16; 1.4, 1.5, 3.2)

1. **Create** (`POST /api/v1/connect/share-tokens`, `connect-share-tokens:write`, in the source environment): the vendor's `connection-id`, `source-id` and `expires-in-seconds`, and ours `consent-record-id` (required; decision 19). The source type follows the id's prefix (`inq`, `acc`, `ver`, or Phase 7's transaction prefix). Checks, in this order, each with its problem:
   - the connection exists and the caller's organisation is its source (else the generic 404), and it is `active` (409 `connection_not_active`);
   - the destination organisation has an environment of the caller's kind (422 `environment_kind_mismatch`; connect R13: sandbox only to sandbox, production only to production);
   - the source object exists in the caller's environment (the generic 404) and is not redacted (409 `source_redacted`); a verification must be decided (409 `source_not_decided`); the source is not a copy and contains none (409 `source_is_copy`: a cloned inquiry, a cloned verification, or an inquiry holding one; review H03, decision 77, so a copy is never passed on under a new organisation's name);
   - the policy shares this source type, and for a verification its type (422 `share_source_not_allowed`); a report id is refused (422 `share_source_not_supported`; report sharing is deferred, C286);
   - the consent record (C236) is in the caller's environment, `side: source`, `outcome: accepted`, its subject is the source object or the inquiry that holds it (a `flow` record's subject is the inquiry whose session recorded it and covers only that inquiry's objects; an account subject is allowed only for an `attested` record, whose text the operator supplies; review P34), an `attested` record is allowed only where the policy's `attested-consent` admits it (`none` refuses every attested record, `non-biometric` refuses one for a share carrying `biometric`; reason `attested_not_accepted`; review P03), its recipient is the connection's destination, its purpose is the policy's, its categories cover the share's (C230.4), `explicit-biometric` is true when the share carries `biometric`, it is not withdrawn, not expired and not already cited by another token (422 `consent_required`, with a `reason` of `missing`, `subject`, `recipient`, `categories`, `explicit_biometric`, `attested_not_accepted`, `withdrawn`, `expired` or `already_used`; one consent authorises one token, ours, connect 2.1's specific-consent rule); the token stores the categories computed here (C230.4);
   - `expires-in-seconds` is 60 to 1,814,400 (21 days), default 1,209,600 (14 days) (connect R7, the vendor's range and default), at most the environment's `share-token-max-seconds` (60–1,814,400, default 1,814,400; decision 20), and the token may not outlive its consent (422 `share_token_outlives_consent`; without `expires-in-seconds` the token takes the earlier of 14 days and the consent's expiry).

   201 with `status: created`, `direction: outbound`. Events `connect/share-token.outbound-created` (source environment) and `connect/share-token.inbound-created` (the destination environment of the same kind).
2. **List** (`GET /connect/share-tokens`, `connect-share-tokens:read`): filters `connection-id`, `status`, `direction` (the vendor's); outbound tokens of the caller's environment and inbound tokens whose destination environment is the caller's; cursor pages; `source-data` never in a list.
3. **Retrieve** (`GET /connect/share-tokens/:id`): the source sees `creator`, `source` and `consent-record`; the destination sees neither `creator` nor `source` (connect R10) and, after redemption, `destination`; both sides see `data-categories` and `source-consent {method, witnessed, categories, explicit-biometric, text-sha256}` (ours, review P03: the destination can see that a share rests on an attestation; no evidence, no subject); `peek-source-data=true` is C233.
4. **Expire** (`POST /connect/share-tokens/:id/expire`, `connect-share-tokens:write`, the source only; connect R11): `created` → `expired` with `expiry-reason: source_expired`; 409 `share_token_not_expirable` in any other status.
5. **Expiry by time:** a minute sweep (`connect-token-expiry`, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, batches of 500) marks `created` tokens past `expires-at` as `expired` with `expiry-reason: time`; a redemption also refuses a token past `expires-at` before the sweep reaches it.
6. **Statuses and reasons:** `created`, `pending`, `redeemed`, `expired`, `failed` (the vendor's, without `ready`: no prefetched copy exists to cache; connect R9, decision 21). Failure reasons: `source_not_found`, `source_redacted`, `import_failed`, `unrecoverable_error` (the vendor's) and ours `connection_inactive`, `consent_withdrawn`, `consent_expired`, `consent_scope_exceeded` (review H04) and `source_is_copy` (review H03). `expiry-reason` (ours): `time`, `source_expired`, `connection_deactivated`, `consent_withdrawn`. `redeemed`, `expired` and `failed` are final.
7. **Attributes:** the vendor's `status`, `direction`, `created-at`, `updated-at`, `pending-at`, `redeemed-at`, `expires-at`, `failed-at`, `failure-reason`, `redeem-mode`, `source-data` (peek only); ours `expired-at`, `expiry-reason`, `source-type`, `environment-kind`, `redeem-summary` (C234.8).
8. **A handle, not a secret** (connect R16, 1.1, correction 5; decision 22): the id is a ULID handle and is not hashed at rest; every call is authenticated and bound: the token's connection, the caller's organisation and side, and the environment kind. Every refusal of a token the caller may not see (unknown, another organisation's, the other environment kind's, a third party's) is the same 404 body, built by one function, with a test that the bodies are byte-identical.
9. **Honesty:** a token is a one-time permission to copy, bound to one recipient; it is not a credential and proves nothing about the person.

### C233. Peek (connect R15; 1.6)

1. **Route:** `GET /api/v1/connect/share-tokens/:id?peek-source-data=true` (the vendor's parameter), the destination only, scope `connect-share-tokens:peek`, and for a user session also `pii:view`; the token is `created` and its connection `active` (409 `share_token_not_redeemable` or `connection_not_active` otherwise).
2. **The envelope** (the vendor's shape): `source-data` of type `connect/source-data/<subtype>` (`inquiry`, `account`, `transaction`, `verification/government-id`, `verification/selfie`, `verification/database`, `verification/document`, `verification/email-address`, `verification/phone-number`); every exposed attribute is `{visibility: 'full' | 'presence', value}`, where `presence` carries a boolean saying whether the source holds a non-null value; attributes the policy does not share are absent; inquiries, accounts and transactions add a `fields` bucket; an inquiry adds `related-objects` (its shareable verifications), each its own filtered snapshot.
3. **Never in a peek** (ours): file bytes, images and face data. A file is at most `{visibility: 'presence'}`; a biometric check result follows the policy's `peek` entry only when `biometric` is on.
4. **Built at read time** from the source's current rows through the cross-tenant module (C240.5); nothing is cached (no `ready`).
5. **Audit and limits:** an audit entry in the destination's environment (the peek) and in the source's (its token was peeked), naming the actor as C240.4 allows and never a value; rate policy `connect-peek` (C240.3), which fails closed like login (503 `connect_peek_unavailable` when Valkey is unreachable; review P27); and a per-token cap of 10 peeks (ours, provisional; counted in `peek_count` under the token row lock, shown in `redeem-summary` and to the source; 429 `share_token_peek_limit` beyond it), so a peek is not a channel for watching a person's data change over 21 days.
6. **Honesty:** a peek shows what the source holds at that moment; the source can change or redact it before redemption, and the redemption copies what exists then.

### C234. Redeem: clone and hydrate (connect R12, R14; 1.3, 3.2)

1. **Route** (`POST /api/v1/connect/share-tokens/:id/redeem`, `connect-share-tokens:redeem`, the destination): the vendor's body, `destination-id` (optional) and `meta` with `field-mappings[] {source-field-name, destination-field-name}` (dot notation for nested destinations), `account-id`, `auto-create-account-reference-id` and `auto-create-account-type-id`; ours, labelled: `meta.inquiry-template-id` (and optional `meta.inquiry-template-version-id`) for a clone of an inquiry, because every OpenVerify inquiry is pinned to a template version (`inquiries.inquiry_template_version_id` is NOT NULL); `meta.inquiry-id` for a clone of a verification, which attaches to an open destination inquiry (a verification belongs to an inquiry here); `meta.destination-consent-record-id`, a destination-side consent record (C236.5), required (decision 24).
   - **Mode:** without `destination-id`, clone (inquiry and verification sources only); with it, hydrate (account, inquiry or transaction destinations from an inquiry, account or transaction source). Any other pairing is 422 `share_redeem_mode_invalid`. A third mode, `fill-inquiry` (ours, internal, never accepted on this route; reviews H01, F23), is C234.11's, used only by client-side redemption (C238.4). `account-id` and `auto-create-*` exclude each other and `destination-id` (422 `share_redeem_meta_conflict`, the vendor's rule).
   - **Synchronous checks, under the token row lock:** `created` → `pending` (409 `share_token_already_redeemed` when `pending` or `redeemed`, 409 `share_token_expired`, 409 `share_token_not_redeemable` when `failed`); the connection is `active`; the caller's environment is the token's destination environment (else the generic 404; a token is bound to one destination environment, so the other kind's caller gets the same 404 and no kind check is needed here, review P25); the destination object, template, account or account type exists there (422 `destination_not_found` with a pointer); the destination consent record (review P02), mirroring C232.1's list, with the same 422 `consent_required` and its `reason`: in the caller's environment, `side: destination`, `outcome: accepted`, its `share-token-id` equal to this token (`subject`), its recipient the caller's organisation and its purpose the policy's (`recipient`), its categories covering the token's stored categories (`categories`), `explicit-biometric` true when they include `biometric` (`explicit_biometric`), not withdrawn, not expired and not cited by another redemption (`withdrawn`, `expired`, `already_used`); and the token's reserved inquiry, if any, recorded no decline (409 `consent_declined_in_flow`, C238.1). An `Idempotency-Key` repeat returns the first answer (C12). Answer: 202 with the token `pending`.
2. **The job** (queue `connect-redeem`, ids only; uncapped in 11A, and admitted under C251's cap from 11B on, where `integration-11b` adds the queue with a test; review F12): one transaction in the destination environment re-reads, through the cross-tenant module, the source (`source_not_found`, `source_redacted`, `source_is_copy`), the connection (`connection_inactive`, holding the row `FOR SHARE`), both consent records under their locks (`consent_withdrawn`; `consent_expired` when either is past `expires-at` at commit; the biometric flag re-checked; review P02) and the token (still `pending` under its lock); computes the categories of the rows it is about to copy and ends `consent_scope_exceeded` when any lies outside the token's stored categories (review H04: a selfie added to the source after creation is not carried); builds the copy filtered by the token's policy version; writes the destination rows; sets `redeemed`; stages `connect/share-token.outbound-redeemed` (source) and `.inbound-redeemed` (destination) and the audit entries. A destination field that fails validation under an explicit mapping, or a copy over the limits of 9, ends `import_failed`; a transient database error is retried three times and then ends `unrecoverable_error`; every failure is final (the vendor's "not retryable") and stages `connect/share-token.failed` on both sides.
3. **Clone of an inquiry:** a new inquiry in the destination environment under the named template version, with its `fields` filtered by the policy, an account by `account-id`, by `auto-create-*`, or by the ordinary resolution of any new inquiry; each shareable verification copied as a new `ver_` row with its status, extracted data (when `extracted-data` is on), checks (when `check-results` is on, each with tier, stage, provenance, result and reasons), and files (when `files` is on) re-encrypted under new data keys as new objects; the inquiry's status follows C235.4. **Never copied:** face templates and their background rows, report links, signals, session records, device facts, the source's notes and tags, and any field outside the allowlist (ours, connect 2.2, decision 23). A cloned selfie creates no face template, so it joins no repeat search in the destination.
4. **Clone of a verification:** a new verification on the open destination inquiry `meta.inquiry-id` (status `created` or `pending`; 409 `destination_inquiry_not_open`), copied as in 3.
5. **Hydrate** (the vendor's): fields only, no verification and no report; matched by name, then by `field-mappings`; only allowlisted fields; a name-matched field the destination does not declare, or of another type, is dropped and named in `redeem-summary.dropped-fields` (keys only); a mapped field that fails validation fails the job (`import_failed`); write-once fields keep Phase 9's rules. A hydrated inquiry runs the destination's own template and stops where its data is missing (the vendor's), through Phase 9's engine.
6. **Transactions** (Phase 7's resource): hydrate into and from transactions, under the same rules.
7. **Snapshot at redemption** (connect 3.2): the job copies what the source holds when it runs; the shares are separate rows, never references (connect 5).
8. **`redeem-summary`** (ours): counts of copied verifications, checks, files and fields, the dropped field keys, and the policy version applied; never a value.
9. **Limits** (provisional, ours, with a basis in the C10 upload limits): at most 50 verifications, 200 files and 512 MiB of file bytes per redemption; beyond them, `import_failed`.
10. **Honesty:** a redemption copies evidence; it re-runs nothing. A hydrated object's checks are the destination's own; a cloned object's are the source's (C235).
11. **`fill-inquiry`** (ours, internal; reviews H01, F23; frozen by `contracts-11a`, built by `api-redeem`): client-side redemption into the open inquiry the token is reserved for (C238.5), from an inquiry or a verification source only (any other source type is refused at `POST /api/v1/inquiries` with 422 `share_redeem_mode_invalid`). Fields are hydrated as in 5, with `connect-hydrate` provenance; every shareable verification, check and file is copied as in 3, as a clone with `connect-clone` provenance, `cloned: true` and the console's Cloned marker, and counts only under C235.4. `redeem-summary` counts both. With `accept-cloned-results` off, a cloned verification on the live inquiry neither satisfies nor fails a step and does not move the inquiry; the person completes every step live.

### C235. Provenance and what a cloned pass counts for (connect R20, R21; 3.2; ADR 0002 §3b, §3f)

1. **`provenance`** (ours, machine-readable; connect R20) on every cloned inquiry, verification, check and file: `{kind: 'connect-clone', share-token-id, source-organization-name, source-environment-kind, source-created-at, source-status, cloned-at}`; on a hydrated object: `{kind: 'connect-hydrate', share-token-id, source-organization-name, hydrated-at, fields[]}` (the hydrated keys). The source's object ids are not disclosed (ours, least disclosure; the token links the two sides' audit).
2. **Checks keep their labels:** tier, stage and ADR 0002 provenance stay as the source recorded them, with `cloned: true` beside them.
3. **`accept-cloned-results`** (ours; decision 25) is a template-version setting: `{verification-types[], max-age-days (1–3,650)}`; absent means off. Publishing a version that sets it needs the acknowledgement `cloned-results`, which is audited (Phase 9's publish acknowledgements).
4. **The decision rule:**
   - **Off (the default):** a cloned inquiry is created in `needs_review`, whatever the source's status, and a cloned verification counts toward no required check: in a flow, the step runs as if no copy existed. A cloned failure is not the destination's decision either, so it also lands in review, never in `failed` or `declined` by itself.
   - **On:** a cloned verification of a listed type whose source decision is younger than `max-age-days` **at the destination's decision** (not at redemption; review H07) counts toward that step's required checks when it passed. A cloned failure never declines or fails anything by itself: it routes the inquiry to `needs_review` (review H07: only passes are relied on, so no adverse decision rests on checks the destination never ran, and Phase 8 C128.3's basis and contest route are not bypassed). A cloned inquiry runs the destination's own decision rules with the counted clones, so it is decided by the destination's rules, never by the source's status. In a flow the server's position skips a satisfied step (Phase 9 C151); the decision engine and Phase 8's decision record name the share token and set `relied-on-cloned: true`. A clone whose destination-side consent was withdrawn (`provenance.consent-withdrawn-at`, C237.6) stops counting from that moment.
5. **Never a fresh pass** (rule 5): no cloned result is shown, served or evented as a check the destination ran; the console's marker and banner (C242) and the API's `provenance` say so.
6. **Honesty:** a destination that turns the setting on relies on another organisation's checks and configuration; the platform makes that visible and recorded, and the docs say it is the destination's business and legal decision.
7. **Clones outside the decision engine** (review H02; each in 11A's acceptance): (a) cloned rows stage no `verification.*` status event and no check-level event; the destination learns of them through `connect/share-token.inbound-redeemed` with `redeem-summary`, and every `inquiry.*` event about a cloned inquiry carries `provenance` (a golden sample and a test); (b) Phase 8's condition reads and actions treat a check or verification with `cloned: true` as absent unless the template version's `accept-cloned-results` lists its type and C235.4 counts it (a hook in Phase 8's condition evaluator to `services/connect/cloned-results.ts`, placed by `foundation-11a`, with a test that a workflow on `verification.*` or a check status cannot approve on a clone); (c) Phase 9's analytics exclude cloned checks and verifications from pass rates and failure distributions (a hook in its analytics query, placed by `foundation-11a`, built by `api-redeem`, with a test).
8. **Binding the person to the copy** (review P08; decision 25): a template whose destination-side `share-consent` step leads into `accept-cloned-results` must keep a live `selfie` step whose face match runs against the cloned portrait (a cloned government ID's portrait, which needs the policy's `biometric` and `files`), unless the version carries the separate audited acknowledgement `cloned-results-unbound` (Phase 9's publish acknowledgements; publish refuses otherwise with `cloned_results_without_binding`). `selfie` may be listed only with that acknowledgement, because a cloned selfie binds nothing to the person in the flow. The flow never renders a hydrated or cloned value to the person (a client-view test over every step), so whoever opens a forwarded link learns nothing from the copy.

### C236. Consent records on both sides (connect R24, R26, R27; 2.1, 2.6)

1. **The resource** (`consent-record`, `cnsr_`, ours): attributes `side` (`source`: consent to disclose to a named recipient; `destination`: consent to receive and process), `purpose`, `recipient-organization-id`, `recipient-name` (as shown), `data-categories[]`, `explicit-biometric`, `text {locale, keys[], sha256}`, `biometric-text-sha256`, `method` (`flow` or `attested`), `witnessed` (true only for `flow`), `evidence` (`flow`: `{session-id, accepted-at}`; `attested`: `{attested-at}`; the attested reference, the IP and the user agent are sealed and served only with `pii:view`), `expires-at`, `withdrawn-at`, `withdrawn-via` (`api`, `operator` or `flow`, C237.6), `withdrawal-reason`, `outcome` (`accepted` or `declined`, C236.11), `status` (derived: `active`, `expired`, `withdrawn`, `redacted`, `declined`; review H27), `created-at`; relationships `subject` (an inquiry, account or transaction for the source side, C232.1; for the destination side the share token until redemption, then the destination object, C236.10), `connection`, `share-token`.
2. **Routes:** `GET /api/v1/connect/consent-records` (filters `subject-id`, `side`, `status`, `connection-id`) and `GET /:id` under `connect-share-tokens:read`; `POST /connect/consent-records` (attested) under its own scope `connect-consent-records:attest` (review P03: held by no built-in role but `admin`, never implied by `connect-share-tokens:write`, so the key that creates tokens cannot also attest the consent that authorises them unless the operator grants both on purpose); `POST /:id/withdraw` (C237) under `connect-share-tokens:write`.
3. **Texts** (connect 2.1, [L2] ¶108): every record holds the SHA-256 of the exact text presented (placeholders filled, NFC, as Phase 9 C162.9 and Phase 10 C200.2 define the logical form); a `flow` text is stored once per digest in `consent_texts`, sealed (C230.5), and an `attested` text on its own record (review P11); the console shows it with `pii:view`.
4. **`flow` records** come only from the `share-consent` step (C238): the server composes the text, serves it with its digest, and on the person's affirmative act records the session, the time and the sealed IP and user agent.
5. **`attested` records** (decision 27): `POST /connect/consent-records` with `side`, `connection-id`, the subject (`subject-id` for the source side, `share-token-id` inbound to the caller for the destination side), `data-categories[]`, `explicit-biometric`, `expires-in-seconds` (60–1,814,400), `evidence-reference` (1–500 characters, sealed), `attested-at` (within the last 30 days, not in the future), `text-locale` and `text-body` (1–20,000 characters: the operator's own text that was shown, required, because a record must hold a copy of what was presented); the server computes and stores the digest and seals the text on the record. The record is labelled "not witnessed" in the API (`witnessed: false`) and in both consoles, and the destination sees the label through the token's `source-consent` (C232.3). An attested record is refused within the step's `consent-valid-seconds` after a witnessed decline for the same recipient and purpose (409 `consent_declined_in_flow`; review H05). The match covers the subject **and its account** (verification V08): the attestation is refused when a declined `flow` record exists for that recipient and purpose on the attested subject, on any inquiry of the subject's account (when the subject is an account), or on the account of the subject inquiry. That way an account-subject attestation cannot override a decline witnessed on one of its inquiries. A Testcontainers case covers the account path.
6. **Recipient named** ([L2] ¶65): the source-side text always names the destination organisation; a source-side record's recipient is the connection's destination and cannot be changed.
7. **Biometric categories** ([L2] ¶91-93, [L3] ¶76-82; decision 23): a share that carries `biometric` needs `explicit-biometric: true`; in a flow it is a separate, unticked tick with its own text and digest; face templates are never in any share.
8. **Expiry:** a record expires at `expires-at` (the step's `consent-valid-seconds` or the attested `expires-in-seconds`), and a token cannot outlive it (C232.1).
9. **Honesty:** a `flow` record shows what the server served, that the client reported showing it, and that an affirmative act happened in a session the platform witnessed; it does not prove who held the device. An `attested` record is the operator's statement and is shown as such.
10. **The destination record follows the copy** (review P10): when a redemption commits, the destination record's subject becomes the destination object (the clone, the hydrated object or the filled inquiry), with the token id kept as a plain string, so the ordinary redaction cascade (C241.3) reaches its sealed evidence; a Testcontainers test redacts a clone and finds the destination record's evidence cleared.
11. **A decline is recorded** (review H05; decision 76): a decline in `share-consent` creates a `flow` record with `outcome: declined` (session and time; no IP, user agent or text copy beyond the digest), which no token or redemption can cite; both consoles show it on the subject's page, and C236.5 and C234.1 refuse what would override it.

### C237. Withdrawal (connect R25; 2.1)
