4. **Probes and scaling:** API `/ready` (readiness) and `/health` (liveness); the worker's exec probe (C250.2); `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` above the longest job lease; HPAs on CPU for the API, dashboard and flow; the worker scaled by replicas with an example for queue-depth scaling in the guide (KEDA's Redis scaler over BullMQ's keys is untested, so it is an example, not a template); each replica's database pool times replicas is checked against `external.postgres.maxConnections` by a `fail` in the template.
5. **Model weights** (decision 5; reviews P05, F05): a network policy selects pods, not containers, and names CIDRs, not hosts, so no container of the vision pod ever fetches. `models.source: job` (the default) runs `python3 -I scripts/fetch_models.py` (pinned SHA-256) in a separate `models-fetch` Job, a `pre-install,pre-upgrade` hook with its own pod, no Secret and its own egress policy (`0.0.0.0/0` minus the private ranges, or `networkPolicy.egress.modelHosts`), into a PVC that vision mounts read-only; `pvc` mounts a volume the operator filled once with the same script. The PVC is `ReadWriteOnce` by default with one vision replica; more replicas need `accessMode: ReadOnlyMany` or `ReadWriteMany`, which the schema requires. **The volume's lifecycle in `job` mode** (verification V03), because Helm runs `pre-install` hooks before it creates the release's ordinary resources:
   - **The claim.** Without `existingClaim`, the chart renders the PVC itself as a `pre-install` hook with a lower `helm.sh/hook-weight` than the Job, `helm.sh/resource-policy: keep` and no hook-delete policy. It is created once, before the Job, and kept across upgrades and uninstall; the guide says how to delete it.
   - **Versioned directories.** The Job writes into `/models/<manifest digest>/`, where the digest is the SHA-256 of `models/manifest.json` that the chart renders. It exits 0 at once when that directory is already complete, so an upgrade with the same manifest fetches nothing. Vision reads `OPENVERIFY_MODELS_DIR=/models/<digest>`, so a new manifest rolls vision onto the new directory after the hook, and no live mount is rewritten. The Job removes every directory except the current one and the previous one.
   - **Upgrades on a `ReadWriteOnce` claim.** On upgrade (`.Release.IsUpgrade`), the Job carries a required pod affinity to the vision pods' node, so it attaches the claim where vision already holds it.
   - **Tests.** A helm-unittest asserts the hook weights, the resource policy, the digest directory and the upgrade affinity.

   The vision pod has no egress in every mode, and a helm-unittest asserts it per mode.
6. **The code runner** (Phase 8 P17, hardening H8, ours in mechanism): a sidecar shares its pod's network namespace, so an egress-denying policy on a worker pod with a runner sidecar would also cut the worker's own egress. The chart therefore runs the runner as its own Deployment with no egress and ingress only from worker pods, and the worker reaches it at `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_CODE_RUNNER=tcp:<service>:<port>` with a shared token (`OPENVERIFY_CODE_RUNNER_TOKEN`, 32 bytes minimum, a secret of both processes); the `tcp:` transport is a Phase 8 amendment built by `images-11c` as a named borrowed edit beside the `socket:` one, with the same request limits. Compose keeps the Unix socket.
7. **Honesty:** the chart encodes the documented deployment; an operator's own cluster policies, network plugin and secret store decide what it achieves (C264.6).

### C264. Chart security (hardening 7.2; B5-E1, B6-T1, B6-S1, B8-S1, B10-I1, B12-E1; H8, H9)

1. **Restricted Pod Security everywhere:** `runAsNonRoot: true`, a non-zero `runAsUser`, `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`, `capabilities.drop: [ALL]`, `seccompProfile: RuntimeDefault`, `readOnlyRootFilesystem: true` with an `emptyDir` `/tmp`, `automountServiceAccountToken: false`, only the allowed volume types; the guide labels the namespace `pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce: restricted`.
2. **Network policies** (default deny both ways, then allow lists): DNS to the cluster resolver; vision accepts only the API and worker and has **no egress** (Phase 5 C60.2, H9); the code runner accepts only the worker and has no egress (H8); Postgres, Valkey and S3 egress only from the API, worker and migrate (in-cluster by selector, external by `networkPolicy.egress.dataStores`), SMTP from the API and worker (`smtp`), the identity providers from the API (`identityProviders`); the `models-fetch` Job alone reaches the model hosts (C263.5); the flow and dashboard reach no data store; the dashboard reaches only the API; the API and worker reach the public internet for webhooks, providers and list syncs (`0.0.0.0/0` minus the private, link-local, CGNAT and metadata ranges by default, the SSRF guard's second layer, B10-I1). The guide says that policies do nothing without a network plugin that implements them.
3. **One Secret per process** (B5-E1): the API (KEK and previous keys, blind-index key, pepper and previous peppers, vision token, BFF token, flow-server token, provider secrets the API reads), the worker (KEK, blind-index key, vision token, its provider secrets, the runner token), migrate (the migration URL, the KEK for the backfill), the dashboard (BFF token), the flow server (its token), the runner (its token), vision (its token); mounted as files with `defaultMode: 0440` and the pod's `fsGroup` equal to the process's gid, and read through `*_FILE` (C249.2; review P18, with a helm-unittest assertion and a start test at that mode); the chart creates no Secret by default, and the guide documents an External Secrets example.
4. **Vision's controls** are asserted one by one by a chart test: uid 10001, read-only root, no capabilities, no privilege escalation, a 4 GiB memory limit, no egress (Phase 5 C60.2).
5. **Ingress:** HSTS (C255.1), `maxBodySize` matching the upload limits (10 MiB plus multipart overhead), the path `/api/v1/internal/` denied (C250.3), and TLS required; the flow and console hosts separate from the API host.
6. **Honesty:** these templates are a secure default, not a guarantee; the guide lists what only the operator can provide (C277.3).

### C265. Chart tests (hardening 7.3; decision 12)

1. **Lint:** `helm lint` with Helm 4.3.1 and 3.22.1 over the default values and every `ci/` file.
2. **Unit tests** (helm-unittest v1.2.1): every pod restricted (1); vision and the runner without egress (2); one Secret per process and no default Secret (3); vision's controls (4); the hook annotations and the GitOps switch (C263.3); the internal path denied and HSTS set (5); no static `frame-ancestors`; the pool check's `fail`; image references by digest when a digest is given.
3. **Schemas:** kubeconform v0.8.0 over the rendered manifests against the Kubernetes 1.35, 1.36 and 1.37 schemas, offline, from a schema bundle fetched by `scripts/fetch_k8s_schemas.py` with its SHA-256; Trivy `config` over the rendered chart.
4. **Install:** `ct install` on kind (1.35 and 1.37 node images by digest) with Postgres, Valkey and RustFS deployed from plain manifests in `deploy/helm/openverify/ci/fixtures/` (the pinned images, no subchart), driven by `deploy/helm/openverify/ci/kind.sh` (review F14; owned by `chart-policy-11c`) whose subcommands `create <1.35|1.37>`, `load` (`kind load docker-image` of the five images), `fixtures`, `models` (verification V15: no download inside a test run; the kind install uses `models.source: pvc`, and `models` copies the weights that `python3 scripts/fetch_models.py` already placed on the host into the node and binds them through a PersistentVolume and claim in `ci/fixtures/`; `job` mode's rendering, hooks and egress are covered by helm-unittest, C263.5), `install`, `smoke`, `upgrade` and `delete` are each one call under the e2e lock, measured at the first checkpoint and split further if one exceeds 10 minutes; then a smoke: the migrate hook succeeds, every pod is ready, `/ready` answers 200, and a sandbox inquiry is created through the API; then a `helm upgrade` of the same chart with changed values re-runs the hook and the smoke (review H16). It runs in Validation at 11C's checkpoints and in `release.yml`, never per push (C267).
5. **Honesty:** a kind install proves the chart renders and starts; it does not prove behaviour under a real cloud's storage, network plugin or load.

### C266. The web SDK packages and the demo site (Phase 9 C159.7, C177 and its decision 20; PLAN §3 layout)

1. **`@openverify/js`** (Phase 9's embed client) is made publishable: `exports` with `types`, and Phase 9 C159.1's ESM and IIFE builds (the IIFE for a script tag; review F27), `sideEffects: false`, a README, the licence, `files` limited to `dist/`, `publishConfig.provenance: true`; the release-age rule and the lockfile apply as for every package.
2. **`@openverify/react`** (`packages/sdk-react`, new; ours: the public `sdk-react` name of PLAN §3 under OpenVerify's scope): a thin `<OpenVerifyFlow>` component and a `useOpenVerify` hook over `@openverify/js`, React 19 as a peer dependency, no state beyond the embed's own events, tests with React Testing Library; it adds no capability the embed lacks.
3. **`apps/demo-web`** (new): a small sample customer site (Vite) that creates an inquiry through its own tiny server with an API key from the environment, embeds the flow with `@openverify/js`, verifies a webhook with `@openverify/node`, and shows the result; synthetic data only; never published as a package, built in the e2e stack, and named a sample in its README.
4. **`@openverify/node`** is made publishable the same way (it is `private: true` today).
5. **Publication** waits for the owner (Q4, C285); until then `release.yml` packs each with `pnpm pack` and keeps the tarballs and their SBOMs as workflow artifacts.
6. **Honesty:** the packages are clients; the server decides every step (Phase 9 ADR 0008), and their READMEs say so.

### C267. The release workflow and CI minutes (the CI cadence; hardening 5.3, 7.3)

1. **`release.yml`** (`workflow_dispatch` on `main` only, input `version`; top-level `permissions: contents: read`; every action pinned by commit; `persist-credentials: false`), started by the orchestrator, never by an agent:
   - **build:** the five production images for `linux/amd64` (arm64 deferred, C286), the chart package, the npm tarballs;
   - **verify:** Trivy `image` and Grype on each image (C259.2's gate: every critical or high fails unless allowlisted), Syft SBOMs and the licence check (C261), the chart's kind install and upgrade through `kind.sh` (C265.4), the flow image's no-guidance variant (C289), gitleaks over every staged artifact's unpacked contents (Phase 10 C217.4's rule); no DAST (C283.4, review F15);
   - **stage:** every artifact, SBOM and checksum uploaded as workflow artifacts (30-day retention);
   - **publish** (owner-gated, C262.2): only in the `release` environment and with `OPENVERIFY_PUBLISH=true`; otherwise the run ends after staging with a notice.

   About 35 to 45 minutes a run (estimate; measured by `integration-11c`), dispatched once after 11C's push (staging only), once after 11F's push, and once per release; Phase 10's `android-release.yml` (its 30–40 emulator minutes, Phase 10 decision 50, plus the SBOM and licence step) is dispatched on the final commit too (review H23); each run's minutes recorded in PROGRESS.
2. **Per push** (`ci.yml`): the new `supply-chain` job (C259.1), estimated at 7 to 9 minutes on a 2-vCPU runner with cached binaries; `eslint-plugin-security` inside the existing lint (about +1 minute); the `e2e` job unchanged (it stays on host processes, C257.3; review F03). In all about +8 to +10 minutes on a full run of about 100 (about $0.05 to $0.06 at $0.006 a minute). `integration-11c` measures both jobs at its checkpoint and splits a job that comes within 25% of its timeout (Phase 8 F08's rule).
3. **Never in shared CI** (decisions 8, 50): the load test (C268), DAST (C283), `ct install` per push, a nightly matrix of anything; they run locally under the e2e lock, and `ct install` also in `release.yml` (DAST stays local, C283.4).
4. **`infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`** gains the `supply-chain` job's steps and pins, `release.yml`'s permissions, environment, branch check, variable gate and commit-pinned actions.
5. **Honesty:** every minute figure is an estimate until an integration agent measures it with the runner's load.

### C268. The HTTP load test: tools, harness and method (hardening 6; PLAN §6 corrected; decision 1)

1. **Tools** (decision 1): k6 is AGPL-3.0 (`LICENSE.md` at v2.3.0) and fails rule 4 even as unmodified tooling, so PLAN §6 is corrected (C282) and k6 joins the Excluded list. **vegeta v12.13.0** (MIT, constant rate, built to avoid coordinated omission) measures open-model per-route latency; the multi-step scenarios and worker throughput run on **a small driver of ours** in `load/driver/` over `httpx` (BSD-3-Clause; its `certifi` is already a §5 dev-tooling row), with an open-model scheduler (a constant arrival rate, so it does not omit coordinated delays) and its own tests. **Locust 2.46.7** (MIT) was the first choice, but its `gevent` brings `zope.interface` and `zope.event` under ZPL-2.1, which rule 4 does not list, and `pyzmq`'s wheels bundle libzmq (MPL-2.0); adopting it is an exception to rule 4 and waits for the owner (Q12; review F09). `contracts-11d` resolves and records every transitive licence of `load/` before its lockfile is committed. Artillery is not adopted (MPL-2.0 tooling with telemetry on by default and 93 direct dependencies); autocannon is not adopted (no release since 2024).
2. **The harness** (`load/`, a uv project of its own with Python 3.12 and its lockfile, so no load tool enters the vision image): `load/driver/` (the open-model driver), `load/scenarios/` (one file per scenario of C269, on the driver's interface, so Locust could replace it under Q12 without rewriting a scenario), `load/vegeta/` (target files generated from the seeded ids), `load/sampler.py` (every 5 s: BullMQ queue depth and lag, Postgres connections, the API's own response times from pino), `load/report.py` (the medians and the report table), each with pytest tests over recorded fixtures at ≥ 80% coverage; `infra/scripts/install-vegeta.sh` in the gitleaks pattern.
3. **Data** (rule 3): `pnpm --filter @openverify/api seed:load --scale <small | reference>` (ours) creates, through the migration role, two organisations with both environments, accounts, inquiries with synthetic captures, report templates, a self-connection and a cross-organisation connection, and the Phase 6 heavy-headed sanctions tables at 100,000 and 2,000,000 names from `@openverify/watchlist/testing`; it refuses production mode and any database whose name is not `openverify_load`. **Spread under the shipped limits** (reviews H10, F06): for each scenario the seed fixes how many API keys, sessions, synthetic users and emails and environments it creates, and the generator sends client addresses from the documentation ranges through `api.trustProxy`, so that every request stays under the limits the platform ships (`api` 300 a minute per key, `flow-ip` 600 a minute per address, `connect-redeem` 60 a minute per key and environment, the login limiter's 50 per address and 10 per email in 15 minutes); the method commit lists the counts per scenario.
4. **The stack:** the compose `app` profile on the production images (C257.3; the load stack is compose, not the e2e driver), with a load override (`infra/load/docker-compose.load.yml`: `pg_stat_statements` preloaded, resource limits as the chart's defaults, the database `openverify_load`; it raises no rate limit, and if a scenario cannot fit under the shipped limits the override's raised value is listed in the method commit and printed beside the figure as a labelled configuration), on one named reference machine (the development machine), never a shared CI runner.
5. **Method** (hardening 6.3): an open model for latency (vegeta, and the driver's constant arrival rate), a step-up to find the knee, a 30-minute soak at 70% of the knee; three takes and the median of the take p95s as the headline, the client's p95 beside the server's; p95 is computed over the scenario's expected statuses only, and every scenario has an error budget (unexpected statuses below 0.1%) beyond which the take is `invalid`, never `met` (review H10); `pg_stat_statements`' top 20 and the queue lag per take. A separate short scenario measures the login limiter's and the API limiter's refusal path at their limits.
6. **Timing rules** (the Phase 6 Workflows' rules, adapted for load; reviews H11, F01; decision 64): every take runs under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`, alone on the machine (no test suite, validation, build or other Workflow agent running). **HTTP load takes** start only after the stack has idled until the 1-minute load average is at most 4, recorded as the idle baseline; the load during the take and at its end is recorded beside the figure, never gated on (a take at 70% of the knee ends loaded by design). **Matcher takes** (C270, C271) keep the 6C rule: at most 4 at the start and the end. **Each generator** (vegeta and the `httpx` driver of `load/driver/`; Locust only under Q12) is pinned to separate cores (`taskset`), the stack's containers to the others, and each generator's CPU share is recorded per take. The driver also records its schedule lag per take (the gap between each request's planned and actual send time), and a take whose lag exceeds the method commit's bound is `invalid`, since a late sender hides coordinated delays (review H11; verification V16). **Every take is listed** in the report; a take is discarded only by a rule written in the method commit. The report records the CPU model, cores, memory, disk class, the commit and every image digest; the method is committed before the first take.
7. **The report:** `docs/calibration/load-phase11.md`, with each budget `met` or `missed` and its gap.
8. **Honesty:** one machine's figures carry ratios, not absolutes, to other hardware; a passing load test says nothing about behaviour under failure (Valkey down, a database failover), which the docs state as untested.

### C269. Scenarios and budgets (hardening 6.4; decision 8)

1. **Budgets** (hardening 6.4's proposals, ours, accepted as proposed; recorded, never gated in shared CI; a missed budget is reported with its gap and never moved to fit, as owner decision 1 after 6B rules for C78.7):

   | Scenario | Budget |
   |---|---|
   | API reads (`GET /inquiries/:id`, `GET /accounts/:id`, list pages of 25) at 100 requests/s | p95 ≤ 150 ms, errors < 0.1% |
   | API writes (`POST /inquiries`, `PATCH`, tags, approve, decline) at 50 requests/s | p95 ≤ 300 ms, errors < 0.1% |
   | Flow upload (a 1–3 MB synthetic image, encrypted and stored) at 10 uploads/s | p95 ≤ 1.5 s, errors < 0.1% |
   | Console login (Argon2id 19 MiB, t=2) at 5 logins/s, through the Valkey limiter (C246), spread over distinct synthetic users and addresses (C268.3) | p95 ≤ 500 ms, errors < 0.1%; memory per concurrent hash recorded |
   | Verification, submit to decision, per worker at `OPENVERIFY_WORKER_CONCURRENCY=4` with one vision instance | throughput reported; per-analysis **median** against the budgets as set (C32.4's 5 s for the government ID, C41.5's 3 s for the selfie, `vision-document`'s 4 s for a 3-page PDF, `phase-5.plan.md:627`), the p95 beside each as recorded only (review F26) |
   | Event to first webhook attempt at 50 events/s | p95 ≤ 5 s |
   | Screening (one report run) | C78.7: p95 ≤ 100 ms at 100,000 names, ≤ 250 ms at 2,000,000 (C271) |
   | Screenings per hour per worker process, at concurrency 1 and 4, both scales | reported; floor 10,000 an hour per process at 2,000,000 names |
   | Re-screen after a list update (200,000 subjects, 1,000 changed entries) | 60 s (C78.7's re-screen budget; C271) |
   | Connect redemption (an inquiry with three verifications and six files) at 5 redemptions/s over at least five destination API keys in one destination environment, each under `connect-redeem`'s 60 a minute (C240.3; `seed:load` creates them, verification V11) (ours) | job p95 ≤ 5 s; API answer p95 ≤ 300 ms, errors < 0.1% |
   | Account export (an account with 20 inquiries) (ours) | reported |
   | Workflow engine steps (Phase 8) | reported, beside Phase 8's 1,577 effectful steps/s at 32 clients |

2. **Fairness** (C251.5): one environment floods report runs at 10× its cap while another runs its normal load; the second's report p95 stays within 2× its idle p95.
3. **Soak:** 30 minutes at 70% of the knee: no process grows its memory by more than 10% after warm-up, and every queue's lag returns to zero after the load stops.
4. **During a list sync** (C270.3): screening p95 while a 2,000,000-name sync runs, against idle; measured by `calibration-matcher-11d` alone, in 6D's configuration with the load stack stopped (C271.3), and that figure judges C270.3's GIN choice (verification V12).
5. **Honesty:** the budgets are ours, chosen before measuring; the report says where each came from.

### C270. The matcher's write cost (owner decision 9 after 6C, its write-cost item; PLAN §6 Phase 11)

1. **An incremental key-frequency rebuild** (184 s a sync at 2,000,000 names in 6C): a sync updates the key frequencies by the delta of the entries it adds, removes and changes, in the same transaction as those entries, instead of rebuilding them; `pnpm --filter @openverify/api watchlists:rebuild-key-df` keeps the full rebuild. **Identity test:** after a seeded sequence of syncs (adds, removals, renames, variant-group changes), the incremental frequencies equal a full rebuild's exactly, at both scales on development tables, and every created match is identical by the 6D digest (C271.3).
2. **The re-key's batch size** (2,414 s in 6C): the candidates (500, 2,000, 5,000 and 10,000 entries a batch) are measured with peak memory at 100,000 names, three takes each, and only the chosen size (the fastest within a 2 GiB heap) is confirmed once at 2,000,000 names against the current size (review F01: four sizes by three takes at 2,000,000 names would be about eight hours); it is committed as a performance constant with its basis; the re-key stays resumable and its output identical.
3. **GIN pending lists during a long sync:** 6C empties the pending lists after each sync; during a long sync they grow and slow retrieval. Three options are measured on the 2,000,000-name table (screening p95 during a sync, C269.4, and the sync's own time): `fastupdate = off` on the trigram indexes, a lower `gin_pending_list_limit` on them, and a `gin_clean_pending_list` call every N batches; the one that keeps screening within 2× idle at the lowest sync cost is taken, as a migration or a sync setting, with the figures in the report; if none does, none is taken, the status quo stays, and the figures are reported (review H27).
4. **Rules** (owner decisions 1 and 8 after 6C): no C78.4 constant, threshold, preset or cap changes; every change is switchable and keeps every created match identical; the calibration measures each change's share.
5. **Honesty:** write-cost figures are for the synthetic development tables; real publications change in other shapes.

### C271. The C78.7 budgets Workflow 6D leaves open (conditional; owner decision 1 after 6C)

1. **The condition:** `contracts-11d` reads `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6d.md` and the Phase 6 closing entry of `docs/PROGRESS.md` first. If 6D met all three budgets (100 ms at 100,000 names, 250 ms at 2,000,000, the re-screen's 60 s), the optimisation work is closed and `matcher-c787-11d` does not start; the **measurement is never skipped** (review H09): 11D changes the write path (C270) and Phases 7–10 change the stack, so C271.3 measures all three budgets at 11D's base and head whatever 6D found, and the head's figure is the release's verdict. If either document is missing (6D unfinished at the Phase 11 base, which cannot happen if Phase 10 waited for it, but is checked), 11D waits.
2. **If a budget is missed:** `matcher-c787-11d` works only the missed budgets, sized from 6D's profile (the phase split of state, retrieval, loads and the rest), under the same rules as 6D: no budget moves, no recall is traded for latency, every created match is identical, every change switchable and measured for its share; candidates are those 6D's report names as untried.
3. **Measured** with `benchmark:performance` in the same Workflow as the HTTP load test (PLAN §6 Phase 11), but not beside it (review H11): `calibration-matcher-11d` runs every C78.7 and re-screen take with the load stack stopped, in 6D's configuration (the Testcontainers Postgres with the C73.8 flags, no other stack running), stated in the report; base beside head, with 6D's seed method (a fresh seed drawn after the method commit) and the digest of full created matches at base and head.
4. **After 11D:** each budget is `met` or `missed` at the head with its gap in `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase11.md`; a budget still missed (including one 6D met and the head misses) is an open line of the release checklist with both figures and its profile, named in the release notes (C284.4), and a head miss after 6D met reopens the C270 change that caused it under its switch (off until fixed); there is no further Workflow for it unless the owner asks.
5. **Honesty:** the budgets judge the development machine; the report states its load and hardware beside each figure.

### C272. Database tuning measured (hardening H10, 8.1; owner decision 9 after 6C)

1. **`jit = off` for the application role:** the load test's read, write and screening scenarios run with the runtime role's `jit` on and off (`ALTER ROLE … SET jit = off`), three takes each; the self-hosting guide recommends it only if no scenario's p95 regresses by more than the margin fixed in the method commit, with the figures; it is never a pool option (PgBouncer in its default configuration refuses a startup `options` parameter).
2. **Timeouts per role:** `statement_timeout` and `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` for the runtime role, set from the longest legitimate statement the load test records (with a margin) and written in the guide; the migration role has none.
3. **Pools:** connections per process measured at the knee; the guide's sizing table and the chart's pool check (C263.4) take them.
4. **Honesty:** settings that help here may not help another workload; the guide gives the measurement method so an operator can repeat it.

### C273. The threat model (hardening 3; PLAN §10; decision 15)

1. **Format** (decision 15): `docs/security/threat-model.md`, Markdown as the source of truth, with a Mermaid data-flow diagram per boundary group and one table of rows `| id | boundary | STRIDE | threat | status | evidence | mitigation or owner |`. Status is `control` (exists, with evidence paths), `gap` (names a phase, a release or a finding), `accepted` (a documented residual risk) or `operator` (only the deployment can meet it). OWASP Threat Dragon v2.6.2 (Apache-2.0) is an optional workshop editor whose models are exported back to Markdown; nothing depends on it.
2. **Content:** hardening 3.2's 46 rows over B1–B14, re-taken against Phases 7–11 as built, plus at least:
   - **B2** (the embed and the preview, Phase 9 C175.2): the preview's origin checks, the embed's `postMessage` origin and source checks, messages without PII, `frame-ancestors` per request;
   - **B4** (the console): OIDC mix-up, `state` and `nonce` replay, login CSRF, unverified or mutable email and multi-tenant issuers, just-in-time role escalation through a custom role, SSO without a second factor, the identity-provider deprovisioning lag (C248), the idle timeout (C245), the shared login limiter (C246), the first-admin invitation in logs (C250.5);
   - **B3** (the API) and **B9** (storage): the export's leak, its exfiltration rate and its objects at rest (C253), the pepper rotation window (C247), key scope escalation (B3-E1), the runtime role deleting audit evidence (C249.3);
   - **B12** (the code runner): the `tcp:` transport with a shared token inside the namespace (C263.6);
   - **B11** (Android): T1, I1 and S1 with Phase 10's evidence (C256.3);
   - **B13** (Connect, fully designed): a spoofed or wrong destination, token replay and double redemption, enumeration through ids and the create's name oracle (C230.2, C231.10), sharing beyond the consent or the policy (C230.4, C234.2), a forged or stale consent (attested) and a self-attested consent by the token's creator (C236.2, C230.3's `attested-consent`), an attestation overriding a witnessed decline (C236.11), a cross-tenant read or write through a bug (C240.5, C240.7), a redemption racing a withdrawal, deactivation or redaction, sandbox principals changing a production connection (C231.9), notices lost after token deletion (C241.4), the person completing a client-side redemption not being the subject (C235.8), peek as a repeat-read channel (C233.5), counterparty audit entries naming the other organisation's staff (C240.4), a copy re-shared under a new name (C232.1's `source_is_copy`), misuse of a copy by the destination (accepted), fabrication by the install's operator (accepted under shape 1);
   - **B14** (the supply chain): a compromised scanner release or action (the Trivy case), an unpinned base image, an SBOM that misses vendored code;
   - **BX-D1:** operator patterns (C252).
3. **Accepted risks** (a section of their own, each with its monitoring): the API limiter failing open without Valkey (H18, the metric `rate_limiter_store_unavailable` and its alert); spoofable liveness and document forensics (PLAN §9); Phase 1's column-bound ciphertexts (B7-T1); a forwarded flow link used first by someone else (B1-S1, with `docs/flow/handoff.md` as the detail, H12); the blind index's linkability inside one environment; shape 1's trust in the install's operator; redacted data surviving in backups until they expire; the fixed Valkey password where the operator does not set ACL users; plain-text transport inside one namespace (C249.1, C277.4); a withdrawal recalling no copy already made (C237.5). Each "monitoring" names a metric registered in `metrics-registry.ts` (C250.7) or a documented operator check.
4. **The parsing test** (`packages/docs-tools`, ours: a private package for document generators and their tests, under the 80% gate): every boundary considers all six STRIDE letters (a row, or a "not applicable because" line); every row has a known status; every `control` cites at least one path that exists; every `gap` names a phase, the next release or a finding id; every id is unique and every id the ASVS checklist cites exists.
5. **Upkeep:** a full review at each phase end beside the ASVS review, recorded with its commit in PROGRESS; PLAN §7 gains the rule that every phase plan has a line "threat-model rows added or changed" (C282).
6. **Honesty:** the model is the project's own first pass; it names what it found, not everything an attacker could find.

### C274. The ASVS 5.0.0 Level 2 checklist (hardening 4; decision 7)

1. **The file:** `docs/security/asvs-5.0.0-l2.csv`, one row per Level 2 requirement id (253: 70 at L1 and 183 at L2), columns `id` (`v5.0.0-<chapter>.<section>.<requirement>`), `level`, `status` (`met`, `partial`, `gap`, `not_applicable`, `operator`), `evidence` (repository paths: tests, contract tests, docs, CI jobs), `notes` (our words), `threats` (threat-model ids), `reviewed_at`, `reviewed_commit`.
2. **No requirement text** (CC BY-SA 4.0, share-alike; not legal advice): only ids, our statuses, our notes and our evidence are committed. `scripts/fetch_asvs.py` pins the 5.0.0 flat JSON export (162,870 bytes, SHA-256 `8201b20eec2908c3380ac600c91c8ba746346fbb808859366abb232027532311`) and never commits it; `--verify-ids` derives the Level 2 id list from the fetched export and compares it with the committed `docs/security/asvs-5.0.0-l2-ids.txt`, so tests need no network.
3. **The evidence test** (`packages/docs-tools`): the CSV holds exactly the committed ids; every evidence path exists; a `met` row cites at least one test or CI job. 11F's documentation-and-coverage reviewer (read-only) checks each `met` row's evidence against its requirement (all of them, or a recorded sample of at least one per chapter) and writes the result in its review report. `fix-records` turns each row the check finds unsupported into `partial` with its reason, and records the check's result, with its commit, in the 11F entry's draft for PROGRESS (verification of review H15); a `not_applicable` row has a reason; a `gap` row names a phase or the next release; every `threats` id exists in the threat model. A generated `docs/security/asvs-summary.md` gives the counts per chapter and status.
4. **Applicability after Phase 11:** V10 (OAuth and OIDC) and V6.8 now apply to the console's OIDC client side (C248), V9 to the ID tokens it verifies; V17 (WebRTC), V4.3 (GraphQL) and V4.4 (WebSocket) stay not applicable with their reasons. The day-one gaps' **targets** (review H15: statuses are set from evidence, not in advance): G-IDLE, G-LOGIN, G-REGEX, G-DEVIMG, G-CLASS, G-KEYDOC `met`; G-CSP and G-FRAME `met` with Phase 9's evidence; G-HSTS `partial` (met by the chart's ingress, the operator's on compose, named in `notes`); G-TLS and G-LOGINV `partial` with their operator part; G-SECRETS `partial` (files now, a KMS adapter in the next release); G-SBOM `partial` while SBOMs exist only as 30-day workflow artifacts (until they are published, or a committed source-tree SBOM is regenerated by a test). A row is `met` only when it is met on every supported install path (compose and the chart); otherwise it is `partial` with the path named in `notes`.
5. **Wording** (decision 7): "self-assessed against OWASP ASVS 5.0.0 Level 2", never "certified" or "compliant" (OWASP certifies nothing); a docs test refuses those words next to ASVS or MASVS anywhere in `docs/`, the READMEs (the npm packages' included), `SECURITY.md`, the chart's `Chart.yaml` description and `NOTES.txt`, and the release notes, and refuses "compliant" and "complies" anywhere in `docs/compliance/` except in a sentence that denies them (review H18).
6. **Upkeep:** reviewed at each phase end with the threat model; a change to a cited evidence file marks its row's `reviewed_commit` stale in the summary.
7. **Honesty:** the checklist is a self-assessment with evidence a reader can follow; most rows rest on the project's own tests, which share the project's blind spots.

### C275. The MASVS v2.1.0 checklist for Android (hardening 4.4; Phase 10 C213, C219)

1. **The file:** `docs/security/masvs-2.1.0.csv` for `openverify-sdk`, `openverify-nfc` and each optional artifact that exists, one row per MASVS v2.1.0 control (the groups `MASVS-STORAGE`, `-CRYPTO`, `-AUTH`, `-NETWORK`, `-PLATFORM`, `-CODE`, `-RESILIENCE` and `-PRIVACY`, each group name re-read by `contracts-11e` against the release), in C274's columns, with Phase 10's evidence (the source scans, the saved-state canary, `FLAG_SECURE` on every window, the overlay protections, dependency locking and verification, the ABI dump, the boundary test).
2. **Licence:** `contracts-11e` reads MASVS's licence at the v2.1.0 tag first; if it is share-alike, only ids are committed, as for ASVS. `scripts/fetch_masvs.py` pins the release's export.
3. **The same evidence test** as C274.3, and the same wording rule.
4. **Honesty:** a library checklist covers the SDK, not the host app it runs in; the README says the host's own MASVS duties remain.

### C276. The security records (G-KEYDOC, G-CLASS, G-LOGINV, G-SBOM; ASVS v5.0.0-11.1.1, 11.1.2, 14.1.1, 14.1.2, 16.1.1, 15.1.1)

1. **`docs/security/crypto-inventory.md`** (key management policy and cryptographic inventory): every key and secret (the KEK and its previous keys, data keys, the blind-index key and subkeys, the pepper and its previous peppers, webhook secrets, TOTP secrets, WebAuthn credentials, every token family, the vision, flow-server, BFF and code-runner tokens, OIDC client secrets, TLS material the operator supplies), each with its algorithm and length, where it is stored, which process reads it, its rotation procedure and command, and its usage limit (the 2^32 wrap budget per KEK); a test fails when a secret `config-registry.ts` marks (C277.2) is missing.
2. **`docs/security/data-classification.md`:** the classes (biometric; identity documents; identity fields; contact; device and network; financial and crypto; operator and audit; secrets; public and list data), each with its encryption at rest, default retention, logging rule, permission, export inclusion (C253) and share category (C230.4); a test maps every `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` entry and every field type to a class. It doubles as the compliance guide's record-of-processing input.
3. **`docs/security/logging.md`:** each process's log events and levels, the pino redaction paths, what never reaches a log, where logs may go (a separate store the operator runs, ASVS v5.0.0-16.4.3), their retention; a test compares the documented redaction paths with the logger's configured list.
4. **`docs/security/vulnerability-policy.md`:** the remediation time frames (decision 6), the allowlist rules (C259.2), how a fix ships, and the reporting channel; the root `SECURITY.md` names GitHub's private vulnerability reporting as the channel, which exists only on a public repository, so the file says the channel opens with the public release (Q1).
5. **`docs/security/README.md`:** the index, with the wording rule of C274.5.
6. **Honesty:** the records describe the code at their commit; their tests catch drift only where a machine can compare.

### C277. The self-hosting guide (hardening 8.1, 7.4; H10, H19, H21; PLAN §10)

1. **`docs/self-hosting/`** parts: `README.md` (the processes, what each talks to, the boundaries, linking the threat model); `install-compose.md` (one host, the `app` profile on the production images); `install-kubernetes.md` (the chart, Kubernetes ≥ 1.35, Helm 4.3 or 3.22, the namespace label, a network plugin that enforces policies); `requirements.md` (hardening 7.4's table: PostgreSQL 17 with pgvector, a Redis-protocol store with AOF and `noeviction`, S3-compatible storage with versioning and the two delete permissions, SMTP, optional SMS, TLS and an ingress, secrets, observability, model weights); `configuration.md` (generated, 2); `sizing.md`; `secrets-and-rotation.md`; `backups-and-restore.md`; `upgrades.md`; `database.md`; `valkey.md`; `first-run.md`; `break-glass.md`; `monitoring.md`; `hardening-checklist.md` (generated from the ASVS and MASVS rows whose status is `operator`, after `docs-security`'s ASVS checkpoint); `verify-artifacts.md` (which says "no artifact of this release is signed" until 11G's publish job exists, with a docs test keyed to the publish switch, and gives the `cosign verify` commands only then; review H16). `upgrades.md` says that this is the chart's first release, so an upgrade from an earlier release is untested; the kind run tests a `helm upgrade` of the same chart with changed values (C265.4).
2. **The configuration reference is generated** from `apps/api/src/config-registry.ts` (ours, created by `foundation-11b`: every `OPENVERIFY_*` variable with its description, default, whether it is a secret, its `_FILE` form, and a `process` field naming the processes that read it: api, worker, migrate, dashboard, flow, vision, code-runner; and **pattern entries** for names composed at run time, such as `OPENVERIFY_PROVIDER_{NAME}_{MODE}` and the provider secret fields, marked `secret: true`; review F10), with a test that scans every source file of every process (not only `config.ts`) for `OPENVERIFY_` reads and fails on an unregistered name, and a docs test that the committed page equals a fresh render. The chart's per-process Secrets (C264.3) and the crypto inventory (C276.1) read the same registry.
3. **What only the operator can do** (requirements and the hardening checklist): TLS between hosts and to the stores, a secrets store, log shipping, backups with the keys apart, a network plugin, HSTS when no chart ingress is used.
4. **Accepted transport risk:** plain text inside one namespace is the operator's choice, stated with the startup warnings of C249.1.
5. **Key rotation procedures:** the KEK (move to `OPENVERIFY_KEK_PREVIOUS`, set the new key, roll every process, `kek:rewrap`, remove the old key when the command reports nothing left); the blind-index key with `blind-index:rebuild`; webhook secrets with dual signing; API keys (expiry, `clone`, `expire`); the pepper (C247.1, with `api-keys:pepper-status`); the vision, flow-server, BFF and runner tokens (their `_PREVIOUS` forms where they exist, and the restart order); OIDC client secrets; database, Valkey and S3 credentials.
6. **Backups and restore:** physical backups with WAL archiving for production, `pg_dump` for small installs; the KEK and the blind-index key backed up apart from the data; S3 versioning and replication; a restore drill checklist (restore, `db:migrate` no-ops, a sealed value opens, a file downloads); redacted data stays in backups until they expire, so the backup window is the real erasure delay, which the compliance guide repeats.
7. **Database and Valkey settings:** the two roles and `db:grant-runtime` (C249.3); per-role `statement_timeout`, `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` and, by C272's result, `jit = off` (H10, owner decision 9 after 6C); `log_parameter_max_length=0` and `log_parameter_max_length_on_error=0` (C73.8); `pg_upgrade` with pgvector installed in the new cluster first; Valkey AOF, `noeviction`, ACL users.
8. **First run** (H21): `org:create` (C250.5); bind providers per environment; the first list syncs, watching the first real EU and UN syncs and the first `pep:sync`; `trust-anchors:sync`; retention settings; the UN opt-in as Q10 allows.
9. **Break-glass** (H19): `user:reset-mfa` and `user:allow-password`, who may run them, the audit entries they write, how to review afterwards.
10. **Monitoring:** queue lag and depth, in-flight against cap (C251.4), the limiter store metric (H18), report and sync errors, sync freshness, webhook failure rates, vision child crashes, certificate expiry.
11. **Honesty:** the guide's sizing starts from the load report's machine (C268.7) and says so.

### C278. The compliance guide (hardening 8.2; H11, H13, H20; PLAN §9, §10; not legal advice)

1. **`docs/compliance/`**, opening on every page with "not legal advice" and, until Q7 is answered, "not reviewed by counsel": `README.md` (the index, and the split between what the software provides and what only the operator can do); `roles.md` (the operator as controller, and as processor for its own clients with Article 28 terms; a hosting operator that is processor for two organisations moves data between them through Connect only on both controllers' instructions, and keeps `OPENVERIFY_CONNECT_ENABLED` off where its terms do not allow it (C231.10, review P09); the project as neither, an inference to confirm with counsel).
2. **Topics** (hardening 8.2's table, each row's software side with its contract or path): biometric data as special-category data (UK GDPR Article 9(1) and 9(2)(a), read; the EU text re-read before publication); the DPIA and its inputs (threat model, data classification, check catalog); data subject requests (C253's export, redaction for erasure, the UK GDPR's Article 12A and the Data (Use and Access) Act 2025's ss. 76 and 78, the EU's Article 12(3) month); automated decisions (an index to Phase 8's `automated-decisions.md`, H13, with the Act's s. 80); Illinois BIPA (740 ILCS 14 and Public Act 103-0769); Washington RCW 19.375 (notice, consent **or** a mechanism to prevent later commercial use, as the fact-check corrected; the photographs exclusion and its open question for templates); Texas Business and Commerce Code ch. 503 and Colorado's 2024 amendment; California's CCPA as amended and the CPPA regulations' dates (effective 2026-01-01; ADMT duties from 2027-01-01; attestations and audit certifications from 2028-04-01); the EU AI Act's Annex III 1(a) carve-out for 1:1 verification, and 1:N uses as a question for counsel; device and network data with the strictly-necessary basis (WP29 9/2014 §7.5, CNIL délibération 2020-091 ¶49, UK PECR Sch. A1 para 4 from 2026-02-05; H11) and Phase 10's probe list; third-party recipients (generated from the provider registry: every adapter, off until bound); licensed and public-sector data (sanctions per ADR 0005, the UN list's status under Q10, CSCA master lists fetched by the operator (H20), the SCUBA files pending Phase 10 P1); retention and backups; breach response (Article 33's 72 hours, state laws); sharing (both controllers' duties, consent records, the agreement reference, withdrawal and what cannot be recalled; the duty to notify each recipient of erasure and rectification and to name recipients on request, Articles 19 and 15(1)(c), which the kept disclosure record serves (C241.4, review P04); restricted transfers under Chapter V when the destination is established outside the source's jurisdiction, whatever server holds both rows, and the information Article 14 requires for an attested (API) redemption; BIPA §15(b)'s written purpose, retention term and release for the destination (C238.9; review P13); each re-read under C278.3 or marked so).
3. **The re-read rule** (hardening owner 20; decision 45): before the guide is committed, `docs-compliance` reads each statute and regulation it cites from its official text (EUR-Lex for the EU GDPR, ilga.gov for BIPA, the Texas statutes site, Colorado's, the California Civil Code); a source it cannot read (a challenge page, a failed TLS check, a page that needs script) is never bypassed, and its row says "not re-read on <date>: <reason>" in place. Every other fact keeps its citation.
4. **DSAR in practice:** what C253's export covers and does not (C253.3), the search an operator must still make, the exemptions it must judge (the `operator-review` part of the manifest, and tipping-off offences such as the UK Proceeds of Crime Act 2002 s. 333A, re-read under C278.3 or marked so), and the time limits. **Questions for counsel** (listed under Q7; reviews P12, P16): whether operator-mediated withdrawal meets Article 7(3)'s "as easy as" rule; whether excluding face templates from the access export holds; which exemptions apply to the `operator-review` part.
5. **A record-of-processing template** generated from the data classification (C276.2) and the recipient list.
6. **Honesty:** the guide maps the law's demands onto the software's features; it does not say that any deployment complies (C274.5's test covers this guide), and counsel's review is the owner's (Q7).

### C279. The check catalog and the API reference (hardening 8.3; PLAN §4, §10)

1. **`docs/checks/catalog.md`, generated** by `packages/docs-tools` from `CHECK_REGISTRY` (89 checks at `91c83e02`, plus those Phases 7–10 add), `REPORT_REGISTRY` and Phase 7's signal registry: one table per verification type and per report type with id, tier, check type, stage, inputs, whether a model is used, the configuration keys with defaults, the calibration document and its headline figures (read from a machine-readable summary block each calibration report carries, with a test that each figure appears in the cited report; review H27), and what the check does not detect. The last column comes from `docs/checks/limits.json` (ours, keyed by check id), with a test that every registry id has an entry and every family page mentions every id of its family.
2. **The vendor-name column** (PLAN §4's mapping, where an id differs from the vendor's check name) is rendered only when the vendor-naming switch of C280.6 is on; otherwise the column is absent.
3. **The API reference:** `docs/api/openapi.json` (generated, unchanged) and a generated `docs/api/README.md`: every path grouped by tag, with its scope (declared in each route's `config.scopes`), idempotency (C12's rule by method), its rate policy (from the rate-limit plugin's route mapping), the `OpenVerify-Version` header, pagination, and the webhook events with their samples; the problem document's shape and a link to each family's page for its codes, because routes declare no problem codes and a per-route column would be hand-written and drift (review F18; declaring codes on every route is deferred, C286); a test that the page equals a fresh render.
4. **Honesty:** the catalog is generated from the registries, so it says only what the registries and their limits file state.

### C280. The mapping from the source vendor's public API (hardening 8.4; PLAN §10; Q8)

1. **Generated** by `packages/docs-tools` from `docs/research/persona-surface.md` (the 183 path files of the vendor's public OpenAPI at the pinned commit, and the event, check and report identifiers) and OpenVerify's `docs/api/openapi.json`: one table per section of `persona-surface.md` (verification checks, verification types, report types, list-item types, webhook events, path files) and a Connect table (the scopes such as `connect.read` and `connect_share_token.write`, the SDK's `shareToken` option and the hosted flow's `inquiry-id` plus `share-token` entry, which OpenVerify replaces with `meta.share-token-id`; review H24): every identifier mapped to its OpenVerify counterpart, or "not available" with a reason code (`next-release`, `not-planned`, `licensed-data`, `vendor-retiring`); a test fails when an identifier of any section has no row, or when either side changes without the table.
2. **The concerns table** (hardening 8.4): base path, authentication, the version header, key casing (kebab-case only here), idempotency (the same header), pagination (`page[after]`, `page[size]`; no `page[before]` on `GET /inquiries`), the webhook signature (the same `t=`, `v1=` scheme under another header name and secret), ids (`<prefix>_<ULID>`; source ids carried in `reference-id` or an account field, with a mapping table), checks and reports (the catalog's column), events (the Connect names of C239 among them).
3. **Migration notes:** historical captures and results are imported, if at all, as account fields or attachments labelled with their source, never as passed checks (rule 5); a cloned-result route does not exist between platforms.
4. **Factual only:** identifiers as behaviour, no copied text, no logo, and a line that OpenVerify is not affiliated with the vendor.
5. **Where it lives:** `docs/migration/from-hosted-api.md` while the switch is off; `docs/migration/from-persona.md` when it is on.
6. **The vendor-naming switch** (pending the owner, Q8): the generator takes `--vendor-name`; with it off (the default until Q8 is decided), every vendor identifier that contains the vendor's name (its version and signature header names, its host) is rendered by role ("the source API's version header"), and the page names no vendor; 11G re-renders it with the name only if the owner confirms that a factual docs page may name the source system. **If the owner declines,** the neutral page is the release's page.
7. **Honesty:** a mapping says where behaviour corresponds; it does not claim equivalence of results.

### C281. The sharing guide (connect 6.1 item 9, "Ours, keep and label" 15)

1. **`docs/connect/`:** `README.md` (what Connect is, the trust assumption of one install, what redemption cannot recall); `connections.md` (lifecycle, activation in the console, policies and their versions, the agreement reference); `share-tokens.md` (lifetime, single use, expiry by the source, by deactivation and by withdrawal, environment kinds, the handle model); `consent.md` (both controllers' duties, flow and attested records, the explicit biometric tick, withdrawal and its limits; not legal advice); `peek-and-redeem.md` (the envelope, clone and hydrate, mappings, account linking, limits); `provenance.md` (the marker, `accept-cloned-results` and what it means to rely on another organisation's checks, and C235.8's binding rule: a live selfie matched against the cloned portrait, or the audited `cloned-results-unbound` acknowledgement and the risk it accepts; review P08); `client-side.md` (the server-created inquiry, the `share-consent` step on web and Android with its withdrawal exit, no token in any URL, C235.8's binding of the person to the copy, and that the flow never shows a hydrated or cloned value to whoever opens the link); `events-and-workflows.md`; `mapping.md` (the vendor's eight paths against ours, under C280.6's switch); `limits.md` (no sharing between installs, no report sharing, no wallet credentials, with their reasons).
2. **Honesty:** every "ours" behaviour is labelled with its reason, and every limit is stated where a reader would assume the opposite.

### C282. Records, ADR and corrections (connect "Corrections" 1–5; hardening 10.16)

- **ADR 0010** "Connect, release hardening and deployment" (the six rules of the Summary; the two deployment shapes; the owner-gated publication; the tool rules), written by `contracts-11a` and extended by `contracts-11c` (decision 46).
- **PLAN** (decision 47): §2's "Connect / Reusable" row (peek with `full`/`presence`, field mappings, client-side redemption through a server-created inquiry, environment-kind matching, the asynchronous redeem, both organisations on one install, consent records as ours, reusable personas not built; connect correction 2); §3's technology table (vegeta and the `load/` driver for load, Locust under Q12, the scanners, the Helm chart under `deploy/helm/`, production images, the KEK from an environment variable or a file with a KMS adapter in the next release; "Helm (later)" removed); §3's hardening list (HSTS at the ingress, the internal listener, the per-tenant quotas); **§6 Phase 11: "k6 load test" replaced by "HTTP load test (vegeta and a driver of ours; k6 is AGPL-3.0 and fails rule 4)"**, the seven Workflows and the pending items Q1–Q12; PLAN's "Next release" section gains C286's next-release items (review H25: shape 2 sharing, SAML and SCIM, the KMS adapter, arm64, the wallet work, the self-service withdrawal link, the video liveness challenge, the `/metrics` endpoint); §7: every phase plan gains a "threat-model rows added or changed" line, and the threat model and checklists are reviewed at each phase end; §9: a supply-chain row citing the Trivy compromise of 2026-03-19, and the biometric-law row's "DSAR tooling" pointing at C253; §10: each line with the contract that meets it.

- **`docs/plans/phase-6.plan.md` C88:** a dated note that Phase 11 defers report sharing (connect correction 4; C286).
- **`CLAUDE.md`'s command list** (not its rules): the new commands (`org:create`, `db:grant-runtime`, `api-keys:pepper-status`, `user:allow-password`, `seed:load`, `watchlists:rebuild-key-df`, the load and chart commands) are proposed by `integration-11e` in its report and written by the orchestrator, since `CLAUDE.md` is the owner's file.

### C283. Dynamic testing with ZAP (hardening 5.5; decision 48)

1. **Scans** (ZAP v2.17.0, its image by digest, run with `docker run` under the e2e lock against the reusable e2e stack in its images mode (C257.3), never against a production install): the API scan over the stack's `/openapi.json` with a sandbox API key holding every sandbox scope; a baseline (passive) scan of the hosted flow (a session opened by the e2e helper) and of the console (a seeded sandbox admin's session cookie supplied through a replacer rule, so MFA is not scripted).
2. **Rules:** `security/zap/rules.tsv` sets each rule's action (`FAIL`, `WARN`, `IGNORE`), every `IGNORE` with its reason; a high finding fails the run; findings are written to `docs/security/dast-<date>.md` (route, rule, status, the fix or the reason). **Credentials and the raw output** (review P30): the sandbox key and the admin session are created per run and revoked at its end; ZAP's raw reports (which record request headers) stay outside git with the e2e artifacts; the committed report is generated from them with every header removed and passes gitleaks before it is committed.
3. **What it does not cover** (stated in the report): cross-tenant authorisation (C240.2's matrix is the evidence), business logic, the flow's later steps, the console behind real MFA; ZAP is a regression net for headers, error bodies and injection.
4. **Where:** locally only, in 11F Stage 1 and before each release's final dispatch, on the images mode of the e2e stack (C257.3); the dated report is the release's evidence (review F15: a DAST job in `release.yml` would need the whole stack with its weights and a seeded sandbox, a large share of the e2e job's 70 minutes, on top of the image builds); never per push, nightly or in `release.yml`.
5. **Honesty:** a clean scan means ZAP's rules found nothing on the routes it reached.

### C284. The release checklist's machine-checkable part

1. **`docs/release/checklist.md`:** the items of this plan's "Release checklist", each with its state (`done`, `open`, `owner`), its evidence and its date, kept by the integration agents and the orchestrator.
2. **`pnpm release:check`** (`packages/docs-tools`): every checklist item marked `done` cites evidence that exists; the threat model, ASVS and MASVS tests pass; no `gap` row lacks a phase or release; every Q1–Q12 and Phase 10 P1–P5 has its state recorded in PROGRESS; the version strings agree (the root and published `package.json`s, `Chart.yaml`'s `appVersion`, `android/gradle.properties`' `openverifyVersion`, the changelogs); every item of the "Deferred" section of each `docs/plans/phase-*.plan.md` that has one (Phases 6–11: C88, C110, C145, C177, C223 and C286) and every row of C288's "Carried (earlier plans and the progress log)" table, which places the Phase 0–5 carries by hand because those plans have no "Deferred" section, is under "Carried forward" or "Next release" in PROGRESS, or closed as moot with its reason (review H25 and its verification); every "owner to confirm" or pending row of `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` is listed (H19); a checklist line that depends on Phase 4 decision 11 or Phase 10 P2 cannot read `done` while that exception is open unless the release ships the decline outcome (H20); the 11F verifier's table holds no confirmed CRITICAL or HIGH that is not fixed (H17); mediums past their time frame are listed (H14); and every disclosure a contract sends to the release notes is present in C284.4's file (H21). **Two modes** (verification V01): the default fails on every rule above that agents can satisfy and reports each owner-dependent item (Q1–Q12, Phase 10 P1–P5, a shipped "owner to confirm" row, an open P2) with its state, exiting 0 while those are only open; it runs in Validation from 11E on (it is built by `docs-release`, F16), so it is the gate of 11E, 11F and 11G and never waits on the owner. `pnpm release:check --tag` adds the release's own refusals: any checklist line still `open`, any shipped component without a decision or a built decline outcome (C261.2, Q11), and a flow image with `onnxruntime-web` as the shipped one while P2 is open (H20); a confirmed CRITICAL or HIGH not fixed fails both modes. `--tag` runs only in the release checklist's tag step, and its output is the release gate's summary.
3. **Honesty:** the script checks that evidence exists and agrees, not that it is sufficient; the owner's review of the checklist is the gate.
4. **The release notes** (review H21; decision 75): `docs/release/notes.md`, generated by `packages/docs-tools` from the checklist states, the calibration verdicts (C269, C271), C289's outcomes for every declined or open owner item, the image digests with each base's distribution source archive (C257.5), each artifact's SHA-256 when nothing is signed (C262.4), the open SFace review (Q6), the built-in consent texts' review state (C238.10) and every missed budget with its gap; built by `docs-release` in 11E, refreshed by `fix-records` (11F) and `integration-11g`; a test that the committed file equals a fresh render, and `release:check` fails when a required disclosure is missing.

### C285. npm publication (owner-gated: Q4; Phase 9 C159.7, C175.2)

1. **What is built now** (C266): publishable `@openverify/node`, `@openverify/js` and `@openverify/react`, packed with `pnpm pack`, each with an SBOM, kept as `release.yml` artifacts.
2. **When granted:** `release.yml`'s publish job publishes them under the owner's `@openverify` npm scope with provenance (which needs a public repository and the GitHub OIDC trust the owner configures), in the `release` environment.
3. **If declined:** the tarballs and their SHA-256 are the release's artifacts, and the READMEs show installing from a tarball or from source.
4. **Honesty:** a README names a registry only once the package resolves there.

### C286. Deferred

- **Connect** (each with its reason): sharing between two installs (connect shape 2, R36: needs a key exchange, a trust bootstrap and a signed envelope, for which connect 3.4 records an SD-JWT design; the next release); report sharing (connect R8, K1; Phase 6 C88: a cloned screening is a "no match" for lists, versions and a matcher the destination never chose, which ADR 0005 allows only beside the search actually made; destinations run their own reports on hydrated data); the `ready` prefetch (a cached PII copy to redact for a latency gain); reusable personas, their events (`inquiry.reusable-persona-created`, `inquiry.reusable-persona-used`, `inquiry.reusable-persona-updated`, `inquiry.reusable-persona-assurance-failed`, `checkpoint.reusable-persona-used`; review H24) and `from-reusable-persona` (R28: the vendor is retiring them); the cross-network history check (R29: needs a population of many customers' verifications); OAuth object grants (R30) and claim-only results with Privacy Pass (R31): specialised, and Connect covers the sharing need; a self-service withdrawal link (C237.4: no end-user account exists yet); the wallet verifier and issuer (R32–R34: OpenID4VP 1.0, HAIP 1.0, SD-JWT VC, the Digital Credentials API, ISO/IEC 18013-5 and TS 18013-7, OpenID4VCI 1.0; per-operator registrations, real wallets on devices, libraries that moved on 2026-10-29; Phase 5 C68 and its decision 44 stand; when adopted, the newest pinnable versions are re-read, today `@openid4vc/*` 0.7.0, `@sd-jwt/*` 0.22.0, `@owf/mdoc` 0.8.2 and `jose` 6.2.12, with `pako` ("MIT AND Zlib") decided then; connect owner 11, 12); the EUDI relying-party registration (R35: documented in `docs/connect/limits.md`, nothing built).
- **Hardening:** SAML SSO and SCIM (Phase 9 S15: SAML's XML-signature surface needs its own library review and tests, and OIDC already covers sign-in with the three identity providers the vendor names; SCIM's deprovisioning is covered meanwhile by disabling a user, which ends its sessions, C245.3; the next release); a KMS adapter for the KEK (PLAN §3: a real KMS needs a cloud account to test, and a sandbox-only adapter would prove nothing; secrets as files cover the mount; the next release); arm64 images (vision's native wheels and the minutes; the next release); CodeQL (Q9); a nightly scan, DAST or emulator matrix (minutes; the CI cadence); chaos tests (Valkey down, a database failover; stated as untested, C268.8); a `/metrics` endpoint (metrics are structured log events in this release, C250.7); problem codes declared per route (C279.3); KEDA and Gateway API templates (an example and an optional route only); per-process Valkey ACL users in the development compose file (documented only); pgAudit (named as an operator option; its licence not re-read).
- **Android:** built-in certificate pinning (C256.2: a recipe instead); everything under PLAN "Next release: iOS" (unchanged by this phase; nothing here builds for iOS).
- **Carried from earlier plans:** a multi-frame or video liveness challenge (Phase 4 decision 8, "a later phase"; Phase 10 C223 defers video capture; the next release); Phase 9's teams and availability and the public `print` routes (Phase 9 C177, the next release, unchanged).
- **Closed as moot:** Phase 2's "API gap, left for a later phase" (`GET /verifications/:id` with files and redaction state; Phase 9 C173.2 builds `include=files` and `redacted-at`).

### C287. Fixtures and end to end

1. **Fixtures** (synthetic only, rule 3): C244.2's Connect fixtures; OIDC discovery documents, JWKS and ID tokens signed by test keys generated at run time (never committed); a load seed; export manifests; chart `ci/` values; the PII-scan allowlist's positive and negative cases.
2. **Testcontainers:** double redemption, redemption against expiry, deactivation, withdrawal and source redaction in both orders; the authorization matrix (C240.2); the cross-tenant import boundary, a test per cross-environment write and the export's counterparty read, and the generated probe over every path (C240.5, C240.7); consent texts insert-if-absent under concurrency; the idle timeout race; the Valkey login limiter across two instances; the pepper rotation's compare-and-set; the runtime role's refusals and the migrate lock with two runs; `/ready` with each store down; the internal listener; the quota sweep's fairness; the export of an account touching every resource family; the UN switch locked; a conditional 304 and the forced full check.
3. **Full-stack specs** (the reusable stack with shards, under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`, each ≤ 90 s with its budget in a header line):
   - **11A:** two organisations on one install: create a connection, activate it in the console with the step-up, consent in the source's flow (`share-consent`, source side), create a token, peek, redeem by API into a clone, see the Cloned banner and `needs_review`; a second token redeemed client-side through an inquiry created with `meta.share-token-id`, with the destination's `share-consent` and a template that accepts cloned results, skipping its government-ID step while its live selfie is matched against the cloned portrait (C235.8); the same with `accept-cloned-results` off, where every step runs live and the clones carry the Cloned marker (review H01); withdraw a third consent and see its token expire; every webhook received and verified;
   - **11B:** an idle console session signed out; OIDC sign-in against the in-process provider, with just-in-time provisioning off and on; an account export downloaded and its manifest checked; a flooding environment held at its cap while another's report completes;
   - **11C:** the e2e stack's images mode (C257.3): the 11A and 11B specs re-run on the production images, locally at 11C's checkpoints;
   - **11F:** the ZAP scans (C283).
4. **The Android side:** host tests of the `share-consent` renderer against golden client views, the request fixtures under `contracts/fixtures/flow-requests/android/` parsed by the server's zod schemas (Phase 10 C224.4), and Phase 10's `:openverify-sdk:stackTest` extended with the client-side redemption, inside the e2e-locked section (e2e lock outside, Gradle lock inside).

### C288. Coverage of the gap tables and the carried items (decision 70)

| Rows (connect gap tables) | Placement |
|---|---|
| R1 (objects and prefixes) | C230.1, C230.2 |
| R2 (one direction, one per pair, self-connections) | C230.2, C231.1 |
| R3 (activation by the destination, console only) | C231.3 (decision 18) |
| R4 (deactivate, reactivate, no deletion, `archived`; tokens expire on deactivation, ours) | C231.4, C231.5, C231.7, C230.2 |
| R5 (the share policy, ours) | C230.3, C231.6 (decision 17) |

### C289. The owner-gated parts and their switches

| Item | Built in 11A–11F | Switch | 11G, when granted | If declined |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 repository visibility | everything, private | — | CodeQL (Q9), attestations and keyless signing become possible; `SECURITY.md`'s channel opens | stays private: no attestations, no keyless signing, no CodeQL; artifacts staged only |
| Q2 registry and image publication | images, chart package, SBOMs, scans | `OPENVERIFY_PUBLISH`, environment `release` | push images by digest and the chart as an OCI artifact; release notes with the base sources | images built from the documented Dockerfiles; staged artifacts are the release |
| Q3 signing identity | the signing and attestation steps | the same | cosign keyless (public) or a key in the owner's secret store; provenance attached | nothing signed; checksums published in the release notes |
| Q4 npm | publishable packages and tarballs | the same | publish with provenance | tarballs are the release |
| Q5 release age for binaries, images, actions; uv 0.13.3 | every new pin ≥ 14 days | — | record the rule; keep uv 0.13.3 as an exception, or replace it with the newest uv dated at least 14 days before the change | the rule is recorded as covering them and uv is replaced |
| Q6 SFace's licence review; weights in images | weights fetched at start | `models.source` | bake weights into the vision image only if the review clears SFace and the owner wants it | weights stay fetched; the release notes say the review is open |
| Q7 counsel's review of the compliance guide | the guide with "not reviewed by counsel" | — | remove the line once counsel's review is recorded | the line stays |
| Q8 the vendor's name in the migration page | the neutral page | `--vendor-name` | re-render as `from-persona.md` with the name | the neutral page stays |
| Q9 a paid scanner | the SAST baseline | — | CodeQL job (public repository, or a Code Security licence) | baseline only |
| Q10 the UN list's opt-in | the locked switch and the acknowledgement | `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_UN_OPT_IN_ALLOWED` | document and allow the opt-in | `un-sc` stays unenablable, as Phase 6 left it |
| Q11 MIT-0 (`nodemailer`, runtime) and EPL-1.0 (JUnit 4, test only) | everything; the API image staged as `pending-owner` (C261.2) | — | record both as accepted in `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` | `nodemailer` is replaced by an MIT mailer or a minimal SMTP client behind the existing mailer port, test-first; JUnit 4 has no drop-in removal while Robolectric needs it, so the outcome is a recorded exception request and the release notes say so |
| Q12 ZPL-2.1 (`zope.interface`, `zope.event`, through Locust's `gevent`; dev tooling in `load/`, never shipped) | `load/` on vegeta and the `httpx` driver (C268.1), with no ZPL component | `load/`'s lockfile | granted before 11D's Stage 1a: `contracts-11d` adds Locust and it replaces the driver for the multi-step scenarios, with libzmq (MPL-2.0, inside `pyzmq`'s wheels) and certifi in §5; granted later: nothing changes in this release (the driver stays and 11D's report stands, since no calibration agent runs after 11D), the grant is recorded, and Locust is adopted in the next release's load work (verification V16) | the driver stays; nothing changes |
| Phase 10 P2 and Phase 4 decision 11 (Eigen, MPL-2.0, compiled into `onnxruntime-web`'s WASM, shipped to browsers) | the flow image twice: with `onnxruntime-web` and, as the decline outcome, built with `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_FACE_GUIDANCE=off` (no `onnxruntime-web` in the bundle; the detector reports `unavailable`, `guidance-machine.ts`'s existing path), both tested in 11C (review H20) | the build flag | stage and, under Q2, publish the image with guidance | the image without guidance is the release's; the release notes say on-device face guidance is off |


## Workflow 11A (Connect: connections, share tokens, consent)

**Families and checkpoints:** (C) connections and tokens; (R) redemption and provenance; (K) consent and the `share-consent` step; (U) clients, console and workflows. Stage 1a and 1b commit by family, each checkpoint with every suite green and recorded in its TDD file. Ten agents (decision 66).

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-11a`.** It runs alone. It first writes `docs/research/phase11-inventory.md` (every claim with its `path:line`): it re-verifies the Starting point; reads Phase 9 as built (flow-spec v2's step union and client-view module, the engine's position and waiting state, screen and consent exits, the consent digest check, `COPY_KEYS`, the publish checks and acknowledgements, the console shell, settings pages and role editor), Phase 8 as built (the action registry, effect keys, the trigger list, the decision record, the admission slots), Phase 7 as built (the transaction prefix and resource, accounts' resolution, `PERMISSION_INFO`), Phase 10 as built (the step registry, `flow-spec-members.json`, the key map, the consent-text rows, `stackTest`); names every file 11A changes by a borrowed edit; and records drift as "Workflow 11A Stage 1a amendments". Then it commits, in order:
  - **Common:** ADR 0010 (its Connect half); the ids `cxn`, `cnst`, `cxnp`, `cnsr` with the prefix test; every 11A problem code with its golden fixture (C231–C238); the scopes and the permission of C240.1 with `PERMISSION_INFO`, the built-in role defaults and `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS`; the rate policies `connect-redeem` and `connect-peek` in `RATE_LIMIT_POLICIES`; the event names of C239.1 in `EVENT_NAMES` with the `eventSubject` rule, payload unions, samples and dashboard group; the optional `provenance` member on the existing `inquiry.*` payload schemas with a cloned-inquiry golden sample (C235.7(a); verification V09); the environment settings `share-token-max-seconds` and `consent-proof-retention-days`.
  - **(C):** the zod resources and route schemas of C230–C233 (connection, share policy with `attested-consent`, share token with `data-categories` and `source-consent`, the source-data envelope per subtype), frozen, with golden fixtures for each side's view; `packages/core/src/connect/categories.ts` (`DATA_CLASSES`, `SHARE_CATEGORIES`, `FIELD_TYPE_CLASS`) with its table test (C230.4; review F07); the install switch and the `connect-inbound` setting (C231.10); `okhttp-tls` in the Android test scope with its `verification-metadata.xml` entry, under the Gradle lock (C256.2; review F24).
  - **(R):** the redeem body and `redeem-summary` (C234.1, C234.8), the internal `fill-inquiry` mode (C234.11), `provenance` (C235.1), the publish codes `cloned_results_without_binding` and the acknowledgements `cloned-results-unbound` and `share-consent-default-text` (C235.8, C238.10), and the template-version setting `accept-cloned-results` with the `cloned-results` acknowledgement in Phase 9's publish schema (a named borrowed edit).
  - **(K):** the consent-record resource and routes (C236, C237); the `share-consent` step in flow-spec v2's union with its client view and its three exits (C238.1, C238.3, C238.4, and C237.6's withdraw exit with the session response's `withdrawable-share-consents` member; verification V02) as named borrowed edits to Phase 9's `packages/flow-spec` schema and `packages/contracts` flow API, with golden documents; the publish codes of C238.2; the `share.consent.*` keys in `COPY_KEYS` (`legal: consent`); `meta.share-token-id` on the inquiry create schema (C238.5); the `share-consent` members in `android/openverify-sdk/flow-spec-members.json`, classified (Phase 10 C194.4).
  - **(U):** the three action keys and the three trigger names of C243.2 in Phase 8's registry (declarations only, C243; verification V09).

  It owns any path its deliverables need.
- **Stage 1b: `foundation-11a`** (plumbing only; one migration extended per checkpoint, with the PGlite migration time measured before and after), beside Stage 2:
  - **(C):** the five tables of C230.5 with their indexes; the sealed columns in `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` and the rewrap list; repository stubs `packages/db/src/repositories/connect-{connections,share-policies,share-tokens}.ts`, each with its `*.cross-env.ts` module (C240.5; verification V04); the routes frozen at 501 (`apps/api/src/routes/server/connect-connections.ts`, `connect-share-tokens.ts`, `connect-redeem.ts`, `routes/dashboard/connect.ts`); the queue `connect-token-expiry` with an `unbuiltJob` stub; the rate policies wired; `apps/api/src/services/connect/cross-tenant.ts` as an interface listing every entry point C240.5 names (`listSharesTouching` among them), with C240.5's import-boundary rule and test (green from the start, since only the stub imports the `*.cross-env.ts` stubs) and the counterparty entry points of the event and audit writers as stubs it alone imports;
  - **(R):** the provenance and reliance columns of C230.5 (review F08); the queue `connect-redeem` with an `unbuiltJob` stub; the decision-engine hook that calls `services/connect/cloned-results.ts` (a stub returning "not counted", owned by `api-redeem`), as a named borrowed edit to Phase 9's decision file; the same function's hooks in Phase 8's condition evaluator and Phase 9's analytics query (C235.7; review H02), each a named borrowed edit;
  - **(K):** repository stubs `consent-records.ts` and `consent-texts.ts`, `consent-records.ts` with its `*.cross-env.ts` module; routes frozen at 501 (`routes/server/connect-consent-records.ts`, `routes/flow/share-consents.ts`); the hooks, each a call in a shared file to a stub in an owned file: the inquiry create route's `meta.share-token-id` (`services/connect/client-side.ts`, `api-consent`), Phase 9's client-view builder for the new step type (`services/flow-views/share-consent.ts`, `api-consent`), Phase 9's publish checks (`services/connect/publish-checks.ts`, `api-consent`; C238.2's hook, verification V06), the redaction cascade (`services/connect/redaction-hooks.ts`, `api-consent`), the retention pass (`services/connect/retention.ts`, `api-consent`);
  - **after the last checkpoint:** the OpenAPI snapshot.
- **Stage 2: `connect-logic`** (after Stage 1a common, (C) and (K); checkpoints in order: (1) policy and categories, (2) the envelope filter, (3) mappings and provenance, (4) the cloned-results rule, the share-consent text and the publish rules, so each Stage 3 agent waits only for the one it needs): C230.3–C230.4, C232.1's consent coverage, C233.2–C233.3, C234.5's mapping resolution, C235.1, C235.4 as a pure decision function, the publish rules of C238.2's hook as pure functions over a template version, C238.3's text composition and digest (reusing Phase 9's logical-form digest function), all pure in `packages/core/src/connect/` with table-driven Vitest suites. It owns `packages/core/src/connect/` and its tests.
- **Stage 3** (each after its family's Stage 1b checkpoint **and** its named `connect-logic` checkpoint):
  - **`api-connect`** (C; after checkpoint 2): C231 (create, list, activate with the step-up, deactivate with the token expiry in one transaction, reactivate, policy versions), C232 (create with every check in order, list, retrieve per side, expire, the minute sweep, the one 404), C233 (peek), C239.1's events for these routes, C240.2–C240.5 (the matrix, the limits, the audit entries, the cross-tenant module with every entry point, the export's `listSharesTouching` included), and `seed:dev --connect` (C244.1). It owns `routes/server/connect-{connections,share-tokens}.ts`, `routes/dashboard/connect.ts`, `services/connect/{connections,share-tokens,peek,cross-tenant,audit}.ts`, `jobs/connect-token-expiry.ts`, the three connect repositories with their `*.cross-env.ts` modules, the seed flag, and their tests.
  - **`api-redeem`** (R; after `api-connect`'s create checkpoint and `connect-logic` checkpoint 4): C234 (the route's synchronous part, the job, clone, hydrate, transactions, limits, `redeem-summary`, file re-encryption through the storage service), C235 (provenance on every copied row, the cloned-results function behind the decision hook, `relied-on-cloned` on Phase 8's decision record as a named borrowed edit), C241.1's pending-job failure, and the Testcontainers races (double redemption, redemption against expiry, deactivation, withdrawal and source redaction, each in both orders).
