It owns `routes/server/connect-redeem.ts`, `jobs/connect-redeem/`, `services/connect/{redeem,clone,hydrate,cloned-results}.ts` and their tests.
  - **`api-consent`** (K; after checkpoint 4, and for the client-side exits after `api-redeem`'s job checkpoint, which builds `fill-inquiry`; review F23): C236 (both methods, texts stored by digest), C237 (withdrawal and its effects), C238's server part (the client view, the three exits, the client-side redemption from `meta.share-token-id`, the waiting position until the job ends, and every publish check and acknowledgement of C238.2's hook), C241.1–C241.5 (the redaction and retention hooks). It owns `routes/server/connect-consent-records.ts`, `routes/flow/share-consents.ts`, `services/connect/{consent,withdrawal,client-side,publish-checks,redaction-hooks,retention}.ts`, `services/flow-views/share-consent.ts`, the two consent repositories (with `consent-records.cross-env.ts`) and their tests.
  - **`clients-connect`** (U; after (K) and checkpoint 4): C238.6 (the web flow's step, five locales, its mocked Playwright spec, the withdrawal exit of C237.6) and C238.7 with C256.1 and C256.2 (the Android renderer, `FlowApi` methods with the withdrawal of C237.6, the displayed-text digest check, strings through the key map, host tests and goldens; the pinning recipe's host test and its guide section), as two checkpoints (web, then Android). Borrowed edits: the `share.consent.*` keys in Phase 9's five web catalogs and the locale export re-run; the key-map entries and the generator re-run (Phase 10 C199.1); the Android request fixtures under `contracts/fixtures/flow-requests/android/` with their zod parse test. It owns `apps/flow/src/steps/share-consent/`, `apps/flow/e2e/share-consent-*.spec.ts`, `android/openverify-sdk/src/commonMain/kotlin/**/{ui/steps/ShareConsentStep.kt,flow/ShareConsent.kt}`, its `strings_share_consent.xml` files, `screenshots/clients-connect/` and their tests. Every Gradle call runs under the Gradle lock (Rules).
  - **`console-connect`** (U; after `api-connect`'s and `api-consent`'s first checkpoints): C242 (settings pages, token pages with peek and the redeem form, consent records on the inquiry, account and transaction pages through the `OptionalSection` slots of the Phase 7 and 8 plans (or the slot the inventory names), the Cloned marker and banner, the hydrated-field list), with RSC payload tests. It owns `apps/dashboard/src/app/**/connect/`, `apps/dashboard/src/components/connect/` and their tests; the three object pages' section lines are named borrowed edits.
  - **`workflow-connect`** (U; after `api-redeem`'s first checkpoint): C243 (the three actions and the three triggers on Phase 8's engine). It owns `apps/api/src/workflows/actions/connect/` (or the path the inventory names for Phase 8's actions) and its tests.
- **Stage 4: `integration-11a`:** the 11A full-stack spec (C287.3) under the e2e lock, with webhooks verified by `@openverify/node`; the generated cross-tenant probe over every OpenAPI path with an id parameter (C240.7, review P24), under the Testcontainers suite it owns in `apps/api/src/security/cross-tenant-probe.test.ts`; `:openverify-sdk:stackTest` extended with the client-side redemption (C287.4); the OpenAPI snapshot, `@openverify/node`'s `connect` group and the fixtures; `persona-surface.md`'s Connect rows (C282); the full validation; defects fixed test-first in any path; missing hooks added and reported; the amendments of Stages 2–3; a draft of the 11A entry for PROGRESS in its report. It owns `e2e/`, `docs/api/openapi.json`, `packages/node/src/` (the new group), `docs/research/persona-surface.md` and `docs/plans/phase-11.plan.md`.

**Shared files (the C54 rule for 11A).** Stage 2–4 builders never edit these (each hook is a call in the shared file to a stub in an owned file): `packages/contracts/src/{index,ids,event-names,flow-api,rate-limits}.ts` and the 11A schemas after Stage 1a; `packages/flow-spec/src/` (Phase 9's, after `contracts-11a`'s borrowed edit); `packages/core/src/permissions.ts`; `packages/db/src/schema.ts` and `encrypted-columns.ts` (after Stage 1b); Phase 9's engine, decision and publish files outside their hook lines; Phase 8's action registry outside `contracts-11a`'s declarations; `apps/api/src/{container,worker,config,server}.ts`; the Android public API and the ABI dump (unchanged by 11A: the renderer is internal, and a test asserts the dump is unchanged); `android/openverify-sdk/flow-spec-members.json` after Stage 1a; every `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `libs.versions.toml`, `verification-metadata.xml` and `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` (11A adds no runtime dependency; `contracts-11a` adds `okhttp-tls` in test scope in Stage 1a, C256.2). A missing hook is a `contractDeviation` the integration agent adds. Every other path named here has exactly one owner.

**Acceptance of 11A:** a connection is created, activated in the console with a step-up, deactivated and reactivated with tokens expiring as C231.4 says; a token is refused without a covering consent and created with one; peek shows only the policy's attributes in the vendor's envelope; clone and hydrate copy exactly what the policy allows, never a face template, with `provenance` on every copy; a cloned pass counts only under `accept-cloned-results`, and a cloned failure never declines; clones stage no `verification.*` event, and neither a workflow condition nor the analytics counts a clone that C235.4 does not (C235.7); a copy cannot be re-shared; a redemption copies nothing outside the token's stored categories; both consent methods record their texts by digest, attestation needs its scope and the policy's leave, a decline is recorded and cannot be overridden, and withdrawal expires an unredeemed token; a sandbox key cannot change a connection, and the cross-tenant probe answers 404 on every path; the `share-consent` step works on the web and in the Android SDK (host tests and `stackTest`), with no token in any URL; every race test passes in both orders; the authorization matrix, the import boundary and the cross-tenant probe pass; the 11A spec passes on the shards.

## Workflow 11B (platform hardening)

**Families:** (A) authentication and sessions; (P) platform operations; (D) data rights and ingestion. Nine agents. 11B starts after 11A is pushed.

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-11b`.** It runs alone. It re-verifies 11A as built and reads Phase 9's C168 as built (which session-ending events, MFA rules and team actions exist), Phase 2's key routes (what C12 enforces on create and clone), Phase 8's slots table and every enqueue site, Phase 6's sync and source files, and writes C252.1's pattern inventory; records drift. Then, by family:
  - **Common:** every 11B problem code with its golden fixture; the id `aexp`; the scope `accounts:export`; the rate policy `account-export` (C253.7); the Valkey login limiter's interface and its Lua script's contract (C246.1, outside `RATE_LIMIT_POLICIES`); the environment settings `queue-in-flight-caps`, `account-export-ttl-days` and `watchlist-full-check-hours`; the names, defaults and descriptions of every new `OPENVERIFY_*` variable (to be entered in `config-registry.ts` by `foundation-11b`); `openid-client` 6.8.8, `oauth4webapi` 3.8.8 and `jose` in the catalog with their `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` rows and dates read on its day.
  - **(A):** the SSO provider resource and routes (C248.3–C248.4), the end-sessions routes (C245.3), the session-expiry answer (C245.2).
  - **(P):** `/ready`'s schema (C250.1), the commands' names (`org:create`, `db:grant-runtime`, `api-keys:pepper-status`, `user:allow-password`).
  - **(D):** the account-export resource and routes (C253.1), the manifest's JSON Schema `openverify.account-export.v1`, the `conditional` member of `watchlists/sources.json` and the validation attributes on the sources document (C254.2).
- **Stage 1b: `foundation-11b`** (plumbing only), beside Stage 2: one migration (`user_sessions.last_seen_at`; `sso_providers` with its issuer pin, tenant pin and `required_amr`/`required_acr`; `user_sso_identities (user_id, provider_id, issuer, subject, linked_at)`, unique on `(provider_id, issuer, subject)` (reviews P07, F11); `users.password_login_allowed_until` (F11); `account_exports`; `api_keys.pepper_generation`; the slots table generalised from Phase 8's to `(environment_id, queue)` with Phase 8's rows migrated, and `admitted_at` on verifications, report runs, share tokens and account exports (F12); the sources' `last_validated_at`, `validated_by` and `last_full_check_at`; the two `SECURITY DEFINER` functions of C249.3 and the audit `BEFORE DELETE` trigger), with every sealed column (`sso_providers.client_secret`, any sealed export metadata) in `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` and the rewrap list and the schema test's expected set changed in that commit (review P35); `apps/api/src/config-registry.ts` listing every existing variable, including the pattern entries and the `process` field of C277.2, with the test that every process's sources read only registered names; each 11B variable's parsing in `config.ts` from `contracts-11b`'s list, so no Stage 2 agent edits `config.ts` (review F30); the hooks in `worker.ts` for the heartbeat and the drain (C250.2, C250.4), calling stubs in `platform-ops`' files (F30); `metrics-registry.ts`' interface (C250.7); routes frozen at 501 (SSO, end-sessions, exports); the queue `account-export` with an `unbuiltJob` stub; the hooks, each a call in a shared file to a stub in an owned file: the session check in `principal-resolver.ts` (`operator-auth/session-policy.ts`, `auth-sessions`), the admission call at every enqueue site the inventory lists (`services/quotas/admit.ts`, `quotas`), the internal-listener registration in `server.ts` (`http/internal-listener.ts`, `platform-ops`), the sync's validator step (`watchlists/conditional.ts`, `ingestion-11b`).
- **Stage 2** (parallel; each after Stage 1a common and its family):
  - **`auth-sessions`** (A): C245, C246, C247 (the idle and absolute limits, the session-ending events Phase 9 left out, the Valkey login limiter failing closed for login, pepper rotation with its compare-and-set and `api-keys:pepper-status`, the scope bounds on create and clone). It owns `operator-auth/{session-policy,login-rate-limiter}.ts`, `packages/db/src/api-keys.ts`, the end-sessions routes, the command and their tests.
  - **`sso-oidc`** (A): C248 (providers, start and callback, verification through `openid-client`, matching, just-in-time provisioning, enforcement, the break-glass command, the organisation hint on login, the console's Settings → Security → SSO page), with an in-process test provider under `apps/api/src/test-support/oidc/` that serves discovery, JWKS and tokens signed by keys generated per run. It owns `apps/api/src/operator-auth/oidc/`, `routes/dashboard/sso.ts`, `services/sso-providers.ts`, `packages/db/src/repositories/sso-providers.ts` and `sso-identities.ts`, `apps/dashboard/src/app/**/settings/security/sso/`, the console's SSO route handlers `apps/dashboard/src/app/(auth)/sso/` (start and callback, with the browser binding of C248.4; review F21), and their tests; the login page's SSO button (a named borrowed edit to Phase 9's login page) and the request logger's serializer, which records the callback without its query string (C248.4; verification V13), are its named borrowed edits; the BFF calls the handlers make are frozen by `contracts-11b`.
  - **`platform-ops`** (P): C249 (TLS settings and warnings, `*_FILE` secrets, the migration URL, `db:grant-runtime`, the migrate lock), C250 (`/ready`, the heartbeat and `worker-health`, the internal listener, `org:create`), C255.1's optional HSTS header. It owns `apps/api/src/config/secret-files.ts` and `config/store-tls.ts` (the `_FILE` and TLS logic, which `config.ts` calls through `foundation-11b`'s hooks; review F30), `apps/api/src/metrics-registry.ts` (C250.7), `packages/db/src/migrate.ts`, `apps/api/src/migrate.ts`, `routes/system.ts`, `http/internal-listener.ts`, the worker's heartbeat and drain modules, the two commands and their tests; `watchlist-key-df.ts`'s call to the GIN cleanup function (C249.3) and the HSTS header in the flow server and `next.config.ts` (C255.1) are its named borrowed edits.
  - **`quotas`** (P): C251 (admission, the fair sweep, the setting, metrics and the warn line). It owns `apps/api/src/services/quotas/`, `jobs/quota-sweep.ts`, `packages/db/src/repositories/queue-slots.ts` and their tests; the repair sweep's admission check (C251.1) is its named borrowed edit.
  - **`dsar-export`** (D): C253 (the route, the job, the manifest, the sealed objects, lifetime, limits, redaction ordering). It owns `services/account-export/`, `jobs/account-export/`, `routes/server/account-exports.ts`, `packages/db/src/repositories/account-exports.ts` and their tests.
  - **`ingestion-11b`** (D): C254 (the locked UN switch and acknowledgement; conditional requests with the forced full check) and C252 (the entry points made to use the restricted matcher or refuse, the ESLint rule, the catastrophic-pattern tests, each entry point a named borrowed edit). It owns `apps/api/src/watchlists/{conditional,un-opt-in}.ts`, `watchlists/sources.json`, the ESLint rule's file in `packages/config/eslint/` and their tests; the root `eslint.config.js` line that loads that rule is its named borrowed edit (review F28's verification).
- **Stage 3: `integration-11b`:** the 11B full-stack specs (C287.3); C255.2–C255.4's verification tests (the console's enforced CSP on every route, the flow server's headers, the origin check on every state-changing handler, fixing a missing check test-first); the OpenAPI snapshot, `@openverify/node` and the fixtures; the full validation; defects fixed test-first; the amendments; a draft of the 11B entry for PROGRESS. It also extends the generated cross-tenant probe with 11B's families (C240.7; verification V14). It owns `e2e/`, `docs/api/openapi.json`, `packages/node/src/`, the verification tests under `apps/dashboard/src/security/`, `apps/api/src/security/cross-tenant-probe.test.ts` and `docs/plans/phase-11.plan.md`.

**Shared files (11B):** the 11A list with 11A's owned files now shared, `config.ts` and `worker.ts` among them without exception (review F30); `principal-resolver.ts`, `server.ts` and every enqueue site outside their hook calls; `config-registry.ts` and `metrics-registry.ts` outside the entries each agent adds for its own variables and metrics (a named borrowed edit per entry); Phase 6's `watchlists/sync.ts` outside its hook call; Phase 8's admission code outside the generalisation `foundation-11b` makes.

**Acceptance of 11B:** an idle console session ends at 30 minutes and every C245.3 event ends a user's sessions; two API instances share one login budget, and login fails closed without Valkey while other routes fail open; a rotated pepper re-hashes keys as they are used, and no key exceeds its creator's scopes; OIDC sign-in works against the in-process provider with PKCE, `state`, `nonce` and the per-provider callback, links identities by subject, refuses an unverified or out-of-domain email and an unpinned multi-tenant issuer, and requires the local factor when the provider's `amr` falls short; the runtime role cannot run DDL or delete audit rows directly, a list sync and a re-key complete under it without warnings, two migrations serialise, `/ready` reports each store, and the internal routes answer only on their listener; a flooding environment is held at its cap and another environment's job is admitted before the flooder's queued jobs drain, asserted by counts and order (the p95 ratio is judged only by `calibration-load-11d`, review H12); every operator pattern entry point refuses the catastrophic pattern; an account export covers every linked family and expires; the UN switch stays locked; a 304 never outlasts the forced full check; the 11B specs pass on the shards.

## Workflow 11C (supply chain and deployment)

**Families:** (I) images; (S) scanning and SBOMs; (K) the chart; (W) the web packages. Nine agents. 11C starts after 11B is pushed.

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-11c`.** It runs alone. It re-verifies 11A and 11B as built and measures the latest CI run's jobs; reads every pin of C258.3 on its day (date, licence file at the tag, checksum file), with kind, its node images, kubectl and the schema source (review F13); runs the Syft pnpm spike, a helm-unittest install under Helm 4.3.1's plugin system and the CycloneDX Gradle plugin's spike on `:openverify-sdk` in a scratch worktree that is discarded (C261.1; reviews F13, F29); then commits: the CycloneDX Gradle plugin with its verification metadata (under the Gradle lock) and `eslint-plugin-security` in the root `package.json` with the lockfile; the `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` rows and Excluded entries of C282 for the tools; `infra/scripts/install-<tool>.sh` skeletons with their pins (each failing closed until its owner fills it); `security/allowlist.yaml` with its JSON Schema; the `release.yml` skeleton that exits with a notice; the chart's `Chart.yaml`, `values.yaml` keys and `values.schema.json` (frozen); the image names and their entry points; `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_API_URL` and `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_FACE_GUIDANCE` (C257.1, C289); ADR 0010's deployment half.
- **Stage 2** (parallel):
  - **`images-11c`** (I): C257 (the five Dockerfiles, compiled commands, the compose `app` profile and the e2e stack on them, `apps.Dockerfile` removed after its test moves, `infra/tests/images.test.sh`), C258.1 (digests everywhere with the unpinned-reference test and `refresh-digests.sh`), and C263.6's `tcp:` transport for the code runner (a named borrowed edit to Phase 8's runner client and server). It also builds the flow's runtime configuration and its no-guidance build flag (C257.1, C289). It owns `infra/docker/`, `services/vision/Dockerfile`, `infra/docker-compose.yml`, the e2e images-mode override `e2e/setup/docker-compose.images.yml` (C257.3; the stack driver's branch is `integration-11c`'s), `infra/tests/{images,apps-image-context}.test.sh`, `infra/scripts/refresh-digests.sh`, the API's `tsconfig.build.json`, `tsconfig.commands.json` and command entry files, and the dashboard's `output: 'standalone'` (a named borrowed edit to `next.config.ts`); the flow changes of C257.1 are named borrowed edits.
  - **`scanners-11c`** (S): C258.2 and C259 (each installer filled with its checksum and signature check, its installer test, `infra/scripts/supply-chain.sh` running every check of C259.1 locally and in CI, the allowlist's expiry rule, Trivy's checks bundle pinned). It owns `infra/scripts/install-*.sh` (except gitleaks'), `infra/scripts/supply-chain.sh`, `infra/tests/install-*.test.sh`, `security/allowlist.yaml` and its test.
  - **`sast-pii-11c`** (S): C260 (the `eslint-plugin-security` rules in `@openverify/config` with their reasons, every existing finding fixed or suppressed with a reason, the suppression ceiling test, the PII configuration and its allowlist with positive and negative cases, the pre-commit hook's second scan and the root `eslint.config.js` line that loads `security.js`, each a named borrowed edit). It owns `packages/config/eslint/security.js`, its test, `security/gitleaks-pii*.toml`; each fix elsewhere is a named borrowed edit listed in `contractDeviations`.
  - **`chart-11c`** (K): C263 (components, values, the migrate hook and its GitOps switch, probes, HPAs, the pool check, models, the runner as its own Deployment). It owns `deploy/helm/openverify/` except `templates/networkpolicies.yaml`, `templates/_security.tpl`, `tests/` and `ci/`.
  - **`chart-policy-11c`** (K; after `chart-11c`'s first checkpoint): C264 and C265 (the restricted security context helper, network policies, one Secret per process, the ingress rules, the unit tests, kubeconform with `scripts/fetch_k8s_schemas.py`, Trivy `config`, `ct install` on kind with the fixtures and its smoke). It owns `deploy/helm/openverify/templates/{networkpolicies.yaml,_security.tpl}`, `deploy/helm/openverify/{tests,ci}/` (with `ci/kind.sh`, C265.4) and `scripts/fetch_k8s_schemas.py`.
  - **`sdk-web-11c`** (W): C266 (`@openverify/js` and `@openverify/node` made publishable, `packages/sdk-react`, `apps/demo-web` with its e2e use). It owns `packages/sdk-react/`, `apps/demo-web/`, the publish fields of `packages/sdk-js/package.json` and `packages/node/package.json` (named borrowed edits) and their tests.
  - **`sbom-release-11c`** (S; after `images-11c`'s first checkpoint): C261 and C262's steps and C267.1 (`release.yml` with build, verify, stage and the gated publish job, the SBOM licence check with its tests, `infra/tests/release-workflow.test.sh`). It owns `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `security/check-sbom-licences.py`, its tests and `infra/tests/release-workflow.test.sh`; the AAR SBOM and licence step in Phase 10's `android-release.yml` is its named borrowed edit (review H23).
- **Stage 3: `integration-11c`:** the `supply-chain` job in `ci.yml` (C267.2; the `e2e` job is unchanged); the e2e stack driver's images mode (`OPENVERIFY_E2E_IMAGES=1`, C257.3; review F03), with its duration measured; `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` for both workflows; the CI jobs measured; the 11A and 11B specs re-run in the images mode; the kind install and upgrade locally through `kind.sh`; the full validation; defects fixed test-first; a draft of the 11C entry for PROGRESS. After the push, the orchestrator dispatches `release.yml` once (staging only) and records its run id and minutes. It owns `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`, `e2e/` except `images-11c`'s `e2e/setup/docker-compose.images.yml`, and `docs/plans/phase-11.plan.md`.

**Shared files (11C):** the 11B list; the chart's `values.schema.json` and `Chart.yaml` after Stage 1a; `security/allowlist.yaml` outside the entries each agent adds with a reason; every Dockerfile after `images-11c`'s last checkpoint.

**Acceptance of 11C:** five production images build, run read-only as non-root and contain no dev tooling; every image reference and tool is pinned by digest or checksum; the `supply-chain` job passes on the tree with an allowlist whose every entry has a reason and an expiry; the SAST rules run in lint with their suppressions counted; the PII scan passes with its allowlist and catches its positive cases; SBOMs are produced for every image, tree, AAR and package, and the licence check passes, with every shipped "owner to confirm" or pending row reported as `owner` and its artifact labelled `pending-owner`; the flow image runs against two API URLs without a rebuild, and its no-guidance variant builds and passes its smoke; the chart passes lint on both Helm versions, its unit tests, kubeconform on 1.35–1.37 and Trivy `config`, and installs and upgrades on kind (in `models.source: pvc`) with a sandbox inquiry created, the vision pod without egress in every `models` mode (helm-unittest); the web packages pack; `release.yml` stages every artifact and publishes nothing; the measured per-push minutes are recorded.

## Workflow 11D (performance)

Ten agents (review F01: the calibration is split in two). 11D starts after 11C is pushed. **Timing rules** (C268.6): every take runs under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`, alone on the machine; no load take runs beside a test suite, a validation or another agent's build (Stage 3 is alone for that reason, one calibration agent after the other).

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-11d`.** It runs alone. It reads 6D's calibration and Phase 6's closing PROGRESS entry and records C271's condition (`met`, or the missed budgets with 6D's profile); re-reads the watchlist sync, re-key and key-frequency code as 6D left it; commits the load method (C268.5–C268.6, the margins for the jit and share verdicts, the fresh-seed rule), `load/`'s project skeleton with its lockfile (`httpx` and its tree; Locust only if Q12 is granted by then) and the licence row of every resolved dependency, the vegeta pin, `seed:load`'s signature, the report templates; and the switches the matcher changes will hide behind (each off).
- **Stage 2** (parallel):
  - **`load-harness`:** C268.2–C268.4 and C269's scenarios (the driver and its scenario files, vegeta targets, the sampler, the report generator with its tests, `seed:load`, the load compose override), proven on the `small` scale only (timings in Stage 2 are context, never results). It owns `load/`, `infra/load/`, `infra/scripts/install-vegeta.sh`, the `seed:load` command and their tests.
  - **`matcher-write-11d`:** C270.1–C270.3 (the incremental frequencies with the identity test, the re-key batch candidates, the three GIN options behind switches), every change switchable and digest-identical. It owns the sync and re-key files under `apps/api/src/watchlists/` (never `packages/watchlist/src/keys/`, whose name keys C270.4 forbids changing; review F27), `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlist-key-df.ts`, the migration if a GIN option needs one, and their tests.
  - **`matcher-c787-11d`** (only if C271's condition records a missed budget): C271.2, on the files 6D's profile names, under 6D's rules, as named borrowed edits where they overlap `matcher-write-11d`'s, committed only after its checkpoint on the same file.
- **Stage 3** (alone, one agent after the other; reviews F01, H11):
  - **`calibration-load-11d`:** every HTTP take of C268, C269 (except C269.4, `calibration-matcher-11d`'s) and C272 at the reference scale (the budgets, fairness at the default cap and at the ceiling, the soak under the long-take exception, the limiter refusal scenario, the jit comparison and the role timeouts), the verdicts and `docs/calibration/load-phase11.md`. It owns that file and `load/results/` (outside git except the committed report tables).
  - **`calibration-matcher-11d`** (after `calibration-load-11d` has stopped the load stack): C270's write-cost figures (the incremental rebuild, the re-key candidates at 100,000 names and the chosen size confirmed once at 2,000,000, the GIN options with C269.4's screening during a sync) and C271's C78.7 and re-screen takes at base and head with digests, each switchable change's share, in 6D's configuration, and `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase11.md`. It owns that file and `packages/watchlist/results/` (outside git).
- **Review:** two read-only reviewers (the load method, its honesty and the budgets' treatment; the matcher changes' equivalence and the write path's races).
- **Verify:** one adversarial verifier for every CRITICAL and HIGH finding, recording the 11D amendments.
- **Fix:** `fix-11d` over every path, re-measuring with the calibration agents' commands and timing rules every figure a fix touches, recorded in the two reports; it then runs the full validation last, which is 11D's gate before the push (review F16).

**Acceptance of 11D:** the load report publishes every scenario with its budget `met` or `missed` and its gap, the machine, the loads and the digests; fairness and the soak have verdicts; the matcher's write cost is measured before and after with identical matches; C78.7 and the re-screen are measured at base and head whatever 6D found, and the head's verdict (`met`, or `missed` with its gap) is the release's (review H09); the jit verdict and the role timeouts are measured; nothing is gated in shared CI.

## Workflow 11E (documentation and the security records)

Eight agents (review F16 adds `docs-release`). 11E starts after 11D is pushed.

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-11e`.** It runs alone. It reads every document Phases 7–11 wrote and the generators' inputs as built (the registries, `config-registry.ts`, the OpenAPI document, `persona-surface.md`, the provider registry); reads MASVS's licence at v2.1.0; creates `packages/docs-tools` (private, under `createVitestConfig()`'s 80% gate) with the generator interfaces as stubs, one directory per area (`src/security/`, `src/records/`, `src/selfhost/`, `src/compliance/`, `src/catalog/`, `src/release/`), and owns its `package.json` and `src/index.ts`, which are shared files from then on (review F17); commits `scripts/fetch_asvs.py` and `scripts/fetch_masvs.py` with their pins, the committed id lists, the docs tree's file names and the wording test (C274.5); and the switch of C280.6, off.
- **Stage 2** (parallel):
  - **`docs-security`:** C273, C274, C275 (the threat model with every row and its parsing test; the ASVS and MASVS checklists with their evidence tests and the summary). It owns `docs/security/{threat-model.md,asvs-*,masvs-*}` and `packages/docs-tools/src/security/`; its ASVS checkpoint (the CSV and its evidence test green) is the one `docs-selfhost`'s hardening checklist waits for.
  - **`docs-records`:** C276 (the inventory, classification, logging and vulnerability documents with their tests; `SECURITY.md`). It owns `docs/security/{crypto-inventory,data-classification,logging,vulnerability-policy,README}.md`, `SECURITY.md` and `packages/docs-tools/src/records/`; the data classification is generated from C230.4's `categories.ts` (F07), and its checkpoint is the one `docs-compliance`'s record-of-processing template waits for.
  - **`docs-selfhost`:** C277 (the guide and the configuration generator; the hardening checklist after `docs-security`'s ASVS checkpoint). It owns `docs/self-hosting/` and `packages/docs-tools/src/selfhost/`.
  - **`docs-compliance`:** C278 (the guide, the re-read of each legal source with its result marked in place, the record-of-processing template generator). It owns `docs/compliance/` except Phase 8's `automated-decisions.md` and Phase 10's integrity template, which it indexes, and `packages/docs-tools/src/compliance/`; the record-of-processing template starts after `docs-records`' classification checkpoint.
  - **`docs-catalog`:** C279, C280 (neutral) and C281 (the catalog, API reference and mapping generators, `docs/checks/limits.json`, the sharing guide). It owns `docs/checks/catalog.md`, `docs/checks/limits.json`, `docs/api/README.md`, `docs/migration/`, `docs/connect/` and `packages/docs-tools/src/catalog/`.
  - **`docs-release`** (reviews F16, H21): C284 (`docs/release/checklist.md`, `pnpm release:check` with every rule of C284.2, and the generated `docs/release/notes.md` of C284.4), built against fixtures so it is green before 11F's records exist, and reviewed in 11F. It owns `docs/release/`, `packages/docs-tools/src/release/` and their tests; the root `package.json`'s `release:check` script is a named borrowed edit.
- **Stage 3: `integration-11e`:** C282 (ADR 0010 final, PLAN, `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`, `persona-surface.md`, the Phase 5 §8 addendum, the Phase 6 C88 note); the threat-model and ASVS review recorded with its commit; the `CLAUDE.md` command list proposed in its report; the full validation; a draft of the 11E entry for PROGRESS. It owns `docs/PLAN.md`, `docs/adr/0010-*.md`, `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`, `docs/research/{persona-surface,phase5-nfc}.md`, `docs/plans/phase-6.plan.md` (the note only) and `docs/plans/phase-11.plan.md`.

**Shared files (11E):** every code path (11E changes no code except `packages/docs-tools`; a doc that finds a code defect reports it as a `contractDeviation` for 11F's fixers); the registries, `config-registry.ts`, `metrics-registry.ts` and `categories.ts` (read only); `packages/docs-tools/package.json` and `src/index.ts` after `contracts-11e`.

**Acceptance of 11E:** `pnpm release:check` and the generated release notes exist with their tests (C284); every document of PLAN §10 exists, generated where the repository holds the facts, and each generator's test passes; the threat model covers all six letters per boundary with live evidence; the ASVS and MASVS checklists hold exactly their ids with evidence and the wording rule passes; the compliance guide marks every source it could not re-read; the migration page names no vendor while the switch is off; PLAN no longer names k6.

## Workflow 11F (dynamic testing, review and fixes)

Nine agents; 11F starts after 11E is pushed.

- **Stage 1: `dast-11f`** (alone, under the e2e lock): C283 end to end on the production-image stack, every high finding fixed test-first in any path, the report written. It owns `security/zap/`, `docs/security/dast-*.md` and, for its fixes, any path.
- **Stage 2: review of all of Phase 11** (read-only, parallel):
  - **privacy, security and law:** Connect's consent, recipients, withdrawal, biometric categories and the cross-tenant path; the export; OIDC; logs and errors; images, the chart's secrets and policies; the compliance guide's claims and marks;
  - **correctness and honesty:** Connect's state machines and races, provenance and the cloned-results rule, quotas, sessions, the load and calibration reports' honesty, C78.7's treatment, every "ours" labelled;
  - **supply chain, deployment and CI:** the pins and installers, the scanners' gates, `release.yml`, the chart, the images, the minutes, the licence rows and exclusions;
  - **documentation and coverage:** every carried item and deferral of C288 and C286, the generated documents, the wording rules, the vendor-naming switch, the release checklist, and the evidence of every `met` row of the ASVS and MASVS checklists against its requirement (C274.3; all of them, or at least one per chapter), written in its report for `fix-records`.
- **Stage 3:** one verifier for every CRITICAL and HIGH finding; it records the 11F amendments.
- **Stage 4:** path-owned fixers:
  - **`fix-server`:** `apps/api`, `packages/` (except `sdk-*`), `contracts/`, `e2e/`, `load/`, `services/vision`;
  - **`fix-clients`:** `apps/flow`, `apps/dashboard`, `apps/demo-web`, `packages/sdk-*`, `android/`;
  - **`fix-records`:** `docs/`, `deploy/`, `infra/`, `.github/workflows/`, `security/`, `scripts/`, `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`, `SECURITY.md`; it keeps the checklist's states and the release notes current (C284, built in 11E), applies the documentation reviewer's `met`-evidence result to the checklists and records it in the 11F PROGRESS draft (C274.3), measures the CI jobs again, and runs the full validation last, after the other two fixers' final commits (review F16). After the 11F push, the orchestrator dispatches `release.yml` once and records its run id and minutes.

**Acceptance of 11F:** ZAP's scans report no open high finding; every CRITICAL and HIGH review finding is fixed, or refuted by the verifier with its reason; a confirmed CRITICAL or HIGH is never closed by a record (PLAN §6 and §10; review H17), and `pnpm release:check` reads the verifier's table and fails on a confirmed one not fixed; `pnpm release:check` (its default mode, C284.2) passes with every owner item shown as open or decided; `fix-records` runs the full validation after the other two fixers' last commits, which is 11F's gate before the push (review F16); `release.yml`'s staged run is green.

## Workflow 11G (the owner-gated release artifacts)

Up to five agents. 11G runs after 11F is pushed, and only for the items whose owner decision is recorded in PROGRESS (Q1–Q12, and Phase 10 P2 for the flow image); an item still pending is skipped and stays open on the release checklist with its default. If nothing has been decided, 11G does not start, and Phase 11 is recorded done with Q1–Q12 open once 11F's CI and the staged `release.yml` run are green (Acceptance).

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-11g`.** It re-verifies 11A–11F as built and the decisions recorded, and confirms each owner step exists before any part uses it (the registry, the npm scope, the `release` environment with its branch rule, the repository variable, the visibility); it records what is missing as "not ready" and skips that part.
- **Stage 2** (parallel, each only if allowed):
  - **`publish-11g`** (Q1–Q4): the publish job's final wiring (the registry, the chart as an OCI artifact, npm with provenance, cosign keyless or the owner's key, the attestations), tested with a dry run that pushes nothing (`--dry-run` where the tools offer it); the first publication is the orchestrator's dispatch with the owner's approval in the environment, never an agent's.
  - **`codeql-11g`** (Q9, or Q1 public): a CodeQL job for JavaScript/TypeScript, Python and Kotlin, informational, never a required gate, with its minutes costed.
  - **`records-11g`** (Q5, Q6, Q7, Q8, Q10, Q11, Q12, P2): the release-age answer applied (the uv pin and the recorded rule); the weights-in-images answer (`models.source` default and the vision image, only if the SFace review cleared); counsel's review recorded and the guide's line and the consent texts' `counsel-unreviewed` marker removed; the vendor-named migration page and catalog column re-rendered; the UN opt-in documented and allowed; Q11's rows recorded, or `nodemailer` replaced test-first if declined; Q12's grant recorded (a grant after 11D's Stage 1a changes nothing in this release: the driver stays, 11D's report stands, and Locust waits for the next release's load work; verification V16); the flow image with guidance released if P2 is granted. Each is its own commit with its test.
- **Stage 3: `integration-11g`:** the full validation; `release.yml` dispatched by the orchestrator; a draft of the 11G entry for PROGRESS; the release checklist updated.

**Acceptance of 11G (per item):** each granted item is built and tested and its checklist line is `done`; each declined item's outcome (C289's last column) is in the release notes and PROGRESS; each pending item is `owner` on the checklist with its default.

## Rules for every agent

- **TDD:** RED first, seen failing for the intended reason; the RED commit compiles and typechecks (TypeScript, Kotlin, Python); then GREEN; then refactor on green. Evidence, with the commands run and their output, goes in `docs/tdd/phase11-<agent>.tdd.md`. A document generator is tested like code: its RED test is the committed page differing from a fresh render.
- **Shared files:** builders never edit the shared-file lists above. Route schemas, the OpenAPI snapshot, `@openverify/node`, the API fixtures, the chart's values schema, `security/allowlist.yaml` outside an agent's own reasoned entry, and the Android public API change only through a Stage 1a or integration agent. A missing hook or schema change is a `contractDeviation`. Dependencies and tool pins land only in a Stage 1a (`contracts-11b` the OIDC libraries; `contracts-11a` `okhttp-tls` in test scope; `contracts-11c` every tool and the CycloneDX and ESLint plugins; `contracts-11d` the `load/` tools and vegeta), with their licence rows and dates in the same commit; whoever adds a dependency refreshes the lockfiles (and, on Android, `verification-metadata.xml`) in that commit.
- **Gates:** each Stage 1a and 1b checkpoint is committed with every suite green; a Stage 2 or 3 agent starts only after the checkpoints this plan names for it; the integration agent's full validation is the Workflow's gate before the orchestrator pushes; a failed CI job after a push is the next Workflow's first fix, test-first, never retried.
- **Borrowed edits** (the Phase 6–10 rule): wait until `git status --porcelain <file>` is empty, make the minimal change, commit it at once, and list it in `contractDeviations`.
- **Nothing for iOS** (owner, 2026-10-29): Phase 11 builds no iOS code; the Android `share-consent` renderer stays in `commonMain` with no platform API, and `:openverify-sdk:assemble` (the iOS klibs) stays green at every checkpoint, as Phase 10's rule keeps it.
- **Shared resources:**
  - commits are path-scoped under `flock /tmp/openverify-git.lock`, after `git diff --cached --quiet`;
  - every Gradle invocation runs under `flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock`, in the foreground with a 600,000 ms timeout (exit 75 means "lock busy, retry", never a failed gate; Phase 10 decision 61);
  - the full-stack e2e, `stackTest`, `ct install` on kind, ZAP and every load or calibration take run under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock` (the e2e lock outside, the Gradle lock inside), with the load average written beside every timing; only the stack start and the long takes of the exception below may run in the background;
  - **a load or calibration take never runs beside a test suite, a validation or another agent's build** (the Phase 6 timing rules): 11D's Stage 3 runs alone, and an integration agent's validation never overlaps it;
  - flaky tests are made deterministic, never retried; time comes from the injected `Clock`; tests that order rows stamp `created_at`.
- **Calls that fit 10 minutes:** every command is split so one call finishes within 10 minutes (Validation), measured by each Stage 1b or integration agent at its first checkpoint with the load average; a call that grows past it is split further, never given a longer timeout. **One named exception** (review F01): a calibration take that cannot be split (the 30-minute soak, the 2,000,000-name re-key confirmation, a sync during which screening is measured) runs in the background under the e2e lock, alone on the machine, writing a heartbeat file every 10 s, and the agent waits with a Monitor until-loop on the take's end marker; no other command runs meanwhile, and the take's start, end and heartbeat gaps are recorded beside its figure.
- **CI cadence** (owner, 2026-10-28 and 2026-10-29): Workflow agents never push. The orchestrator pushes `main` once per Workflow after a green full local validation, never for a single fix; there are no check branches or pull requests; only failed jobs are re-run; the next Workflow starts after the push without waiting for CI, but the phase is recorded done only once the last push's jobs and the staged `release.yml` run are green (decision 69). `release.yml` and Phase 10's `android-release.yml` are dispatched by the orchestrator only, with their minutes recorded.
- **No network in tests:** no test calls an outside host (no identity provider, registry, OSV, model host or vendor); the OIDC provider is in-process; the scanners' queries run in `supply-chain.sh`, which is a check, not a test; every download goes through a pinned `scripts/fetch_*` or `infra/scripts/install-*` before tests.
- **Honesty (ADR 0002, 0005, 0010):** a cloned result is never shown, served or counted as a fresh pass; every "ours" behaviour is labelled with its reason; no certification or compliance is claimed (ASVS and MASVS are self-assessed); no budget moves after the figure it judges is measured; every timing is labelled with its machine and load; a source the compliance guide could not re-read is marked so in place.
- **PII:** no shared field, consent evidence, export content, secret token value (session, one-time-link, invitation and API-key values), OIDC code or token, or other secret in logs, events, problems, metrics, screenshots or git; `cnst_`, `cxn_`, `cxnp_` and `cnsr_` ids are handles and may be logged (review P33). Synthetic data only (rule 3); the load seed, the OIDC fixtures and the Connect fixtures use `example.com` names and documentation IP ranges; the PII-pattern scan runs in the pre-commit hook.
- **Licences and versions:** rule 4 (permissive only; MPL-2.0 and EPL-2.0 only as unmodified tooling never shipped, each in §5; AGPL, GPL and LGPL fail even as tooling); every pin at least 14 days old on its adoption day (released on or before 2026-10-15 for this plan's pins, re-read by the adopting Stage 1a); Q1–Q12 are never assumed granted.
- **Content:** no vendor name, branding or copied text in product UI, strings, messages or code; docs name the vendor only where Q8's switch is on (the research notes, which already name it, excepted), enforced by a vendor-name test over `docs/` except `docs/research/`, `docs/plans/`, `docs/PLAN.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`, `docs/adr/` and the switched pages (review H18); public identifiers only as behaviour.

## Tests, evidence and validation

**Where each test runs:**
- **Vitest** (colocated, every package ≥ 80%): the Connect logic and services, sessions, the limiter, the pepper, OIDC against the in-process provider, quotas, patterns, the export, ingestion, the document generators and the record tests (`packages/docs-tools`), the release check;
- **Testcontainers** (C287.2): every race and lock, the authorization matrix, the role split, the migrate lock, `/ready`, the internal listener, the quota sweep, the export, the conditional sync;
- **Playwright** (C287.3): the mocked flow suite with the `share-consent` screen; the full-stack specs on the reusable stack with shards;
- **Android** (`androidHostTest`, `commonTest`; Phase 10's split gate): the `share-consent` renderer, its digest check, the pinning recipe's host test, the request fixtures; `stackTest` against the stack;
- **pytest:** `load/` and `security/check-sbom-licences.py`;
- **Shell contract tests** (`infra/tests/`): the installers, the images, the CI and release workflows, compose;
- **Chart tests** (C265): lint, unit, schemas, Trivy `config`, and `ct install` on kind.

**Evidence:** each agent's `docs/tdd/phase11-<agent>.tdd.md` (commands, outputs, the load average beside every timing); the calibration reports (`docs/calibration/load-phase11.md`, `watchlist-phase11.md`); the DAST report; the threat model's and ASVS checklist's review commits in PROGRESS; each CI and `release.yml` run id with its minutes in PROGRESS.

**Validation** (every call within 10 minutes; the splits below are measured by `foundation-11a` at the base and adjusted by each integration agent):

```bash
pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm format:check
pnpm turbo run lint --force                    # eslint-plugin-security and the pattern rule included (11B, 11C on)
pnpm turbo run typecheck --force
pnpm turbo run test --force --filter=@openverify/api -- --shard=1/2   # then --shard=2/2; the merged 80% gate as unit-api-coverage applies it
pnpm turbo run test --force --filter=@openverify/dashboard
pnpm turbo run test --force --filter='!@openverify/api' --filter='!@openverify/dashboard'   # every other package, docs-tools and sdk-react among them
pnpm --filter @openverify/api test:integration  # split into file groups if it exceeds 10 minutes
pnpm --filter @openverify/db test:integration
pnpm --filter @openverify/api openapi:write && git diff --exit-code docs/api/openapi.json
python3 scripts/fetch_models.py && python3 scripts/fetch_data.py
(cd services/vision && uv run ruff check && uv run ruff format --check && uv run mypy src tools)
(cd services/vision && uv run pytest <first half of the test paths>)      # 9 min 2 s whole today: split in two
(cd services/vision && uv run pytest <second half>)
(cd load && uv run ruff check && uv run ruff format --check && uv run mypy . && uv run pytest)   # 11D on
python3 -I scripts/fetch_asvs.py --verify-ids && python3 -I scripts/fetch_masvs.py --verify-ids  # 11E on
# Android (Phase 10's split gate, each under the Gradle lock; only exit 75 = lock busy):
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew :openverify-sdk:testAndroidHostTest)
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew :openverify-sdk:screenshotTest -Proborazzi.test.verify=true)
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew koverVerify $ABI_CHECKS)
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug :demo-app:assembleRelease :demo-app:bundleRelease)
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew :openverify-sdk:assemble lint)
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew cyclonedxBom)               # 11C on
bash infra/scripts/supply-chain.sh             # 11C on: osv-scanner, pnpm audit and signatures, pip-audit, zizmor, Trivy config, the chart checks, the PII scan
for t in infra/tests/*.test.sh; do bash "$t"; done   # installers, images, CI and release workflows, compose
for f in infra/docker/*.Dockerfile; do docker build -f "$f" .; done && docker build services/vision   # one call per image if over 10 minutes
(cd deploy/helm/openverify && helm lint . && helm unittest . && helm template . | kubeconform -strict -kubernetes-version 1.35.0 -schema-location <fetched>)   # with Helm 4.3.1 and 3.22.1; 1.36 and 1.37 too
for v in 1.35 1.37; do for c in "create $v" load fixtures models install smoke upgrade delete; do flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock bash deploy/helm/openverify/ci/kind.sh $c; done; done   # 11C checkpoints only; one call per subcommand
pnpm --filter @openverify/flow e2e             # the mocked flow suite with share-consent
OPENVERIFY_E2E_REUSE_STACK=1 pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e e2e:stack:up      # background allowed
for i in $(seq 1 $N); do OPENVERIFY_E2E_REUSE_STACK=1 pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e exec playwright test --shard=$i/$N; done
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew :openverify-sdk:stackTest)   # inside the e2e-locked section
bash security/zap/run.sh                       # 11F on, inside the e2e-locked section
pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e e2e:stack:down
pnpm release:check                             # 11E on (built by docs-release); the default mode, never --tag (C284.2)
gitleaks git --redact . && gitleaks dir --redact --config security/gitleaks-pii.toml .
```

The CI-only parts: Phase 10's `android-instrumented` device tests at every push; `release.yml` on dispatch (image scans, SBOMs, the kind install and upgrade, staging); Phase 10's `android-release.yml` matrix with the AAR SBOMs on dispatch. The load test, the calibration and DAST are local only (C267.3, C283.4).

## Risks

| Risk | Likelihood | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| A share reaches the wrong organisation, or more than the consent covers | Low | Tokens bound to their connection, side and environment kind on every call; one cross-tenant module behind an import boundary, with a generated probe over every path; consent coverage checked at create and at redemption; the policy filter in pure, table-tested code; the authorization matrix (C232, C240) |
| A cloned pass is relied on silently | Low | `accept-cloned-results` off by default with an audited acknowledgement; `needs_review` otherwise; `provenance` and the marker everywhere; the decision record names the token (C235) |
| Consent evidence overstated (a digest read as proof of reading; an attested record read as witnessed) | Med | `witnessed` and "not witnessed" in the API and both consoles; the server composes the text; the Android SDK checks it shows what it echoes; the docs' limits (C236.9, C238.8) |
| Redemption races withdrawal, deactivation, expiry or redaction | Med | One lock order (connection, consent, token) with `FOR UPDATE` against `FOR SHARE`, re-checks at commit, a Testcontainers race per pair in both orders (C234.2, C237.2) |
| The OIDC integration is subtly wrong (mix-up, replay, unverified email, role escalation) | Med | A maintained library (`openid-client`) with PKCE, `state` and `nonce`; identities linked by subject, email only for a verified first link inside the required domains; just-in-time provisioning off by default and, when on, bounded by permissions (no organisation-level permission and none of the C248.5 list, checked at save and at each sign-in); threat rows and tests against an in-process provider (C248) |
| A supply-chain compromise of a scanner or action (the Trivy case) | Med | Binaries by checksum and signature, never a third-party scanner action; images by digest; zizmor's pin audits; 14-day release age (C258) |
| CI minutes grow past the budget | Med | One `supply-chain` job (+8 to +10 minutes estimated, measured by `integration-11c`); image scans and `ct install` only in `release.yml`, dispatched three times, DAST local only; no nightly run; load tests never in CI (C267) |
| A load or calibration figure taken on a busy machine | High | The e2e lock, Stage 3 alone and one calibration agent at a time; HTTP takes from an idle baseline at most 4 with the load during the take recorded, matcher takes at most 4 at both ends; the generator on separate cores; every take listed; three takes and the median, base beside head (C268.6, C271.3) |
| 6D leaves a C78.7 budget missed and 11D cannot close it | Med | C271's conditional scope sized from 6D's profile; no budget moved; the gap recorded on the release checklist with the measured figures and the release notes (C271.4) |
| The chart's network policies give false comfort (no enforcing plugin) | Med | The guide's requirement and a `NOTES.txt` warning; the threat model lists it as `operator` (C264.2, C277.3) |
| The code runner's egress isolation fails in a sidecar | Low | The runner as its own Deployment with no egress, over `tcp:` with a token (C263.6), asserted by a chart test |
| Publication waits on the owner and the release is never published | Med | Everything staged by `release.yml`; each Q has its outcome if declined (C289); the release checklist shows each as `owner` |
| A legal fact in the compliance guide is stale or unread | Med | The re-read rule with failures marked in place; "not legal advice"; counsel's review pending (Q7) (C278.3) |
| Generated documents drift from the code | Low | Every generated page has a test against a fresh render; the threat model and checklists have evidence tests (C273–C280) |
| A Phase 7–10 interface lands differently from its plan | Med | Each Stage 1a's inventory and amendments before any Phase 11 code |
| Parallel Gradle or Docker work in one tree corrupts outputs or times out | High | The Gradle and e2e locks; split calls; `ct install` and image builds at checkpoints only |
| Ten new routes and tables widen the PGlite migration time under load (Phase 8's risk) | Med | One migration per Workflow, timed before and after by each Stage 1b |

## Release checklist (what must be true before the owner tags the release)

This release ships the web platform and Android. **No iOS code, app or artifact ships in it**: the shared Kotlin code is compiled for the iOS targets on Linux to stay iOS-ready (`:openverify-sdk:assemble`), untested on iOS and unpublished, and every iOS item waits for the next release (PLAN §6 "Next release: iOS"; review H26). `docs/release/checklist.md` (C284) holds these lines with their state; `pnpm release:check` verifies the machine-checkable ones.

**Built and green (agents and CI):**
- [ ] Phases 6–11 recorded done in `docs/PROGRESS.md`, each with its last push's CI run green (Phase 10's device jobs and the available legs of its release matrix included) and its run ids and minutes recorded.
- [ ] Every check in PLAN §4 in the registry with tier, type and stage; R-tier checks tested on synthetic fixtures (PLAN §10).
- [ ] End to end, web: an API-created inquiry completed through the web flow triggers a signed webhook and appears in the console's review (PLAN §10; the full-stack spec).
- [ ] End to end, Android SDK: `:openverify-sdk:stackTest` drives the SDK's real client against the stack (Phase 10 C224.4); the demo's minified smoke test is green on the CI emulator (Phase 10 C215.2). These do not run the demo app against the stack, so PLAN §10's Android half also needs the owner step below; until it runs, this part of PLAN §10 reads "partly met" (review H22).
- [ ] Reports, lists, graph, workflows, cases and Connect usable from the console and the API (PLAN §10, with Connect added).
- [ ] ≥ 80% coverage per package and Android module; every suite green locally and in CI; no CRITICAL or HIGH review finding open (PLAN §10).
- [ ] The documentation of PLAN §10: self-hosting, the check catalog, the API reference, the threat model, the compliance guide, the migration mapping (neutral or vendor-named, per Q8), and the sharing guide; each generator's test green.
- [ ] The threat model and the ASVS 5.0.0 Level 2 and MASVS v2.1.0 checklists reviewed at Phase 11's end with their commit; no `gap` row without a phase or release; "self-assessed", never "certified".
- [ ] The `supply-chain` job green; no critical or high finding without an unexpired, reasoned allowlist entry; the DAST report with no open high finding.
- [ ] The production images, the chart (lint, unit tests, kubeconform 1.35–1.37, `ct install` and the upgrade), SBOMs with the licence check, and the web packages staged by a green `release.yml` run on the final commit.
- [ ] `android-release.yml` green on the final commit (review H23): the staged Maven repository with each AAR's CycloneDX SBOM through C261.2's licence check and gitleaks over the unpacked artifacts (`cyclonedxBom` and the check added to Phase 10's workflow as `sbom-release-11c`'s named borrowed edit), dispatched by the orchestrator with its minutes in PROGRESS (C267.1).
- [ ] The load report and the Phase 11 watchlist calibration published; each budget `met` at the head, or `missed` with its gap and named in the release notes (C78.7 measured at 11D's base and head under owner decision 1 after 6C, whatever 6D found).
- [ ] `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` lists every shipped component and tool with its version, licence and date; no shipped row reads "owner to confirm" or "pending" (Q11, Phase 10 P2) and no staged artifact is labelled `pending-owner`, or the release ships each one's decline outcome; k6, Semgrep, Opengrep, yamllint and the other exclusions recorded; the release notes point at each image's base-distribution sources.
- [ ] `docs/release/notes.md` generated and complete: every disclosure a contract requires present (C284.4).
- [ ] Version strings agree across the root and published packages, the chart and the Android SDK; the changelogs have the release's dated entry with its minimum server, minSdk and compileSdk.
- [ ] Every deferral of every plan is under "Carried forward" or "Next release" in PROGRESS, or closed as moot. `release:check` parses the "Deferred" section of Phases 6–11's plans and C288's table for the Phase 0–5 carries, since those plans have no such section (C284.2, C286, C288). PLAN's "Next release" section holds C286's next-release items (C282).

**Owner steps and decisions (not agents):**
- [ ] The manual accessibility audits by a person: the web flow and console (Phase 9 decision 71) and the Android TalkBack, font-scale and keyboard pass (Phase 10 decision 47); until they run, the accessibility statement says "not yet evaluated for conformance".
- [ ] The real-document NFC read on the owner's phone in the demo's reader-check mode, pass or fail only (Phase 10 decision 48), if the JMRTD reader was built (P1).
- [ ] The Android demo app against a running stack, on a device or an emulator that reaches it: an API-created inquiry completed in the demo, its signed webhook verified, and the inquiry in the console's review (PLAN §10's Android half; review H22). No KVM exists on the development machine and the CI emulator does not reach the stack, so this is the owner's step.
- [ ] Phase 10's P1 (SCUBA's GPL files), P2 (Eigen in ONNX Runtime, which also governs `onnxruntime-web` in the flow image, Phase 4 decision 11), P3 (Play Integrity), P4 (Maven Central) and P5 (the 16 KB runner): each decided, or open with its stated outcome in the release notes; while P2 is open, the release ships the flow image built without `onnxruntime-web` (C289; review H20), and `release:check` refuses `done` otherwise.
- [ ] Q1–Q12 (this plan): each decided, or open with its default and its outcome in the release notes (C289); in particular the repository's visibility (Q1, the same question as Phase 10 decision 50's visibility note), the registry (Q2), the signing identity (Q3), npm (Q4).
- [ ] The SFace licence review by counsel (Q6; Phase 0's "before release" carry), or the release notes saying it is open and that the weights are fetched, never shipped in an image.
- [ ] Counsel's review of the compliance guide and the built-in `share.consent.*` texts, with the questions C278.4 lists (Q7), or the "not reviewed by counsel" line and the texts' `counsel-unreviewed` marker kept (C238.10).
- [ ] The final `release.yml` and `android-release.yml` dispatches on the tagged commit (by the orchestrator), and the owner's approval in the `release` environment if anything is published.
- [ ] `pnpm release:check --tag` passes on the tagged commit (C284.2), and the owner tags the release.

## Acceptance

- [ ] **Connect (C230–C244):** connections with versioned policies, activation in the console by the destination with a step-up, deactivation expiring unredeemed tokens; tokens of 60 s to 21 days (default 14 days), single use under a lock, bound to their recipient, environment kind and consent; peek in the vendor's envelope; clone and hydrate with mappings and account linking, never a face template, with `provenance` on every copy; a cloned pass counted only under `accept-cloned-results`; consent records on both sides with stored texts, attestation labelled, withdrawal expiring unredeemed tokens; the `share-consent` step on the web and in the Android SDK with no token in any URL; the vendor's four events and ours; seven scopes, two rate policies, one 404, audit on both sides; the console pages and the workflow actions; the 11A spec green.
- [ ] **Platform hardening (C245–C256):** the idle timeout and the session-ending events; the Valkey login limiter failing closed for login; pepper rotation and scope bounds; OIDC sign-in (SAML and SCIM deferred); TLS settings with warnings, secrets as files, two database roles and a locked migrate; `/ready`, worker health and the internal listener; `org:create`; per-tenant queue quotas; bounded operator patterns; the access export; the locked UN switch and conditional requests; HSTS and the verified CSP, frame and origin checks; the Android pinning recipe.
- [ ] **Supply chain and deployment (C257–C267):** five production images by digest; tools by checksum; the `supply-chain` job and the SAST and PII rules; SBOMs with the licence check; the chart with restricted pods, default-deny policies, one Secret per process and its tests; the publishable web packages and the demo site; `release.yml` staging everything and publishing nothing without the owner; the minutes measured.
- [ ] **Performance (C268–C272):** the load report with every budget's verdict, fairness and the soak; the write cost reduced or measured with identical matches; C78.7 and the re-screen measured at base and head, the head's verdict recorded with its gap if missed; the jit verdict.
- [ ] **Documentation and records (C273–C282):** every document with its test; the threat model and both checklists with live evidence; ADR 0010; PLAN without k6; the licence file, the surface index and the Phase 5 and 6 notes corrected.
- [ ] **Review (C283–C284):** DAST without an open high finding; every CRITICAL and HIGH review finding fixed or refuted by the verifier, none closed by a record; `pnpm release:check` green in its default mode (`--tag` is the owner's tag step, C284.2).
- [ ] **Owner-gated (C262, C285, C289):** each Q1–Q12 built in 11G, declined with its stated outcome, or open with its default; the flow image's decline outcome for Phase 10 P2 built and tested.
- [ ] **Deferred (C286) and covered (C288):** every row and carried item placed; every deferral in PROGRESS under "Carried forward" or "Next release".
- [ ] **Process:** every existing suite green at each checkpoint; every validation call within 10 minutes; each agent's `docs/tdd/phase11-*.tdd.md`; PROGRESS records each Workflow. **Done means CI-green** (decision 69): Phase 11 is recorded done only after the final push's CI jobs and one staged `release.yml` run on that commit are green, with their run ids and minutes in PROGRESS; until then PROGRESS says "done locally; CI pending". The release itself is the owner's tag (the release checklist).

## Decisions for the owner (made here; confirm or change)

Each item states the default the build uses until the owner decides, so none blocks a Workflow, and its source: the Connect note's "Needs the owner" (connect owner 1–12), the hardening note's (hardening owner 1–20), either note's fact-check or gaps, or this plan. The owner's standing approval is to take the industry-best default and record it. Where a note's default conflicts with a binding rule, the item says so and takes the compliant option. Twelve items are **not decided by this plan**, because they are an exception to a `CLAUDE.md` rule (rule 4's licences, the bar on naming Persona, the release-age rule for toolchain binaries), need the owner's own accounts, keys, visibility or money, or need a lawyer; each is recorded as pending with its recommended default, and the Workflows are ordered so that everything else builds, tests and is staged without it (11A–11F), with the dependent part last (11G), behind a switch, and a stated outcome if the owner declines (C289).

**Pending the owner (not decided here)**

- **Q1. Repository visibility at release** (hardening owner 3; Phase 10 decision 50's visibility note, the same question). It decides CodeQL, GitHub artifact attestations, whether keyless signing exposes the repository's identity, environments without a paid plan, and the vulnerability-reporting channel. **Recommended default:** stay private until the release; make the repository public at the release, which enables keyless signing, attestations, CodeQL and private vulnerability reporting at no cost; while private, publish nothing. **Gates:** C262, C276.4's channel, Q9. **If declined:** everything stays staged as workflow artifacts; no attestation, no keyless signature, no CodeQL.
- **Q2. A container registry, and publishing the images and the chart** (hardening owner 4; the owner's account). **Recommended default:** publish at the release to the project's own registry under the owner's account (for example GHCR), images by digest with an SBOM and a signature each, the chart as an OCI artifact, release notes pointing at each base's sources; the flow image's publication also follows Phase 4 decision 11 and Phase 10 P2 (Eigen inside `onnxruntime-web`). **Gates:** C262, C267.1's publish job. **If declined:** operators build the images from the documented Dockerfiles and install the chart from the repository; the staged artifacts are the release.
- **Q3. The signing identity** (hardening 5.4; the owner's identity or keys). **Recommended default:** cosign keyless from the public repository's release workflow, with SLSA provenance through GitHub attestations; a key held in the owner's secret store only if the repository stays private and the owner accepts managing it. **Gates:** C262. **If declined:** nothing is signed; the release notes publish each artifact's SHA-256.
- **Q4. npm publication** (Phase 9 C175.2; the owner's npm account and the `@openverify` scope). **Recommended default:** publish `@openverify/node`, `@openverify/js` and `@openverify/react` with provenance once the repository is public. **Gates:** C285. **If declined:** the packed tarballs with their SHA-256 are the release; READMEs show installing from a tarball or source.
- **Q5. The release-age rule for toolchain binaries, base images and GitHub Actions, and uv 0.13.3** (hardening owner 6, 5.4; an exception to the release-age rule is reserved). uv 0.13.3 was pinned in CI and the vision Dockerfile on 2026-10-20, its release day. The note's default offers "accepted as an exception or replaced"; the exception is the owner's, so this plan's **recommended default** is the compliant option: the rule covers binaries, images and actions, and uv 0.13.3 is replaced by the newest uv released at least 14 days before the change, unless the owner grants the exception. Every pin this plan adds already follows 14 days (C258.4). **Gates:** 11G's `records-11g` change only. **If the owner reads the rule as not covering binaries:** nothing changes, and the reading is recorded.
- **Q6. The SFace licence review, and model weights inside images** (hardening owner 5, H17; Phase 0's "before release" carry; needs a lawyer). **Recommended default:** weights are fetched at start into a volume by `fetch_models.py` with their pinned digests (decision 5) until counsel clears SFace's upstream licence (the upstream repository has no LICENSE file); then the owner may choose to bake them. **Gates:** C263.5's default and the vision image's contents. **If declined or never reviewed:** the weights stay fetched, and the release notes say the review is open.
- **Q7. Counsel's review of the compliance guide** (hardening owner 20, its second half; needs a lawyer). **Recommended default:** publish the guide with "not legal advice" and "not reviewed by counsel" on every page; counsel reviews before any operator relies on it in production. The review also covers the built-in `share.consent.*` texts (C238.10) and the questions C278.4 lists (reviews P12, P16): whether operator-mediated withdrawal meets Article 7(3), whether the access export may exclude face templates, and which exemptions govern the export's `operator-review` part. **Gates:** the removal of that line and of the texts' `counsel-unreviewed` marker. **If declined:** the line and the marker stay.
- **Q8. Naming Persona in the migration page** (hardening owner 17; `CLAUDE.md` bars Persona's name "in product UI, branding, logos, or copied text", and the task reserves any exception to that bar). **Recommended default:** a factual, docs-only page naming Persona as the source system, with no logo, no copied text and a not-affiliated line, since the rule's text covers product UI and branding and PLAN §10 already requires "the Persona mapping". **Until decided:** the generator renders a neutral page (`docs/migration/from-hosted-api.md`) that names no vendor and describes the vendor's named headers by role (C280.6), and the catalog's vendor-name column and the sharing guide's mapping follow the same switch. **If declined:** the neutral page is the release's.
- **Q9. A paid scanner** (hardening owner 2's second half; GitHub Code Security for CodeQL on a private repository costs money). **Recommended default:** buy nothing; the SAST baseline (decision 2) gates; CodeQL runs, informational, only once the repository is public (Q1). **Gates:** 11G's `codeql-11g`. **If declined:** the baseline only.
- **Q10. The UN Security Council list's opt-in** (Phase 6 decision 21 after 6B; hardening H2; connect K10). The UN's site terms grant non-commercial use only; rule 4 bars non-commercial weights and libraries, and whether an operator-fetched runtime list under such terms may be enabled is a question about that rule, so it is the owner's. **Recommended default:** allow an environment admin to enable `un-sc` with an audited acknowledgement of the UN's terms, under ADR 0005's reading that runtime data an operator fetches under its own terms is the operator's decision (as Phase 5 decision 40 treats CSCA master lists); until confirmed, the switch exists and is locked (C254.1). **If declined:** `un-sc` stays unenablable, as Phase 6 left it.
- **Q11. MIT-0 and EPL-1.0** (review H19; an exception to rule 4's list). `nodemailer` 10.0.15 is MIT-0 and ships in the API image (`THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md:88`); JUnit 4 (`junit:junit` 4.13.2) is EPL-1.0, test scope only through Robolectric (`:380`); both rows still read "owner to confirm". **Recommended default:** accept both: MIT-0 grants more than MIT (it drops the attribution condition), and EPL-1.0 is the file-level weak copyleft EPL-2.0's tooling exception already allows, used unmodified and never shipped. **Gates:** C261.2 labels the API image `pending-owner`, and `release:check --tag` refuses the tag until Q11 is answered or its decline outcome is built; staging and the default `release:check` of every validation are not blocked; they report Q11 as open (C284.2; verification V01). **If declined:** `nodemailer` is replaced by an MIT mailer or a minimal SMTP client behind the existing mailer port, test-first (in the next Workflow to run, or in 11G's `records-11g`); JUnit 4 cannot be removed while Robolectric needs it, so the outcome is a recorded exception request and the release notes say so (C289).
- **Q12. ZPL-2.1 for Locust's dependencies** (review F09; an exception to rule 4's list). Locust's `gevent` requires `zope.interface` and `zope.event` (ZPL-2.1, permissive and OSI-approved but not in rule 4's list), and `pyzmq`'s wheels bundle libzmq (MPL-2.0, §5 tooling). **Recommended default:** allow ZPL-2.1 as unmodified dev tooling in `load/`, never shipped, listed beside §5. **Gates:** Locust's adoption only, and only when granted before 11D's Stage 1a; a later grant is recorded and Locust waits for the next release (C289; verification V16); the load harness is built on vegeta and the `httpx` driver either way (C268.1). **If declined:** the driver stays, and nothing else changes.

**Owner steps (not decisions; carried to the release checklist):** the manual accessibility audits (Phase 9 decision 71, Phase 10 decision 47); the real-document NFC read (Phase 10 decision 48); the Android demo app against a running stack (PLAN §10's Android half, review H22); Phase 10's P1–P5; the GitHub `release` environment with its branch rule and the `OPENVERIFY_PUBLISH` variable once Q1–Q4 are answered; the `release.yml` dispatches' approvals; the release tag.

**Tools and policy**
1. **The load-test tools** (hardening owner 1; PLAN §6 corrected; changed by review F09). Default: vegeta v12.13.0 (MIT) and a small `httpx` (BSD-3-Clause) driver of ours with an open-model scheduler; Locust 2.46.7 (MIT) only if the owner grants Q12 for its ZPL-2.1 dependencies before 11D's Stage 1a; k6 excluded (AGPL-3.0 fails rule 4 even as tooling); Artillery not adopted (MPL-2.0 with telemetry on by default). PLAN §6's "k6 load test" becomes "HTTP load test (vegeta and a driver of ours)" (C268, C282).
2. **SAST without Semgrep, Opengrep or CodeQL** (hardening owner 2). Default: ESLint with `eslint-plugin-security` 4.2.0, ruff's `S` rules and Android lint, gating; Semgrep's engine and Opengrep (LGPL-2.1) and Semgrep's rules (source-available) excluded; CodeQL only under Q1 or Q9 (C260).
3. **SBOMs while the repository is private** (hardening 5.4). Default: built in `release.yml` and in Validation, kept as workflow artifacts, attached only when published (C261.3).
4. **Container images** (hardening owner 4, its build half). Default: five production images built and scanned on every staged release; publication is Q2 (C257).
5. **Model weights** (hardening owner 5, its build half; changed by reviews P05 and F05). Default: fetched by a separate `models-fetch` hook Job into a volume vision mounts read-only (or into a volume the operator fills), never by a container of the vision pod and never baked into the vision image; YuNet (MIT) ships inside the flow image because every browser already receives it (C257.1, C263.5).
6. **Remediation time frames** (hardening owner 7; ASVS v5.0.0-15.1.1). Default: critical 7 days, high 30, medium 90, low the next release; an allowlist entry expires within 90 days (C259.2, C276.4).
7. **The ASVS target and its wording** (hardening owner 8). Default: OWASP ASVS 5.0.0 Level 2, self-assessed, with MASVS v2.1.0 for the Android SDK; never "certified" or "compliant", enforced by a docs test; only ids committed, never the CC BY-SA text (C274, C275).
8. **The load-test budgets** (hardening owner 9). Default: hardening 6.4's budgets accepted as proposed, with Connect's added; recorded, never gated in shared CI, never moved to fit (C269).

**The platform**
9. **Console session timeouts** (hardening owner 10; G-IDLE). Default: 30 minutes idle and 12 hours absolute, both configurable; every session ended when a user is disabled, loses its roles, resets MFA or a password (C245).
10. **The login limiter** (hardening owner 11; G-LOGIN). Default: Valkey, failing closed for login only; the API limiter keeps failing open (H18) (C246).
11. **The DSAR access export** (hardening owner 12, H14). Default: built in Phase 11, the last chance in this release: a per-account JSON manifest with files, audited, asynchronous, expiring (C253).
12. **Kubernetes and Helm** (hardening owner 13). Default: Kubernetes ≥ 1.35 (1.34 reached end of life on 2026-10-27); tests on Helm 4.3.1 and 3.22.1; kubeconform on 1.35–1.37 (C263, C265).
13. **TLS to the data stores** (hardening owner 14). Default: required by the guide across hosts; production warnings on plain text, no refusal (in-cluster setups vary) (C249.1).
14. **A separate migration role** (hardening owner 15; B7-E1). Default: yes; two connection secrets, `db:grant-runtime`, and a session advisory lock around `db:migrate` (C249.3, C249.4).
15. **The threat-model format** (hardening owner 16). Default: Markdown with ids, Mermaid diagrams and a parsing test; Threat Dragon optional (C273).
16. **The PII-pattern scan** (hardening owner 18, H15). Default: a second gitleaks configuration with a reviewed allowlist, in CI and the pre-commit hook (C260.3).

**Connect**
17. **A share policy per connection** (ours; connect R5). Default: required at creation, versioned, frozen per version, accepted at activation; a change returns the connection to `pending` (C230.3, C231.6).
18. **The activation channel** (connect owner 2; R3; changed by reviews P01 and P06). Default: the console only, by the destination's user with the organisation-level `connect-connections:activate` and Phase 9's step-up (10 minutes, `step_up_required`; an OIDC re-authentication for an SSO session); no API-key activation (C231.3).
19. **No share without consent** (ours; connect R7, R24). Default: every token cites a source-side consent record covering its recipient, purpose and categories; one record authorises one token (C232.1).
20. **The token's lifetime, single use, revocation and environments** (connect owner 4; R11, R13, R14). Default: 14 days, 60 s to 21 days, an environment maximum, never past the consent's expiry; single use under a row lock, a repeat answering 409 `share_token_already_redeemed`; revocation by the source's expire, by deactivation of the connection and by withdrawal of the consent, each expiring an unredeemed token with its reason; sandbox only to sandbox and production only to production, checked at creation and at redemption (C232, C234.1, C237.2).
21. **No `ready` prefetch** (connect R9). Default: no cached PII snapshot; tokens go `created` → `pending` (C232.6).
22. **A handle, not a secret** (connect R16, correction 5). Default: the `cnst_` id is not hashed; every call is bound to its recipient; one indistinguishable 404 (C232.8).
23. **Biometric data in shares** (connect owner 6; R26). Default: images, liveness and match results only when the policy allows `biometric` and the person gives the separate explicit tick; face templates never (C230.3, C236.7).
24. **The destination's consent at redemption** (ours; connect 2.6). Default: required, recorded in the destination's flow (`share-consent`) or attested (C234.1, C236.5).
25. **What a cloned pass counts for** (connect owner 3; R21; changed by reviews H02, H07 and P08). Default: `accept-cloned-results` off; with it off a cloned inquiry lands in `needs_review` and a cloned verification counts toward nothing; turning it on needs an audited acknowledgement and a maximum age measured at the destination's decision; only cloned passes are relied on, a cloned failure routes to review; a client-side reuse needs a live selfie matched against the cloned portrait or the `cloned-results-unbound` acknowledgement; clones emit no `verification.*` events, count in no workflow condition or analytics unless counted here (C235).
26. **The data-sharing agreement** (connect owner 9; R27). Default: a required, sealed, audited free-text reference at activation; the platform does not judge it (C231.3).
27. **Attested consent** (connect owner 7; changed by review P03).
