Default: allowed under its own scope `connect-consent-records:attest` (`admin` only), with a required evidence reference, time and the operator's own text; for a share carrying `biometric` only where the destination accepted `attested-consent: all` in the policy (default `non-biometric`); labelled "not witnessed" in both consoles and to the destination (C236.5, C230.3).
28. **Withdrawal** (connect owner 10; R25; changed by review P12). Default: a flow exit while the session lives, then operator-mediated by API and console, documented in the share-consent text from the step's `withdrawal-contact`; withdrawn destination consents stop the copies counting; both withdrawal events are workflow triggers; whether this meets Article 7(3) is counsel's (Q7); a self-service link later (C237).
29. **Consent proof after the data is gone** (connect owner 8). Default: none kept by default; `consent-proof-retention-days` (0–3,650) keeps only the bare fact for the operator's legal-claims period (C241.3).
30. **The scope of Connect** (connect owner 1). Default: organisations on one install (shape 1); sharing between installs in the next release (C286).
31. **Report sharing** (connect owner 5; Phase 6 C88). Default: deferred; destinations run their own reports on hydrated data (ADR 0005) (C286).
32. **Wallets and credential issuance** (connect owner 11, 12). Default: deferred to the next release with Phase 5 C68; the C62 trust store stays generic; `pako`'s "MIT AND Zlib" is decided only when those libraries are adopted (C286).
33. **Client-side redemption** (ours; connect R17). Default: only through an inquiry the destination's backend creates with `meta.share-token-id`; no token in any URL or client view (C238.5).
34. **Events** (connect R18). Default: the vendor's four token events plus ours for expiry, failure, connections, consent and source redaction; no `ready` (C239).
35. **Scopes and limits** (connect R19, R16; changed by reviews P01, P03, P27). Default: seven `connect-*` scopes (the attest scope added) and `connect-connections:activate`, the connection writes organisation-level; `connect-redeem` and `connect-peek` at 60 a minute per key and per environment, peek failing closed and capped at 10 per token (C240, C233.5).
36. **Token rows' retention** (connect "Ours" 14; changed by review P04). Default: `expired` and `failed` tokens deleted 30 days after they end, as one-time links; a `redeemed` token kept as the disclosure record while either side's object exists (C241.4).

**Hardening details**
37. **SSO** (Phase 9 C175.2 and its decision 48; Phase 9 S15; changed by reviews P06, P07, P20, P21). Default: OIDC sign-in with `openid-client` 6.8.8 (MIT), PKCE, a callback per provider with `iss` checks, identities linked by subject (email only for the first link, verified and inside the required domains), multi-tenant issuers only with a tenant pin, the local factor when the provider's `amr` falls short, just-in-time roles bounded by permissions, enforcement with a break-glass command; SAML and SCIM in the next release (C248, C286).
38. **Per-tenant queue quotas** (hardening owner 19, H1; changed by reviews P15, F12). Default: per-environment in-flight caps per queue, 50 by default, lowered per environment and never above the install ceiling, admission recorded in `admitted_at` so the repair sweep respects it, on Phase 8's slots model (C251).
39. **Conditional requests** (H3; Phase 6 decision 10 after 6B). Default: per-source opt-in; a 304 validates, a full SHA-256 check still runs at least every 24 hours (C254.2).
40. **HSTS** (G-HSTS). Default: set by the chart's ingress (one year, subdomains, no preload); an optional header in the apps for installs without such an ingress (C255.1).
41. **Certificate pinning on Android** (Phase 10 decision 38; B11-S1). Default: a documented recipe through the transport seam with a host test; not built into the SDK (C256.2).
42. **Pins** (hardening 5.2, 10.9). Default: images by digest everywhere, Testcontainers included; binaries by checksum and signature; never a third-party scanner action; every new pin at least 14 days old (C258).
43. **The scanning gate** (hardening 5.3; changed by review H14). Default: one per-push `supply-chain` job; every critical and high, with or without a fix, fails unless allowlisted with a reason and an expiry, in the job and the image scans alike; databases fresh per run; overdue mediums listed by `release:check` (C259).
44. **The chart** (hardening 7.2). Default: no subchart and no Bitnami dependency; restricted Pod Security; default-deny policies; one Secret per process; the migrate hook with a GitOps switch; no static `frame-ancestors` (C263, C264).
45. **Re-reading the law** (hardening owner 20, its first half; hardening gaps). Default: `docs-compliance` re-reads every cited statute and regulation from its official text before the guide is committed and marks in place any it cannot read, never bypassing a challenge or a TLS failure (C278.3).
46. **ADR 0010.** Default: written by `contracts-11a` and `contracts-11c`, finalised by `integration-11e` (C282).
47. **Correct the records** (connect corrections 1–5; hardening 10.16). Default: PLAN §2, §3, §6, §7, §9, §10; `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`; `persona-surface.md`; the Phase 5 §8 addendum; the Phase 6 C88 note (C282).
48. **DAST** (hardening 5.5; changed by review F15). Default: ZAP's API and baseline scans against the e2e stack's images mode, locally in 11F and before each release's final dispatch, the dated report as the evidence; never in `release.yml`, against production, or nightly (C283).
49. **One per-push job** (the CI cadence). Default: every new per-push check in one `supply-chain` job, so its minutes round once; estimated +8 to +10 minutes a full run, the `e2e` job unchanged (C267.2).
50. **No nightly runs** (the CI cadence). Default: no nightly scan, DAST, load or matrix; `release.yml` dispatched once after 11C, once after 11F and once per release (C267).
51. **The code runner in a cluster** (Phase 8 P17; H8, corrected). Default: its own Deployment with no egress over a `tcp:` transport with a token, because a sidecar shares the worker's network namespace and an egress deny would cut the worker too (C263.6).
52. **Secrets as files; no KMS yet** (G-SECRETS; PLAN §3). Default: `<NAME>_FILE` for every secret; a KMS adapter in the next release, since a real KMS needs a cloud account to test (C249.2, C286).
53. **The SBOM tool** (hardening 5.1). Default: Syft for images and trees, the CycloneDX Gradle plugin for Android; cdxgen only if Syft's pnpm cataloguer proves incomplete (C261.1).
54. **Pepper rotation** (B3-S1). Default: a previous pepper with lazy re-hashing and a status command (C247.1).
55. **The internal routes** (Phase 9 C175.2, V13). Default: `OPENVERIFY_INTERNAL_LISTEN` with the ingress deny as a second layer (C250.3, C264.5).
56. **Operator patterns** (H16). Default: every entry point through Phase 9's linear-time matcher or refused, with an ESLint rule against dynamic `RegExp` (C252).
57. **The first production organisation** (hardening gap: no production seed). Default: `org:create` with a single-use invitation (C250.5).
58. **The web packages** (Phase 9 C177 and its decision 20). Default: `@openverify/js` and `@openverify/node` publishable, `@openverify/react` and `apps/demo-web` built in 11C (C266).
59. **Images for `linux/amd64` only** in this release; arm64 in the next (C286).
60. **The export's format** (H14; changed by review P16). Default: a schema-versioned JSON manifest with `subject` and `operator-review` parts; files streamed from their live objects one by one, never copied; every route behind `accounts:export`, a user's `pii:view` and the step-up, at 10 exports an hour per environment, failing closed; no archive library added (C253).

**Process and records**
61. **The Phase 6 write cost** (owner decision 9 after 6C). Default: the incremental key-frequency rebuild, a measured re-key batch size and a measured GIN option, every match identical (C270).
62. **C78.7** (owner decision 1 after 6C). Default: conditional on 6D's published calibration; worked only if a budget is missed; never moved; a remaining gap recorded on the release checklist (C271).
63. **`jit = off` for the application role** (H10; owner decision 9 after 6C). Default: recommended only if the load test shows no regression beyond the method's margin (C272.1).
64. **The timing rules** (changed by reviews F01 and H11). Default: every load and calibration take alone under the e2e lock; HTTP takes from an idle baseline at most 4, with the load during and after the take recorded; matcher takes at most 4 at both ends; the generator on separate cores; every take listed; one named background exception for takes longer than 10 minutes; three takes and the median (C268.6, Rules).
65. **Nothing for iOS** (owner, 2026-10-29). Default: no iOS code; the Android renderer in `commonMain`; the release checklist says so.
66. **Seven Workflows.** Default: 11A (10 agents), 11B (9), 11C (9), 11D (10), 11E (8), 11F (9), 11G (up to 5, gated part by part), each pushed once after a full local validation.
67. **The fact-checks.** Default: taken as corrected. Connect: RFC 9901's salt length is a RECOMMENDED minimum (it affects only the deferred two-install design) and the Token Status List's revision 21 is dated 2026-06-21. Hardening: RCW 19.375.020(1)'s notice, consent or mechanism are alternatives (C278.2); the request-discipline correction is noted; the two partly unverified facts do not bear on the plan: Threat Dragon's analytics (the tool is optional and outside every automated path) and zizmor's PyPI upload time (zizmor is installed from its GitHub release by checksum, so the GitHub date governs).
68. **The hardening note's gaps.** Default: each answered by a contract: the unread legal texts by the re-read rule (decision 45); `uv audit` not used (pip-audit and osv-scanner instead); keyless signing's exposure treated as real (Q3); pgvector's upgrade notes and pgAudit's licence re-read by `docs-selfhost` before naming them; KEDA as an example only; Next.js' origin checks tested (C255.4); Android's user CAs stated by Phase 10 C213.4; Gatling and SLSA's generator not adopted or an alternative; MASVS's groups and licence read by `contracts-11e` (C275); RE2's closure by C252's inventory; a production seed by `org:create`; the consent text shown by Phase 9 C162.9 and C236; the numbers by 11D.
69. **When Phase 11 is done.** Default: only once the final push's CI jobs and a staged `release.yml` run on that commit are green, with run ids and minutes in PROGRESS; "done locally; CI pending" until then. The release is the owner's tag after the release checklist.
70. **Every carried item placed:** C288's tables place every gap row R1–R36, connect K1–K12, hardening H1–H22 and G-*, and every deferral of the earlier plans and PROGRESS that names Phase 11, hardening or a later phase; C286 lists what moves to the next release and why.

**Added by the review**

71. **Connect is opt-in** (review P09). Default: off unless the install sets `OPENVERIFY_CONNECT_ENABLED` (false in the images and the chart, true in the development and test seeds) and the receiving organisation turns on `connect-inbound`; a create towards an organisation that has not opted in is the unknown-id 422; the source sees the destination's name only from activation (C231.10, C230.2).
72. **Metrics as structured log events** (review H13). Default: no metrics library or endpoint; every named metric is a pino event with a `metric` field, registered in `metrics-registry.ts`, and the docs teach log-based alerting; a `/metrics` endpoint in the next release (C250.7, C286).
73. **The flow image reads its API origin at run time** (review F04; verification V07). Default: `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_API_URL` read by the flow server, served as `/config.json`, `connect-src` as a header; one digest serves every install; a bundle built with `VITE_OPENVERIFY_API_URL` (development, `vite preview`, the per-push e2e, the mocked suite) keeps today's build-time origin and its `<meta>` `connect-src` (C257.1).
74. **The e2e stack keeps host processes per push** (review F03). Default: an images mode (`OPENVERIFY_E2E_IMAGES=1`) for 11C's checkpoints and DAST (the load stack is compose with its own override, C268.4; verification V10), never in the per-push `e2e` job, so CI minutes do not grow by five image builds (C257.3, C267.2).
75. **Generated release notes** (review H21). Default: `docs/release/notes.md` generated from the checklist, the calibrations, C289's outcomes and the digests, checked by `release:check` (C284.4).
76. **A decline is a record** (review H05). Default: a `declined` consent record that nothing can cite, which blocks an attestation (on the declined subject or its account, verification V08) or an API redemption that would override it within the step's validity (C236.5, C236.11).
77. **A copy is never re-shared** (review H03). Default: a source that is or contains a clone is refused at creation and at redemption (`source_is_copy`); carrying an origin chain across hops is not built (C232.1).

**Review**

Three adversarial reviews read the first draft on 2026-10-29 (privacy, security and law P01–P35; honesty and product coverage H01–H27; feasibility and fit with the code F01–F30): 92 findings, 1 CRITICAL, 31 HIGH, 40 MEDIUM and 20 LOW. `docs/plans/phase-11-review-resolution.md` disposes of each: 88 accepted (P04 and F09 with another mechanism than the reviewer's, and H03, H07, H13, F15 and F18 with the option named in the resolution), 4 accepted in part (P08, P17, P24, H06, each with the part not taken and its evidence), none rejected; nine findings raised by two lenses are resolved once. **The verification round** found 85 resolved, 7 partly resolved (P08, P13, H11, H15, H25, F13, F28) and 16 new defects (1 HIGH, 7 MEDIUM, 8 LOW). Each is applied with the smallest edit:
- `release:check`'s two modes (C284.2);
- the flow withdrawal exit specified and frozen, on Android too (C237.6, C238.7);
- the models volume's lifecycle (C263.5);
- the cross-tenant boundary's split repositories (C240.5);
- the audit delete trigger's exemptions (C249.3);
- one publish-check hook (C238.2);
- the flow's build-time origin kept beside the run-time one (C257.1);
- the decline guard across a subject's account (C236.5);
- the remaining list, ownership and wording fixes.

Decisions 1, 73, 74 and 76 are reworded in place, and no decision or owner item is added.

- **Decisions changed in place:** 1 (vegeta and a driver of ours; Locust only under Q12), 5 (weights by a separate fetch Job), 18 (Phase 9's step-up, organisation-level activation), 25 (cloned results: only passes relied on, age at the decision, binding, no events or workflow or analytics counting), 27 (attestation's own scope and the policy's `attested-consent`), 28 (the flow withdrawal exit; Article 7(3) to counsel), 35 (seven scopes; peek capped and failing closed), 36 (redeemed tokens kept as the disclosure record), 37 (SSO linking, tenant pins, `amr`, permission-bounded just-in-time roles), 38 (the install ceiling; admission recorded), 43 (every critical and high gated; fresh databases), 48 (DAST local only), 60 (the export streams live objects), 64 (the load timing rules and the background exception), 66 (11D ten agents, 11E eight). **Added:** 71–77. **Owner items added:** Q11 (MIT-0 and EPL-1.0) and Q12 (ZPL-2.1 for Locust's dependencies), and a C289 row for Phase 10 P2's decline outcome in the flow image; Q7 gains the questions for counsel.
- **Main changes:** Connect's consent model closed at both ends (C232.1, C234.1, C234.2, C236, C237.6): categories fixed at creation, the destination record checked item by item and following the copy, attestation separated from token creation, a decline recorded, a copy never re-shared; clones kept out of events, workflow conditions and analytics (C235.7) and bound to the person in client-side reuse (C235.8) through their own `fill-inquiry` mode (C234.11); connections organisation-level and opt-in (C231.9, C231.10); the disclosure record kept for Articles 15 and 19 (C241.4); SSO hardened against account takeover and missing second factors (C248); the runtime role made workable for the sync and unable to erase the audit log (C249.3); metrics as registered log events (C250.7); the export without copies, rate-limited and split for review (C253); the flow image configurable at run time with a no-guidance variant (C257.1, C289); the e2e stack's images mode instead of image builds per push (C257.3); the vision pod egress-free in every mode (C263.5); scanning gates and pins completed, yamllint excluded (C258, C259); the load harness spread under the shipped limits with error budgets and achievable timing rules, C78.7 always measured at the head (C268, C269, C271); generated release notes and a release check built in 11E (C284); the release checklist's Android, licence and deferral lines made true to their evidence.

## Workflow 11A Stage 1a amendments (contracts-11a)

Written by `contracts-11a` from `docs/research/phase11-inventory.md` section 1 (read at `4db86122d`). Where a path or a name below differs from the plan above, this section and the inventory win. Items 1 to 24 are the drifts the inventory found and the decisions taken in their place; the items after them are recorded at each family's checkpoint.

1. **Counts.** `API_SCOPES` holds 68 and `OPERATOR_PERMISSIONS` 9 at the base (the plan's 28 and 8 were Phase 6's); `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` holds 85 rows (the plan's 39). 11A adds seven scopes and one permission (75 and 10).
2. **The rewrap list** is `REWRAP_TABLES` in `packages/db/src/repositories/key-rotation.ts:18`, not in `encrypted-columns.ts`. `foundation-11a` adds each sealed Connect column to `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` (`packages/db/src/encrypted-columns.ts:22`) and its table to `REWRAP_TABLES`, with both tests changed in the same commit.
3. **No `unbuiltJob` exists.** An unbuilt service or action rejects with `notBuilt(name)` (`apps/api/src/workflows/not-built.ts:5`, a `NotImplementedError`, 501). `foundation-11a`'s stub processors for the queues `connect-token-expiry` and `connect-redeem` throw `notBuilt('<queue>')`, so a job enqueued before its builder lands fails loudly and is never marked done; the queue files follow the one-`*-queues.ts`-file-per-family pattern under `apps/api/src/jobs/`.
4. **`eventSubject`** is `packages/contracts/src/event-names.ts:271` (the plan's "the event stager"); the webhook subject is `toEventSubject` (`apps/api/src/events/subject.ts:218`). Both map the `connect/` heads since the common checkpoint.
5. **The rate limiter's key (V11).** The limiter keys one bucket per policy and caller key (`apps/api/src/rate-limit/index.ts:101-103`), and the plugin's caller key is the API key id or the user session id (`apps/api/src/plugins/rate-limit.ts:190`), so today a user session has one bucket whatever environment it acts in. C240.3's "one bucket per principal and environment pair" is therefore a key, not a map entry: for `connect-redeem` and `connect-peek` the caller key is `<principal id>:<environment id>`. `foundation-11a` wires it with the policies (a test that one session acting in two environments has two buckets, and one API key one); `api-connect` and `api-redeem` only name the policies.
6. **The import rule (C240.5)** goes in the root flat config `eslint.config.js` (the repository's own rules live in `packages/config/eslint/`, which holds no `no-restricted-imports` today); the source test sits beside `cross-tenant.ts`.
7. **Settings storage.** `require-mfa` is the column `organizations.require_mfa` (`packages/db/src/tables/tenancy.ts:35`); environment settings are keys of `environments.settings` (jsonb, `EnvironmentSettings`, `:153`). So `share-token-max-seconds` and `consent-proof-retention-days` are environment keys (no column; done at the common checkpoint), and `connect-inbound` is a new column `organizations.connect_inbound boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, which `contracts-11a` (C) adds in the migration `0035_phase11_connect.sql` (orchestrator decision 11). `foundation-11a` extends that same migration with its tables and columns (regenerating `0035` and `meta/0035_snapshot.json` with `drizzle-kit generate`, never adding `0036`).
8. **`accept-cloned-results`** is a member of `templateSettingsSchema` (`packages/contracts/src/template-settings.ts:19-43`), with its resolved form (orchestrator decision 22); there is no "definition document" and no column (C230.5's last bullet).
9. **The step's names (orchestrator decision 17).** The type literal is `share_consent` (flow-spec 2's snake_case, beside `device_handoff`); its members are camelCase: `side`, `connectionId`, `consentValidSeconds`, `withdrawalContact` and the copy override member flow-spec 2's other steps use. Route paths (`/api/v1/flow/share-consents/:step-id/...`), resource attributes (`consent-valid-seconds` on a consent record, `withdrawable-share-consents` on the session), problem codes and publish codes keep the plan's kebab and snake names. Wherever the plan writes `share-consent` for the step type, read `share_consent`.
10. **Hooks' places** (orchestrator decision 11): the client-view hook goes in `flowSessionMembers` at `apps/api/src/flow-engine/session.ts:250-280` beside the `nfc` view's builder (`apps/api/src/services/flow-views/nfc.ts`, the precedent); the publish hook at `apps/api/src/services/publish/flow-v2.ts:233` (`flowPublishReport`); the template preview also calls `clientViewOf` (`apps/api/src/services/templates/preview.ts:220`) and gets the same hook so a preview never shows an uncomposed text.
11. **The decision path** is `decideInquiryOutcome` and `requiredVerifications` (`apps/api/src/jobs/inquiry-outcome.ts:147`, `:61`), `settleInquiry` (`apps/api/src/services/inquiry-settlement.ts:54`), `stepVerificationOutcome` (`apps/api/src/flow-engine/verification-state.ts:73`) and the decision record's service (`apps/api/src/services/decisions/record.ts`). `foundation-11a`'s C235.4 hook is one call in `requiredVerifications` and one in `stepVerificationOutcome`, each to `services/connect/cloned-results.ts`.
12. **Where a workflow condition reads a check (C235.7(b))** is the trigger view (`packages/workflow-engine/src/expressions/trigger-view.ts`) and `fetch_object` (`apps/api/src/workflows/actions/objects/`). The hook goes where the API builds the run's view of a verification or check (the objects' fetch), because `packages/workflow-engine` cannot import the API; the engine's trigger view gets nothing new.
13. **The analytics hook (C235.7(c); orchestrator decision 21)** is a predicate in `apps/api/src/services/analytics/rollup-sql.ts` that leaves out every verification and check with a `connect-clone` provenance; `refresh.ts` and `read.ts` are unchanged.
14. **The object pages' slots.** Inquiries and accounts take `loadSection(can(<permission>), () => <Section>(slotProps))` in `apps/dashboard/src/app/{inquiries,accounts}/[id]/page.tsx` and one `SlotSection` line in `components/lifecycle/{inquiry,account}-view.tsx` (`optional-section.tsx:37`, `inquiry-view.tsx:331`, `account-view.tsx:315-317`). The transaction page (`apps/dashboard/src/app/transactions/[id]/page.tsx`) has no slot: `console-connect`'s section there is a named borrowed edit of the page itself, with a `PageSection`.
15. **`workflow-connect`'s folder** is `apps/api/src/workflows/actions/connect/` (one folder per family, with `index.ts`), registered from its own files through `RESERVED_CONNECT_ACTION_KEYS` (the (U) checkpoint).
16. **Triggers need no declaration** (orchestrator decision 23): a trigger is a `trigger-event` config over `EVENT_NAMES`, and `isTriggerableEvent` (`packages/contracts/src/workflow-definition.ts:107-113`) refuses only `workflow-run.*`, `workflow.published` and `workflow-version.published`, so every Connect event is triggerable from the common checkpoint on.
17. **What a clone or a hydrate meets (Phases 7 to 9), the default for `api-redeem`.** No clone or hydrate copies signals or signal details, a session's network, device or handoff rows, session records or `created_by_session_id`; a copied inquiry is made without `transitionInquiryInTx`'s outbox writes (no signal computation, no `list_screenings` row, no `graph_index_jobs` row: its provenance says what it is, and the destination screens and indexes its own inquiries); a verification marked `from_rejected_handoff` is never the source of a clone (409 `source_not_decided` is not the answer: it is simply not a shareable verification, so a share holding only such a verification copies none). A hydrated transaction is written through the transaction service (`apps/api/src/services/transactions/transactions.ts:210`) against a published type version of the destination (422 `transaction_type_not_published` otherwise), so its field validator, card guard (`card_number_refused`), write-once rule, searchable keys under the destination's blind-index subkey, sealed fields and events apply; a source field the destination type does not declare is dropped and listed in `redeem-summary.dropped-fields`; its files are re-encrypted as new objects; no relation, label or status history is copied. A hydrate into an account goes through the account write path that calls `indexAccountAttributesInTx`. No list, list item, list match, importer, graph row or graph query, case and its attachments, comments and holds, workflow run, decision record (a clone gets its own), flow step record, theme asset or analytics row is ever a share source or a copy.
18. **Transaction redaction (K7B-1).** An inquiry's or account's redaction reaches its transactions through `redactTransactionInTx` (`apps/api/src/services/transactions/redaction.ts`, called from `apps/api/src/services/redaction.ts:90`) in the same transaction, so C241.1's `source_redacted` hook for a transaction source is a call inside `redactTransactionInTx` itself, where the cascade reaches it too; the inquiry and account hooks are calls in `services/redaction.ts`.
19. **The flow routes' guards.** The three share-consent exits meet `assertSessionNotHandedOff` (`apps/api/src/auth/flow-session.ts:124`): a handed-off desktop session answers 409 `session_handed_off` on each of them, the withdraw exit included, like every non-GET flow route outside `/api/v1/flow/handoffs`. `getEffectiveConsent` is unchanged: the share-consent record is a `consent_records` row, never `inquiry_sessions.consent`.
20. **The consent digest.** `consentTextDigest` (`apps/api/src/services/templates/consent-texts.ts:72`: placeholders filled, NFC, SHA-256 hex) is API-side, so `packages/core` cannot import it. `connect-logic`'s pure composition in `packages/core/src/connect/` returns the logical text, and the API digests it with `consentTextDigest`; `connect-logic`'s tests take the digest from `node:crypto` over the same NFC string and a cross-check test in the API holds the two equal.
21. **`consent_text_mismatch` (422), `flow_step_not_current` (409) and `step_up_required` (403)** already exist with their goldens (`packages/contracts/src/problem.ts:620-627`; `contracts/fixtures/problems/flow-engine/consent-text-mismatch.json`; `contracts/fixtures/problems/access/step-up-required.json`) and are reused by name; Connect's own codes are `CONNECT_PROBLEM_STATUSES` (27 codes, `packages/contracts/src/problem.ts:917`) with one golden each under `contracts/fixtures/problems/connect/`, and the one 404 is `CONNECT_NOT_FOUND_PROBLEM` with `contracts/fixtures/problems/connect/not-found.json`.
22. **The common family's event set** is sixteen names (`CONNECT_EVENT_NAMES`: five `connect/connection.*`, eight `connect/share-token.*`, three `consent-record.*`), with payload resources, samples (`contracts/fixtures/api/connect/event-*.json`), the console group `Connect` and words; `inquiry.*` payloads carry an optional `provenance` (`packages/contracts/src/provenance.ts`), with the sample `contracts/fixtures/api/connect/event-inquiry-cloned.json`.
23. **Android's contract fixtures.** `Fixtures.list` (`android/openverify-sdk/src/androidHostTest/kotlin/dev/openverify/sdk/model/Fixtures.kt:17`) reads only the files directly inside `contracts/fixtures/api/`, so the Connect goldens live in `contracts/fixtures/api/connect/` and are outside `ContractFixturesTest`'s set: the SDK never holds an API key and never reads them. A Connect fixture the SDK must decode (the flow session with `withdrawable-share-consents`, the share-consent client view) goes directly in `contracts/fixtures/api/` with a decoder entry, or in `contracts/fixtures/flow-spec/`.
24. **`.env.example` and compose.** `OPENVERIFY_CONNECT_ENABLED` is declared in `.env.example` (`true`: a development install), passed by `infra/docker-compose.yml` to the API and the worker as `${OPENVERIFY_CONNECT_ENABLED:-false}` (an install without the variable is off), held by `infra/tests/compose.test.sh`, and set `true` by `e2e/src/env.ts`. The API reads it as `config.connect.enabled` (default `false`).
25. **(C) The frozen Connect schemas** are `packages/contracts/src/connect.ts` (exported from the package root): the policy (`sharePolicySchema`, `storedSharePolicySchema`, `sharePolicyVersionSchema`, `SHARE_POLICY_LIMITS`, `sharePeekPathSchema`, `parsePeekPath`, `shareFieldKeySchema`), the connection (`connectionResourceSchema`, `connectionDocumentSchema`, `connectionListDocumentSchema`, `createConnectionBodySchema`, `listConnectionsQuerySchema`, `activateConnectionBodySchema`, `changeSharePolicyBodySchema`, `connectionParamsSchema`), the share token (`shareTokenResourceSchema`, `shareTokenDocumentSchema`, `shareTokenListDocumentSchema`, `createShareTokenBodySchema`, `listShareTokensQuerySchema`, `getShareTokenQuerySchema`, `shareTokenParamsSchema`, `shareTokenSourceConsentSchema`, `redeemSummarySchema`) and the peek envelope (`sourceDataSchema`, `sourceDataVerificationSchema`, `peekableAttributeSchema`, `peekFilePresenceSchema`, `sourceDataType`). Each side's view is held by the schema itself: a connection answered with `direction: outbound` carries no `agreement-reference` member and an `inbound` one no `creator`; a token answered `inbound` carries no `creator`, `source` or `consent-record`, `destination` only once `redeemed`, and `source-data` only while `created`. A self-connection's answers are `direction: self` (connection) and `outbound` with `destination` once redeemed (token). Goldens: `contracts/fixtures/api/connect/{connection-*,share-token-*,share-policy,source-data-*}.json` (one envelope per subtype).
26. **(C) The peek path** is `<subtype>.<attribute>` for every subtype, or `<subtype>.fields.<key>` for `inquiry`, `account` and `transaction` only (a verification has no `fields` bucket); no attribute is named `fields`. The policy refuses a path it does not share: a field key outside that object type's allowlist, a subtype whose source type is not shared, or a verification type the policy does not list.
27. **(C) `share_policy_invalid` (422) and the route's validation.** The body schemas carry the policy's refinements, so a policy that breaks a rule fails Fastify's body validation. `foundation-11a` registers the create and policy-change routes so that a body whose every failing issue lies under `/data/attributes/share-policy` answers 422 `share_policy_invalid` with one `errors[]` entry per issue (`location: body`, the JSON pointer of the issue, `~1` for a `/` inside a peek path), as `contracts/fixtures/problems/connect/share-policy-invalid.json` shows; any other body error stays Phase 1's 400.
28. **(C) Query booleans.** `peek-source-data` is the string `true` or `false` (`booleanFilterSchema`), as every boolean query member of the API is; the route compares the string.
29. **(C) `connect-inbound`** is `organizations.connect_inbound` (migration `0035_phase11_connect.sql`, `boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`), `SETTING_LEVEL['connect-inbound'] = 'organization'`, the repository function `setOrganizationConnectInbound(db, id, value)`, `CONNECT_INBOUND_DEFAULT = false` and the optional member `connect-inbound` of `dashboardSettingsSchema` and `dashboardUpdateSettingsBodySchema` (`GET`/`PATCH /api/v1/dashboard/settings`, audited in `settings.updated` like every change). An environment-scoped `settings:write` gets 403 for it. `api-connect` reads `organizations.connect_inbound` of the destination at create (C231.1's byte-identical 422 when it is off). `console-connect` edits it on its Connect settings page (the console type `ConnectSetting` names it).
30. **(C) The test tenant.** `createTestTenant` (`packages/db/src/testing.ts`) writes its organisation with SQL and the columns every schema has, so upgrade tests can seed a tenant before 0035; the row it returns carries `connectInbound: false`. Two existing tests changed for it, minimally: `packages/db/src/migration-0034.test.ts` no longer asserts that 0034 is the last migration, and `apps/api/src/services/access/mfa-default.test.ts` reads the SQL insert of `require_mfa` as a creation path. A later migration that adds a column to `organizations` or `environments` needs the same care in `createTestTenant`.
31. **(C) The install switch** is `config.connect.enabled` (`ConnectInstallSettings` in `apps/api/src/config.ts`, read by the API and the worker from `OPENVERIFY_CONNECT_ENABLED`, default `false`, only `true` or `false`). It reaches every Connect route's guard and the publish hook through the container (`foundation-11a`).
32. **(C) `okhttp-tls` (KP11-1).** Added to `:openverify-sdk`'s `androidHostTest` dependencies only; the lockfile gained one line from one cold Gradle home, `android/gradle/verification-metadata.xml` and every other lockfile (the consumer check's three included) are unchanged, and no staged POM or module names it. `HostTestDependenciesTest` (`dev.openverify.sdk.testing`, the `rest` group) holds the class on the host-test classpath and the lockfile line to host-test configurations at the okhttp version. `clients-connect`'s pinning test uses `okhttp3.tls.HeldCertificate` with `mockwebserver3`. The licence row (`THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`, okhttp-tls) names both test scopes, with the date 2026-11-12.
33. **(R) The redeem contract** (`packages/contracts/src/connect.ts`): `redeemShareTokenBodySchema` (`data.attributes.destination-id`, any id, optional; `meta` = `redeemShareTokenMetaSchema` with the public `field-mappings` (`shareFieldMappingSchema`: `source-field-name` a field key, `destination-field-name` a field key with up to four dots), `account-id`, `auto-create-account-reference-id`, `auto-create-account-type-id`, and ours `inquiry-template-id`, `inquiry-template-version-id`, `inquiry-id` and the required `destination-consent-record-id`), and three pure rules the route applies after validation: `shareRedeemModeOf(body)` (`clone` without `destination-id`, `hydrate` with it), `shareRedeemMetaConflicts(body)` (the JSON pointers of the members in conflict, for 422 `share_redeem_meta_conflict`; the reference without its type is a conflict) and `shareRedeemPairAllowed(sourceType, mode, destinationType)` (for 422 `share_redeem_mode_invalid`). A conflict or a wrong pairing is a 422 from the route, never a 400 from validation. At most `SHARE_FIELD_MAPPINGS_MAX` (600) mappings. The answer is 202 with `shareTokenDocumentSchema` (golden `contracts/fixtures/api/connect/share-token-redeem-accepted.json`); bodies `redeem-clone-inquiry.json`, `redeem-clone-verification.json`, `redeem-hydrate-account.json`.
34. **(R) `fill-inquiry`** is `SHARE_REDEEM_MODES`' third value and never a route mode (`SHARE_REDEEM_ROUTE_MODES = ['clone', 'hydrate']`); its request is `fillInquiryRedemptionSchema` (`share-token-id`, `inquiry-id`, `destination-consent-record-id`), which `api-consent` hands `api-redeem`'s job. `shareRedeemPairAllowed` admits it from an inquiry or a verification into an inquiry only.
35. **(R) `accept-cloned-results`** is `acceptClonedResultsSchema` (`verification-types`: one or more distinct `SHARE_VERIFICATION_TYPES`; `max-age-days` 1 to 3,650, `ACCEPT_CLONED_RESULTS_MAX_AGE_DAYS`), a nullable optional member of `templateSettingsSchema` and `resolvedTemplateSettingsSchema` (`packages/contracts/src/template-settings.ts`); `TEMPLATE_SETTINGS_DEFAULTS` and `resolveTemplateSettings` serve `null` (off) when it is absent, so every resolved settings answer now carries the member (four exact-equality tests changed by one member each; golden `contracts/fixtures/api/connect/template-settings-accept-cloned-results.json`).
36. **(R) Publish.** `CONNECT_PUBLISH_RULE_CODES` (`packages/contracts/src/problem.ts`, beside `SIGNAL_PUBLISH_RULE_CODES`) holds `cloned_results_without_binding`, carried in `errors[].code` of a template's `definition_invalid` (golden `contracts/fixtures/problems/connect-publish/definition-invalid-cloned-results.json`, pointer `/data/attributes/settings/accept-cloned-results/verification-types/<i>`); (K) adds the rest of C238.2's codes to the same list. `TEMPLATE_ACKNOWLEDGEMENT_KINDS` gains `cloned-results` (target a verification type, one per listed type), `cloned-results-unbound` (target a verification type) and `share-consent-default-text` (target a `share_consent` step id), each with its console words in `FLOW_BUILDER_COPY.acknowledgements`. `api-consent`'s `services/connect/publish-checks.ts` requires and records them through Phase 9's existing acknowledgement path.
37. **(R) Provenance** is unchanged from the common checkpoint (`provenanceSchema`, `connectCloneProvenanceSchema`, `connectHydrateProvenanceSchema`, `isCloneProvenance`, each with the optional `consent-withdrawn-at`); a test now holds that no member discloses a source object id.
38. **(K) The consent record** (`packages/contracts/src/consent-records.ts`): `consentRecordResourceSchema` (its refinements: only a `flow` record is `witnessed` and can be `declined`; the evidence matches the method; `status: declined` exactly when `outcome: declined`; `withdrawn-at` and `withdrawn-via` together), `consentRecordDocumentSchema`, `consentRecordListDocumentSchema`, `listConsentRecordsQuerySchema` (`filter[subject-id]`, `filter[side]`, `filter[status]`, `filter[connection-id]`), `attestConsentRecordBodySchema` (C236.5; a source side names `subject-id` (an inquiry, account or transaction) and no token, a destination side `share-token-id` and no subject), `withdrawConsentRecordBodySchema` and `consentRecordParamsSchema`. The withdrawal's optional reason is the member `withdrawal-reason` (the plan's `reason`), as the resource names it, because `reason` alone is a name every exit masks; its closed list is `subject-request`, `operator-decision`, `other`. The IP and the user agent are served as `ip-address` and `user-agent`, sealed and only with `pii:view`, with `evidence-reference` and `text-body` (`PII_CONSENT_RECORD_ATTRIBUTES`). Goldens: `contracts/fixtures/api/connect/consent-record-*.json`.
39. **(K) The step.** `shareConsentStepSchema` (`packages/flow-spec/src/v2/schemas/steps.ts`): `{id, type: 'share_consent', name?, side: 'source' | 'destination', connectionId? (a source side's, required there, refused on a destination side), consentValidSeconds? (60 to 1,814,400; `SHARE_CONSENT_VALID_SECONDS`, 14 days by default), withdrawalContact? (1 to 500 characters, no control character; publish requires it)}`. There is no `copyKeys` member: the version's operator copy for the `share.consent.*` keys already overrides the built-in texts (Phase 9's per-version copy), so C238.1's `copy-keys?` needs no member of the step. Both accept and decline continue on `next` (the default ports): declining never ends the flow. It is in `FLOW_V2_STEP_TYPES` and `V2_ONLY_STEP_TYPES` (a spec-1 client gets the placeholder), not a verification and not a server step. The golden documents are `contracts/fixtures/flow-spec/share-consent-flow.json` and `contracts/fixtures/flows/v2/every-step.json` (one step of every type).
40. **(K) The client view** is `shareConsentClientViewSchema` in `clientStepViewSchema`, with camelCase members `{id, type, side, recipientName, sourceName, categories, text: {locale, body, sha256, keys}, biometric?: {body, sha256}}` (the plan's `recipient-name`, `source-name`), and nothing else: no connection, token or source id, no withdrawal contact or validity. `clientViewOf` answers `null` for the type, because the view is composed only by the API: `api-consent`'s `services/flow-views/share-consent.ts` builds the composition and calls `shareConsentClientViewOf(step, composition)` (`ShareConsentComposition`), from `foundation-11a`'s hook in `flowSessionMembers` and in the template preview. Golden: `contracts/fixtures/flow-spec/share-consent-client-view.json`.
41. **(K) The exits** (`packages/contracts/src/flow-api.ts`): `flowShareConsentParamsSchema` (`{stepId}`, the screen exit's spelling: the routes are `POST /api/v1/flow/share-consents/:stepId/accept`, `/decline` and `/withdraw`), `flowShareConsentAcceptBodySchema` (`text-sha256`, optional `biometric-text-sha256`, `explicit-biometric`), `flowShareConsentDeclineBodySchema` and `flowShareConsentWithdrawBodySchema` (each `{}`); each answers 200 with `flowSessionResponseSchema` (the withdraw at the same position). The session's one new member is `withdrawable-share-consents` (`flowWithdrawableShareConsentSchema`: `step-id`, `side`, `recipient-name`; at most 20). Goldens: `contracts/fixtures/api/connect/flow-share-consent-{accept,decline,withdraw}.json` and `contracts/fixtures/api/flow-session-share-consent.json` (directly in `api/`, decoded by the SDK's `ContractFixturesTest`).
42. **(K) `meta.share-token-id`** is an optional `cnst_` member of `createInquiryMetaSchema` (`packages/contracts/src/inquiries.ts`); golden `contracts/fixtures/api/connect/inquiry-create-share-token.json`. It is in `meta` only, never an attribute.
43. **(K) Publish codes.** `CONNECT_PUBLISH_RULE_CODES` holds all seven of C238.2's hook (`cloned_results_without_binding`, `share_consent_connection_invalid`, `share_consent_after_completion`, `share_consent_after_cloned_step`, `share_consent_connect_disabled`, `share_consent_withdrawal_contact_missing`, `share_consent_retention_missing`) and `CONNECT_PUBLISH_WARNING_CODES` the warning `share_consent_connection_not_active`. Golden: `contracts/fixtures/problems/connect-publish/definition-invalid-share-consent.json`.
44. **(K) Copy keys.** Eight `share.consent.*` keys in `COPY_KEYS`, each `legal: 'consent'` and `consent: false` (so `copy-keys.test.ts`'s rule, `consent: true` for `consent.*` keys only, is unchanged, and Phase 9's consent-step machinery never takes them for a consent step's texts): `share.consent.title` (`{recipient}`), `share.consent.source` (`{source}`, `{recipient}`, `{categories}`, `{expires}`, `{withdraw}`), `share.consent.destination` (the same and `{retention}`), `share.consent.biometric-source` (`{source}`, `{recipient}`), `share.consent.biometric-destination` (`{source}`, `{recipient}`, `{retention}`), `share.consent.accept`, `share.consent.decline`, `share.consent.withdraw` (`{recipient}`). The closed list is `SHARE_CONSENT_PLACEHOLDERS`. Their en source texts and the four drafted locales are `clients-connect`'s (no test asks a catalog text of every key). `COUNSEL_REVIEW_STATUS` (`packages/flow-spec/src/counsel-review-status.ts`, beside `locale-review-status.ts`) marks each `counsel-unreviewed`, with `counselUnreviewed(key)`.
45. **(K) Android.** `share_consent` is in `CLIENT_STEP_TYPES` (`StepRegistry.kt`), rendered by the `unsupported` renderer until `clients-connect` replaces that line with its renderer. `COPY_KEY_RULES` (`OperatorCopy.kt`) has the eight rules; `SERVER_COMPOSED_COPY_KEYS` and `CopyKeyPlacement.ServerComposed`: an operator's override of one of them is composed into the view by the server, so the SDK neither places nor refuses it (without this, any override would render the step unsupported). `i18n/key-map.json` has a `serverComposed` list (`{flowKey, legal, contract}` per key, schema changed with it), which `LegalKeyMapTest` reads as mapped and holds equal to `SERVER_COMPOSED_COPY_KEYS`; `generateLocaleStrings` reads only `entries`, so nothing is bundled or drafted (orchestrator decision 18). `flow-spec-members.json` classifies the eight members of the `share_consent` view as honoured (C238.7) and `withdrawable-share-consents` as honoured, `addedAfterSpec2: true`, not appearance-only (C237.6). The Android request index lists none of the share-consent requests yet (`f8b279306`): `clients-connect` adds the three entries, their MockEngine-written files and their `docs/android/privacy.md` rows in one commit, because `WireDataSafetyTableTest` holds a row to every listed route and refuses a row for a route the SDK does not send.
46. **(K) Tables keyed by step type.** Adding the type reached: the console builder's labels (`apps/dashboard/src/lib/copy/flow-builder.ts`, "Sharing consent") and step template (`flow-model.ts`: `{side: 'destination'}`), so the palette offers the step; the web flow's `stepPosition` (`apps/flow/src/flow/flow-position.ts`: the unsupported screen until `clients-connect`'s `steps/share-consent/` and its dispatch case); the API's v1 view (`apps/api/src/services/stored-flow.ts` now reads flow-spec's `V2_ONLY_STEP_TYPES`). Nothing else in the API switches on the type today.
47. **(U) Actions** (`packages/workflow-engine/src/actions/`): `RESERVED_CONNECT_ACTION_KEYS` (`create_share_token`, `redeem_share_token`, `expire_share_token`) and `ConnectActionKey` in `registry.ts`, in `ACTION_KEYS` (47 keys) and so in `actionKeySchema`; `connect.ts` freezes `CONNECT_ACTION_CONTRACTS` (`createShareTokenConfigSchema` with `connection-id`, `source-id`, `consent-record-id` as references and an optional `expires-in-seconds`; `redeemShareTokenConfigSchema`, the redeem body flattened with `destination-id` beside `meta` and the required `meta.destination-consent-record-id`, no mode; `expireShareTokenConfigSchema`; outputs carrying `share-token-id`, `status` and `expires-at`, `redeem-mode` or `expiry-reason`, every member `plain`) and `CONNECT_ACTION_ROWS` (scopes `connect-share-tokens:write`, `:redeem`, `:write`; the events each can cause). `workflow-connect` builds the entries and handlers from these in `apps/api/src/workflows/actions/connect/`, and its `entries.ts` (`createConnectActionEntries(): readonly AnyActionRegistryEntry[]`, a stub returning `[]` until then) is already spread into `WORKFLOW_ACTIONS` (`apps/api/src/workflows/definitions/registry.ts`), so no one edits either registry file again. While it is empty the three keys fail closed (`action_unknown` at publish). If its actions need sink rows for the taint pass, `workflow-connect` adds a `CONNECT_ACTION_SINK_ROWS` line beside `CASE_ACTION_SINK_ROWS` in the same file as a named borrowed edit.
48. **(U) Triggers.** No declaration: `connect/share-token.inbound-created`, `consent-record.withdrawn` and `connect/share-token.consent-withdrawn` are `trigger-event` configs over `EVENT_NAMES`, each triggerable, mapped by `eventSubject` to `connect/share-token`, `consent-record` and `connect/share-token`, and strict with nothing resolved in `eventFilterSubjects` (`apps/api/src/workflows/triggers/connect-triggers.test.ts`).
49. **(U) The pacing integration test.** `apps/api/src/providers/business-registry/pacing.integration.test.ts` failed once at the (C) checkpoint at load 13.68 (`expected 268.58 to be greater than or equal to 380`) and passed in every later run; 11A does not touch it. The pacer falls back to per-process turns when a limiter command fails (`acquireByLimiter`'s `catch { return false; }`; the limiter's `commandTimeout` is 500 ms), which the test's "a loaded machine cannot break a lower bound" does not cover. For 11B or 11F: make the test assert that no limiter command failed before it reads the elapsed time, or give its connections a command timeout no loaded run reaches.

## Workflow 11A Stage 1b amendments (foundation-11a)

Written by `foundation-11a`. Where a path, a name or a split below differs from the plan above or from the Stage 1a amendments, this section wins for the plumbing it names. Item 1 is the measured split every 11A agent uses from this commit on; the items after it are recorded at each of `foundation-11a`'s checkpoints (C, R, K, openapi).

1. **The validation split, measured (checkpoint split).** Every figure was taken once, in the main tree at `a089e66cf` (the end of Stage 1a, which is where Stage 1b starts; see the note below), one call at a time, in the foreground, each under the lock it needs, with `connect-logic` running beside it (its suites raise the load), on this host (12 cores, 31 GiB). Wall time, lock wait (0 s on every call) and the 1-minute load before and after:

   | Call | Wall | Load before / after | Result |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | PGlite migration (`createTestDatabase` twice per process, e2e lock; cold = empty `OPENVERIFY_TEST_DB_CACHE_DIR`) | cold 5,985 and 6,520 ms; warm 1,302 and 1,226 ms; second copy 1,039 to 2,331 ms | 0.19 to 0.82 | 0035 holds one column |
   | API unit, 8 blob shards (`--shard=k/8 --reporter=blob`, own `--coverage.reportsDirectory` each) | 150, 199, 186, 186, 168, 152, 175, 165 s | 0.71 to 11.35 | |
   | API merge (`--merge-reports`, the 80% gate) | 36 s | 9.00 / 6.33 | 9,728 tests in 730 files, 94.41% statements |
   | dashboard, whole (`turbo run test --force --filter=@openverify/dashboard`) | 458 s | 5.36 / 7.67 | 6,803 in 581 files, 95.59% |
   | dashboard, 2 blob shards + merge | 229, 231 s + 7 s | 6.80 to 8.18 | the same counts and coverage |
   | watchlist, e2e and db in one turbo call | 403 s | 6.11 / 2.96 | 820, 139 and 2,219 tests |
   | db alone / watchlist and e2e | 174 s / 279 s | 2.82 to 9.44 | |
   | the other 12 packages, then `@openverify/js` | 177 s, 5 s | 0.62 / 14.58 | |
   | forced typecheck | 142 s | 11.69 / 6.84 | |
   | forced lint: api / dashboard and contracts / the other 14 | 203 / 223 / 321 s | 1.23 to 6.84 | |
   | integration: db / graph / api 1-3 of 3 | 90 / 33 / 77, 157, 90 s | 1.65 to 15.33 | 83 / 13 / 99, 103, 107 |
   | Android (1) ui / flow / rest with `--rerun` | 151 / 45 / 98 s | 2.27 to 8.84 | rest 1,758, none skipped |
   | Android (2) screenshots with `--rerun` | 51 s | 2.09 / 2.10 | |
   | Android (3) to (8), (10), (11) | 10 to 25 s each | 1.94 to 4.26 | up to date: no Android source changed since `contracts-11a`'s last gate, so its cold-home figures (Stage 1a measurements: 58 to 198 s a call) stay the governing ones |
   | Android (9): the release checks' tests / the three static checks | 1 s / 13 s | 3.22 | 98 tests |
   | mocked flow suite (e2e lock) | 119 s | 2.70 / 12.52 | 332 passed |
   | e2e: stack down and the turbo build / `e2e:stack:up` | 30 s / 102 s | 5.22 to 11.59 | |
   | e2e: chromium shards 1-4 of 4 | 279 / 339 / 289 / 347 s | 1.39 to 5.22 | 48 / 48 / 49 / 47 |
   | e2e: console sweep (`--no-deps`, used stack) / `stackTest` / down | 301 / 45 / 2 s | 2.06 to 3.32 | 104 / 2 of 2 classes |
   | vision: ruff, format and mypy / pytest half 1 (the first 54 test files by name) / half 2 (the other 54) | 1 / 186 / 394 s | 0.81 to 1.55 | 1,260 / 1,379 (2,639) |
   | `infra/tests/*.test.sh`, one call each | 0 to 92 s | 2.48 | 10 of 11 exit 0; `flow-headers.test.sh` fails one check because the `openverify/apps:dev` image is not built on this host since the WSL restart (an environment fact, not a defect) |

   **The splits** (each call stays under about half of the 10-minute cap, leaving room for the 150 s heavy-slot wait and the lock waits):
   - the API unit suite: K = 8 for an agent alone, K = 12 beside others (rule 8, unchanged), then the merge, whose exit code is the gate;
   - the dashboard: always **two blob shards and a merge** (the whole run took 458 s at load 5 to 8, 76% of the cap before any wait): `cd apps/dashboard && pnpm exec vitest run --coverage --coverage.reportsDirectory=<dir>/coverage-<k> --shard=<k>/2 --reporter=blob --outputFile=<dir>/blobs/blob-<k>-2.json`, then `pnpm exec vitest run --merge-reports=<dir>/blobs --coverage --coverage.reportsDirectory=<dir>/coverage-merged`;
   - watchlist, e2e and db: **two calls**, `--filter=@openverify/db` alone and `--filter=@openverify/watchlist --filter=@openverify/e2e` (one call took 403 s);
   - the other 12 packages and `@openverify/js`, the forced typecheck, the three forced lint calls, the five integration calls and the Android gate's eleven calls: unchanged;
   - the e2e gate: unchanged (N = 4; the longest shard 347 s, under the 8-minute rebalance line);
   - the vision suite: the two halves by sorted file name stay, but the second half took 394 s at load 0.8 to 1.6, so an agent running it at a higher load splits it in two by file name again (three calls); the 80% gate is the whole run's, so a split run passes `--cov-fail-under=0` on every call but the last and `--cov-append` on every call after the first (not measured here: integration-11a measures it when it runs the vision rows).

   **Why `a089e66cf` and not the base `4db86122d`.** A worktree at the base would need its own `pnpm install`, its own Gradle project directory (a cold compilation), its own model and fixture fetches and an e2e stack built from it, and the base lacks the Stage 1a tests every suite now runs, so it would time a smaller suite than the one the splits must hold. `a089e66cf` is the tree every Stage 1b-3 agent starts from; it differs from the base by Stage 1a's contracts only (migration 0035 holds one column so far).
2. **(C) The tables as built** (`packages/db/src/tables/connect.ts`, migration `0035_phase11_connect.sql` regenerated by `drizzle-kit generate --name phase11_connect` with `organizations.connect_inbound` kept and its header restored). `connect_connections` (`cxn_`; unique index `connect_connections_source_destination_key` on the ordered pair, a self-connection allowed; checks `_status_valid`, `_creator_type_valid` (`user`, `api-key`), `_deactivated_by_valid`, `_deactivated` (time and side together), `_activated`; index `connect_connections_destination_idx`). `current_policy_id` and `accepted_policy_id` are nullable and reference the connection's own versions through `(id, policy id) -> connect_share_policies(connection_id, id)` (`connect_connections_current_policy_fk`, `_accepted_policy_fk`), because the two tables reference each other: `api-connect`'s create writes the connection, then version 1, then sets `current_policy_id`, in one transaction. `connect_share_policies` (`cxnp_`; unique `(connection_id, version)` and `(connection_id, id)`; `version >= 1`; no update by any repository, no trigger). `connect_share_tokens` (`cnst_`, `environment_id` the source's; `destination_environment_id` references `environments`; `policy_id` through `(connection_id, policy_id)`; `data_categories text[]` held to `SHARE_CATEGORIES` by `_data_categories_valid`; `consent_record_id` unique (`connect_share_tokens_consent_record_id_key`: one consent, one token, C232.1), `destination_consent_record_id` and `reserved_inquiry_id` unique where set; each final status with its time and reason (`_expired`, `_failed`, `_redeemed`); the source id's prefix held to its type; indexes `_outbound_idx (environment_id, created_at, id)`, `_inbound_idx (destination_environment_id, status, created_at, id)`, the partial `_expires_at_created_idx (expires_at) where status = 'created'`, `_connection_id_status_idx`, `_source_idx (environment_id, source_type, source_id)` for the redaction hook and the access export). The foreign keys of `consent_record_id` and `destination_consent_record_id` to `consent_records` arrive with that table at (K). The value lists live in the table file (the db package cannot import the contracts) and `apps/api/src/services/connect/value-lists.test.ts` holds them equal to the contracts'. The 0035 upgrade test seeds two tenants on 0034 and connects them after the upgrade.
3. **(C) Sealing.** `connect_connections.agreement_reference` is sealed for its row (`SEALED.connectAgreementReference`, AAD `connect_connections.agreement_reference#<cxn id>`), in `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` and `REWRAP_TABLES`; the expected set of `schema-v2.test.ts`, the table list of `testing.test.ts` and the rewrap seed of `key-rotation.test.ts` changed in the same commit. It holds no data subject's data (C241.5), so `redaction.test-support.ts` gains `CONNECT_AGREEMENT_TABLES = ['connect_connections']`, excluded from the subject-PII seed check like the other non-subject categories (found by the (C) gate).
4. **(C) The repositories and the cross-environment entry (C240.5, V04).** Own-side stubs on the package entry: `connect-connections.ts` (`createConnection`, `getConnectionOfOrganization`, `listConnectionsOfOrganization`, `lockConnectionForUpdate`, `updateConnection`), `connect-share-policies.ts` (`createSharePolicyVersion`, `getSharePolicyVersion`, `listSharePolicyVersions`), `connect-share-tokens.ts` (`createShareToken`, `getOutboundShareToken`, `listOutboundShareTokens`, `lockOutboundShareTokenForUpdate`, `updateOutboundShareToken`, `expireDueShareTokens`), with `ConnectRepositoryNotBuiltError` and `connectRepositoryNotBuilt(name, owner)` (`connect-not-built.ts`). Their twins `connect-connections.cross-env.ts` (`counterpartyEnvironments`, `destinationEnvironmentOfKind`), `connect-share-policies.cross-env.ts` (`getInboundTokenPolicy`) and `connect-share-tokens.cross-env.ts` (`getInboundShareToken`, `listInboundShareTokens`, `lockInboundShareTokenForUpdate`, `updateInboundShareToken`, `expireCreatedTokensOfConnection`, `listSharesTouching`) are never on the entry: they are served only by a new package entry, `@openverify/db/cross-env` (`packages/db/src/cross-env.ts`, as the namespaces `connectConnectionsCrossEnv`, `connectSharePoliciesCrossEnv`, `connectShareTokensCrossEnv`), which needed one `exports` entry in `packages/db/package.json` (no dependency, no lockfile change; a named borrowed edit of a shared file: the boundary cannot be an import rule while the twins are on the entry every module imports). Every stub rejects `... is not built yet (api-connect)`; `api-connect` owns these files and may change their signatures.
5. **(C) The import rule** is in `phase5Config()` (`packages/config/eslint/phase5-rules.js`: `CONNECT_CROSS_TENANT_MODULE`, `CONNECT_CROSS_TENANT_IMPORT_PATTERNS`), which the root config already spreads, not in the root `eslint.config.js` (this amends Stage 1a amendment 6, by the orchestrator decision of 2026-11-09 and the config-protection hook). It refuses `@openverify/db/cross-env`, every `*.cross-env` module and `services/connect/{counterparty-events,audit}` (and their sibling names inside `services/connect/`) in every file but `apps/api/src/services/connect/cross-tenant.ts` and `packages/db/src/cross-env.ts`; `packages/config/src/eslint-connect.test.ts` pins it, and `apps/api/src/services/connect/cross-tenant.source.test.ts` holds the tree (every source file of `apps/` and `packages/`, test files aside) and that `cross-tenant.ts` imports exactly the three guarded modules.
6. **(C) `cross-tenant.ts`** (`api-connect`'s) declares `CrossTenant` and `crossTenant` with the entry points `getInboundShareToken`, `listInboundShareTokens`, `readPeekSource`, `readRedemptionSource`, `stageCounterpartyEvents`, `appendCounterpartyAudit`, `expireTokensOnDeactivation`, `expireTokenOnWithdrawal` and `listSharesTouching(db, environmentId, objectIds)`, each a stub that rejects with `notBuilt`; the inbound reads are cross-environment too (a token row lives in the source environment). The counterparty entry points are `services/connect/counterparty-events.ts` (`stageCounterpartyEvents(tx, events)`, `CounterpartyEvent`) and `services/connect/audit.ts` (`appendCounterpartyAuditEntries(tx, entries)`, `CounterpartyActor` `{organization-id, kind}`), both `api-connect`'s stubs; `audit.ts` holds only the counterparty entry point (an own-side Connect entry goes through the ordinary audit writer), because the rule refuses the whole module outside `cross-tenant.ts`. The shared event and audit writers are unchanged.
7. **(C) The frozen routes.** `routes/server/connect-connections.ts` (`connectConnectionRoutes`), `connect-share-tokens.ts` (`connectShareTokenRoutes`), `connect-redeem.ts` (`connectRedeemRoutes`), registered in `routes/server/index.ts`, and `routes/dashboard/connect.ts` (`connectDashboardRoutes`, inside the dashboard's authenticated scope, `DashboardRoutesOptions.connect`), each answering 501 (`notBuilt`) with 501 among its documented problems until its owner builds it. The switch is `deps.connect` (`ApiDependencies.connect?: ConnectInstallSettings`, wired from `config.connect` by the container for the server and the dashboard routes); each plugin adds `connectSwitchHook(settings)` (`services/connect/switch.ts`, with `connectEnabled`) as its first `onRequest` hook, so while Connect is off an authenticated caller with the scope gets the one 404 (`connectNotFound()`, `services/connect/not-found.ts`, the body of `CONNECT_NOT_FOUND_PROBLEM`); a caller without the route's scope still gets the auth plugin's 403, which runs first. `ConnectProblemError(code, detail, {errors?, reason?})` (`services/connect/problems.ts`) takes its status from `CONNECT_PROBLEM_STATUSES`. The create and policy-change routes set `attachValidation` and call `refuseInvalidSharePolicyBody(request, schema)` (`services/connect/share-policy-validation.ts`, with `jsonPointerOf`), which re-parses the body to rebuild RFC 6901 pointers (the type provider's `instancePath` does not escape `/`). The retrieve route's static scope is `connect-share-tokens:read`: `connect-share-tokens:peek` and a user's `pii:view` are `api-connect`'s checks in its service.
8. **(C) The rate policies.** `enforceConnectPolicy(rateLimiter, policy, principal)` and `connectRateLimitKey(principal)` (`services/connect/rate-limits.ts`): the key is `<API key id or user session id>:<environment id>`; `connect-redeem` fails open (C12), `connect-peek` fails closed with 503 `connect_peek_unavailable`. Wired on the redeem route and on the retrieve route with `peek-source-data=true` only. `GET /api/v1/rate-limits` still lists only the `api` limit: listing the Connect buckets (C240.3's last sentence) is open for `api-connect` or `integration-11a`.

Its pass `runConnectTokenExpiry(deps)` (`jobs/connect-token-expiry.ts`, `api-connect`'s) rejects with `notBuilt('connect-token-expiry')`; the worker strips failure data, so a pass fails as `NotImplementedError`.

The openapi checkpoint is the last such commit.

Phase 8: `verificationCountsInWorkflows(db, verification)` in `fetch_object`'s verification row (`workflows/actions/objects/table.ts`): an uncounted clone answers `object_not_found`. For Workflow 11D (integration-11a carries it): 11A added a migration and changed the rollup SQL, so `analytics:benchmark` needs `--teardown` and `--seed` before `--measure`.

## Workflow 11A Stage 2 amendments

Recorded by integration-11a from the Stage 2 report (2026-11-13). Binding where they differ from the contracts above.

### connect-logic

1. **Structural inputs.** `packages/core/src/connect/` cannot import `@openverify/contracts` or `@openverify/flow-spec`. It mirrors three name lists (`SHARE_TEXT_PLACEHOLDERS`, `SHARE_CONSENT_TEXT_KEYS`, `CONNECT_PUBLISH_ERROR_CODES` with `CONNECT_PUBLISH_WARNINGS`). Their cross-checks against `SHARE_CONSENT_PLACEHOLDERS`, `COPY_KEYS` and `CONNECT_PUBLISH_RULE_CODES` are carried to 11F's fix-server as one test in `apps/api/src/services/connect/` (api-consent's cross-check test was not written).
2. **The digest** of a composed share-consent text is `consentTextDigest(body, {}, false)`, the SHA-256 of NFC(body); it never re-fills a template (one-pass placeholders, C238.3).
3. **The publish hook takes the given acknowledgements** (C235.8), passed at publish only. A draft's validation shows `cloned_results_without_binding` until it is acknowledged. Built by api-consent's named borrowed edit to `flow-v2.ts` and `inquiry-templates.ts`.
4. **Orders fixed in code** (each pinned by tests):
   - the source and destination consent-coverage orders;
   - the category assignment;
   - "shareable" = listed, decided, not a copy, not from a rejected handoff;
   - the peek shapes (a selfie's own attributes and file-typed fields are a presence at most; checks are one attribute; `related-objects` the first 50);
   - hydrate: mappings first, exact types;
   - cloned results: a stale or withdrawn failure is ignored;
   - the publish dominance readings.
5. **No `source-decided-at` in the clone provenance.** `clonedResultVerdict` reads the clone's own `completed_at` (copied from the source decision), else `source-created-at`. A `source-decided-at` member is carried to 11F (contracts with fix-server).
6. **The share-consent client view serves only `text.body` and `biometric.body`.** The title and the accept, decline and withdraw labels come from each client's catalog. An operator's override of those four labels is therefore not shown on Android (it is on the web). The decision is carried to 11F (the client view's label members, or a documented limit, in 11E's docs).
7. **ICU.** `{expires}` is a duration from `Intl.NumberFormat`. A Node upgrade between the serve and the accept could change the text and answer `consent_text_mismatch`; recorded for 11E's self-hosting notes.

## Workflow 11A Stage 3 amendments

Recorded by integration-11a from the six Stage 3 reports (2026-11-13). Each subsection is that agent's contract deviations, as settled; "settled by integration-11a" names the Stage 4 commit.

### api-connect

1. Routes and services as built: the handoff in its report and `docs/tdd/phase11-api-connect.tdd.md`.
   - `ConnectActing.principal` is optional, so that a workflow run can create and expire tokens.
   - `cross-tenant.ts` gained eight read entry points; `RedemptionSource` and `listSharesTouching` take the shapes the report gives.
2. **Choices the plan left open.**
   - An activation of a connection that is not `pending`, or a reactivation of one that is not `inactive`, answers 409 `invalid-transition`.
   - A user without `environments:production` gets 403 on an organisation-level write.
   - A source-only action asked by the destination answers the one 404.
   - A non-shareable id prefix answers the one 404; `rep_` answers 422 `share_source_not_supported`.
   - An `expires-in-seconds` over the environment's maximum gets a generic 422 with a pointer.
   - A self-connection's `deactivated-by` is `source`.
   - The source sees the destination's name from the first activation on.
3. **Audit across sides.**
   - A counterparty's entry has no principal and is served as `system`, with `{counterparty: {organization-id, kind}}`.
   - Counterparty events are staged with an empty cause.
4. **Settled by integration-11a:**
   - The activation receives the server API's ports (`31d53f8ae`).
5. **Carried:**
   - `GET /api/v1/rate-limits` does not list `connect-redeem` and `connect-peek` (C240.3). 11B: platform limits and quotas.
   - The earlier share-policy versions are not served (no route), so the console's diff between versions is built but not shown. 11F, fix-server.
6. **TDD:** the peek's RED followed its GREEN, and the matrix and lock tests had no RED (recorded in the agent's TDD file).

### api-redeem

1. **Settled by integration-11a** (`a69c6dc01`, `20ac91d65`, `842dfad43`, `ad8743a63`, `d26cb8b67`):
   - `crossTenant.markInboundShareToken`;
   - `readRedemptionSource` carrying `policyVersion` and `material`, mapped to `RedemptionRead` in `redeem.ts`;
   - the worker passing `events` and the workflow services as `api` to `processConnectRedeem`.
   `InboundTokenChanges.updatedAt` is required.
2. **The redemption request** (mode, template ids, account linking, mapping keys) is stored in the destination's audit entry `connect-share-token.redeem-requested` and read back by the job. A `redeem_request` column is a later migration.
3. **Job lock order.** The destination record is locked FOR SHARE, then the cross-tenant read locks the connection, then the token. No cycle exists with deactivation or withdrawal.
4. **Carried:**
   - C241.1's token lock inside the redaction hook (api-consent's file). 11B, data family.
   - `clonedInquiryReview` for a live inquiry that `fill-inquiry` filled with a fresh, listed, failed clone. 11F, fix-server.
   - The extracted data of database, email, phone and selfie clones is not copied, and `auto-create-account-type-id` is checked but the default type is used. 11F, fix-server.
   - The verification and check resources serve no `provenance` or `cloned` (C235.2). 11F, contracts with fix-server; the console's mount lines are in console-connect's subsection.
   - No recovery sweep for `pending` tokens whose job was lost. 11B, platform operations.
5. **KP10-54 (meanwhile).** A rolled-back copy deletes the objects it put (`process.ts`), so no object is left without a row on that path.

## Workflow 11A Stage 4 amendments (integration-11a)

Recorded 2026-11-13. Evidence: `docs/tdd/phase11-integration-11a.tdd.md`.

1. **Defects found by the Stage 4 tests, fixed test-first:**
   - The dashboard inquiry read dropped a clone's `provenance` (no Cloned banner): `routes/dashboard/resources.ts`.
   - A verification step refused its live attempt beside a cloned pass that does not count, at create and at submit ("already passed"), which broke H01: `services/flow.ts` now counts only `countedVerifications`.
   - The activation ran on the system clock and an outbox-only publisher.
   - The inquiry create did not ask for `connect-share-tokens:redeem` with `meta.share-token-id`.
2. **The cross-tenant probe's path and reach.**
   - It is `apps/api/src/security/cross-tenant-probe.integration.test.ts`: the unit suite leaves `*.integration.test.ts` out, and only that suffix runs in the Testcontainers project.
   - It walks every server-API operation with an id parameter (253). It probes the 75 whose families 11A seeds: `inq`, `itmpl`, `itmplv`, `acc`, `ver`, `iqse`, `key`, `wbh`, `evt`, `cxn`, `cnst` and `cnsr`.
   - The answer must be 404, or the same validation 400 a made-up id gets, with `instance`, `requestId` and the named id left aside.
   - "Identical" is read per operation: B on A's id answers exactly what B on a made-up id answers, so no answer is an oracle. Bodies differ between families by their `detail`.
   - Every Connect path answers the one generic Connect 404.
   - The 55 unseeded families, the two name parameters, and the 22 dashboard and 17 flow operations (sessions, not keys) are listed in the file for 11B, which owns it next. Any other family fails the probe.
   - It found no oracle.
3. **The 11A full-stack spec covers part of C287.3** (`e2e/tests/connect.spec.ts`, 21 s on the gate's stack):
   - the connection, with its activation in the console with a TOTP step-up (and a deactivation and reactivation on a used stack);
   - consent in the source's flow;
   - the token, peek, a redemption by API into a `needs_review` clone with its Cloned banner and table;
   - an attested consent withdrawn, its token expired on both sides;
   - every webhook on both sides verified with `@openverify/node`.

   The client-side redemption is driven by the SDK (amendment 4), with accept-cloned-results off: every step runs live beside the clone, H01's first half. **Not driven, carried to 11F:** C235.8's template that accepts cloned results and skips its government-ID step while the live selfie is matched against the cloned portrait. That needs C238.4's waiting position (Stage 3 api-consent 3) and a biometric policy. The verification-level Cloned marker (H01's second half) needs the contract of Stage 3 console-connect 4.
4. **stackTest's third class** is `AndroidConnectFlowStackTest`. It uses the fixture `e2e/fixtures/android-11a/flow.json` (builder `e2e/src/android-connect-flow.ts` with its assert-only test) and the system property `openverify.e2e.androidConnectFlow` (a named borrowed edit of `android/openverify-sdk/build.gradle.kts`).
   - It makes or reuses the partner's connection, activating it through the dashboard API after a TOTP sign-in when needed.
   - It drives the source flow and the destination flow with the SDK's own client, and sees the token redeemed in `fill-inquiry` mode with a live and a cloned government ID on the inquiry.
   - Its consent steps show `notice.play-integrity` beside `notice.session-integrity`. 10A's and 10C's classes and fixtures are unchanged.
5. **The e2e stack** seeds `seed:dev --connect` and records `connect {partnerOrganizationId, partnerEnvironments, seededConnectionId, partnerAdmin}` in its record (mode 0600). The console sweep reaches all four Connect pages (108 tests). The share-token page is reached from tokens the 11A spec makes, which the sweep runs after, on the used stack (as it requires).
6. **`@openverify/node`'s connect group** is three flat groups: `connectConnections`, `connectShareTokens` (`peek`, `redeem(id, meta, destinationId?)`) and `connectConsentRecords` (`attest`, `withdraw(id, attributes?)`). It adds `isConnectEventName` and the types `Connection`, `ShareToken`, `ConsentRecord` and `ConnectEventName`. The activation is console-only and has no client method.
7. **The contract changes,** each with a regenerated OpenAPI snapshot:
   - the share token's `creator` gains `workflow-run`;
   - every built Connect route, the three share-consent exits and the inquiry create stop documenting 501.
8. **Floors (KP11-3).** The SDK host-test floor is 2,732 (532 + 427 + 1,773 = 2,691 + 5 + 36) in `ci.yml` and `ci-workflow.test.sh`, now 411 checks. The demo's, the optional modules' and the device minimums are unchanged: 11A added no device test.
