## Workflow 11B Stage 1a amendments (contracts-11b)

Written by `contracts-11b` from `docs/research/phase11-inventory.md` section 2 (read at `cdff9dac8`, the 11B base). Where a path, a name or a default below differs from the plan above (C245-C255, the Workflow 11B bullets), this section and the inventory win. Items 1 to 30 are the drifts the inventory found and the defaults taken in their place; the items after them are recorded at each family's checkpoint (common, A, P, D).

1. **The base, from the records** (orchestrator decision 2 before 11B): `cdff9dac8` and `9e1cb5e9e` are ancestors of `HEAD`; `cdff9dac8:docs/PROGRESS.md:20` is "## 2026-11-13: Phase 11, Workflow 11A done (...)", "done locally; CI pending"; `origin/main` holds `cdff9dac8` (recorded, not required). `gh run list --branch main --limit 5`: five completed runs, the three newest (43408690288 on the 11A push, 43137166420, 43018061783) with no step started in any job (the spending limit), so **no completed run has a failed job to fix first**. No "Owner decisions after Workflow 11A" exist; Q10 stays unanswered, so C254.1's switch is built locked and stays so.
2. **Counts at the base.** 75 `API_SCOPES` and 10 `OPERATOR_PERMISSIONS`; 91 rows in `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS`; 40 rate policies; `config.ts` reads 80 `OPENVERIFY_*` names, `apps/api/src` names 177, the dashboard 13, the flow app 9, vision 3. 11B adds one scope (76), one policy (41) and, by item 25, fifteen variables before their `_FILE` forms.
3. **Ending sessions means revoking them (C245.3).** Phase 9 ends a session by setting `revoked_at` (`revokeUserSessionsForUser`, `packages/db/src/repositories/users.ts:363`); a revoked session is refused at once and its row is deleted later by the C16 pass, with its audit entries kept. C245.3's "deleted" reads "revoked as Phase 9 does".
4. **Which C245.3 events exist (inventory 2.4).** Built by Phase 9: deactivation (`services/users.ts:574`), the console MFA reset (`:1018`), `user:reset-mfa` (`services/mfa.ts:332`), every factor enrolment revoking the user's other sessions (`routes/dashboard/mfa-factors.ts:92`, which covers "the first factor of an organisation that requires MFA"), and a user's own "end my other sessions" (`POST /dashboard/sessions/revoke-others`, `routes/dashboard/sessions.ts:265`), which 11B reuses and never duplicates. Not built: an admin ending another user's sessions (C245.3's `POST /api/v1/dashboard/users/:id/end-sessions`, item 37) and a role change. "Loses its last role" is unreachable as written: every role write keeps at least one role (`access.ts:45-55`, `users.ts:80-84`) and a role in use cannot be deleted (`services/roles.ts:357-359`); its reachable form is **a role change after which the user holds no role in the environment a session works in** (its permissions there are empty, `session-context.ts:86-91`), and auth-sessions revokes those sessions in the role change's transaction, with an audit entry. "Changes or resets its password" is **not applicable** while no route changes or resets a password (a password is set only by accepting an invitation); auth-sessions adds a test that no such route exists, so the line fails the day one is added without the revocation.
5. **`last_seen_at` and the once-a-minute write (C245.1).** The sliding write exists: `touchUserSession` moves `user_sessions.updated_at` at most once a minute (`packages/db/src/repositories/users.ts:518-534`, `SESSION_LAST_SEEN_RESOLUTION_SECONDS`), called from the dashboard's `authenticate` (`operator-auth.ts:583-594`) and not from the server API's resolver (`principal-resolver.ts:17-25`). foundation-11b adds `user_sessions.last_seen_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()` (backfilled from `updated_at`) and points the touch at it; both resolution paths go through `findUserSessionContext` (`session-context.ts:44`), where foundation-11b places the one hook to `operator-auth/session-policy.ts` (auth-sessions), before any permission is read. `GET /dashboard/sessions` serves `last-seen-at` from the new column. The idle and absolute limits and the touch take the injected `Clock`, never the database's `now()` (the `updated_at` trigger's).
6. **The poll flag (C245.2).** A route that a page calls in the background declares the Fastify `config` member `activity: false`, as `allowWithoutMfa: true` is declared (`routes/dashboard/sessions.ts:209`); such a request is authenticated and checked against both limits but does not move `last_seen_at`. The one background poll the console makes is the rendition retry (`apps/dashboard/src/components/rendition-image.tsx:105`, `:127`), so its API route (the file rendition read, `apps/api/src/routes/dashboard/file-renditions.ts`) is the one marked; auth-sessions marks it as a named borrowed edit.
7. **The login limiter's interface and Lua contract (C246.1)**, frozen in `packages/contracts/src/console-auth.ts` (item 33): the `LoginRateLimiter` interface keeps `attempt({ip, email})`, `reset({email})` and `size()` (`size()` answers 0 for the Valkey limiter, which tracks no key in process). The step-up (`operator-auth.ts:531`) uses the same limiter, so it also fails closed with 503 `login_unavailable` while Valkey is unreachable; MFA codes are bounded by their challenge's own attempts (C15) and do not call `attempt`.
8. **The creator's-scope bound is built (C247.3).** `ensureCanGrant` (`apps/api/src/services/api-keys.ts:120-130`) bounds every route that mints a key: create (`:247`), clone (`:435`) and Phase 9's rotate (`services/api-keys/rotate.ts:61`); 11A added none. A user without `environments:production` holds no production session (`session-context.ts:88-90`), so it cannot mint a production key. Default kept: the answer stays 403 `forbidden` naming the missing scopes; **no `scope_exceeds_creator` code** is added, so no client or console word changes. auth-sessions' work under C247.3 is the tests over the three routes.
9. **The pepper's identity (C247.1).** A stored HMAC does not say which pepper made it, so `api-keys:pepper-status` cannot count "keys still under a previous pepper" from the hash. foundation-11b's column is **`api_keys.pepper_key_id text`** (amending the bullet's `pepper_generation`): a non-secret identifier of the pepper, the first 16 hex characters of HMAC-SHA-256(pepper, `openverify/api-key-pepper-id/v1`), `NULL` for a key made before 11B (counted as under the current pepper only once a lookup proves it). A lookup tries the current pepper, then each previous one; a hit under a previous pepper re-hashes in the same request with a compare-and-set on `(secret_hash, pepper_key_id)`.
10. **Organisations have no slug (C248.4, C248.8).** foundation-11b adds `organizations.slug text` (nullable, unique, `^[a-z0-9](?:[a-z0-9-]{1,61}[a-z0-9])$`, the console's words "sign-in name"), with `createTestTenant`'s care (Workflow 11A Stage 1a amendment 30). It is the organisation-level setting `organization-slug` (`SETTING_LEVEL`, `settings:write` through an organisation-wide assignment, audited; 409 `organization_slug_taken` when another organisation holds it), served `null` until set. Without a slug an organisation has no SSO start (the start answers 404 `sso_not_configured`, the same body as an unknown slug), and a provider cannot be saved (a 422 pointer at the provider body's root naming the missing slug). `org:create` gains an optional `--slug` (item 46).
11. **The SSO browser binding lives in Postgres (C248.4).** Rule 11 forbids a state, nonce or PKCE verifier in a Valkey value, so the single-use record is a row of `sso_login_states` (foundation-11b): `binding_sha256` (primary key, the SHA-256 of the cookie value), `organization_id`, `provider_id`, `purpose` (`sign-in` or `step-up`), `session_id` (a step-up's `uss_`, else `NULL`), `state_sha256`, `nonce` and `code_verifier` sealed for the row (`ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` and `REWRAP_TABLES`, with `ID_EXPRESSIONS` if needed, the three tests in the same commit), `return_to`, `created_at`, `expires_at` (10 minutes), `consumed_at`; consumed with one `UPDATE ... WHERE consumed_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now RETURNING`, deleted by the C16 pass a day after expiry. It holds no data subject's data: its table joins `OPERATOR_PII_TABLES`' neighbour category in `redaction.test-support.ts` (an operator-auth category foundation-11b names).
12. **The probe's families (C240.7).** 11B's id families are `aexp` (account exports), `ssop` (SSO providers, item 36) and the export file id, which is a platform file id (`file_` or `txnf_`, item 48), so the probe meets `name:fileId` unless its schema names one prefix; `POST /dashboard/users/:id/end-sessions` takes a `usr_` id, a family the probe already carries. foundation-11b adds `aexp`, `ssop` and the file id's family to `CARRIED` in the commits that put those routes in the snapshot (orchestrator decision 13).
13. **The pattern inventory (C252.1)** is inventory 2.8: one entry point outside C148.3's matcher (a flow-spec 1 text field's `validation.pattern`, `packages/flow-spec/src/flow.ts:69-77`, compiled natively and never matched), one operator-input `RegExp` in the console (`apps/dashboard/src/components/workflows/editor/document-model.ts:441`), ajv's code generation from a transaction `json` field schema (no regex; keyword allowlist), and no pattern in workflow expressions, webhook filters, CSV columns or list items. ingestion-11b owns the two changes and the catastrophic-pattern tests at the flow-spec 1 entry and at the matcher's existing entries. C252.2's selectors join `phase5Config()`'s shared `no-restricted-syntax` in `packages/config/eslint/phase5-rules.js` (or a file it imports), repeating the PII log-key selectors where both apply (orchestrator decision 21); the root `eslint.config.js` is not edited (the review F28 line becomes this).
14. **Maintenance statements (C249.3)** are inventory 2.9: `ANALYZE` at `watchlist-key-df.ts:30` and `watchlists.ts:537-540`, `gin_clean_pending_list` at `watchlist-key-df.ts:60-61`; no `VACUUM`, `REFRESH`, `TRUNCATE`, `LOCK` or `REINDEX` exists. `db:grant-runtime` grants `MAINTAIN` on exactly `watchlist_entries`, `watchlist_names`, `watchlist_identifiers` and `watchlist_crypto_addresses`; the GIN cleanup moves into `openverify_gin_clean_pending_lists(source_id)`. The GUCs `openverify.transaction_redaction`, `report_redaction`, `retention` and `kek_rewrap`, `SET LOCAL statement_timeout`, `jit` and `hnsw.*` need no ownership; the graph query's per-statement timeout must not be undercut by a role-level `statement_timeout`. `analyze graph_attributes` runs only in the benchmark's builder.
15. **Commands and roles (C249.3; orchestrator decision 9):** the migration role runs `db:migrate`, `seed:dev`, `kek:rewrap` and `db:grant-runtime`; the runtime role runs `trust-anchors:sync` and `trust:sandbox-utopia` (row writes only), `blind-index:rebuild`, `face-index:rebuild`, `graph:reindex`, `watchlists:sync`, `watchlists:rekey`, `pep:sync`, `network:sync`, `retention:run`, `user:reset-mfa`, `org:create`, `api-keys:pepper-status` and `user:allow-password`; the syncs and the re-key rely on the `MAINTAIN` grants of item 14. The benchmarks (`graph:benchmark` with R008, `face-scan:benchmark`, `analytics:benchmark`) are development tools, never pointed at a production database.
16. **C251's queues (inventory 2.6).** The rule: a queue whose job holds one environment's work joins the caps unless that environment's own rate policy bounds what it can hold in flight. **Capped:** `process-verification`, `report-run`, `connect-redeem`, `account-export`, `workflow-step` (Phase 8's, under `workflow-in-flight-cap`), `list-screen`, `list-import`, `list-item-embed`, `graph-index`, `graph-query`, `inquiry-signals` (its 500-a-tick sweep bounds the install, not an environment), `list-rescreen`, `integrity-decode` (with the outbox KP10-27 adds), `case-rendition`, `case-attachment-scan` and `case-bulk` (no rate policy bounds it). **Outside, with the reason:** `export`, `report-bulk`, `case-export` (their hour policies, at most 10, 5 and 10 an hour per environment; a bulk upload's report runs are capped as report runs); `workflow-step-batch`, `workflow-trigger`, `workflow-wait-match` (they feed the capped steps); `flow-signal-snapshot` (a branch waits at most 30 s for it, C150.5, so holding it would change the flow's path); `analytics-rebuild` (one per environment by job id, from the nightly pass); `webhook-delivery` (its own schedule and timeout); every repeatable pass and sweep. Admission rows: `admitted_at` is new on `verifications`, `report_runs`, `connect_share_tokens`, `account_exports`, `list_items`, `case_bulk_actions`, the attachment-scan and rendition rows and the integrity outbox; an existing `enqueued_at` that only the sweep or producer stamps is the admission stamp of `list_screenings`, `importers`, `graph_index_jobs`, `graph_queries`, `inquiry_signal_computations` and `list_rescreen_requests`. A held `list-import` keeps its importer `pending`.
17. **The caps setting and Phase 8's (C251.2).** `queue-in-flight-caps` is `{<queue>: 1 to 1,000}` over the capped queues except `workflow-step` (`QUEUE_IN_FLIGHT_CAP_QUEUES`, item 31); Phase 8's `workflow-in-flight-cap` (schema 1 to 10,000, unchanged so stored values parse) stays the `workflow-step` queue's entry. Both are refused at write above `OPENVERIFY_QUEUE_IN_FLIGHT_CAP_MAX` with C55.7's generic 422 and a pointer (no new code), and the effective cap is the lower of the stored value and the ceiling, so an old value above it is clamped, never refused on read. Phase 8's install variable `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_IN_FLIGHT_CAP` stays the workflow-step default and is clamped by the ceiling too. foundation-11b passes the ceiling into the settings service beside `otpSmsMaxPerHourCeiling` (`apps/api/src/services/settings.ts:136-160`, `:303-315`); quotas builds the rest.
18. **C254.2 reaches the watchlist sources only**: `network/sources.json`'s strict schema has no such member and 7A named no source (inventory 2.7).
19. **C254.1, frozen here, built by ingestion-11b.** The plan says `contracts-11b` "builds the switch and the acknowledgement"; this Stage freezes their contract (item 51) and ingestion-11b builds the behaviour. While `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_UN_OPT_IN_ALLOWED` is false (its default everywhere) every attempt is refused and `un-sc` stays exactly as Phase 6 left it.
20. **The export's scope (C253.1, C248.5).** Phase 9 ships `accounts:export` as the accounts list's CSV export (`permissions.ts:94`, `packages/core/src/exports.ts:43`). The access export's scope is a new one, **`accounts:access-export`**: an API scope (a key may hold it), environment-level, admin only by default, not a `:read` (so no viewer holds it), in `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS` as the rule's record, with its `PERMISSION_INFO` label. Wherever C248.5 and C253 say `accounts:export`, read `accounts:access-export`; Phase 9's scope keeps its meaning.
21. **One fail-closed path (C253.7; orchestrator decision 16).** The catch inside `enforceConnectPolicy` becomes one shared helper beside `enforceRoutePolicy` in `apps/api/src/plugins/rate-limit.ts` (item 34), called by `enforceConnectPolicy` for `connect-peek` (its answer, key and tests unchanged) and by dsar-export for `account-export`.
22. **C240.3's listing (KP11-11; orchestrator decisions 17, 26).** `GET /api/v1/rate-limits` answers the state its hook recorded for the request; a Connect bucket the request did not touch is read without consuming: the `RateLimiter` port gains `peek(key, rule): Promise<RateLimitDecision>` (one read-only Lua script over the GCRA key), and the route lists `connect-redeem` and `connect-peek` beside `api`, each with the caller's bucket for the environment it acts in (`connectRateLimitKey`), only while Connect is enabled (an install without it answers as today). When the `api` state is missing the route still answers 503; when only the peek read fails, the Connect entries are left out and the 200 stands. The resource schema does not change. `account-export` is not listed (as no other route policy is). integration-11b builds it.
23. **The export's Connect families (KP11-16; orchestrator decision 27).** The export holds the tokens on either side and only the exporting environment's own consent records (`listSharesTouching`, `cross-tenant.ts:197-201`), never the counterparty's (C240.4, review P26). Each redeemed share names its recipient as `{organization-id, organization-name}`, the name as the subject was shown it (the source-side consent record's `recipient-name`); a share received names its source from the copy's provenance (`source-organization-name`). No counterparty user, key or audit row is named.
24. **Phase 10 in the export (orchestrator decision 7).** The integrity verdict goes in `operator-review`; a challenge token is never exported; the two integrity C16 steps join C253.4's ordering.
25. **The variables 11B adds** (foundation-11b enters each in `config-registry.ts` and parses it in `config.ts`; every secret also reads `<NAME>_FILE`, C249.2):

    | `OPENVERIFY_CONSOLE_ABSOLUTE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 43,200; 3,600 to 86,400; at least the idle limit | no | api | C245.1's absolute limit (the former constant `USER_SESSION_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS`) |
    | `OPENVERIFY_API_KEY_PEPPER_PREVIOUS` | unset; at most two, comma-separated, each at least 32 bytes | yes | api, worker | C247.1 |

    | `OPENVERIFY_POSTGRES_CA_FILE` | unset; an absolute path | no | api, worker, migrate | C249.1 |
    | `OPENVERIFY_REDIS_CA_FILE` | unset; an absolute path | no | api, worker, migrate | C249.1 |

Setting a variable and its `_FILE` form is a start-up error; a world-readable file is a warning.
26. **Problem fixtures stay out of Android's reach (rule 17; orchestrator decision 5).** 11B's goldens go in new subfolders `contracts/fixtures/problems/{sessions,sso,account-exports,watchlists-opt-in}/` and the API goldens in `contracts/fixtures/api/{sso,account-exports}/`, none of which `ContractFixturesTest` reads (`Fixtures.list` reads only the top level of `contracts/fixtures/api/`; the problem folders it reads are the top level, `problems/flow/` and `problems/flow-engine/`), so 11B needs no `serverOnly` entry and changes nothing under `android/`.
27. **The dependency commit lands last (rule 29).** `openid-client`, `oauth4webapi` and `jose` change `pnpm-lock.yaml`, a root-toolchain file that keeps the full gate at any checkpoint it lands in; nothing uses them before sso-oidc (Stage 2, after this Stage), so their commit (with the licence rows) is the last commit before the (D) checkpoint, whose full gate is this Stage's final one. They remain the common family's deliverable.
28. **Existing tests changed by a contract change, minimally** (rule 12): the two tests that list every rate policy in order (`packages/contracts/src/rate-limits.test.ts:28`, `apps/api/src/phase9a-template-plumbing.test.ts:116`) gain `account-export`; the prefix tables' exact maps (`packages/contracts/src/ids.test.ts`, `packages/db/src/ids.test.ts`) gain `aexp` and `ssop`; the scope lists gain `accounts:access-export`; `apps/api/src/package-scripts.test.ts` gains the four commands. Each is listed in its commit and in the report's contractDeviations.
29. **Storage has no streaming read** (`FileStorage.get` answers the whole object, `apps/api/src/ports.ts`); C253.1's "streamed" reads "served from the live object, whole", within C10's object limits.
30. **The request log already omits query strings** (`serializeRequest`, `apps/api/src/logger.ts:68-73`, logs the route pattern), and the stored API log keeps only server-API requests with a principal (`plugins/api-logs.ts:1-9`), so the SSO callback is never stored there. sso-oidc's borrowed edit of the serializer is a log test proving it, and a change only where a log site still logs `request.url`.
31. **(common) The three environment settings** (`packages/contracts/src/dashboard-api.ts`): `queue-in-flight-caps` (`queueInFlightCapsSchema`, a partial record over `QUEUE_IN_FLIGHT_CAP_QUEUES` (`cappedQueueSchema`, `CappedQueue`: the fifteen capped queues of item 16 without `workflow-step`), each 1 to 1,000; `QUEUE_IN_FLIGHT_CAP = {min: 1, max: 1000, default: 50, ceilingDefault: 50}`), `account-export-ttl-days` (`ACCOUNT_EXPORT_TTL_DAYS` 1 to 30, 7) and `watchlist-full-check-hours` (`WATCHLIST_FULL_CHECK_HOURS` 1 to 168, 24); `HARDENING_SETTING_DEFAULTS`, `HardeningSettings`, `resolveHardeningSettings`; optional members of `dashboardSettingsSchema` and `dashboardUpdateSettingsBodySchema`, served with their defaults by `GET /api/v1/dashboard/settings` and stored on the environment by `PATCH` (audited in `settings.updated`), both in `apps/api/src/services/settings.ts`; `EnvironmentSettings` (`packages/db/src/tables/tenancy.ts`) holds the three keys. A `PATCH` of `queue-in-flight-caps` replaces the whole map. The console's `HardeningSetting` type keeps the three out of the retention form (`apps/dashboard/src/components/operators/settings-fields.ts`). The install ceiling's write check and clamped read are foundation-11b's hook beside `otpSmsMaxPerHourCeiling` (item 17).
32. **(common) Every 11B problem code** (`packages/contracts/src/problem.ts`), each with its golden in a folder Android does not read: `CONSOLE_SESSION_PROBLEM_STATUSES` `{session_expired: 401, login_unavailable: 503}` in `problems/sessions/` (`session-expired-idle.json`, `session-expired-absolute.json`, `login-unavailable.json`), with `SESSION_EXPIRED_REASONS` (`idle`, `absolute`), `sessionExpiredReasonSchema`, `sessionExpiredProblemSchema` (the reason required), and `problemSchema.reason` widened to take it; `SSO_PROBLEM_STATUSES` `{sso_not_configured: 404, sso_state_invalid: 400, sso_email_unverified: 403, sso_user_unknown: 403, sso_password_disabled: 403, organization_slug_taken: 409}` in `problems/sso/`; `ACCOUNT_EXPORT_PROBLEM_STATUSES` `{account_export_pending: 409, account_export_too_large: 422, account_export_item_redacted: 410, account_export_unavailable: 503}` in `problems/account-exports/`, with `ACCOUNT_EXPORT_LIMITS = {items: 5000}` and `accountExportTooLargeProblemSchema` (`item-count`, `item-limit`); `WATCHLIST_OPT_IN_PROBLEM_STATUSES` `{watchlist_source_locked: 409}` in `problems/watchlists-opt-in/`. Reused, not declared again: Phase 9's `step_up_required` (403) and `forbidden` (C247.3, item 8), and C55.7's generic 422 for a cap above the ceiling. `login_unavailable` carries `Retry-After` (`LOGIN_UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS`, 30) in the header, not the body; `sso_password_disabled` is answered only after the password was verified (otherwise the generic 401), so it names no email.
33. **(common) `packages/contracts/src/console-auth.ts`** (exported from the root): `CONSOLE_SESSION_TIMEOUTS` (`idleSeconds` 300 to 86,400, 1,800; `absoluteSeconds` 3,600 to 86,400, 43,200, the value of `USER_SESSION_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS`), `SESSION_LAST_SEEN_WRITE_SECONDS = 60`, `LOGIN_LIMITS = {windowSeconds: 900, perAddress: 50, perEmail: 10}`, `LOGIN_LIMIT_KEY_LABEL = 'openverify/login-limit/v1'`, `LOGIN_UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS = 30`, and `loginLimitKeys({addressHmac, emailHmac})`, which answers `['openverify:login-limit:v1:a:<hex>', 'openverify:login-limit:v1:e:<hex>']` and throws a `RangeError` on anything but a 64-character lowercase hex HMAC, so a raw address or email can never name a key. The Lua contract is its header: `KEYS[1]` the address key, `KEYS[2]` the email key, `ARGV` the two limits and the window in milliseconds; refused when either count has reached its limit (counted against neither key), the answer the milliseconds until the later blocking window ends; otherwise both incremented, a key's `PEXPIRE` set when its count becomes 1, the answer 0; `reset` deletes the email key. The HMACs are of `ip:<address>` and `email:<normalised email>` (`normalizeEmail`, `packages/db`) under the subkey derived from `OPENVERIFY_BLIND_INDEX_KEY` with that label, as the blind index derives its subkeys.
34. **(common) The fail-closed helper** (`apps/api/src/plugins/rate-limit.ts`): `enforceFailClosedPolicy(rateLimiter, policy: FailClosedRateLimitPolicy, callerKey: string, unavailable: () => Error): Promise<void>`, the 429 `RateLimitedError` past the limit (detail "Too many requests for the <policy> limit of N per W seconds. Retry after S seconds.", connect-peek's words before), `unavailable()` when the store does not answer; beside it `enforceKeyedPolicyFailOpen(rateLimiter, policy, callerKey)` for a keyed policy that fails open (`connect-redeem`), both over one private `enforceKeyedPolicy`. `enforceConnectPolicy` (`services/connect/rate-limits.ts`) now calls them and has no catch of its own; its key, answers and tests are unchanged (`apps/api/src/routes/connect-routes.test.ts`, 503 `connect_peek_unavailable`). `RATE_LIMIT_FAIL_CLOSED_POLICIES = ['connect-peek', 'account-export']` and `FailClosedRateLimitPolicy` are in `packages/contracts/src/rate-limits.ts`. dsar-export calls `enforceFailClosedPolicy(deps.rateLimiter, 'account-export', environmentId, () => <503 account_export_unavailable>)` first on the create route (C253.7).
35. **(common) The id, the scope and the policy.** `ID_PREFIXES.accountExport = 'aexp'` in both tables (`packages/contracts/src/ids.ts`, `packages/db/src/ids.ts`). The scope is **`account-access:export`** (amending item 20's default `accounts:access-export`, which breaks the scope grammar `<resource>:<action>` with a one-word action that `packages/core/src/permissions.test.ts` holds; the chosen name keeps Phase 9's `:export` action family, whose scopes are each admin's alone): the last of `API_SCOPES` (76), in `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS`, environment-level, held by `admin` only, labelled "Export everything held about one account, for an access request" in the `accounts` area of `PERMISSION_INFO`. Wherever C248.5, C253 or an earlier item says `accounts:export` or `accounts:access-export` for the access export, read `account-access:export`. The policy `account-export` is the last of the three maps: 10 per 3,600 s, one bucket per environment (the caller key is the environment id), labelled "Account access exports, per environment" on the console's rate panel; it is not listed by `GET /api/v1/rate-limits` (item 22).
36. **(A) SSO providers** (`packages/contracts/src/sso.ts`, exported from the root; C248.3). Id `ssop` (ours) in both prefix tables. Resource `sso-provider`: `display-name` (ours, 1 to 100), `issuer` (an `http(s)` URL; `http` refused in production by the server), `client-id`, `client-secret-set` (the secret itself is write-only and never served), `allowed-email-domains` (1 to 50 distinct lowercase domains, required), `tenant-pin` (`{claim: 'tid' | 'hd', value}` or `null`; `SSO_TENANT_PIN_CLAIMS`), `required-amr`, `required-acr` (each at most 10 values), `jit-provisioning`, **`default-role-ids`** (role ids, amending the bullet's `default_role_keys`: only built-in roles have keys, and a custom role must be usable as the just-in-time role; foundation-11b's column is `default_role_ids text[]`), `enforced`, `redirect-uri` (ours, read-only: `<OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_PUBLIC_URL>/sso/callback/<id>`, `null` without the variable), `created-at`, `updated-at`. Schemas: `ssoProviderAttributesSchema`, `ssoProviderResourceSchema`, `ssoProviderDocumentSchema`, `ssoProviderListDocumentSchema`, `createSsoProviderBodySchema` (the secret required; `tenant-pin`, the amr and acr lists, `jit-provisioning`, `default-role-ids` and `enforced` defaulting to `null`, `[]`, `[]`, false, `[]`, false), `updateSsoProviderBodySchema` (any member, a new secret included; never empty), `ssoProviderParamsSchema`. Routes (foundation-11b freezes them at 501; sso-oidc builds): `GET` and `POST /api/v1/dashboard/sso-providers`, `GET`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /api/v1/dashboard/sso-providers/:id`, under `settings:read` / `settings:write` through an organisation-wide assignment, each write behind Phase 9's step-up; audited as `ACCESS_11B_AUDIT_ACTIONS.ssoProvider{Created,Updated,Deleted}`. `SSO_ID_TOKEN_ALGORITHMS` = RS256, ES256, PS256, EdDSA. `JIT_FORBIDDEN_PERMISSIONS` (`settings:write`, `api-keys:read`, `api-keys:write`, `connect-connections:activate`, `connect-consent-records:attest`, `account-access:export`, `environments:production`) and `jitRoleRefusals(permissions)` (those and every organisation-level permission, in the order given), checked at save and at each just-in-time sign-in (C248.5). `API_LOG_REDACTED_KEYS` gains `client-secret` and `binding`.
37. **(A) The BFF calls of the SSO route handlers** (C248.4; review F21). The console's handlers are `apps/dashboard/src/app/(auth)/sso/[slug]/start/route.ts` and `apps/dashboard/src/app/(auth)/sso/callback/[provider]/route.ts`; the route group does not change the URLs (`/sso/<slug>/start`, `/sso/callback/<ssop_ id>`), which join the proxy's public paths as sso-oidc's named borrowed edit of `apps/dashboard/src/proxy.ts:18-23` (a `/sso/` prefix, the set otherwise exact). Each call carries the BFF token and the client address as the sign-in does (`createClientAddressResolver`):
    - `GET /api/v1/dashboard/sso/:organization-slug/start` (`ssoStartParamsSchema`; query `ssoStartQuerySchema`: optional `provider-id` and `return-to`, a console path with one leading `/`), public, answers 200 `ssoStartResponseSchema`: `{kind: 'redirect', authorization-url, binding, expires-at}` (`ssoRedirectSchema`) or, for an organisation with several providers and none named, `{kind: 'choose', providers: [{id, display-name}]}` (`ssoChooseSchema`, ours); 404 `sso_not_configured` for an unknown name, no provider or a provider of another organisation, one body. The handler sets the binding as the cookie `SSO_BINDING_COOKIE` (`__Host-openverify-sso`; `SSO_BINDING_COOKIE_INSECURE`, `openverify-sso`, under `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_INSECURE_COOKIES`), HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Lax (the identity provider's redirect is a top-level cross-site GET), path `/`, max-age `SSO_LOGIN_STATE_TTL_SECONDS` (600), then redirects the browser to the URL.
    - `GET /api/v1/dashboard/sso/callback/:provider-id` (`ssoCallbackParamsSchema`; query `ssoCallbackQuerySchema`, the provider's query passed unchanged: `state` always, `code` or `error`, optional `iss`, other members allowed), public, with the cookie's value in the header `SSO_BINDING_HEADER` (`x-openverify-sso-binding`), answers `ssoCallbackResponseSchema`: 201 a session (`dashboardSessionResponseSchema`, as the sign-in), 202 a second factor (`dashboardMfaChallengeResponseSchema`, C248.6), or 200 a step-up (`stepUpResponseSchema`); 400 `sso_state_invalid`, 403 `sso_email_unverified` or `sso_user_unknown`, 404 `sso_not_configured`. The handler clears the cookie on every answer, sets the session cookie on 201, goes to the second-factor page on 202 and back to the return path otherwise; the browser sees one generic message per failure.
    - The binding is `ovsb_` and 43 base64url characters (`ssoBindingSchema`, `SSO_BINDING_PREFIX`); the API stores only its SHA-256 (amendment 11).
38. **(A) The step-up of an SSO session** (C248.6; KP11-18): `POST /api/v1/dashboard/sessions/current/step-up/sso` (session, `allowWithoutMfa` as the passkey step-up is), body `ssoStepUpBodySchema` (`{}`), answers 200 `ssoRedirectSchema` for a re-authentication (`prompt=login`, `max_age=0`); the callback of a step-up record checks `auth_time` within `STEP_UP_WINDOW_SECONDS` and the provider's `required-amr`/`required-acr`, sets `mfa_verified_at` and answers 200 `stepUpResponseSchema`. sso-oidc proves it satisfies the Connect activation's step-up.
39. **(A) The sign-in name and the login hint** (C248.8; amendment 10): `organizationSlugSchema` (`packages/contracts/src/dashboard-api.ts`), the organisation setting `organization-slug` (nullable, optional in `dashboardSettingsSchema` and the update body, `null` in `DASHBOARD_SETTINGS_DEFAULTS`, `SETTING_LEVEL['organization-slug'] = 'organization'` in `packages/core/src/permissions.ts`), 409 `organization_slug_taken`. `GET /dashboard/settings` serves `null` (`apps/api/src/services/settings.ts`) until foundation-11b's `organizations.slug` exists and sso-oidc stores and serves it; until then a `PATCH` naming it changes nothing (foundation-11b adds the column, sso-oidc the write and the 409). The C4 login body `dashboardLoginBodySchema` takes an optional `organization` (a sign-in name), which narrows the accounts checked; an unknown name answers the generic 401 after the same dummy hash.
40. **(A) Ending one user's sessions** (C245.3): `POST /api/v1/dashboard/users/:id/end-sessions` (`users:write`, C168.1's superset rule, not on oneself: 409 as the MFA reset answers, the caller uses `revoke-others`), body `endUserSessionsBodySchema` (`{}`), answers 200 `endUserSessionsResponseSchema` `{sessions-ended}`; every live session of the user is revoked (amendment 3) and `user.sessions-ended` audited in every environment. foundation-11b freezes it at 501 and adds it to the snapshot; auth-sessions builds it.

## Workflow 11B Stage 1b amendments (foundation-11b)

Written by `foundation-11b` (Workflow 11B Stage 1b, beside the six Stage 2 agents). Each item is a drift from the plan or the Stage 1a amendments, recorded at the checkpoint that made it; `docs/tdd/phase11-foundation-11b.tdd.md` holds the evidence.

1. **(common) The configuration registry is generated, then described.** `apps/api/src/config-registry.ts` holds every variable a process reads (120 entries at the common checkpoint, from a scan of each process's sources: api, worker, migrate, dashboard, flow, vision, code-runner), with the pattern entries for names composed at run time (the provider bindings and secrets, the seed passwords). Its test counts a process's reads (`env.NAME`, a quoted name, a composed prefix), not mentions in comments or in words shown to a user: the console names `OPENVERIFY_QUEUE_IN_FLIGHT_CAP_MAX` in the queue panel's note without reading it (`afa1edec7`).
2. **(common) The 10-minute measurement.** The API unit suite in 12 blob shards and `pnpm test:integration` in its five calls each fit 10 minutes beside the six Stage 2 agents (longest 227 s at the common checkpoint, 295 s at (A), at load up to 25); no split changes. Three shards in one call do not fit (load 20 to 37), so each shard is its own call.
3. **(A) The SSO path parameters are camelCase.** Fastify's router reads `-` after a parameter as the start of a fixed part, so `:organization-slug` and `:provider-id` never match. The routes are `GET /api/v1/dashboard/sso/:organizationSlug/start` and `GET /api/v1/dashboard/sso/callback/:providerId` (`ssoStartParamsSchema.organizationSlug`, `ssoCallbackParamsSchema.providerId`, `packages/contracts/src/sso.ts`), as every path parameter of the API is (amending Stage 1a amendment 37's spelling; the URLs are unchanged).
4. **(A) The session policy hook** is `enforceSessionPolicy` (`apps/api/src/operator-auth/session-policy.ts`, auth-sessions'), called through `findUserSessionContext` for both resolvers before any permission is read, with the route's `config.activity` flag (`false` for the rendition retry route) and the configured limits; `endUserSessions` is called at both role writes (`PUT /dashboard/users/:id/roles`, `PATCH /dashboard/users/:id` with role ids). The frozen route plugins return `Promise.resolve()` (`62977eacf`: the GREEN's lint fix had dropped `async` without it, breaking the API typecheck for one hour).
5. **(D) `account_exports`** (`packages/db/src/tables/account-exports.ts`): `id` (`aexp_`), `environment_id`, `account_id` (an `acc_` id; no foreign key, accounts are only deleted with their environment), `creator_type` and `creator_id` (`user` or `api-key`), `status` (`pending`, `ready`, `failed`, `expired`), `admitted_at` (C251.1), **`item_ids text[]`** (ours: the ids of everything the manifest lists, under a GIN index, so C253.4's "deletes the exports that list it" is one overlap query), `item_count` (0 to 5,000), `byte_count`, `error_code` (`account_export_too_large` or `account_export_unavailable`), `storage_key` and `wrapped_dek` (the sealed manifest's object and key; `wrapped_dek` in `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` with the `file` AAD and the table in `REWRAP_TABLES`), `ready_at`, `expires_at`, `created_at`, `updated_at`; one pending export per account (`account_exports_one_pending_key`); a ready or expired export has its times and count, only a ready one its object, key and size, only a failed one its code. Its redaction category is `ACCOUNT_EXPORT_TABLES` (`apps/api/src/services/redaction.test-support.ts`).
6. **(D) The watchlist sources' columns** add, beside amendment 50's `last_validated_at`, `validated_by` (`http-304` or `sha256`) and `last_full_check_at`, the validators a conditional request sends: **`last_etag`** (at most 256 characters) and **`last_modified`** (at most 64), which `conditional-policy.ts`'s `ListValidators` needs and amendment 50 did not name.
7. **(D) The validator hook** is `checkSourceConditionally(context): Promise<{kind: 'download', headers} | {kind: 'not-modified'}>` (`apps/api/src/watchlists/conditional.ts`, ingestion-11b's), called in `syncWatchlistSource` under the source's session lock before a publisher's file is downloaded (never for an import or the live SPARQL source). A `not-modified` records the check as `unchanged` through the same path as an equal digest (`recordUnchanged`, extracted in `sync.ts`); a `download` passes its headers to `downloadSourceFile`, which gained an optional `headers` member (a hook line in `apps/api/src/watchlists/download.ts`). `syncWatchlistSource`'s name and signature are unchanged; `benchmark:typecheck` and `benchmark:test` (119) pass.
8. **(D) The export deletion hooks** are `accountExportHooks.subjectRedactedInTx(tx, {environmentId, itemIds, at}): Promise<string[]>` (the manifests' object keys, deleted after the cascade's commit with its other objects), called right after the transactions' lock in both the inquiry and the account cascades of `services/redaction.ts`, and `accountExportHooks.retention({db, storage}, {now, limit}): Promise<{expiredAccountExports}>`, the first step of `runRetention`, whose report gains `expiredAccountExports` (`retention.test.ts` and `retention-run.test.ts` gained the key). Both are in `apps/api/src/services/account-export/hooks.ts` (dsar-export's).
9. **(D) The queue** `account-export` (`apps/api/src/jobs/account-export-queues.ts`: `ACCOUNT_EXPORT_QUEUE`, `AccountExportJob {environmentId, exportId}`, `createAccountExportQueue`, one attempt, the export's id as the job id) has its worker behind `StartWorkersOptions.accountExport` (`main()` passes `{}`), over `processAccountExport` (`apps/api/src/jobs/account-export/process.ts`, dsar-export's, rejecting `notBuilt('account-export')`), and its producer is `ApiDependencies.accountExports` (the container wires it; `context.test.ts` gained the key).
10. **(D) The probe.** `aexp` and `name:fileId` (the file id is a `file_` or `txnf_` union, so its schema names no single prefix) are in `CARRIED`; and `POST /api/v1/accounts/{id}/export` is on the seeded `acc` family, so while it is frozen at 501 the probe lists it apart (`FROZEN_OPERATIONS`): a 501 to A's id and to a made-up id alike tells B nothing; once built it must answer the 404 like every other operation.
11. **(P) `queue_slots`** (`packages/db/src/tables/queue-slots.ts`): `(environment_id, queue)` primary key, `in_flight_cap` (an override, `null` follows the setting), `last_served_at`, `created_at`, `updated_at`; `queue` is one of `QUEUE_SLOT_QUEUE_VALUES` (`workflow-step` and the fifteen of `QUEUE_IN_FLIGHT_CAP_QUEUES`); the rotation index is `(queue, last_served_at nulls first)`. Phase 8's `workflow_environment_slots` rows became its `workflow-step` rows and that table is dropped (hand-written in 0036; drizzle-kit generated `queue_slots` while the schema still declared the old table, and the meta snapshot drops it by hand, so a later generate sees no change). `touchWorkflowEnvironmentSlot`, `getWorkflowEnvironmentCap` and `listEnvironmentsWithReadySteps` keep their names and read the `workflow-step` rows; Phase 8's tests that wrote the old table write `queue_slots` (`phase8-engine-tables.test.ts`, `workflow-engine-repositories.test.ts`, `workflow-steps-rotation.integration.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/workflows/engine/sweep.test.ts`; rule 12).
12. **(P) `admitted_at`** is on every row amendment 16 names: `verifications`, `report_runs`, `connect_share_tokens`, `account_exports` (D), `list_items`, `case_bulk_actions`, `case_attachments` (**two**: `scan_admitted_at` and `rendition_admitted_at`, one per queue its rows feed), `file_renditions` (an inquiry file's rendition) and `inquiry_integrity_challenges` (the integrity decode's row until KP10-27's outbox, quotas'). The repositories that list every column gained it where a row literal needed it (`inquiry-integrity.ts`'s returned columns; `apps/api/src/jobs/records.test.ts`' verification row).
13. **(P) The admission hook wraps the producers** instead of a call at each of inventory 2.6's sites: `withVerificationAdmission` and `withReportRunAdmission` (`apps/api/src/jobs/admitted-producers.ts`) ask `admitOnProduce(deps, {queue, environmentId, id})` (`apps/api/src/services/quotas/admit.ts`, quotas') before every `process-verification` and `report-run` enqueue, and enqueue only when it answers true; the container (defaults only, overrides untouched) and the worker's `main()` build their producers so, so the flow's and server's submits, the OTP submit, a decision hook's attempt, the workflow actions, the report creations, the bulk reports and the outbox and repair sweeps all pass through it. The stub stamps `admitted_at` (`stampQueueRowAdmitted`, `packages/db/src/repositories/queue-admission.ts`, once per row) and answers true. `workflow-step` keeps Phase 8's dispatcher over `queue_slots`, and `connect-redeem` is integration-11b's (KP11-12). quotas' sweep must enqueue through an unwrapped producer.
14. **(P) The ceiling's pass-through** (amendment 17): `ApiDependencies.queueInFlightCapMax` (the container from `config.queueInFlightCapMax`) and `AdminRouteContext.queueInFlightCapMax` (default `QUEUE_IN_FLIGHT_CAP.ceilingDefault`) beside `otpSmsMaxPerHourCeiling`; the settings service's write check and clamped read are quotas'.
15. **(P) The migration role's functions (C249.3)**: `openverify_delete_expired_audit_logs(environment_id text, now timestamptz, limit integer) returns integer` and `openverify_gin_clean_pending_lists(source_id text) returns void` (amending the bullet's one-argument audit form: the injected clock and a batch size are rule 6's and the C16 drain's), both `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, owned by the role that runs the migration. The audit function reads the environment's `audit-log-retention-days` (absent or below 1: nothing), holds it to at least 30 days, and deletes at most `limit` entries older than that before `now`, oldest first; their `audit_log_ip_index` rows cascade. **The floor is measured from the caller's `now`**: rule 6 makes the C16 pass's time the injected clock (Phase 9's test runs it 31 days ahead), so a runtime that states a false time could delete entries older than 30 days before it; it can never delete one directly. `runAuditLogRetention` calls it through `deleteExpiredAuditLogs(db, environmentId, now, limit)` (`packages/db/src/repositories/audit-logs.ts`); platform-ops moves the GIN cleanup's call.
16. **(P) The delete guards** are `openverify_audit_logs_delete_guard` and `openverify_connect_share_policies_delete_guard`, `BEFORE DELETE ... FOR EACH ROW` over `openverify_refuse_delete()`, which lets a delete through only when `pg_trigger_depth() > 1` (a cascade, the environment's deletion) or the current role owns the table (the migration role, also inside the function), and otherwise raises 42501. No session setting bypasses it (`session_replication_role` needs a superuser); the Testcontainers test (`packages/db/src/delete-guard.integration.test.ts`) runs a separate runtime role. In a single-role install (development, every PGlite test) the runtime is the owner, so nothing changes there.
17. **(P) `GET /ready`** is frozen at 501 in `apps/api/src/routes/system.ts` beside `/health` (public, `readyResponseSchema` 200 and `notReadyResponseSchema` 503 in the snapshot; amendment 42), for platform-ops.
18. **(final) Calls that fit 10 minutes beside six agents** (amending item 2 and rule 8's K): at load 20 to 48 one API unit shard of 12 took 765 s, so the API suite runs in 24 blob shards there (longest 559 s); the db unit suite in one turbo call took 704 s, so it runs as two blob shards and a merge from `packages/db` (241 and 189 s); the API's forced lint took 720 s (208 s at the 11A close) and has no smaller split than its package, so it is recorded, not split. The integration calls fit (longest 505 s). integration-11b, alone, measures its own.

## Workflow 11B Stage 2 amendments

Recorded by `integration-11b` from each Stage 2 agent's report (2026-11-14). The owner's PC restarted
during Stage 2; every Stage 2 agent was relaunched with a continuation note and kept its committed
work. Where an item below names a later Workflow, that Workflow owns it; where it names
integration-11b, the Stage 3 amendments say what was done.

### auth-sessions (C245, C246, C247)

1. **Borrowed edits not named by the plan:** `apps/api/src/operator-auth/operator-auth.ts` (`0e8239d99`): `authenticate` no longer calls `touchUserSession` (it slid `last_seen_at` on every request, a route marked `activity: false` included, defeating C245.2), and `openSession` sets `expires_at` to now plus the configured absolute limit instead of the fixed `USER_SESSION_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS`; `apps/api/src/operator-auth/session-context.ts` (`f9365ec6f`): `findUserSessionContext` no longer filters `expires_at > now`, so a session at its end reaches `enforceSessionPolicy` and answers `session_expired` with reason `absolute`.
2. **Named borrowed edits:** `metrics-registry.ts` (`rate_limiter_store_unavailable`); `apps/api/src/rate-limit/index.ts` and its test (the warn line and metric); `auth/api-key-lookup.ts` (`previousPeppers`, the re-hash); `login-rate-limiter.ts`' `createSharedLoginRateLimiter`; the console's `lib/api/errors.ts`, `app/login/` with `app/login/signed-out/route.ts`, `app/users/`, `lib/api/users.ts`, `components/operators/users-table.tsx`, `lib/copy/settings.ts`; `package-scripts.test.ts`.
3. **Tests changed because the behaviour changed (rule 12):** `operator-auth.test.ts`' expired case, `principal-resolver.test.ts`' expired case, and foundation-11b's frozen end-sessions case in `routes/dashboard/foundation-11b-a.test.ts`.
4. **Decisions:** idle and absolute expiry do not revoke the row (the refusal is a pure function of the row and the injected clock); the slide is one conditional UPDATE guarded by `revoked_at IS NULL` and `last_seen_at > now - idle`, so a request racing an expiry never revives the session; a role change's own audit entry stands for the sessions it ends (adding a separate `user.sessions-ended {cause: role-change}` entry needs the audit context passed at the two hook calls in `services/users.ts`); end-sessions has no step-up; the step-up's guesses count against the same login limiter and fail closed with it (503 `login_unavailable`, `Retry-After: 30`); only `GET /dashboard/files/:id/rendition` is `activity: false` (integration-11b checked the case attachment content route: it is a link, never polled; Stage 3 item 10).
5. **For 11E:** the B4 threat-model row states that the idle timeout is measured on each API instance's clock (a lagging clock extends a session by its skew) and that it does not protect a token stolen while in use (C245.5).

### sso-oidc (C248, KP11-18)

1. **Borrowed edits not named by the plan:** `operator-auth.ts` (`a2f31f3d7`, `a357a2866`: `LoginInput.organization`, `narrowLoginCandidates`, `assertPasswordSignInAllowed` after the password matched, `openSsoSession`, `startSsoMfaChallenge`); `routes/dashboard/sessions.ts` (`4594880d2`, the hint passed through); `routes/dashboard/index.ts` (`ec2b55c90`: the SSO routes receive `admin` for the audit logger, mailer, clock and the console URL); `routes/dashboard/dashboard.test-support.ts` (`sso` harness option); `services/retention.ts` with `retention.test.ts` and `retention-run.test.ts` (`deletedSsoLoginStates`, a day's grace, in batches).

Fastify path parameters cannot hold `-`, so C248.4's `:organization-slug` and `:provider-id` read `:organizationSlug` and `:providerId`.
4. **Not built (C248.6, one clause):** the console does not show the provider's `amr`; no session stores it. Carried to 11F (a `user_sessions` column, a served member and a console column); see Stage 3 item 12.

The console's password form does not send the organisation hint; only the API takes it.

### dsar-export (C253; KP11-14, KP11-15, KP11-16, KP10-54)

1. **Rule 22 over amendment 34's order:** the create answers the generic 404 for an account outside the caller's environment before it counts the `account-export` policy (`839b47923`, `041960118`), because the probe's app has no Valkey and the fail-closed policy answered 503 to B on A's account. In the caller's environment nothing changes: 429 and 503 `account_export_unavailable` come before `pii:view`, the step-up, the item limit and the pending check.
2. **Not covered (C253.4) for retention steps that are not a redaction cascade:** `nullExpiredSignalComponents`, `drainAddresses`, `deleteExpiredDevices`, `redactExpiredListItems`, `purgeExpiredImportObjects`, the report and transaction retention (`redactDueObjects`), `redactExpiredGraphQueries`, `runWorkflowRetention`, `runCaseRetention` and the decision-record retention call no export hook, so an export can outlive such a step by at most `account-export-ttl-days` (1-30, default 7). ### ingestion-11b (C252, C254; K7C-R009; the transaction PDF page count)

1. **C254.2, the validator step:** foundation-11b's hook in `sync.ts` runs only before the download, so `checkSourceConditionally` (`watchlists/conditional.ts`) makes the check's one request itself through the list client the download uses (the body hashed and discarded). A 304 to a conditional request is recorded `http-304`; a 2xx with the applied file's SHA-256 is recorded `sha256` with the full verification and its strong validators; any other 2xx answers `download`, so a changed conditional list is fetched twice. Past `watchlist-full-check-hours` (the shortest any environment set, else 24) no validator is sent, so a 304 never outlasts the forced full check. A post-download hook in `sync.ts` and `download.ts` would remove the double fetch: carried to 11F.
2. **New owned file:** `apps/api/src/watchlists/conditional-records.ts` (the source rows' check columns, the install window, what the sources page serves), beside `conditional-policy.ts`. Only `uk-sanctions-list` opts in; the reasons for the others are in `conditional-sources.test.ts`.
3. **C254.1 is built locked and stays locked.** With `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_UN_OPT_IN_ALLOWED` false (its default in `config.ts`, `.env.example`, compose and the e2e stack) every opt-in and withdrawal answers 409 `watchlist_source_locked` and stores nothing. Unlocking needs three hooks no caller passes yet: the switch in the dashboard admin route context (`container.ts` through `routes/dashboard/admin.ts` into `watchlistOptInRoutes`), `optInSources: await optedInWatchlistSources(db, allowed)` at the five reconcile callers (`worker.ts`, `container.ts`, `watchlists/cli.ts`, `jobs/repeatable/watchlists.ts`, `seed/dev-seed.ts`), and `environmentMayScreenSource` on the screening path; and the sources document has no opt-in member. While the switch is false none of them changes behaviour; they are carried to the Workflow that unlocks the list after the owner confirms Q10 (11G's records-11g flips the documented default; 11F wires the hooks first), never wired while Q10 is pending.
4. **C252:** a flow-spec 1 text field's pattern is checked at write with `compilePattern` (`pattern_refused`), and a stored flow whose pattern the grammar refuses still parses; the workflow editor's step rename is a string scan; every other run-time `RegExp` in shipped code is one of the 27 reviewed call sites in `packages/config/eslint/operator-patterns.js`, each with its reason and site count. The tree scan was made cheaper after it met its 60 s guard under load (`13167aba9`; 5 s alone).
5. **The transaction PDF page count** (owner decisions after Workflow 7B, Later phases): vision's guarded child counts pages through `POST /v1/count/document-pages` (1-10,000; 422 `pdf_encrypted`, `pdf_unprocessable`, `too_many_pages`; 415; 413 past 20 MB; 503 `guarded_worker_failed`), its bounds in `contracts/fixtures/vision/pdf-page-count.json` (rule 28); the API calls it only for a PDF of a field that limits pages; an uncountable PDF is `transaction_file_refused`, a vision outage a retryable 503. The in-process count is gone; no new dependency.
6. **Named borrowed edits:** `packages/config/eslint/phase5-rules.js`; `packages/flow-spec/src/flow.ts` and `flow.test.ts` (rule 12); `apps/dashboard/src/components/workflows/editor/document-model.ts` and the new `step-references.ts`; `services/transactions/json-schemas.ts` (K7C-R009); `services/transactions/files.ts` and its test; `ports.ts`, `services/context.ts`, `vision/index.ts`, `vision/schemas.ts`; `services/vision/src/openverify_vision/app.py`, `guarded/pdf_measure.py`, `guarded/worker.py`, `tools/make_contract_fixtures.py`, `tests/test_contract_fixtures.py`; `packages/db/src/tables/tenancy.ts`; `watchlists/reconcile.ts`; `http/stream.ts` (a 304 is no redirect); `components/report-config/watchlist-sources-view.tsx`; `routes/dashboard/watchlist-sources.ts` and its test; `routes/dashboard/watchlist-opt-in.ts`; `foundation-11b-d.test.ts`.
7. **Route schemas:** dropped 501 from both opt-in routes, added 422 to the opt-in and 409 to the withdrawal: integration-11b, Stage 3 item 6.
8. **For 11E:** only `uk-sanctions-list` sends conditional requests; a 304 is the publisher's statement and the SHA-256 comparison every `watchlist-full-check-hours` is the platform's proof; a changed conditional list is fetched twice.

## Workflow 11B Stage 3 amendments (integration-11b)

Written by `integration-11b` (2026-11-14), alone and last, after the restart. Evidence:
`docs/tdd/phase11-integration-11b.tdd.md`. Nothing was salvaged from an earlier integration-11b run:
none had committed or written anything.

1. **Quotas' missing hook (C251.1).** `StartWorkersOptions.quotas {limits, producers}` schedules `quota-sweep` (15 s) and, when a queue recovers lost work, `quota-recovery` (60 s) over quotas' `quotaRepeatableJobs`; the worker's `main()` passes the UNWRAPPED verification and report-run producers (an admitted producer would hold the row it just admitted) and new `account-export` and `integrity-decode` producers, plus `connect-redeem` while Connect is on (`144a7a1d0`; `worker-quotas.test.ts`).
2. **Install limits everywhere (C251.2).** `installQueueLimitsOf(config)` (`jobs/admitted-producers.ts`) gives the producers' admission in the container and the worker, and the sweep, `OPENVERIFY_QUEUE_IN_FLIGHT_CAP_MAX` and the workflow-step default; an account export is admitted through `admitEnvironment` under its environment's cap (held, not rethrown, when the enqueue fails), so dsar-export's "admitted at once" is gone (`admission.test.ts`, rule 12: its rethrow case became "holds again").
3. **Amendment 17 for workflow-step.** Phase 8's `capOf` returns the lower of the stored cap and the ceiling (`EngineDeps.queueInFlightCapMax`, else the worker's services', else 50); the API's two engines pass it (`17175ef6d`, `abc039f52`; `dispatcher-ceiling.test.ts`). Named borrowed edits: `workflows/engine/{deps,dispatcher}.ts`, `workflows/runs/service.ts`, `workflows/triggers/start.ts`, `workflows/services.ts` (`WorkflowServices` carries `queueInFlightCapMax`).
4. **KP11-12 (C234.2, review F12).** `enqueueRedemption` admits through quotas' `admitOnProduce` in the destination's environment, so the server redeem and the flow's share-consent accept (both through it) and the `redeem_share_token` action's afterCommit (now through it too) put `connect-redeem` under the cap; a token at the cap stays `pending` with `admitted_at` null, the fair sweep admits it, and quotas' recovery leaves it alone while held (`redeem-admission.test.ts`). `admitOnProduce` rather than `admitEnvironment`: a token row a test's port never wrote is enqueued as before. Named borrowed edits: `services/connect/redeem.ts`, `workflows/actions/connect/handlers.ts`.
5. **KP11-11 (C240.3, amendment 22).** `RateLimiter.peek` is optional on the port (so the many counting test limiters keep their shape; the production limiter implements it with the read-only `GCRA_PEEK_SCRIPT`), and `GET /api/v1/rate-limits` lists `connect-redeem` and `connect-peek` beside `api` for an API key and a user session acting in an environment, keyed by `connectRateLimitKey`, only while Connect is on, none when the peek fails; `account-export` is not listed. The resource schema is unchanged; the route's description changed in the snapshot. Named borrowed edits: `ports.ts`, `rate-limit/index.ts` and its test (rule 12: two registered scripts).
6. **Route schemas and the snapshot.** The fifteen built 11B operations drop the frozen 501 (four account-export routes, seven SSO routes, end-sessions, the opt-in pair); `DELETE /dashboard/sso-providers/:id` documents its 204 and answers through the typed reply; the opt-in documents 422 and the withdrawal 409 (`openapi-11b.test.ts`). `@openverify/node` gains `accountExports` (`create`, `retrieve`, `manifest`, `fileContent`) and the types `AccountExport`, `AccountExportManifest`, `AccountExportFileContent`, with a README section. `contracts/fixtures/api/` changed nothing at the top level, so ContractFixturesTest needed no entry and android/ is unchanged.
7. **C255.2-C255.4.** `apps/dashboard/src/security/console-csp.test.ts` and `origin-checks.test.ts` (Stage 3 evidence section 4); no missing origin check was found. Next.js's server-action check counts because `e2e/tests/hardening-11b.spec.ts` proves it on the stack. The flow server's headers are held by `apps/flow/src/server/server-headers.test.ts`, `infra/tests/flow-headers.test.sh` and the same spec; no dashboard-side copy was made.
8. **The 11B specs (C287.3):** `session-idle`, `sso-oidc`, `account-export`, `quota-flood` and `hardening-11b`, each with a 90 s budget in its header, join the chromium shards. The idle spec moves `last_seen_at` in the stack's database; the SSO spec starts sso-oidc's provider in the spec process and answers the authorization visit itself; the flood spec holds the dev sandbox's `report-run` cap at 1 (restored in `finally`) while the production environment's report completes. The export spec checks the manifest, not a listed file: the files route is held by dsar-export's Testcontainers test.
9. **KP11-13 (closed).** The cause is shown deterministically (`pacing-fallback.test.ts`: a failed limiter command makes the pacer fall back to per-process turns); the integration test now asserts no limiter command failed before it reads the elapsed time, on connections with a 10 s command timeout.
10. **The case attachment content route** needs no `activity: false` (auth-sessions' question): the reviewer links to it (`components/cases/reviewer/case-sections.tsx:558`); only `fileRenditionPath` is polled by `RenditionImage`.
11. **KP11-10 (part; carried to 11F).** The probe seeds 22 families (75 of 253 server operations at the 11A close; 115 of 257 now). Carried to 11F (C240.7) with the reason "needs a multi-route seed": the other 49 id families, `name:key`, `name:fieldKey`, `name:fileId`; and the 22+ dashboard operations (with `ssop`, end-sessions and the opt-in pair) and the flow operations, which need a user-session and a flow-session harness inside the probe. A principal-kind 403 before any lookup (`POST /workflows/{id}/publish` with a key) now counts as the validation-first 400 does: the same answer for any id.
12. **Carried, with owners:** C248.6's display of the provider's `amr` (11F: a session column, a served member, a console column); the UN opt-in's three unlock hooks (only after Q10, Stage 2 ingestion-11b item 3); C253.4's export deletion in the C16 steps that are not a redaction cascade (11F); the ten capped queues without admission (11F); the conditional list's double fetch (11F); a separate `user.sessions-ended` audit entry for a role change (11F, if wanted).
13. **Defects the Validation found, fixed test-first:** the `redeem_share_token` step's admission ran on the step's services, which an engine need not give a clock; it now runs on the engine's own database and clock (`ecca921ca`; RED the existing `connect.integration.test.ts` case). K8-35's deadlock race chose its victim by timing at load 13 (`0954589c6`: the entry's transaction sets a longer `deadlock_timeout`). The flood spec created its reports one by one, so a fast sandbox run could leave only one held (`591305c54`: six at once). Phase 8's workflow-settings spec raised the workflow-step cap past the new install ceiling (`4999239ea`: it lowers it; rule 12).
14. **Validation as run** (`docs/tdd/phase11-integration-11b.tdd.md` section 9; load 0.4 to 15.7): every step exited 0 on the final tree, in the order the plan gives. The API unit suite fits K = 8 blob shards alone (156-196 s each, merge 41 s; 10,819 tests at 94.17%); the dashboard 7,222 at 95.67% in two shards; db 2,357 at 89.88%; integration 110 + 13 + 444; vision 2,663 at 97.42%; the Android gate's eleven calls (SDK 2,732, none skipped; 97 screenshot tests; demo 97 and 9; nfc 116; face 51; 98 release-check tests); the e2e gate: mocked 349, shards 50, 52, 47 and 49 (198), sweep 109, stackTest 3 of 3; WebKit theme-csp 7; three images; 11 infra scripts with 1,314 checks; gitleaks clean. 9b and step 16 were not applicable (nothing under android/ changed).
