Each of those rests on its SHA-256 pin and the release's checksum file.
6. **Base images by digest** (`docker buildx imagetools inspect`, index digests, the amd64 image's build date): `node:22.22.1-bookworm-slim@sha256:4f77a690f2f8946ab16fe1e791a3ac0667ae1c3575c3e4d0d4589e9ed5bfaf3d` (2026-03-16); `python:3.12-slim@sha256:aa943ce411423c7582288f18707ff66eddcbaeb510502d747a72aeb34f30d159` (2026-10-28, Python 3.12.16, from docker-library/repo-info's history: today's tag points at a build of 2026-11-05, 9 days old, which no pin may take); `ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:0.13.3@sha256:bee0a2a458fd956c5df7b8b4c5fed2257b0af34866e3da7b51db0be3acb375d1` (2026-10-20; uv's release-age question is Q5, left as it is). The other references images-11c pins (C258.1): `pgvector/pgvector:pg17@sha256:cf134a767f474095eeba57e0117be8e568e011a63f33fbf252f14c9b760f8e6f` (the `pg17` build of pgvector 0.8.6, 2026-08-13; today's `pg17` is 0.8.7 of 2026-11-12, 2 days old), `valkey/valkey:9.1@sha256:3add1a336a920ea54770a001ab6feb3da5fceea96a40373a3a2cd469b815f0b7` (2026-10-26), `rustfs/rustfs:1.0.3@sha256:9ca9559533b537d10482fe923288b676ad08f92a7069d9a17327fbae3cab688b` (2026-10-17), `amazon/aws-cli:2.38.2@sha256:5d39fd52f236ed01c5c25fba66c9aebcfc6cbb88e7bb1b727c5fe1f150540597` (2026-10-19), `axllent/mailpit:v1.31@sha256:23fe39e81bde596a918000b3135c0f85dc8bb4c9ea6be274b051143f3e300387` (2026-10-15), and the WebKit browser server `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.64.1-noble@sha256:2f05f8a734509a7b3f26a912639488ee08f58a7b292d14d62271bcff9038768f` (2026-11-01: 13 days old on 2026-11-14, so images-11c pins it from 2026-11-15 on, or leaves the README's tag as it is if it adopts it earlier). Each digest is re-read on the adopting day by `infra/scripts/refresh-digests.sh`.
7. **Licence rows** (`THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`, checkpoints deps and skeletons): §1 holds a row with version, date and licence for each tool of item 4, the checks bundle, the Kubernetes documents and the kind node images, and the note that published images redistribute their base distribution's packages (C257.5); §2.1.22 the npm additions (safe-regex 2.1.1, regexp-tree 0.1.27), the CycloneDX plugin's build classpath and every package of both uv projects; §5 certifi 2026.10.23 (pip-audit's), pathspec 1.1.1 (`security/python`'s) and Eclipse Sisu 1.1.0 (EPL-2.0, the plugin's build classpath); Excluded the Semgrep CE engine (LGPL-2.1), Semgrep's registry rules, Opengrep (LGPL-2.1), yamllint (GPL-3.0), Bitnami charts and images, and `aopalliance` 1.0 (item 18); a new "Not adopted" section: eslint-plugin-no-unsanitized, CodeQL (Q9), cdxgen. `packages/config/src/supply-chain-pins.test.ts` holds every row.
8. **Spikes** (TDD section 3; scratch worktree removed): (a) Syft's lock cataloguer lists every one of the lockfile's 822 packages but no workspace package; with `javascript-package-cataloger` beside it (`node_modules` excluded) it lists the 18 importers too, so cdxgen is not adopted (item 21); (b) helm-unittest 1.2.1 runs under Helm 4.3.1 (a legacy `cli/v1` plugin) and 3.22.1, a failing assertion exiting 1 under both, so the unit tests run on both majors; (c) the CycloneDX plugin reads all three published modules (JMRTD and SCUBA in `openverify-nfc`'s SBOM, `onnxruntime-android` in `openverify-face`'s), once its direct SBOM is limited to the module's release runtime classpath, and works without `aopalliance`.
9. **Ten Android lockfiles, not five.** Besides `android/settings-gradle.lockfile` and the four modules' `gradle.lockfile`, the demo has three artifact-combination lockfiles (`android/demo-app/gradle-sdk-nfc.lockfile`, `gradle-sdk-face.lockfile`, `gradle-sdk-nfc-face.lockfile`, used under `-PopenverifyDemoArtifacts`), and `android/consumer-check/` has two lockfiles and its own `gradle/verification-metadata.xml`. osv-scanner (C259.1, orchestrator decision 5) reads all ten and both metadata files. A build plugin's classpath is held by `verification-metadata.xml`, not by a lockfile (no buildscript configuration is locked).
10. **The CycloneDX plugin is on the Gradle Plugin Portal only** (Maven Central answers 404 for its marker and its jar), so `android/settings.gradle.kts`' `pluginManagement` gains `gradlePluginPortal()` filtered to the groups `org.cyclonedx.bom` and `org.cyclonedx`; nothing else resolves from the portal. The plugin registers an empty resolvable configuration `cyclonedxBom` in each module it is applied to, so the three published modules' `gradle.lockfile` gain it on their `empty=` line; no other lockfile and nothing in `android/consumer-check/` changed, and the staged POMs are byte-identical to the 11B close's (TDD section 4). The plugin's default scans every configuration (JUnit, Robolectric, Roborazzi, the iOS klibs), so each module's `cyclonedxDirectBom` is limited to its release runtime classpath: `androidRuntimeClasspath` for `:openverify-sdk`, `releaseRuntimeClasspath` for `:openverify-nfc` and `:openverify-face` (188, 195 and 190 components).
11. **The flow's run-time origin is half built** (C257.1, decision 73): the flow server already reads `VITE_OPENVERIFY_API_URL` at start for its `connect-src` header (`apps/flow/server.ts:178`), while the bundle reads it only at build (`apps/flow/src/main.tsx:41`, `apps/flow/vite.config.ts:24`). `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_API_URL` is read by the flow server first with `VITE_OPENVERIFY_API_URL` as its fallback (OD-11C-3), so compose, the per-push e2e and the mocked suite keep working unchanged; images-11c builds `/config.json`, the bundle's fetch and the `<meta>` without `connect-src` when the variable was unset at build, as named borrowed edits, with the names of `apps/flow/src/security/runtime-config.ts` (item 27).
12. **The console's standalone output keeps its custom server.** Next.js's standalone output writes its own `server.js`, which would drop the peer-address header the custom server (C21, `apps/dashboard/server.ts:4-9`) sets for the BFF. C257.1's `node server.js` therefore names the custom server, run beside the standalone output's traced `node_modules`, never Next's generated file (images-11c, with `output: 'standalone'` as its named borrowed edit of `next.config.ts`). The console reads `OPENVERIFY_HSTS_MAX_AGE` only at `next build`, so the image is built with it at 0 (orchestrator decision 22).
13. **The flow server, the console, vision and the runner read secrets from files too** (orchestrator decision 18, freezing C264.3's path for them). Today only the API, the worker and migrate resolve `<NAME>_FILE` (`apps/api/src/config/secret-files.ts:106` from `config.ts:1293`, `:1678`); `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_SERVER_TOKEN` (`apps/flow/server.ts:196`), `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_BFF_TOKEN` (`apps/dashboard/src/lib/config.ts:65`), `OPENVERIFY_VISION_SERVICE_TOKEN` and the new `OPENVERIFY_CODE_RUNNER_TOKEN` are read from the environment only. images-11c gives each the same read, test-first: both forms set is a start error naming the variable; trailing line ends are trimmed; a relative, unreadable or empty file is refused; a world-readable file is the warning `secret_file_readable` (a group-readable one is not: the chart mounts 0440 with `fsGroup`); its start test runs each image with the file at mode 0440. The registry's `fileForm` stays "a secret the API, the worker or migrate read" (its test's rule), which already marks all four.
14. **`db:migrate`'s backfill does not see a KEK given as a file.** `runMigrateCommand` (`apps/api/src/migrate.ts:144-182`) resolves `<NAME>_FILE` only inside `loadMigrateConfig`, which reads no KEK, and the event-id backfill opens sealed payloads with the process keyring, built from `process.env.OPENVERIFY_KEK` (`packages/db/src/crypto.ts:362-368`); the API and the worker install theirs with `setDefaultKeyring(config.keyring)` (`apps/api/src/container.ts:274`, `worker.ts:1834`). With only `OPENVERIFY_KEK_FILE` set (the chart's form), a database with events written before schema v2 would fail its backfill. images-11c, whose start test covers migrate at mode 0440, fixes it test-first (the migrate command installs the keyring from the resolved environment before the backfill) and checks `seed:dev` and `kek:rewrap` the same way, as a named borrowed edit of `apps/api/src/migrate.ts`.
15. **Vision has no start-up digest check.** C257.1 says vision "refuses to start when a file's SHA-256 differs from `models/manifest.json`"; models load lazily on first use (`services/vision/src/openverify_vision/app.py:98-101`) and nothing compares a digest at start. images-11c builds it test-first (a named borrowed edit of `services/vision/src/`), reading the manifest the image carries. The variable keeps its built name, `OPENVERIFY_VISION_MODELS_DIR` (`services/vision/Dockerfile:42`); wherever C257.1 or C263.5 says `OPENVERIFY_MODELS_DIR`, read that. The vision image also carries `scripts/fetch_models.py` and `models/manifest.json` for the chart's models-fetch Job (`fetch_models.py --manifest <path> --dest /models/<manifest digest>`; item 26).
16. **The API's build emits test support.** `apps/api/tsconfig.build.json` excludes only `src/**/*.test.ts{,x}`, so `dist/test-support/` (11B's in-process OIDC provider among it) and the `*.test-support.*` modules are emitted; no production module imports them (inventory 3.3). images-11c leaves them out of the image (orchestrator decision 24) and keeps `@openverify/emrtd`'s `dist/testing/`, which `trust:sandbox-utopia`, the sandbox chip route and the sandbox trust anchor import at run time (decision 10).
17. **The command map has development tools to leave out.** `graph:benchmark` and `scripts/workflow-benchmark.ts` import `@openverify/db/testing`; `analytics:benchmark` and `workflow-docs:write` are development tools. None is compiled into the image; every operator command is (item 25). `face-scan:benchmark` stays in, as C257.1 lists it (it writes a scratch organisation and refuses nothing about production, so the self-hosting guide must say never to run it against a production database; for 11E).
18. **`aopalliance` stays off the build classpath.** The CycloneDX plugin's `maven-core` 3.10.0 brings Guice 5.1.0, which brings `aopalliance:aopalliance` 1.0 under a "Public Domain" declaration that rule 4 does not list (as The Unlicense of `robust-predicates` and 0BSD were not). It is excluded in the root `buildscript` (`configurations.classpath { exclude(group = "aopalliance", module = "aopalliance") }`), the plugin builds every SBOM without it, and `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` lists it under Excluded. The plugin's other classpath licences are Apache-2.0, MIT, BSD-2-Clause and EPL-2.0 (Eclipse Sisu, §5 build tooling).
19. **Smaller drifts.** `@openverify/node` depends at run time on the private `@openverify/contracts` (and `zod`), so making it publishable (C266.4) needs what it uses bundled into its build or a published contracts package; sdk-web-11c decides and records it. The root `package.json` says `0.0.0` while `android/gradle.properties`' `openverifyVersion` is `0.1.0`; the chart takes `0.1.0` and 11E's `release:check` reconciles the version strings. The plan's "five lockfiles" and "the 1.35 and 1.37 node images" read as items 9 and 4 (kind 0.33.0's images are v1.35.8 and v1.37.0). A docs commit's quoted phrase naming the store list beside a hyphenated word met gitleaks' `generic-api-key`, and so did a variable named `..._OPENAPI2JSONSCHEMA_SHA256`: both were reworded (the variable is `KUBECONFORM_SCHEMA_CONVERTER_SHA256`); a name holding "api" or "key" beside a digest trips that rule.
20. **(deps) What the checkpoint froze** (`a283af290`, full gate green): `eslint-plugin-security` 4.2.0 in the root `package.json` beside `eslint-plugin-regexp` (sast-pii-11c loads it from `packages/config/eslint/security.js` through `phase5Config()`, OD-11C-1; no `lint:security` script); the uv tool project `security/pip-audit/` (pip-audit 2.10.1, 30 packages, hashed `uv.lock`, `exclude-newer = "14 days"`); the Python environment `security/python/` for `security/check-sbom-licences.py` and `scripts/fetch_k8s_schemas.py` (pytest 9.1.1, pytest-cov 7.1.0, ruff 0.17.1, mypy 2.4.0 and PyYAML 6.0.3, which kubeconform's converter imports; the scripts themselves run with `python3 -I` and the standard library), whose commands from the repository root are `uv run --project security/python --frozen pytest -c security/python/pyproject.toml --rootdir .` (tests in `security/tests/` and `scripts/tests/`, coverage of exactly the two scripts, `--cov-fail-under=80`), `uv run --project security/python --frozen ruff check security scripts/fetch_k8s_schemas.py scripts/tests`, the same with `ruff format --check`, and `uv run --project security/python --frozen mypy --strict security/check-sbom-licences.py scripts/fetch_k8s_schemas.py`; the skeletons `packages/sdk-react` (`@openverify/react`, private until Q4, peer `react ^19.0.0`, dependency `@openverify/js`) and `apps/demo-web` (`@openverify/demo-web`, private, dependencies `@openverify/js`, `@openverify/node`, `@openverify/react`, React; Vite and the testing libraries as dev dependencies), each with `passWithNoTests` in its `vitest.config.ts` until sdk-web-11c's first test removes it, so the lockfile is final; the CycloneDX plugin (items 10, 18). `workspace-conventions.test.ts`' `PUBLISHED_NAMES` gains `sdk-react` (rule 12: the plan adds the package). integration-11c wires `security/python`'s tests and `security/pip-audit` into the CI jobs it owns.
21. **cdxgen is not adopted** (C261.1's fallback was not needed): the source-tree SBOM is Syft with `--override-default-catalogers javascript-lock-cataloger,javascript-package-cataloger` over the tree, `node_modules`, `android/` and `services/` excluded (`services/vision/uv.lock` and `load/uv.lock` by their own runs). It lists every workspace package and every locked dependency, with dependency edges among the locked packages but no edge from a workspace package to its direct dependencies, and no licences offline (`search-remote-licenses` stays off: no test or check queries the registry for them). The licence check therefore reads the image and package SBOMs, whose installed `package.json` files carry the licences (C261.4).
22. **(skeletons) The installers** (`ade4631f3`): `infra/scripts/install-{cosign,helm,helm-unittest,kubeconform,chart-testing,kind,kubectl,trivy,syft,grype,osv-scanner,zizmor}.sh`, each `set -euo pipefail` with `<P>_VERSION_DEFAULT` and `<P>_SHA256_DEFAULT` (`P` = `COSIGN`, `HELM4` and `HELM3` in one installer that puts `helm` (4.3.1) and `helm3` (3.22.1) in `INSTALL_DIR`, `HELM_UNITTEST`, `KUBECONFORM`, `CHART_TESTING`, `KIND`, `KUBECTL`, `TRIVY`, `SYFT`, `GRYPE`, `OSV_SCANNER`, `ZIZMOR`), plus `TRIVY_CHECKS_BUNDLE_DEFAULT`, `KIND_NODE_IMAGE_1_35`, `KIND_NODE_IMAGE_1_37`, `KUBECONFORM_SCHEMA_CONVERTER_COMMIT` and `KUBECONFORM_SCHEMA_CONVERTER_SHA256`, each with the asset, checksum file and signature identity of items 4 and 5 in its header. Each carries `OPENVERIFY_SKELETON=1` and exits 3 with "not built", writing nothing, until scanners-11c fills it test-first and removes the marker; `infra/tests/supply-chain-skeletons.test.sh` holds the pins and the fail-closed run (skipping an installer once its marker is gone, which its own `install-<tool>.test.sh` then holds). helm-unittest installs by copying the archive's `plugin.yaml`, binary and `LICENSE` into `$HELM_PLUGINS/unittest`, never by its downloading hook.
23. **(skeletons) `infra/scripts/supply-chain.sh`**, scanners-11c's, frozen entry points: `supply-chain.sh <step>` or `all`, the steps in this order: `osv` (item 9's files, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `services/vision/uv.lock`, `load/uv.lock` when it exists, `security/pip-audit/uv.lock`, `security/python/uv.lock`), `pnpm-audit` (`--audit-level high`, then `pnpm audit signatures`), `pip-audit` (vision and `load/`, through `security/pip-audit/`), `zizmor` (`.github/workflows/` and `.github/actions/`, failing at medium), `trivy-config` (`infra/` and the rendered chart, failing at high, the checks bundle by digest), `chart` (`deploy/helm/openverify/ci/chart-checks.sh`, chart-policy-11c's), `pii` (gitleaks with `security/gitleaks-pii.toml` and its allowlist, sast-pii-11c's). Exit 0 pass, 1 findings, 2 usage, 3 a step not built yet; every step applies `security/allowlist.yaml` through one shared check (expired, or more than 90 days ahead: the step fails), writes each database's build date and the medium and low findings to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` when set, and takes its binaries from `SUPPLY_CHAIN_TOOLS_DIR` (default `~/.local/bin`).
24. **(skeletons) The allowlist and `release.yml`.** `security/allowlist.yaml` is `[]`; `security/allowlist.schema.json` (draft 2020-12) is an array of `{id, package | path, reason, expires, added-by}`, `oneOf` package or path, no other member, `expires` a date, the 90-day bound the gate's. `.github/workflows/release.yml`: `workflow_dispatch` with the required string input `version`; `permissions: contents: read`; `concurrency: release`; the jobs `build` (`if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'`), `verify`, `stage` and `publish` (`needs` in that chain; `publish` with `environment: release` and `if: vars.OPENVERIFY_PUBLISH == 'true'`); every checkout `persist-credentials: false`; while it carries `# OPENVERIFY_SKELETON` every job ends with a "not built" notice. sbom-release-11c fills it and moves its checks into `infra/tests/release-workflow.test.sh`; zizmor 1.31.1 finds nothing in the skeleton and actionlint passes.
25. **(images) The image contract** (`41dca4be4`; TDD section 7's handoff has the table): `openverify/api` (`infra/docker/api.Dockerfile`; `node dist/server.js`, `dist/worker.js`, probe `dist/worker-health.js`, commands `dist/commands/<command with ':' as '-'>.js`, `db:migrate` as `db-migrate.js` from `scripts/migrate.ts`, compiled by `apps/api/tsconfig.commands.json`), `openverify/dashboard` (`infra/docker/dashboard.Dockerfile`, item 12), `openverify/flow` and `openverify/flow:<tag>-noguidance` (`infra/docker/flow.Dockerfile`, build argument `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_FACE_GUIDANCE=on|off`; `node server.ts --hostname 0.0.0.0 --port 4173 --dist <bundle>`), `openverify/vision` (`services/vision/Dockerfile`, port 8000, no `HEALTHCHECK`, an `EIGEN-SOURCE.txt` beside the wheel's `ThirdPartyNotices.txt` under its `licenses/`, decision 8), `openverify/code-runner` (`infra/docker/code-runner.Dockerfile`, port 7700 for `tcp:`). Local tags `openverify/<name>:11c-local` and `openverify/flow:11c-local-noguidance`; release tags `<version>` and `<version>-noguidance`. Every image runs as `10001:10001` (vision's and the runner's uid today, C264.4) with a read-only root and a writable `/tmp`; bases by digest (item 6) through the build arguments `NODE_IMAGE`, `PYTHON_IMAGE`, `UV_IMAGE`; labels `org.opencontainers.image.{source (SOURCE_URL), revision (REVISION), version (VERSION), licenses (Apache-2.0), title}` and `dev.openverify.licence-status` (`pending-owner` on `openverify/api` while Q11 is open, `decided` on the others). No build argument, label or layer holds a secret, and no image is built with `VITE_OPENVERIFY_API_URL`.
26. **(chart) What the checkpoint froze** (`52acd7597`; TDD section 6's handoff lists every key): `deploy/helm/openverify/Chart.yaml` (`apiVersion: v2`, `version: 0.1.0`, `appVersion: '0.1.0'`, `kubeVersion: '>=1.35.0-0'`, no dependency, a description that claims nothing); `values.yaml` with every key of C263.2 and of this prompt commented, no secret value, `install.connectEnabled` and `install.watchlistUnOptInAllowed` false, `install.hstsMaxAge` 0, the migrate lock's 600 s under an `activeDeadlineSeconds` of 900, `api.internalListen.port` 3101, the console limits, the cap ceiling 50, `api.ssoPrivateIssuers`, a CA source per store mounted at `/etc/openverify/ca/<store>.pem`, `external.postgres.runtimeRole` `openverify_runtime` and `migrationSecret`, one Secret per process with its `keys` (variable names, read as `<NAME>_FILE=/run/secrets/openverify/<NAME>`), the ingress' HSTS and three body limits (12m, and 28m for the list importer and a transaction file, which chart-policy-11c confirms against the routes), the network policy lists of `{cidr, ports}`, the models modes, Workflow 7A's network and IP-reputation keys, `worker.extraContainers` and `extraVolumes` for the operator's clamd (OD-11C-5), Play Integrity's two worker values, `providers` and `config.extra`, and no `flow.allowedFrameAncestors`; `values.schema.json` (draft 7, generated by code; it refuses any other top-level key, a malformed digest, a cap above 1,000, a lock wait above 3,600 s, a models source other than `job` or `pvc`, two vision replicas on a ReadWriteOnce claim, and, with policies on, a configured external store without `egress.dataStores` (unless in-cluster), SMTP without `egress.smtp` and a private issuer without `egress.identityProviders`); the helpers of `templates/_security.tpl` (`openverify.podSecurityContext` (uid, gid), `containerSecurityContext`, `tmpVolume`, `tmpVolumeMount`, `secretVolume`, `secretVolumeMounts`, `secretFileEnv` (process), `caVolumes`, `caVolumeMounts`, `caFileEnv` (process), `ingressAnnotations` (host, path), `internalPathDeny`; each a dict with `root`), stubs that render the smallest valid form until chart-policy-11c fills them; and `deploy/helm/openverify/.prettierrc.json` (the root's options plus `overrides: [{files: "templates/**", options: {requirePragma: true}}]`, a first creation; rule 26). The relational rules (the deadline above the lock wait, the pools within `maxConnections`) are the templates' `fail`s. Held by `infra/tests/chart-skeleton.test.sh` (51 checks with both Helms).
27. **(images) The variables and their code** (`config-registry.ts` holds 124 entries): `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_API_URL` (flow, not a secret, default unset), `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_FACE_GUIDANCE` (flow, `on` by default, read at build), `OPENVERIFY_CODE_RUNNER_TOKEN` (worker and code-runner, a secret with `_FILE`, at least 32 bytes, refused as a `change-me` placeholder in production), `OPENVERIFY_CODE_RUNNER_LISTEN` (code-runner, `<host>:<port>`), and `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_CODE_RUNNER` described with its `tcp:<host>:<port>` form. `apps/api/src/config.ts` (contracts-11c's own change, so images-11c edits no line of it) parses `tcp:<host>:<port>` into `{kind: 'tcp', host, port, token}`, requires the token in the worker ("is required while OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_CODE_RUNNER is tcp:") and leaves it `null` in the API, which never connects; the refusal reads "must be pool, socket:<absolute path> or tcp:<host>:<port>". `@openverify/workflow-engine/code-runner` exports `CODE_RUNNER_LISTEN_VARIABLE`, `CODE_RUNNER_TOKEN_VARIABLE`, `CODE_RUNNER_TOKEN_MIN_BYTES` (32), `parseCodeRunnerTcpTarget` and the stub `connectCodeRunnerOverTcp`, which images-11c builds beside the socket client with the same frames, limits and request checks (the token first, compared in constant time; a wrong one closes the connection) and the runner's listener beside its socket; until then a worker set to `tcp:` fails at start. `apps/flow/src/security/runtime-config.ts` exports `FLOW_API_URL_VARIABLE`, `FLOW_FACE_GUIDANCE_VARIABLE`, `FLOW_RUNTIME_CONFIG_PATH` (`/config.json`) and `faceGuidanceEnabled`. `.env.example` carries commented placeholders for all four.
28. **(adr) ADR 0010's deployment half** (`4416c3fcd`): rule 4 (nothing published without the owner: the staged artifacts, the `release` environment and `OPENVERIFY_PUBLISH`, Q1-Q4, nothing signed in this release, `pending-owner` labels, the decline outcomes with the `-noguidance` flow image and the install switches off), rule 4a (the five images, the restricted chart, the SBOMs and their licence check, and what the defaults do not cover) and rule 5 (tools pinned, checksummed and signature-checked, packages by lockfile, images by digest with fresh vulnerability databases, the 14 days, no copyleft tooling, the expiring allowlist). `infra/tests/adr-deployment.test.sh` holds them and refuses an affirmed certification, compliance or signature; rule 6 stays integration-11e's.
29. **Android changed in 11C** (the plugin's build lines, three lockfiles and the verification metadata), so integration-11c runs the clean-build pass (Validation 9b, from `./gradlew clean` and one new empty Gradle home, at least an hour after this Stage's cold home) and the four device suites on the phone (orchestrator decisions 2 and 4). contracts-11c changed no SDK source, golden, string, A11yCatalog state, ABI dump or device test.
30. **Plan gaps resolved here.** Every image runs as uid and gid 10001 (one value for every pod's `runAsUser` and `fsGroup`); the models-fetch Job uses the vision image (item 15); a store is "external" for the schema's egress rule once its Secret is set, so the default values lint; the chart's version is the release's, 0.1.0; `external.postgres.key`, `migrationKey`, `valkey.key` and `smtp.key` name the Secret keys by the variables' names; the ingress body limits for Workflow 7B's two larger routes are 28m until chart-policy-11c measures the routes; a new value key needs `values.schema.json` too, which is an 11C shared file after this Stage (a contractDeviation for integration-11c). sast-pii-11c's PII configuration will meet a public service-account identity in item 5, `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` and this Stage's TDD file (kubectl's signer, an address at `k8s-releng-prod.iam.gserviceaccount.com`): not a person, so a reviewed allowlist entry, never a rewording of the identity.

## Workflow 11C Stage 2 amendments

Recorded by `integration-11c` (2026-11-16) from each Stage 2 agent's contractDeviations and handoff, read against the tree at `f482d18ea` and settled in Stage 3 where marked. Each agent's evidence is its `docs/tdd/phase11-<area>.tdd.md`. Where an item here differs from C257-C267 or the Stage 1a amendments, this section wins.

### images-11c

1. **Compiled commands.** `apps/api/tsconfig.commands.json` uses `rootDir: "scripts"` and `outDir: dist/commands` (not the frozen `.`, which emitted a second copy of `src/`); the entries reach `src/` through the package import `#src/*` (`@openverify/source` to `./src/*.ts`, default to `./dist/*.js`). `src/commands/` moved to `src/operator-commands/` and `scripts/migrate.ts` became `scripts/db-migrate.ts`. Nineteen commands are compiled.
2. **Vision's build context.** `docker build --build-context repo=. -t openverify/vision:<tag> services/vision`: the named context `repo` supplies `models/manifest.json` and `scripts/fetch_models.py`; compose passes `additional_contexts: {repo: ..}`; release.yml builds it so.
3. **The flow's run-time origin (C257.1).** Built without `VITE_OPENVERIFY_API_URL`, the bundle's `<meta>` carries `connect-src 'self' https: http:` (not "no connect-src": the `<meta>`'s `default-src 'self'` would otherwise block the API's origin); the server's header narrows it to the API's exact origin. `GET /config.json` answers `{"api-url": "<base URL or ''>"}` with `no-store` (`RUNTIME_API_URL_KEY` in `apps/flow/src/api/runtime-api-url.ts`); `security-meta.test.ts` and `server-headers.test.ts` changed with the plan.
4. **Secrets as files** for the flow server, the console, vision and the runner (orchestrator decision 18): import-free copies of the API's rules in `apps/flow/src/security/secret-files.ts` and `apps/dashboard/src/lib/secret-files.ts` (each server runs from source by type stripping).
5. **The console image** runs its custom `server.ts` over Next's standalone output; `server.ts` hands Next the standalone configuration from `.next/required-server-files.json` when no `next.config.*` is beside it; `.next/cache` links to `/tmp/next-cache`. Built with HSTS 0 (the ingress sends HSTS).
6. **The flow image** uses no `pnpm deploy`: Node, `server.ts`, its four import-free security modules, `@openverify/contracts`' compiled `origin-pattern.js` and the bundle. The no-guidance bundle is built by Vite directly (turbo's strict environment hid `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_FACE_GUIDANCE`); `flow-environment.tsx`'s detector answers null at once when off.
7. **Amendment 14 as built:** `runMigrateCommand` installs the keyring from the resolved environment when `OPENVERIFY_KEK_FILE` is set; `migrationRoleEnv` resolves every `_FILE` and throws `ConfigError`, so `seed:dev` and `kek:rewrap` read files too.
8. **The code runner image** exposes no port (Phase 8's `code-runner.test.sh` keeps "it publishes no port"); the chart's Service names 7700. The tcp: transport's first frame is `{kind: 'auth', token}` (1 KiB, 5 s), compared as SHA-256 digests with `timingSafeEqual`; anything else closes the connection.
9. **Pins as adopted on 2026-11-15:** every digest is Stage 1a item 6's; the Playwright browser server's image is pinned (13 days old the day before). Fifteen Valkey test files share `VALKEY_TEST_IMAGE` (`apps/api/src/test-support/integration-valkey.ts`).
10. **The compose app profile** runs the five images at `11c-local`, each Node service read-only with a tmpfs `/tmp`, `cap_drop: ALL` and `no-new-privileges`; it was proven live in its own project. `compose.test.sh`'s live half started the developer's `openverify` project: settled in Stage 3 (item 5 below).
11. **The e2e images-mode override** puts every app service on `network_mode: host` with the env file's loopback addresses (a test stack only; the compose profile and the chart keep C60.2) and needs `OPENVERIFY_E2E_FLOW_PORT`, `OPENVERIFY_E2E_DASHBOARD_PORT` and `OPENVERIFY_E2E_VISION_PORT` from the driver. On Docker Desktop without its host networking it cannot serve the host (Stage 3 item 3).
12. **Borrowed edits** (each minimal, committed alone): the flow's `server.ts`, `vite.config.ts`, `main.tsx`, `security-meta.ts` and `flow-environment.tsx`; `next.config.ts`; Phase 8's runner (`socket-client.ts`, `socket-server.ts`, `transport.ts`, `index.ts`, `bin/code-runner-server.mjs`); `services/vision/src` (`startup.py`, `settings.py`, `app.py`); `.dockerignore` and `services/vision/.dockerignore`; `compose.test.sh`, `code-runner.test.sh`, `ci-workflow.test.sh` (one line: CI's Node from `api.Dockerfile`); `integration-postgres.ts` and its test; `integration-rustfs.ts` and its test; the two benchmarks' image constants; `migrate.ts`; `kek-rewrap.ts`; `config-registry.ts` (`OPENVERIFY_VISION_MODELS_MANIFEST`); contracts-11c's `egress-config-tcp.test.ts`; `apps/flow/e2e/README.md` and `theme-csp.spec.ts` (the pinned browser server).
13. **New variable:** `OPENVERIFY_VISION_MODELS_MANIFEST` (vision; the image sets `/app/models/manifest.json`; vision refuses to start when a model's SHA-256 differs from its pin).

### scanners-11c

1. **Supporting files:** `infra/scripts/lib/install-common.sh` (the install-gitleaks pattern), `infra/scripts/lib/supply-chain-gate.py` (C259.2's gate), `infra/tests/lib/installer-harness.sh` and `infra/tests/fixtures/supply-chain/*.json` (synthetic recorded reports). The allowlist's test is part of `infra/tests/supply-chain.test.sh`.
2. **Two more entry points** beside the frozen steps: `supply-chain.sh gate <format> ...` and `supply-chain.sh db-age <trivy-version|grype-status> <json>`, for release.yml's image scans; `all` runs neither.
3. **pnpm-audit** runs `pnpm audit --json` and gates at high itself (the same threshold; the summary also gets the mediums and lows, and the allowlist applies). **zizmor** runs `--offline` (no token in the job): its online audits do not run, and the summary says so (an owner question: a read-only token for zizmor only).
4. **Severity rule:** a finding with no severity (all of pip-audit's) fails closed as high.
5. **KP10-52 fixed** (`99fb8b59e`, `a55384f25`): `scripts/fetch_bundletool.py` retries with a doubling delay, fails at once on a 4xx or a mismatch, and keeps a verified cache (`--cache-dir`, `BUNDLETOOL_CACHE_DIR`). Its CI half is settled in Stage 3 (item 2).
6. **The findings on the tree** (pypdf, opentelemetry-api, the Android build tooling and test classpaths, setup-pnpm's `github-env`) and the vision pytest's split into three parts are settled in Stage 3 (items 1, 2 and 9).

### sast-pii-11c

1. **C260.1:** eslint-plugin-security 4.2.0 has a fifteenth rule, `detect-invisible-characters`, on with its reason. The fixes landed while the five noisy rules were at `warn` (a disable directive for an unloaded rule is itself an error); `4a3b24ba5` set every rule's final state and the ceiling 168, 42 and 5 (`detect-unsafe-regex`, `detect-possible-timing-attacks`, `detect-non-literal-fs-filename`). `phase5-rules.js` spreads `securityConfig()` last (OD-11C-1).
2. **C260.3:** the email default also passes the reserved names of RFC 2606 and RFC 6761 (`.test`, `.example`, `.invalid`, `.localhost`); gitleaks 8.30.1 has no Luhn or mod-97 validator, so `security/gitleaks-pii-filter.py` keeps only checksum-valid card and IBAN matches and prints rule, file and line, never the value; the allowlist joins through `[extend]` with each exception under its rule id (8.30.1 ignores `targetRules` in an extended file); generated trees, `e2e/.artifacts/` and `e2e/.env.e2e` are named in the configuration (gitleaks `dir` reads ignored files); the scope covers fixtures, docs, e2e, load, every test file and the seed sources.
3. **Fixes as named borrowed edits:** the transaction number filter's quadratic pattern made linear, test-first; 54 bidirectional characters written as escapes in 12 files; 215 inline suppressions in 173 files, each with its reason; `.githooks/pre-commit`'s second scan; `pre-commit-hook.test.sh` (27 checks).
4. **The reviewed file-system importers** in `eslint-security.test.ts`: a new production module importing `node:fs` must be listed with where its paths come from. Stage 3 added the code runner's entry point (item 6 below).
5. **History allowance:** one IPv6 address just outside 2001:db8::/32 in a since-deleted test file is allowlisted by path, so a full-history scan reads 0.

### chart-11c

1. **Ownership carve-out:** chart-11c owns its own helm-unittest files `deploy/helm/openverify/tests/c263-*_test.yaml` inside chart-policy-11c's `tests/`, so its templates were test-first.
2. **Defaults changed (keys unchanged):** `api.autoscaling.maxReplicas` 6 to 4 (6 replicas with the rolling surge fail C263.4's pool check against the default 100 connections; the defaults use 61); `worker.terminationGracePeriodSeconds` 120 to 960 (above the longest lease in the code, 15 minutes).
3. **The models digest:** the chart carries a byte copy of `models/manifest.json` as `files/models-manifest.json` and mounts `/models/<SHA-256 of the copy>`; chart-checks' lint refuses a copy that differs. The models-fetch logic is the chart's own standard-library program (`files/models-fetch.py`, 15 tests, run as `python3 -I -c` in the vision image with no shell); pvc mode uses the same `/models/<digest>` layout and needs `models.existingClaim`.
4. **Ingress:** the API host is three Ingresses (`/`, `/api/v1/importer`, `/api/v1/transactions`), because a Prefix cannot match inside a transaction file's path; every transaction route gets the transaction-file limit as its outer bound. `gateway.httpRoute` attaches to a Gateway named `<fullname>`; a `parentRefs` key is proposed (an 11C shared file after Stage 1a; carried to 11E's guide with the HTTPRoute example).
5. **clamd** (OD-11C-5): with `worker.extraContainers`, an emptyDir `clamd-socket` at `/run/clamd` and `OPENVERIFY_CLAMD_SOCKET=/run/clamd/clamd.sock`; each extra container gets the restricted context merged over its own.
6. **The runner:** `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_CODE_RUNNER=tcp:<fullname>-code-runner:<port>` on the API and the worker (the API parses it and never connects); the probe `node bin/code-runner-server.mjs --health` checks the tcp: listener (images-11c), proven by the kind runs (7 of 7 pods ready).
7. **The migrate Job:** `db-migrate.js` as the init container, `db-grant-runtime.js <runtimeRole>` as the main one; configuration inline (a pre-install hook runs before the ConfigMaps exist).
8. **Borrowed edit:** contracts-11c's `chart-skeleton.test.sh` positive control uses ReadWriteMany (job mode refuses ReadOnlyMany).

### chart-policy-11c

1. **Borrowed edits:** `chart-skeleton.test.sh`'s frozen helper set gains `openverify.secretSources` and `openverify.caSources`; `values.yaml`'s `ingress.maxBodySize` 12m to 15m (`PATCH /api/v1/cases/:id` takes 14 MiB); the two 28m limits are confirmed against the routes.
2. **TLS required** is the HTTPS redirect plus HSTS on every host, not a render failure without a `tls` entry (that broke chart-11c's cases and valid installs that end TLS at the controller or a load balancer).
3. **HSTS at the ingress** is written for the NGINX controllers (`nginx.org/hsts*`; the community ingress-nginx sends it from its ConfigMap); `openverify.dev/strict-transport-security` records the value for any other controller (11E's guide says how to set it there). No snippet annotation.
4. **The internal path** is a Prefix `/api/v1/internal` to `<fullname>-api-internal-deny`, a Service the chart never creates; no Ingress routes the internal port.
5. **Contract tests** live in `deploy/helm/openverify/ci/tests/` (`chart-checks.test.sh` 47, `kind.test.sh` 72, `chart-policy.test.sh` 44); integration-11c wired them into the supply-chain job. `ci/` holds one install values file (`ci/kind-values.yaml`; `ct install` reads every `ci/*-values.yaml`), the upgrade overlay `ci/upgrade/kind-upgrade-values.yaml` and the render-only `ci/lint/full-values.yaml`.
6. **The kind install** runs in development mode (the smoke seeds with the compiled `seed:dev`, which refuses production); production rendering is covered by `ci/lint/full-values.yaml`, helm-unittest and kubeconform. kubeconform does not check the example HTTPRoute (no CRD schema in the bundles; helm-unittest does).
7. **The kind proof** waited for images-11c's checkpoint `code-runner-tcp` as well as `images` (without it the worker stopped on the stub transport).

### sdk-web-11c

1. **Stage 1a item 19 decided (C266.4):** `@openverify/node`'s tarball carries its private dependencies (`@openverify/contracts`, `core`, `flow-spec`; only the modules its exports reach, 490 files) under `dist/vendor/` with their imports rewritten, so its one dependency is `zod`; 1,577,687 B packed. A test imports the unpacked tarball with only `zod` beside it. The SBOM and licence report list the vendored packages by hand (C261.4).
2. **C266.1, C266.4, C285.1 through a pack tool:** the workspace manifests keep `private: true` and the source condition (`workspace-conventions.test.ts` holds it); `node packages/sdk-react/scripts/pack-release.ts --out <dir> --version <x.y.z> <package dirs>` writes the packed manifest (no private, scripts, dev dependencies or source condition; `@openverify/js` pinned to the release; `publishConfig {access public, provenance true}`), runs `pnpm pack` in a staging directory, and refuses anything but `package.json`, `README.md`, `LICENSE` and `dist/**/*.{js,d.ts}`. release.yml calls it.
3. **Borrowed edits:** `packages/sdk-js/package.json` (publish fields and the `./openverify.iife.js` subpath), `packages/node/package.json` (publish fields), `eslint-security.test.ts` (the demo server's reviewed entry); `apps/demo-web/src/index.ts` restored for `supply-chain-pins.test.ts`.
4. **The demo site** is not a deployed process, so its `OPENVERIFY_DEMO_*` variables are not in `config-registry.ts`; its own Playwright spec runs against a stand-in installation with the e2e package's Playwright. C266.3's "built in the e2e stack" is settled in Stage 3 (item 4).

### sbom-release-11c

1. **Borrowed edits:** `supply-chain-skeletons.test.sh`'s "publish widens no permission" allows release.yml's publish job exactly `contents: read` plus `id-token`, `packages` and `attestations: write`; `android-release.yml` gains the AAR SBOM and licence step, held by `ci-workflow.test.sh`.
2. **C261.1 reading:** each npm SBOM is Syft over the unpacked tarball with `--override-default-catalogers javascript-package-cataloger` and lists the package itself; its dependencies are in `source:pnpm`. **C261.4 limit:** the flow and console bundles compile their npm dependencies into JavaScript no SBOM tool sees; their licences are covered by `source:pnpm` and the ledger.
3. **release.yml's allowlist reader** uses the runner's PyYAML and fails closed without it; per-push checks use `supply-chain.sh`.
4. **Open (blocker, the owner's):** the vision image's `opencv-python-headless` wheel bundles FFmpeg 8.1.1 (LGPL-2.1+), libquadmath and libgfortran, which the ledger never recorded (the Phase 3 research's open decision); the licence check fails `image:vision` on it and release.yml's verify cannot pass until the owner decides (an exception row, or an OpenCV built without FFmpeg and OpenBLAS).
5. **Open (release.yml's C259.2 gate):** every image carries Debian base findings (6 critical and 52 high in each Node image, 19 fixable; 51 high in vision) and the four Node images carry Node 22.22.1 (1 critical, 6 high; fixed in 22.22.2 and later). pypdf's 7 highs are fixed in Stage 3. Moving the bases and Node within rule 3, or reviewed allowlist entries, is needed before a staged run can pass: carried to 11G's release preparation.

## Workflow 11C Stage 3 amendments (integration-11c)

Written by `integration-11c` (2026-11-16), alone and last; evidence `docs/tdd/phase11-integration-11c.tdd.md` (sections 0-5). Every change below is test-first unless it says otherwise.

1. **The fixes the scanners asked for (rule 3).** Vision: pypdf 6.21.4 (2026-10-28, BSD-3-Clause, `LICENSE` blob unchanged) and opentelemetry-api 1.45.2 (2026-10-29, Apache-2.0; it had no ledger row: `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` §2.1.23 adds it); pip-audit clean. Android: `android/build.gradle.kts` raises every reported library in the build-tooling and test configurations to its fixed release as a floor (AGP lint's bcprov 1.85 and guava 33.7.2, Dokka's FreeMarker 2.3.35, jsoup 1.23.2 and the Jackson 2.22.3 family, the test runtimes' protobuf-javalite 3.25.5, jsoup and guava) and forces the plugin classpath's (jdom2 2.0.6.1, jose4j 0.9.6, Jackson 2.22.3, guava 33.7.2-jre, bcprov 1.85, FreeMarker 2.3.35). No release, compile or runtime classpath changed (compared per configuration), the staged POMs are byte-identical and the SBOMs keep 188, 195 and 190 components. The lockfiles and `verification-metadata.xml` were written from one empty Gradle home by the recipe, every gate call with `--write-locks --write-verification-metadata sha256` (AGP creates `androidLintTool` lazily, so only a lint run locks it; `resolveAndLockAll` does not), and the replaced versions' 15 entries were removed (Gradle's writer keeps stale ones, which osv-scanner reads). `android/consumer-check`'s files stay as they are (rule 3): its five findings (its own lint tool and plugin classpath) are reviewed allowlist entries by path until 2027-02-12. osv: 24 failing to 0 (5 allowlisted).
2. **The CI workflow (C259, C267.2, C267.4).** One per-push job `supply-chain` (the 16th key): `permissions: contents: read`, `persist-credentials: false`, no secret or token, `timeout-minutes: 20`; cosign first, then osv-scanner, zizmor, Trivy, Helm 4 and 3, helm-unittest, kubeconform and gitleaks from their installers into `$RUNNER_TEMP/bin`, the verified releases cached on `hashFiles('infra/scripts/install-*.sh', 'infra/scripts/lib/install-common.sh')`, the Kubernetes schema bundles cached on `fetch_k8s_schemas.py`; one step per `supply-chain.sh` step in the frozen order, each with `SUPPLY_CHAIN_TOOLS_DIR` and `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}` (one run shows every finding); then the static halves of 11C's contracts (`supply-chain.test.sh`, `supply-chain-skeletons.test.sh`, the installers' `--offline-only` halves, `release-workflow.test.sh`, `images.test.sh static|pins|override`, `refresh-digests.test.sh`, `adr-deployment.test.sh`, `chart-skeleton.test.sh` with `HELM4`/`HELM3`, the chart's three contracts, the models-fetch program's tests) and security/python's pytest, ruff and `mypy --strict` (contracts-11c item 20). The infra job runs `gitleaks-pii.test.sh` after its gitleaks install (sast-pii-11c). `setup-pnpm` writes nothing to `$GITHUB_ENV` (zizmor `github-env`): the store path is a step output and `COREPACK_ENABLE_DOWNLOAD_PROMPT: '0'` is in ci.yml's top-level env. KP10-52's CI half: the android job and android-release.yml's gate job (a borrowed edit) cache the verified bundletool on `hashFiles('scripts/fetch_bundletool.py')` and fetch with `--cache-dir`. `ci-workflow.test.sh` (521 checks, 0 skipped) holds all of it, fails any workflow using a third-party scanner, chart or SAST action, fails a test script that runs in no job unless named local-only with its reason (only `install-gitleaks.test.sh`), fails a job that starts `e2e:stack:up` while an upload carries `e2e/.artifacts/` without `!e2e/.artifacts/stack.json` (two synthetic cases prove the rule), and holds release.yml's dispatch-only trigger, read-only token, main-only build, `release` environment, `OPENVERIFY_PUBLISH` gate and commit pins. eslint-plugin-security runs in the lint job through `phase5Config()`; no separate step. The e2e job is unchanged (review F03). `release-workflow.test.sh` skips actionlint where it is not installed, as `ci-workflow.test.sh` does (no CI job installs it; adding a tool pin is contracts-11c's).
3. **The e2e images mode (C257.3) is built, and its spec runs are blocked on this host.** `OPENVERIFY_E2E_IMAGES=1` makes `startStack()` (and so `e2e:stack:up`) start the five images through images-11c's override (`e2e/src/images-mode.ts`, 10 tests; `e2e/setup/stack.ts`; `e2e/README.md`): a missing image fails at once with its build command; the env file gains the mode and the three ports; migrate, the API, the worker, vision, the flow, the console and the runner come up with `--no-build --wait`; the API image's compiled `seed:dev --json --mfa-admin --connect` seeds; only the embed client and the sample site are built on the host; each container's log goes to `e2e/.artifacts/logs/<service>.log`; `e2e:stack:down` reads the mode from the env file. On this host the seven services came up healthy in 21 s and the seed ran, but no container port answered: the override's `network_mode: host` is Docker Desktop's virtual machine (4.87.0, WSL2) unless the owner turns on Docker Desktop's host networking, which is off here (a probe on 0.0.0.0 and on 127.0.0.1 was unreachable). The driver names that within 20 s. So 11A's and 11B's specs, 7A's signals, handoff and ip-address-report, 9's flow-embed, ui-preview, flow-bundle and ui-client-address and 8's workflow-code have not run in the images mode; the mode's measured start is 66 s to the diagnostic (infrastructure 7 s, app services 21 s, seed 26 s). A bridge network was not used, because it changes what C257.3 proves (the specs' webhook receiver and OIDC provider listen on the host's loopback; the client-address specs need the peer to be 127.0.0.1). Open for the owner (Docker Desktop's setting) or a Linux Docker engine; recorded with the release gate's items.
4. **C266.3:** the stack builds `apps/demo-web` into `e2e/.artifacts/demo-web` in both modes (`e2e/src/demo-web.ts`). It is not started: running it needs a webhook on the seeded sandbox, which every spec's webhook listing would show, and sdk-web-11c named no full-stack spec. Its own spec runs against a stand-in (`pnpm --filter @openverify/demo-web e2e`).
5. **`compose.test.sh`'s live half** runs as its own project `openverify-compose-test` (`COMPOSE_TEST_PROJECT`; the developer's `openverify` is refused) and removes its own volumes after proving they survive `down` (639 passed; the developer's four volumes untouched).
6. **Defects fixed between Stage 2 areas:** `supply-chain.sh chart` ran `chart-checks.sh` without a subcommand (exit 2); `release-workflow.test.sh` dated its expiry cases in local time while release.yml reads UTC (it failed every evening west of UTC); the gate's path entries never matched an osv finding (it names lockfiles, not one path: a path entry now covers a finding whose every lockfile is under it); `eslint-security.test.ts` lacked the runner's entry point, which reads its token file since the tcp: transport; `supply-chain-skeletons.test.sh` held Stage 1a's empty allowlist (it now holds each entry's shape).
7. **Measurements (rule 27: estimates).** The supply-chain job emulated locally from ci.yml (`CI=true`, an empty tool cache, a PATH without the developer's tools, each step under the e2e lock): 134 s of run steps (installs 33 s, checks 66 s, contracts and Python 38 s); with the setup, an estimate of 4 to 7 minutes against 20 (no split on an estimate). The lint job with the plugin: peak RSS 4.17 GB for `@openverify/api` (237 s) and 3.65 GB for `@openverify/contracts`, both passing under the 4 GB heap (the earlier 3.35 and 2.85 GB record no method). release.yml: sbom-release-11c's 35 to 45 minutes, unmeasured. The CI baseline stays Stage 1a item 2's. The measured times, release.yml's first staged run and android-release.yml's first run stay open (KP10-59); KP10-51 and KP11-4 stay open until the android job's first whole measured run.
8. **The Validation as run** (section 3 of the TDD file): the plan's order, with the 11A and 11B splits kept and these changes: vision's pytest in three parts (files 1-37, 38-74, 75-110; coverage appended, the 80% gate on the last), because the second of two halves reached the 10-minute cap; the image builds are the five production images and the no-guidance flow; the infra tests are 32 scripts (`images.test.sh` as its nine subcommands) and the chart's three contracts; 11C's steps follow (`supply-chain.sh` per step, `chart-checks.sh` per step, `kind.sh` per subcommand on 1.35 then 1.37, gitleaks, the PII filter over the tree and history); the clean-build pass (9b) ran once from a second empty Gradle home, about three and a half hours after the first; the device suites ran last (22 of 22).
9. **Known items.** KP10-52's CI half done (item 2). KP10-51 and KP11-4: open, with the android job's first measured run (KP10-59). Every other carried item stays with the Workflow or owner its detail names (the PROGRESS draft lists them). Failures under load: none met in this Validation.

## Workflow 11D Stage 1a amendments (contracts-11d)

Recorded by `contracts-11d` (claude-opus-5-5) on 2026-11-16, alone on the machine, at 11D's start `69fd529c0` (base `d44e537db`). The evidence is `docs/tdd/phase11-contracts-11d.tdd.md`; the facts with `path:line` are section 4 of `docs/research/phase11-inventory.md`. Each item binds every later 11D agent and wins over the contract text it amends.

1. **The base.** PROGRESS at `d44e537db` records Workflow 11C done; `origin/main` holds it. The 11C push's run 43843705201 started no step in any of its 19 jobs (as the four before it), so no CI failure is 11D's first fix (rules 20, 25). Nothing on the measured path changed from `4758c01d5` to the base.
2. **Drift.** (a) 6D's published report replaces the Starting point's 6C figures (inventory 4.2). (b) `seed:dev`'s `requireMfa: false` is at `apps/api/src/seed/dev-seed.ts:525`. (c) The compose `app` profile runs one database role, `OPENVERIFY_TRUST_PROXY=false` and no memory limit on api, worker, flow or dashboard; the load override supplies the 11D decisions 12, 13 and 20 (item 9). (d) C269.1's workflow row compares with the executor's own figures (10A: 19/50/48 effectful and 96/206/205 control-flow steps a second at 1/8/32 clients; 11C: 17/46/48 effectful), never with the 1,577 pgbench figure. (e) No off-by-default switch registry existed; item 7 adds one.
3. **C271's condition: `missed`, all three budgets** (`docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6d.md:291-297`, `:691-695`; PROGRESS `:1500-1516`):
   - C78.7 at 100,000 names: 6D head 180.9 ms against 100 ms, gap +80.9 ms; phase p95 state / retrieval / loads / rest 3.8 / 60.9 / 43.5 / 73.1 ms.
   - C78.7 at 2,000,000 names: 808.0 ms against 250 ms, gap +558.0 ms; 6.0 / 594.8 / 58.3 / 181.5 ms (retrieval in the 24 cap-reaching and 30 out-of-reach searches of 300).
   - The re-screen at 1,000 changed entries and 200,000 subjects: 71.2 s after migration 0022 (74.3 s before) against 60 s, gap +11.2 s; the screen phase 44.5-44.8 s, a fixed part of about 25 s (subjects 5.9-6.1, specs 4.9-5.2, prepare 7.4-7.5, build 4.5-4.6 s).
   So `matcher-c787-11d` starts. Whatever it achieves, `calibration-matcher-11d` measures all three at 11D's base and head (C271.3, review H09).
4. **C271's candidates** (6D Stage 1 and 2 amendments items 11-13, rescreen-6d item 9, review amendments item 5), each behind its own lever (item 7), with the files it would touch:
   - `retrieval-reach` (11(a)): the cap-reaching and out-of-reach pair lookups at 2,000,000 names: `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlist-reads.ts` (`retrieveCandidates` and its statements), an index only through 11D's migration (item 8). The keys (`packages/watchlist/src/keys/`) and every C78.4 constant stay unchanged, and no lookup may lose a candidate a full lookup keeps (no recall traded: 6C's key-bound lookup lost 3 listed namesakes and was refused).
   - `stored-name-analysis` (11(b)): each list name's analysis stored at sync time and read with the entry, so screening does not analyse it again: a column of `watchlist_names` in 11D's migration, `apps/api/src/watchlists/records.ts` and `sync.ts`'s write, `rekey.ts` (recomputed with the keys, tied to `keys_version`), `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlists.ts` (`writeWatchlistEntries`, `replaceWatchlistEntryParts`), `watchlist-reads.ts` (`loadEntries`), and `packages/watchlist/src/names/` (seeding the analysed-name cache from a stored analysis that equals a fresh one by test).
   - `rescreen-bound-memo` (11(c), rescreen-6d 9(a)): a token-pair memo passed to `nameScoreUpperBound` by the re-screen's name-bound check: `apps/api/src/jobs/reports/rescreen.ts` and `packages/watchlist/src/score/` (an optional memo parameter; every bound and created match identical at every preset).
   - `rescreen-prepared-index` (11(c), rescreen-6d 9(c)): the re-screen's fixed part, a prepared reverse index kept between re-screens of one source and version, or worker threads per chunk: `apps/api/src/jobs/reports/rescreen.ts`, `packages/watchlist/src/reverse-index/`, `packages/db/src/repositories/report-rescreen.ts`, under `rescreen-check`'s no-miss rule.
   Not candidates: namesake grouping and `SET LOCAL jit = off` in `loadEntries` (measured as unsupported, `phase-6.plan.md:1929`) and `token-memo` (removed, `:1927`).
5. **Seeds (6D Stage 1 and 2 amendments item 12).** No owner decision answers "C78.7 over several seeds". The method uses one fresh seed, drawn after the method commit as 6D's was, and the report states the seed dependence beside the 6C gate seed's figure (the gate's three takes). **Open owner question, carried to the release checklist with the budgets:** whether a later calibration judges C78.7 over several seeds (the spread, or a percentile over seeds).
6. **Q12: pending, Locust not adopted.** PROGRESS (`:18`, `:97`) and the plan's owner sections record Q12 as pending; no grant is recorded before this Stage 1a. The orchestrator passed "pending", which agrees. `load/` is built on vegeta and the `httpx` driver; no ZPL-2.1 component and no libzmq enter.
7. **The switches, each off by default** (C270.4, C271.2; review H27). One registry, `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlist-switches.ts` (exported by `@openverify/db`; owner `matcher-write-11d`; the C271 levers' bodies are `matcher-c787-11d`'s), held by `watchlist-switches.test.ts` (every switch off and every tuning `null` by default, toggling restores, out-of-range tunings refused):
   - **Boolean switches**, `WATCHLIST_PHASE11_SWITCHES`, turned on and off with `useWatchlistSwitches({ <name>: true })` (it returns a restore function) and read with `watchlistSwitchEnabled(<name>)`:
     - `incremental-key-df` (C270.1; owner `matcher-write-11d`): the sync updates the key frequencies by its delta in the same transaction instead of `rebuildWatchlistKeyDf`;
     - `gin-fastupdate-off` (C270.3, option 1; owner `matcher-write-11d`): the benchmark's own database runs `ALTER INDEX … SET (fastupdate = off)` on the two name GIN indexes as the owner before the sync step (the runtime role cannot); shipping it is a migration (item 12);
     - `retrieval-reach`, `stored-name-analysis`, `rescreen-bound-memo`, `rescreen-prepared-index` (C271, item 4; owner `matcher-c787-11d`).
   - **Tunings**, `WatchlistWriteTuning`, `null` = off, set with `useWatchlistWriteTuning({ … })` (restore function) and read with `watchlistWriteTuning()`:
     - `rekeyBatch` (C270.2; `matcher-write-11d`): the re-key's batch size, overriding `REKEY_BATCH` (500) for a measurement; an integer 1-100,000. The chosen size is committed as `REKEY_BATCH` with its basis after the calibration (fix-11d), and the override stays a measurement tool;
     - `ginPendingListLimitKb` (C270.3, option 2): the sync's transaction runs `SET LOCAL gin_pending_list_limit = <n>` (kB, at least 64);
     - `ginCleanEveryBatches` (C270.3, option 3): the sync calls `openverify_gin_clean_pending_lists(source_id)` every N written batches (N at least 1).
   - **The benchmark** toggles them as it toggles `SCREENING_SHORTCUTS`: `benchmark:performance --step <scale>-shares --switch <name>` for a boolean switch on a screening step, and the write steps `matcher-write-11d` adds take `--switch <name>` and `--tune <rekey-batch|gin-pending-list-limit|gin-clean-every>=<n>`; `switchOf` (`benchmark-performance.mjs:999-1023`) imports the registry when the step runs, so the base without it still runs every other step. **A test** turns one on with `useWatchlistSwitches` in a `try … finally` (or `onTestFinished`) that calls the restore function. No `OPENVERIFY_*` variable and no settings member is added for them in 11D: a switch the calibration keeps is turned on in code by `fix-11d` (its default flips, with its test), and members a type the benchmark imports gains stay optional (rule 16).
8. **The migration.** 11D needs none to measure: C270's options run in the benchmark's own database (`ALTER INDEX` as its owner), a session setting, or 11B's function. 11D's one migration, if a change ships DDL, is `packages/db/drizzle/0037_phase11d_watchlist.sql` with its `meta` snapshot and journal entry (the base's newest is `0036_phase11_hardening`), its table definitions in `packages/db/src/tables/watchlists.ts` (re-exported by `schema.ts`), and no `CREATE EXTENSION` (no pgvector). **Owner: `matcher-write-11d`**, which commits a checkpoint "migration" either way (with the file, or recording "none needed"); after that checkpoint `matcher-c787-11d` creates or extends the same file for `stored-name-analysis`'s column, as its named borrowed edit. If the calibration takes `gin-fastupdate-off`, `fix-11d` adds the `ALTER INDEX` to 0037 (creating it if nobody did), test-first with the plan test. No second migration in 11D.
9. **The load stack's configuration** (C268.4; decisions 12, 13, 20 before 11D; KP11-37 is `load-harness`'s): compose project `openverify-load`, `docker compose -p openverify-load -f infra/docker-compose.yml -f infra/load/docker-compose.load.yml --env-file /tmp/openverify-load/load.env --profile app …`, brought up and down only under the e2e lock by whoever takes. The env file (mode 0600, generated by `load-harness`'s tooling, never committed or logged) holds the ports, all on 127.0.0.1 and below the ephemeral range the e2e stack draws from: `POSTGRES_PORT` 25432, `REDIS_PORT` 26379, `S3_PORT` 29000, `S3_CONSOLE_PORT` 29001, `MAILPIT_SMTP_PORT` 21025, `MAILPIT_UI_PORT` 28025, `OPENVERIFY_API_PORT` 23000, `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_PORT` 25173, `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_PORT` 23001. The override: `POSTGRES_DB=openverify_load`; `pg_stat_statements` preloaded; migrate runs `db-migrate.js` with `OPENVERIFY_MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL`, then `db-grant-runtime.js openverify_runtime`, and the API and worker run as `openverify_runtime`; its own bridge network with a fixed subnet whose gateway is `OPENVERIFY_TRUST_PROXY` (never `true`, never 127.0.0.1); the console's trusted proxy hops 1, so the documentation-range address a generator sends in `X-Forwarded-For` is the client's (labelled in the report); `OPENVERIFY_WEBHOOK_ALLOW_PRIVATE_NETWORKS=true` with the receiver where the worker reaches it; development mode; the sandbox `deviceIntegrity` provider bound for sandbox environments (as `e2e/src/env.ts`), so the flow asks for its challenge and token; the default network sources imported from synthetic files at the measured sizes (`packages/signals/src/testing/`), City Lite off; memory limits as the chart's defaults (api 1Gi, worker 1536Mi, dashboard 512Mi, flow 256Mi, vision 4Gi, code-runner 1Gi; City Lite's addition of about 168 MiB at rest and 116 MiB at a swap per API or worker process named in the method), no CPU limit, `OPENVERIFY_WORKER_CONCURRENCY` 4; **the core split**: every container `cpuset: "0-9"`, the generators on logical CPUs 10 and 11 (one physical core: vegeta `taskset -c 10`, the driver and the sampler `taskset -c 11`). It raises no rate limit; the fairness scenario's ceiling (item 13) is its one labelled configuration. `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_UN_OPT_IN_ALLOWED` stays false (Q10). The images are the `11c-local` tags rebuilt from 11D's head, each digest recorded.

If none keeps screening during a 2,000,000-name sync within 2 times idle, none is taken and the figures are reported (H27).

Basis: the default ceiling equals the default cap (50), so "at the install ceiling" needs a raised ceiling; 200 is four times the default share, and 2,000 runs (10 times 200) are submitted at the flooder's 5 a second within 400 s, inside one take. The default-cap variant runs with the shipped values.

Each calibration agent runs that test before committing its report. 11E's generator reads the block, never the prose.

No new job (decision 49). `fix-11d` makes the edit.

Both stubs exit 2 with "not built yet" until Stage 2 fills them. seed:load's interface: `pnpm --filter @openverify/api seed:load --scale <small|reference> --step <organisations|principals|accounts|inquiries|lists|transactions|graph|connect|watchlists|verify> [--part <n>] [--secrets-file <path>] [--json]`, resumable, each call under 10 minutes (the 2,000,000-name import in `--part 1` to `8`); it refuses production mode and any database not named `openverify_load`, runs as the migration role through `migrationRoleEnv`, reads only registered variables, and writes the generated secrets (API keys, passwords, TOTP secrets) only to `--secrets-file`, a new mode-0600 file under `/tmp`. `apps/api/scripts/seed-load.ts` joins every rule's path in `security/gitleaks-pii.toml` (KP11-35); its values follow sast-pii-11c's rule (example.com addresses, documentation ranges, 555-01xx numbers, the generators' names).

The address key's per-country handling (W, Ct, Pl) is the next release's (it needs labelled pairs; `LIST_KEYS_VERSION` and the graph's address key would change); the PDF page count of transaction files is 11B's `ingestion-11b`'s, not 11D's.

## Workflow 11D Stage 2 and 3 amendments

Recorded by the review's verifier (2026-11-17) from each Stage 2 and Stage 3 agent's contract deviations, read with its `docs/tdd/phase11-<agent>.tdd.md`. Each item is binding on `fix-11d` and every later Workflow, and where it differs from C268-C272 or the Workflow 11D Stage 1a amendments, this section wins. The Stage 1a amendments (contracts-11d) stay as written above.

### load-harness

1. **Expected statuses follow the API's answers.** `console-login` expects 201 and 202: the password step answers 202 when TOTP is required, and the TOTP step answers 201. `screening-throughput` and `fairness` expect 200 and 201.
2. **`console-login` drives the API's dashboard session routes directly.** It does not go through the console UI.
3. **File access goes through an injected port.** No `src/` module may import `node:fs`, and `apps/api/scripts` is at its `detect-non-literal-fs-filename` ceiling. So `seed-load.ts` takes its file operations from `apps/api/src/seed/load/files.test-support.ts` (`nodeFiles`), `network-benchmark.ts` touches no file, and the runners `load/mmdb/*.mjs` read the files.
4. **Coverage scope.** `load/pyproject.toml` stays frozen, so `run.py`, `receiver.py` and `load/mmdb/*.mjs` are outside the pytest coverage list. They are still tested: `test_run.py`, `test_receiver.py`, and `apps/api/src/network-benchmark.test.ts` for the runners.
5. **Verification takes upload synthetic noise PNGs**, not the manifest captures. The face match therefore decides "no face", and the take times submit to decision, not a pass.
6. **Connect redemptions.** The source inquiries carry no verifications and no files; this replaces Stage 1a item 10's "three verifications and six files each". Each redemption POSTs a source consent, the share token, a destination consent, then the redeem with meta `destination-consent-record-id` and `inquiry-template-id` set to the `load-b` template, because one consent record backs one share token only.
7. **Seeded console users share one synthetic password**, hashed once, so the seed fits its parts. TOTP secrets are per user. Both live only in the 0600 secrets record.
8. **The API limiter is GCRA** (one request every 60/limit s, burst equal to the whole limit). A take of 10 a second for 60 s from one key therefore shows at most one or two refusals. The refusal take's length is the calibration's method decision (`load-phase11.md` amendment 8 chose 120 s). No limit is raised.
9. **New files outside the listed paths**, each a test or module of a listed tool: `apps/api/src/services/graph/benchmark.ts` (the R007 shape option), `synthetic-dense.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/analytics-benchmark-cases.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/network-benchmark.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/lists-benchmark.test.ts` (Vitest runs only `src/**`), and `packages/checks/src/calibration/face-list.ts` with its test.
10. **Named borrowed edits:**
    - `apps/api/src/commands-build.test.ts`: `UNLISTED_DEVELOPMENT_SCRIPTS` gains `lists-benchmark.ts` and `network-benchmark.ts` (9f45b4723);
    - `infra/tests/images.test.sh`: the absent-tool loop gains both (a9a8ac32d);
    - `packages/checks/scripts/calibration-report.ts`: `--face-list` (8bed596e8);
    - `apps/api/scripts/graph-benchmark.ts` and `src/services/graph/synthetic.ts`: R007;
    - `apps/api/scripts/analytics-benchmark.ts`: the 10A cases.
11. **`apps/api/src/seed/load/**` is compiled into the API image** as unreferenced code, because it sits under `src/`. `seed-load.ts` itself stays out of the image. Excluding the folder in `apps/api/tsconfig.build.json` is `fix-11d`'s.
12. **`analytics:benchmark` seals the cases' fields once per batch.** The `cases_fields_until_redacted` constraint requires sealed fields.
13. **`seed:load` stages `list.updated` once per seeded list** through `stageListUpdated` with the outbox-only publisher (154bca607), as the F20 guard requires. After the lists step, a stack's worker therefore delivers `list.updated` events and list re-screen requests.
14. **Method item (a) is not built.** That item is a synthetic network import into the load stack to size City Lite. The stack keeps `dbip-city-lite` off. The override's header comment states the method's figures (+168 MiB at rest and +116 MiB at a swap, per process); they are not measured here.
15. **The face-list FPIR.** It is `1 - (1 - FAR(t))^N` over independent non-mates. FAR is the identity-pair-weighted impostor rate, and the bootstrap resamples identities, with impostor pair groups weighted by `count_a × count_b`. The threshold is the lowest point on a 0.30-0.95 grid (step 0.01) whose FPIR upper end at a gallery of 10,000 is at most 1%. FNIR is the share of genuine pairs below that threshold. The seed is required and has no default.

### matcher-write-11d

1. **Every switch and tuning ships off.** Stage 1a item 7 and `watchlist-switches.test.ts` bind that default, and they win over the area's "turn each on when complete". The head measures them only through the benchmark's `--switch` and `--tune`; `fix-11d` flips what the calibration keeps, each change with its test.
2. **Borrowed edits:**
    - `packages/watchlist/tsconfig.json`'s include gains `scripts/benchmark-write.mjs`;
    - `packages/watchlist/scripts/benchmark-vitest.config.mjs`'s `BENCHMARK_HELPERS` gains the same file, because `benchmark-gates.test.mjs` requires every tested benchmark module to be listed;
    - `benchmark-performance.mjs` gains its header, the flags `--as`, `--take`, `--tune` (repeatable) and `--heartbeat`, `writeKitOf`, the dispatch to the write steps, and `switchOf` learning `WATCHLIST_PHASE11_SWITCHES`.
3. **The write steps are split across two files.** `benchmark-write.mjs` holds the pure helpers, which are tested. `benchmark-write-steps.mjs` holds the steps, imports `apps/api` and `packages/db`, has no unit test, and is checked only by `benchmark:typecheck`, because `benchmark-gates.test.mjs` keeps API-reaching modules out of the package tsconfig.
4. **The write steps read named snapshots.** The calibration takes `2m-snapshot --as before-last` between `2m-import --part 7` and `--part 8`, `2m-snapshot --as table` after part 8, and `100k-snapshot --as table` after `100k-import` and before the re-screen steps. Method item 9 (screening during the last part's sync) runs on copies of `before-last`.
5. **With `incremental-key-df` on, the sync skips the rebuild's opening ANALYZE** of `watchlist_entries` and `watchlist_names`. The sync's own ANALYZEs (at 500, 2,000, 8,000 and more written rows) and the final GIN clean stay. The switch's on/off share therefore includes the change in when planner statistics are refreshed.
6. **C270.1's identity "at both scales"** is the step `<scale>-keydf-identity`. Its variant-group operation switches the source to no groups (`variantGroupsFrom([], 'c'.repeat(64))`), which re-keys every entry, so at 2,000,000 names it is a long take under the background exception. The final screenings of both tables use those same groups.
7. **Tests written after their GREEN.** Some tests hold on either code path, as the property requires: the Testcontainers cases in `key-df-incremental.integration.test.ts`, `rekey-batch.test.ts`'s identity and resumable cases, and `sync-key-df.test.ts`'s sequence case. Each was shown able to fail with a local mutant (the recount disabled), and each file's RED was its own behaviour test.
8. **Checkpoints 3 and 2 share one gate**, run on b3a927aca. They were committed in the order 1, 3, 2, so `sync.ts` was finished before `rekey.ts`.
9. **`WATCHLIST_KEY_DF_APPLY_KEYS` = 50,000** (`packages/db/src/repositories/watchlist-key-df.ts`): the tracker applies its delta once it holds that many keys, which bounds its memory. The value is a choice, not a measurement, and any value gives the same table.
10. **No migration from this agent.** The checkpoint "migration" (8f22439aa) records that none is needed. `0037_phase11d_watchlist.sql` is still free; `runtime-role.ts` is unchanged, because no new table is ANALYZEd, and `db:grant-runtime` need not run again.
11. **Measurement order.**
    - `keydf-sync` runs its untimed restores with the switch on, and the add and the restore share one `publishedAt`, because the rollback guard refuses only an older publication.
    - The first `rekey` call only digests the snapshot (exit 3), so no take's peak memory holds that work. Each record carries the baseline memory before the re-key, and that baseline includes the loaded variant groups.

### matcher-c787-11d

1. **Each lever ships off by default, fully wired in the commit that completes it.** `retrieval-reach` was completed in 4f0340db9, and `rescreen-bound-memo` in 1abc65dfe and 24a86813f. Stage 1a item 7 holds the default off. `fix-11d` flips a default only for a lever the calibration keeps.
2. **`stored-name-analysis` is not built in 11D's Stage 2.**
    - The profile supports it at 2,000,000 names: the uncached list-name analysis is 3.4 s of 13.4 s of `screenCandidates`, and nothing at 100,000 names.
    - Its borrowed edits on `sync.ts` and `rekey.ts` waited for `matcher-write-11d`'s checkpoints 3 and 2, which had not landed.
    - Whoever builds it later:
      - adds `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlist-rows.ts` (`loadEntries`' columns) to their ownership list;
      - revives a stored analysis through `nameToken(raw, role, type)`, because the scorer's token-pair shortcut and the bound memo key list tokens by identity;
      - shows the revived form is cheaper than the analysis (`parseRow` alone is 1.8 s a round at 2,000,000 names);
      - tests the upgrade path (`watchlists:rekey`).
3. **`rescreen-prepared-index` is not built.**
    - The fixed part is about 29 s of a 65 s re-screen at 1,000 entries (subjects 8.1, specs 6.8, prepare 8.3, build 5.7 s).
    - An index kept between re-screens cannot show in the benchmark's one-process-per-step re-screen, and it needs an invalidation rule held by `rescreen-check`'s no-miss rule.
    - Worker threads per chunk need the decrypted subjects in each thread.
    - Both options are carried with these figures.
4. **`rescreen-bound-memo`'s scope is wider than its name.** The token-pair memo alone moved nothing measurable, so the lever also keeps each name pair's whole bound across subjects (24a86813f). The subject name is keyed by its tokens' content in order, and the list name by identity.
    - `ScorerWork` gains the additive member `bounds` (531f568e8).
    - `Unit` gains an optional `contentKey`.
    - `nameScoreUpperBound` gains an optional fifth parameter, `memo`.
    - No signature the benchmark imports changed (rule 16).
5. **Named borrowed edit on `benchmark-performance.mjs`** (fcf5b7d3f, after `matcher-write-11d` checkpoint 1). `rescreen-<n>` takes `--switch <name>`, validated through `switchOf`; the switch is on for that one re-screen, and the record gains the member `switch` (a name or null). The test is the new `packages/watchlist/scripts/benchmark-rescreen-switch.test.mjs`. It is not a helper module, so neither the tsconfig nor `BENCHMARK_HELPERS` changes.
6. **The development profile used scratch tools outside git** under `/tmp/m787`, all since removed, beside the agent's own seed (`11f9c3676b33c9efbcd5fff666143c96`, drawn after the method commit):
    - a switch wrapper;
    - a per-statement probe with `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)`;
    - statement variants;
    - a `CREATE DATABASE … TEMPLATE` snapshot.
    Every timing from them is development context, never a result.
7. **Rule 8, as run.** Two calls passed the 10-minute cap at loads of 18 to 22 and were moved to the background by the harness: the watchlist suite (616 s) and the API's forced typecheck plus lint (672 s). Both were awaited and both exited 0.

### calibration-load-11d

1. **Borrowed edit to the seed, before any take it affects.** In `apps/api/src/seed/load/watchlists.ts`, `allowsMassRemoval(part)` is true only for part 2 and is passed to `syncWatchlistSource` as `allowMassRemoval`, because part 2 replaces the 100,000-name table with the first eighth of the 2,000,000-name one and the sync had refused it as a mass removal. Commits: 1b2119e00, RED b00f35d2c, GREEN 155d2e9d1. This is the report's method amendment 1.
2. **Borrowed edit to vegeta's targets, before the first `lists-match` take.** `load/vegeta/targets.py`'s `rate_flag` writes a whole rate as `-rate=<n>/1s` and a fractional one as `-rate=<rate × 1000>/1000s`, because vegeta exits 2 on `-rate=2.5/1s`. Commits: RED d84439d98, GREEN 3a89c40ca.
3. **The report's method amendments 2-10 bind as written**, each committed before the first take it governs:
    - (2) the nine default network sources are imported from synthetic publisher files at the measured sizes;
    - (3) every take passes `--offset` from a per-pool cursor;
    - (4) the step-up tops: `connect-redeem` 2×, `lists-match` 2×, `graph-queries` 1×, none for `lists-import` and `account-export`, and verification 0.25-4 sessions a second;
    - (5) screening throughput is `reports.completed_at` inside the window per hour, and concurrency 1 is the worker restarted with `OPENVERIFY_WORKER_CONCURRENCY=1`;
    - (6) the soak's knee is the lowest component knee multiple × 160 a second;
    - (7) and (10) the jit comparison alternates on and off with a restart before each take, with six new takes each for reads and writes, and screening at 6 runs a second;
    - (8) the API limiter half runs 120 s at 10 a second;
    - (9) the fairness takes run 0 s of warm-up and 150 s measured (450 s at "ceiling 200").
4. **The step-up rule as read.**
    - A step that is invalid only on the generator's side (lag or CPU) is retaken like a take.
    - A step fails on its p95 against the budget, or against twice the lowest step's p95, or on unexpected outcomes.
    - The `api-reads` step-up ran one step past its first failure.
    - The 100,000-name concurrency-4 screening step-up ran 1, 2, 3, 4 and 6 times.
5. **Untaken retakes leave a row `invalid`, not `not-measured`.** This applies to `webhook-first-attempt`, verification, `account-export`, `lists-import` and the login half. The soak was not retaken, because one driver CPU saturates at 56 requests a second, so an identical retake would be invalid the same way.
6. **Scratch tooling outside git** (`/tmp/cl11d/tools/`) wraps `run.py`, `sampler.py`, `receiver.py` and `report.py`, as described in the TDD file's section 5.
    - The webhook receiver ran on CPU 10.
    - Server p95s are `sampler.py server-times` over the measured window and the measured step's routes.
7. **The soak's driver was stopped by hand at minute 58** after it saturated. The take has no take file, so its memory and queue figures come from the sampler and from the drain check after the stop.
8. **The role-timeouts row is provisional** in `load-phase11.md`, because C272.2 takes the longest legitimate statement of either calibration. `calibration-matcher-11d` recorded that statement as 183,493 ms (`insert into watchlist_key_df`, the 2,000,000-name sync's full rebuild). By this report's formula that gives `statement_timeout` = 370 s, with `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` unchanged at 60 s. Writing it into the report is `fix-11d`'s.
9. **`city-lite-verify` is not measured.** The method's one fetch command (`scripts/fetch_data.py`) got HTTP 403 from DB-IP, and no other fetch is allowed, so the file was neither downloaded nor re-pinned.
10. **The API's whole unit suite was not re-run after the one-function seed edit.** Its forced typecheck and lint, the seed's unit and Testcontainers files, and `list-writes.test.ts` were re-run and pass. `fix-11d`'s Validation runs the whole suite.
