### calibration-matcher-11d

1. **The load wait before a take** (`watchlist-phase11.md` amendment 8, ed77985af). Each idle benchmark container's 250 ms `pg_isready` probe adds about 1 to the 1-minute load on this host. So:
    - the wait before a take is the method's threshold (2.5 or 2.0), plus 1 for each benchmark container up beyond the first, for at most 20 minutes;
    - the timing rule's "load at most 4 at start and end" is unchanged;
    - at most two benchmark containers are up during any timing;
    - `head-m1` was torn down, and its size-100 memo take (28.5 s) is listed and not used.
2. **`rescreen-bound-memo`'s share** (amendment 4) is measured on extra head databases (`c11d-head-m*`). Each on/off pair of one size runs back to back in one lock hold, because a re-screen take needs a fresh database. The share comes from the pairs r2/m2, r3/m3 and r4/m4. It is measured at the 100,000-name scale only, because the re-screen runs on that table only.
3. **Amendments 2, 3, 5, 6 and 7** (eb86f55c3):
    - non-timing steps run side by side: the 2,000,000-name import parts 1-4, the quality steps, and the six workers;
    - the write steps' snapshots are named;
    - the long-take exception covers the 2,000,000-name write steps;
    - write takes keep the timing rule with a 2.0 wait and rotate their order;
    - the head's benchmark container ran `log_min_duration_statement = 1000` (logging only) for its last import part, then reset it, to record the sync's longest statement.
4. **The head's first 1,000-entry re-screen take ended at load 4.37**, so it is invalid and was retaken on the fresh database `c11d-head-r5`. That database's 100- and 300-entry takes are listed and not used.
5. **C270 at 2,000,000 names is not measured.**
    - This covers C270.1's identity and timing, C270.2's 2,000,000-name confirmation, C270.3's three GIN options and C269.4's screening during a sync.
    - Each of those steps reads a copy made by `CREATE DATABASE … TEMPLATE` in `benchmark-write-steps.mjs` (`copyDatabase`). On this host, every copy of the 2.3 GB database ended the Postgres backend with exit code 2 after 600-720 MB: six attempts in all, with FILE_COPY and with WAL_LOG.
    - The benchmark may not change after the seed, so nothing was fixed in Stage 3.
    - `fix-11d` replaces the copy, test-first in `benchmark-write.test.mjs` (for example `pg_dump -Fc` once and `pg_restore` per take). It then runs the blocked steps with the handoff's commands and the method's rules, and records the results in `watchlist-phase11.md`.
    - Until then, `incremental-key-df` stays off, `REKEY_BATCH` stays 500, and no GIN option is taken.
6. **`lists-phase11.md` amendments 1-5** (52c905d4b):
    - one seed serves both reports;
    - the 100,000-item and 1,000,000-item lists are one list grown;
    - `--measure` prints no planted namesakes, digests, unseal share or memory, so each timing take ran under `/usr/bin/time -v` for its maximum RSS;
    - the planted and unlisted probes, the per-probe digests and the unseal share came from a scratch tool outside git that imports the benchmark's own helpers and `matchLists`;
    - the cold list ran at `--faces 10000`.
    Adding those outputs to `lists-benchmark.ts` is `fix-11d`'s.
7. **The summary block's verdict enum has no "recommended".**
    - The fuzzy-name-cap row reads `reported` (configuration "cap 5000 at 1,000,000 items"), with the recommendation in its title.
    - The face-list-threshold row reads `missed` (0.5's FPIR upper end 0.214 against 0.01), with 0.72 recommended in its title.
    - The rekey-batch row reads `not-measured`, with the 100,000-name choice in its title and configuration.
8. **The watchlist end gate** ran as the package test script's own command (`vitest run --coverage`), with its own `--coverage.reportsDirectory` through the heavy-call script, per rules 8 and 21.

## Workflow 11D review amendments

Recorded by `fix-11d` (claude-opus-5-5), 2026-11-17 and 2026-11-18, alone on the machine, from
`64cf94ad3`. The evidence is `docs/tdd/phase11-fix-11d.tdd.md`; the figures are the three
calibration reports, each amended in place (`load-phase11.md` amendment 11, `watchlist-phase11.md`
amendment 9, `lists-phase11.md` findings 1 and 2). Each item binds 11E onward and wins over the
contract text and the Stage 2 and 3 amendments it changes. No budget, margin, method rule, C78.4
constant or preset moved; every matcher and write-path change kept every created match identical
(the identity steps and the tests named below).

1. **P001, fairness (C251.5, C269.2).** The two "met" fairness rows were takes whose flood never
   reached the cap and whose victim was floored by the 2 s poll; they are `invalid`. The harness now
   opens the flood with 500 runs at 50 a second (inside the `reports` GCRA burst), times the victim
   by its reports' own `created-at` to `completed-at` (`PlannedRequest.measure`, `Outcome.server_ms`,
   a duration only), and `sampler.py admissions` shows per window which environment's runs waited
   for a slot; a flood take without one is invalid (`report.flood_reached_cap`). Retaken: **missed
   at the default cap** (victim p95 2,926.0 ms against idle 110.0 ms, ratio 26.6, gap +24.6; every
   flood take reached the cap) and **not measured at "ceiling 200"** (one valid take of five, 3,544.0
   ms against 106.0; every attempt reached the cap, 33 to 61 times idle). The per-environment cap
   bounds the flooder's in-flight runs but not the victim's wait behind them on the shared queue.
2. **P002, the webhook row.** An event created in the measured window that the receiver has not seen
   120 s after the take makes the take invalid and the p95 unknown (`run.py`'s `webhook_figures`,
   `--receiver-drain`); warm-up events are outside the window. The published take re-read: 6,037 of
   15,000 events missing, invalid on that alone.
3. **P003, discard rules 1 and 2 in the committed harness.** `run.py take` waits for the idle
   baseline (load at most 4, no queue depth, 15 minutes; otherwise not started, a `.not-started.json`,
   exit 9) and invalidates a take during which a container's restart count or start time changed
   (`--project`, default `openverify-load`). The published takes applied both through scratch tools,
   so no published verdict changes.
4. **The C271 levers.** `retrieval-reach` ships **on** (`WATCHLIST_SWITCHES_ON_BY_DEFAULT`, `cd408c3ca`;
   its off path kept and tested for identical answers); the head with it on measured C78.7 at 69.0 ms
   (100,000 names, met) and **314.1 ms (2,000,000 names, missed, gap +64.1 ms)**, 300 of 300 subjects
   identical at each scale. `rescreen-bound-memo` stays off (kept by rule 2 with no measurable gain;
   no re-screen figure moves). `stored-name-analysis` and `rescreen-prepared-index` stay unbuilt and
   carried (11F, or a later release by the owner's choice), with the 2,000,000-name budget missed.
5. **The C270 write changes**, measured at 2,000,000 names once finding 1's copy was replaced (a
   snapshot is a `pg_dump` file in the benchmark's container and each take restores it with
   `pg_restore --jobs=4 --exit-on-error`, then `vacuum analyze`; `benchmark-write.mjs`'s
   `snapshotDumpPath`, `dumpCommand`, `restoreCommand`; `CREATE DATABASE … TEMPLATE` is gone):
   - **C270.1 `incremental-key-df`**: identical at both scales and 53% faster on a 1,000-entry change
   (127.5 s against 272.9 s), so kept by rule 5, but **shipped off**: with it on, the C78.7 gate's
   100,000-name first import ran past its 600 s hook (`17fc4237b` on, `05b832733`/`89a508d75` off;
   C271.4). A large sync is slower with it; fixing that (for example the tracker applying a large
   delta as a rebuild) is 11F's or a later release's, measured with an initial import before the
   default flips.
   - **C270.2**: `REKEY_BATCH` = **10,000** (`7915372b0`): the fastest median at 100,000 names, confirmed
   at 2,000,000 (2,149.7 s against 2,270.5 s at 500, heap 1,446 MiB, identical).
   - **C270.3 and C269.4**: every configuration kept screening within 2 times idle during the
   2,000,000-name sync (status quo 1.53: **C269.4 met**); the rule takes **`gin-clean-every=50`**, the
   lowest sync cost (500.3 s against 518.4 s), shipped as `GIN_CLEAN_EVERY_BATCHES` = 50 in
   `apps/api/src/watchlists/sync.ts` (`3ecac9952`; 11B's function, no DDL). `fastupdate = off` is not
   taken, so `0037_phase11d_watchlist` is unused and 11D adds no migration.
   - The gate at the final head: 62.8, 60.0 and 64.1 ms (median 62.8 ms), every machine-independent
   check passing.
   - **Finding 1 is not the template copy alone**: the backend's `exit code 2` struck 3 of 25 restores
   (two GIN index builds, one `COPY`) while the rest completed; each crashed copy was run again before
   any timing. Its cause on this host (Docker Desktop's VM disk under heavy writes; fsyncs of up to
   23.8 s logged) is an owner item for the reference machine.
6. **The list calibrations (owner decisions 3 and 4 after 7B).** `fuzzy-name-candidate-cap` **5,000** and
   `face-list-threshold` **0.72**, both `calibrated` with `lists-phase11.md`'s basis (`eeae81d9f`; docs
   updated in the same commit). The face threshold costs **FNIR 25.3%** (a quarter of mated searches
   missed, on studio portraits; not measured on handheld selfies against a list photo): a release-notes
   line, and the owner may set it back to 0.5. The background thresholds stay provisional and
   `not-calibrated` (no licensed pair set): an open line of the release checklist.
7. **C272.** `jit` off is **not recommended** (reads 3.93 times worse; takes noisy). The runtime role's
   **`statement_timeout` 370 s** (2 × the 2,000,000-name sync's 183.5 s key-frequency rebuild; with
   `incremental-key-df` off the sync still runs it) and **`idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` 60 s**;
   the pool is 10 connections a process at every knee. For 11E's guide (C277.7, C277.11), never set by
   11D.
8. **The harness's other fixes (calibration-load-11d's findings).** Verification's submit and polls carry
   the session's address (retaken: knee 1 session a second over 150 s; a sustained take there saturates,
   so the row stays `invalid`); the login limiter's address half sends one email an attempt (the

9. **Small changes.** `apps/api/tsconfig.build.json` excludes `src/seed/load/**` and

## Orchestrator decisions before Workflow 11E (2026-11-20)

Made by the orchestrator (Claude Code, claude-opus-5-5) after Workflow 11D's close (PROGRESS "## 2026-11-20: Phase 11, Workflow 11D done", `aa00b57bb`, pushed), from read-only re-checks of the 11E script against Phase 10 as closed (read at `4db86122d`, 2026-11-12) and against Workflows 11A to 11D as built (read at `baf7e3229`, 2026-11-20), and from the known items those Workflows left. Items 1-12 are the Phase 10 re-check's, 13-24 the after-11D re-check's, 25-28 the orchestrator's. The 11E script carries all of it; where this section and an earlier plan line differ, this section wins.

1. **Args.** `{base, start, prior, known, device, owner}`. `base` is 11E's base (item 13). This section's commit is `start`, required. `known` is `phase11-prep/known-11e.json`; its owners are the eight 11E labels (contracts-11e, docs-security, docs-records, docs-selfhost, docs-compliance, docs-catalog, docs-release, integration-11e) or `deferred`, embedded by `make-launch.py --workflow 11e --known`. `device` is the phone's serial, or `""`.
2. **11E starts from the records, not a push.** This amends the plan's "11E starts after 11D is pushed" and every "CI-green" condition. contracts-11e checks that PROGRESS holds "## <date>: Phase 11, Workflow 11D done (…)" and that this section exists; its blocker is "11D NOT DONE". Only a completed CI run with a failed job is a first fix; a run that started no step is not one.
3. **No CI run to count on (rule 25).** Nothing in 11E is proved by CI. Documents name CI jobs only as where a check is defined. The release checklist keeps decision 72's CI-green bar and KP10-59 open. verify-artifacts.md and the release notes describe android-release.yml as defined and never run.
4. **The phone (rule 26).** Only integration-11e uses it, alone and last, and only if 11E's diff touches `android/` (only a rule 23 fix may). Otherwise Validation step (25) is logged skipped, and no agent runs adb. The documents cite device runs only from the records: 22 of 22 at `d6af3cc37`, plus 11A-11D's entries.
5. **The Validation block as Phase 10 closed it.** The Android lines are GATE_CALLS' eleven calls, with the two fetches first; (10b) the clean-build pass only on a lockfile or verification-metadata change; (25) the device suites last, under item 4's condition. The analytics benchmark is 11D's. Heavy calls are rule 27, parallel gates rule 28.
6. **C274.3 with no CI** (amends "a met row cites at least one test or CI job"). A met row cites a test or check that ran locally at the reviewed commit. A CI job may be named beside it as where it also runs, never as the only evidence.
7. **MASVS scope (C275.1).** The rows cover openverify-sdk, openverify-nfc and openverify-face; demo-app is not shipped. Phase 10 P3 is declined, so no Play Integrity artifact exists.

## Workflow 11E Stage 1a amendments (contracts-11e)

Recorded by `contracts-11e` (claude-opus-5-5) on 2026-11-20, alone on the machine, at 11E's start `247f6af3c` (base `aa00b57bb`). The evidence is `docs/tdd/phase11-contracts-11e.tdd.md`; the facts with `path:line` are section 5 of `docs/research/phase11-inventory.md`. Each item binds every later 11E agent and wins over the contract text it amends.

1. **The base.** PROGRESS holds "## 2026-11-20: Phase 11, Workflow 11D done (…)" at both `aa00b57bb` and `247f6af3c`, and the 11E decisions exist. `origin/main` is `aa00b57bb` and does not hold `247f6af3c` (recorded, not required). The 11D push's run 44785912432 started no step in any of its 19 jobs (as the four before it), so no CI failure is 11E's first fix (rule 25) and contracts-11e changed no code outside `packages/docs-tools`.
2. **Drift from the plan's Starting point and the prompt.** (a) `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` holds 95 entries (39 in the Starting point, 83 at `bf3a87e3`). (b) `docs/api/openapi.json` has 401 paths and no `webhooks` member; `EVENT_NAMES` holds 151 names, sampled by 32 `contracts/fixtures/api/event-*.json` files and three `webhook-*.json` files. (c) 41 rate policies; 125 configuration entries; 14 metrics; 22 provider kinds. (d) PROGRESS's "**Carried forward:**" blocks are 20, plus one "**Carried forward** (beyond 6B):" at `docs/PROGRESS.md:1906` that release:check must read too. (e) The vendor's name appears in `docs/` outside the exempt paths once as a name (`docs/tdd/phase0-verify.tdd.md:163`), beside 47 uses of the repository's directory name (42 at `baf7e3229`; the 11D TDD files added five) and 3 of `persona-surface.md`. (f) `packages/core/src/connect/categories.ts` maps field types to share categories and share categories to data classes; it holds no column-to-class map, so C276.2's "generated from categories.ts" means: the classes and the field-type rows come from it, and the column rows come from a table in docs-records' generator, held by a test that names every `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` entry (F07's intent kept: the document is never the source).
3. **`packages/docs-tools`** (`@openverify/docs-tools`, private, 0.0.0, under `createVitestConfig()`; `packages/config/src/workspace-conventions.test.ts` passes with it). Its scripts, each the only writer of its area: `docs:security`, `docs:records`, `docs:selfhost`, `docs:compliance`, `docs:catalog`, `docs:release`, `docs:all` (`node --experimental-transform-types --no-warnings --conditions=@openverify/source --import ./scripts/register-ts.mjs src/cli.ts render <area>`) and `release:check` (`… src/cli.ts release-check`; `pnpm --filter @openverify/docs-tools release:check --tag` passes `--tag` through). No runner is added: Node 22.22.1's type stripping with a copy of `packages/checks/scripts/register-ts.mjs` replaces `tsx`. The CLI's exit codes are 0 done, 1 a check failed, 2 usage, 3 not built (a stub; through `pnpm --filter`, pnpm reports any non-zero code as 1). The root `package.json`'s `release:check` is `docs-release`'s named borrowed edit (the plan's 11E bullet), pointing at this script. `turbo.json` gains `@openverify/docs-tools#test` with `cache: false`, because the suite reads the whole repository and a cached pass would hide a document's drift. Dependencies are workspace and catalog entries only (`@openverify/contracts`, `@openverify/core`, `ajv`, `csv-parse`, `csv-stringify`; `@openverify/config`, `@types/node`, `@vitest/coverage-v8`, `typescript`, `vitest`): the lockfile gains one importer and no package. `package.json` and `src/index.ts` are shared files from now on: no Stage 2 agent adds a script or a dependency.
4. **The frozen interfaces** (`src/types.ts`, `src/repository.ts`, `src/fresh-render.ts`, `src/areas.ts`, `src/cli.ts`, contracts-11e's): `Repository` (`root`, `exists`, `readText`, `listFiles`), `createRepository(root)`, `createMemoryRepository(files)` (fixtures), `REPOSITORY_ROOT`, `SKIPPED_DIRECTORIES`; `GeneratedFile {path, content}`, `RenderResult {area, status: 'built' | 'not-built', files}`, `CheckFinding {rule, path, line?, message, severity: 'error' | 'owner'}`, `CheckResult {name, status, findings}`; `DOCS_AREAS`, `AREA_OWNERS`, `AREA_CONTRACTS`; `staleFiles(repo, files)`; `AREAS`; `runCli(argv, deps)`, `main(argv?, io?)`. Each area's entry points, typed and answering `not-built` until the owner fills them: `renderSecurity`/`checkSecurity` (`src/security/index.ts`, docs-security), `renderRecords`/`checkRecords` (docs-records), `renderSelfhost`/`checkSelfhost` (docs-selfhost), `renderCompliance`/`checkCompliance` (docs-compliance), `renderCatalog`/`checkCatalog` (docs-catalog), `renderRelease`/`checkRelease`/`releaseCheck(repo, {tag})` with `ReleaseCheckOptions` and `ReleaseCheckResult {…, mode}` (docs-release). An owner replaces its area's stub test; a check's `owner` findings are what release:check's default mode reports and exits 0 on (C284.2).
5. **How each input is read** (`packages/docs-tools/README.md`): a workspace import through `@openverify/source` (`@openverify/core`: the check, report and signal registries, `VERIFICATION_TYPES`, the Connect categories, `FIELD_SCHEMA_TYPES`; `@openverify/contracts`: the rate-limit maps, `EVENT_NAMES`, `TEMPLATE_FIELD_TYPES`, `ACCOUNT_FIELD_TYPES`); a relative import of the module that holds the fact (`packages/db/src/encrypted-columns.ts`; `apps/api/src/config-registry.ts`, `metrics-registry.ts`, `metrics-documents.ts`, `logger.ts`, `jobs/logger.ts`, `providers/index.ts`, `plugins/rate-limit.ts`), as the watchlist benchmark imports the API's sources; or parsing a committed file (`openapi.json`, `persona-surface.md`, the summary blocks with `ajv` against `contracts/fixtures/calibration/summary.schema.json`, the licence file, PROGRESS, the plans, `Chart.yaml`, `gradle.properties`, the `package.json` files, `release.yml`, `allowlist.yaml`, the CSVs with `csv-parse`, the id lists). A static relative import makes `tsc` type-check what it reaches, so it is kept to modules with few imports.
6. **C279.3's scopes and rate policies (the hard case).** Scopes are readable without a code change: an in-memory build of the app as `openapi.test.ts` builds it (`createContainer(loadConfig(syntheticEnv({})), …).buildApp({ logger: false })`, no database touched) with an `onRoute` hook added before `ready()` saw 690 routes, 596 with `config.scopes` (one throwaway run under `apps/api`, 17 s). docs-catalog loads those modules with a dynamic `import()` of a computed path, typed locally. Rate policies are only partly readable: the plugin's prefix policies (`api`, `flow-session`, `flow-ip`) and the flow routes it names by constant (`apps/api/src/plugins/rate-limit.ts:48-79`) are; every other policy is a call in a handler, and no table maps a route to it. **docs-catalog's fallback:** render what the routes declare (scopes; the prefix and constant policies; every other policy listed with its limit and window and no route), and report the missing route-to-policy declaration as a contractDeviation for 11F (`fix-server`; C286 already defers per-route problem codes). If the in-memory build will not run under this package's Vitest configuration, render the paths from `openapi.json` and report the scopes the same way.
7. **The check catalog's family pages** (C279.1), frozen: government_id → `docs/checks/government-id.md`; government_id_nfc → `nfc.md`; selfie → `selfie.md`; document → `document.md`; database and aamva → `database.md`; database_business and business_website → `business.md`; crypto_wallet → `crypto-wallet.md`; phone_number and email_address → `contact.md`. Every id already appears in its family's page; `signals.md` holds the signal registry, `sandbox-providers.md` is an index.
8. **Logging (C276.3).** Only the API (`REDACT_PATHS`, `PERSONAL_LOG_KEYS`) and the worker (`WORKER_REDACT_PATHS`) configure pino redaction; the console writes console JSON, the flow server logs `{entry, status}`, vision uses Python `logging`, and the code runner writes its configuration errors and warnings, none with a redaction list. The logging test compares the documented paths with those two lists and states the other processes as they are; a missing list is a contractDeviation, never described as present.
9. **Metric names in documents** (11E decisions item 15). `apps/api/src/metrics-documents.test.ts` keeps checking its three documents; docs-tools checks every other page of the docs tree (item 13) with the same two functions, imported relatively, never a copied pattern.

11. **The fetch scripts and the id lists (C274.2, C275.2).** `scripts/fetch_asvs.py` pins `https://github.com/OWASP/ASVS/releases/download/v5.0.0_release/OWASP_Application_Security_Verification_Standard_5.0.0_en.flat.json` (162,870 bytes, SHA-256 `8201b20eec2908c3380ac600c91c8ba746346fbb808859366abb232027532311`, release 2025-05-30, tag commit `5cf9b032440be53ce345ab3c130fda46ba1ce7a2`); `scripts/fetch_masvs.py` pins `https://github.com/OWASP/masvs/releases/download/v2.1.0/OWASP_MASVS.cdx.json` (the CycloneDX export, because the standard library reads JSON and not YAML; 28,264 bytes, SHA-256 `abedfcdf6cbd38ffa30a2ae6fc9f98ad7cddff59f0ad35932117559a2a27e0ae`, release 2024-01-18, commit `8e133d09f4140518ed04cc254b18be9ff4990ffc`), saved as `OWASP_MASVS-2.1.0.cdx.json`. Both download into `data/standards/` (git-ignored, skipped by the PII scan), never committed or shipped. Flags: none (fetch only), `--verify-ids` (fetch, derive the list from the export, compare it with the committed one; exit 1 on any difference, naming it), `--write-ids` (fetch and write the committed list; the only writer), `--dest <dir>`, `--ids <path>`; env `ASVS_RETRY_DELAY_SECONDS` and `MASVS_RETRY_DELAY_SECONDS`. The committed lists: `docs/security/asvs-5.0.0-l2-ids.txt` (four `#` header lines, then 253 lines `<id> <level>` in the export's order, ids `v5.0.0-<chapter>.<section>.<requirement>`, 70 at level 1 and 183 at level 2) and `docs/security/masvs-2.1.0-ids.txt` (four `#` header lines, then 24 lines `<id> <group>`, ids `MASVS-<GROUP>-<n>`). The tests are `services/vision/tests/test_fetch_asvs.py` and `test_fetch_masvs.py` (34; run with `--no-cov`, since a lone vision test file fails vision's `src` coverage gate). docs-security's evidence test reads these lists (a parser per list: skip `#` lines, split on the one space); the CSVs hold exactly these ids.
12. **MASVS's licence and groups (C275.1, C275.2).** `License.md` at the `v2.1.0` tag is CC-BY-SA-4.0, share-alike: only ids are committed, as for ASVS. The export holds one standard, `MASVS-v2.1.0`, with exactly the plan's eight groups in this order: `MASVS-STORAGE`, `MASVS-CRYPTO`, `MASVS-AUTH`, `MASVS-NETWORK`, `MASVS-PLATFORM`, `MASVS-CODE`, `MASVS-RESILIENCE`, `MASVS-PRIVACY`, and 24 controls (STORAGE 2, CRYPTO 2, AUTH 3, NETWORK 2, PLATFORM 3, CODE 4, RESILIENCE 4, PRIVACY 4). The MASVS CSV is one row per control (24), its scope `openverify-sdk`, `openverify-nfc` and `openverify-face` (11E decisions item 7).
13. **The docs tree** (`packages/docs-tools/src/docs-tree.ts`, exported through `src/index.ts`): `DOCS_TREE`, 48 entries `{path, title, owner, contract, generated}`, sorted by path, covering C273-C281 and C284: `SECURITY.md`; `docs/security/` (`README.md`, `threat-model.md`, `asvs-5.0.0-l2.csv`, `asvs-5.0.0-l2-ids.txt`, `asvs-summary.md`, `masvs-2.1.0.csv`, `masvs-2.1.0-ids.txt`, `crypto-inventory.md`, `data-classification.md`, `logging.md`, `vulnerability-policy.md`); `docs/self-hosting/` (C277.1's sixteen pages plus `sso.md`, C248's guide that KP11-32 names); `docs/compliance/` (`README.md`, `roles.md`, `record-of-processing.md`, generated); `docs/checks/catalog.md` and `limits.json`; `docs/api/README.md`; `docs/migration/from-hosted-api.md`; `docs/connect/` (C281.1's ten pages); `docs/release/checklist.md` and `notes.md`. Generated: `docs/api/README.md`, `docs/checks/catalog.md`, `docs/compliance/record-of-processing.md`, `docs/connect/mapping.md`, `docs/migration/from-hosted-api.md`, `docs/release/notes.md`, `docs/security/asvs-summary.md`, `docs/security/data-classification.md`, `docs/self-hosting/configuration.md`, `docs/self-hosting/hardening-checklist.md` and the two id lists (`contracts-11e`'s, written by the fetch scripts). Each of the 46 other files was created as a stub by `pnpm --filter @openverify/docs-tools docs:stubs` (it writes only missing files): a Markdown stub is its title, the marker line `Draft: written in Workflow 11E by <owner>` and one sentence; a CSV stub is the marker line alone; `limits.json` is `{"draft": "<marker>", "title": …}`. `missingFromTree(repo)` and `draftsRemaining(repo)` (path and marker) are what integration-11e's stub check reads: every owner replaces its stubs, and a remaining marker fails the Workflow. A page an owner adds beyond the manifest (a compliance topic page, say) is allowed and is not stub-checked; a manifest path may not be dropped. The Phase 8 and Phase 10 compliance pages are not in the manifest (docs-compliance's borrowed edits).
14. **The wording test** (C274.5; `src/wording.ts`, `checkWording`, rules `asvs-masvs-claim` and `compliance-claim`). Scope (`wordingScope`): every `.md`, `.csv` and `.txt` under `docs/`, every `README.md` in the repository (the npm packages' included; installs and builds skipped), `SECURITY.md`, `deploy/helm/openverify/Chart.yaml` and `templates/NOTES.txt` (`WORDING_FILES`); `docs/release/notes.md` is under `docs/`. "Next to" is one sentence: a Markdown paragraph's wrapped lines are joined, a list item, table row, heading, quote, fenced line, CSV, YAML or text row is a unit, and a unit is cut at `.`, `!`, `?` or `;` (`src/text.ts`, `sentencesOf`). The claim words are certified, certification, certify, certifies, compliant and complies. **Amends C274.5:** a sentence that denies the word (not, no, never, nothing, neither, nor, without, none, cannot, n't) passes for ASVS and MASVS too, not only in `docs/compliance/`, because the plan's own honesty statements ("self-assessed, never certified", ADR 0010's "no certification is claimed") are denials; and a claim word quoted on its own ("certified", `compliant`) is a mention of the word, as the plan's rule text quotes it. No path is exempt. The repository passes it at this commit; REAL ID's "compliant" (`docs/checks/government-id.md:248`) is neither next to ASVS or MASVS nor under `docs/compliance/`. The existing product-string checks (`check-registry.test.ts:611`, `share-consent-texts.test.ts:146`, `selfie-report.test.ts:252`, `pack-real.test.ts:94`, `report-parity.test.ts:32`) stay as they are; none read `docs/`.
15. **The vendor-name test** (the Rules' Content line; `src/vendor-name.ts`, `checkVendorName`, rule `vendor-name`). No earlier Workflow built one over `docs/` (the checks above are product strings), so it is new. A hit is the name as a word, any case, with `-` and `.` as boundaries (so the source API's vendor-named header names are hits), or the vendor's host. Not naming: the repository's directory name `persona-opensource` (evidence paths), the file name `persona-surface.md` and the source API's identifiers that contain `reusable-persona` (behaviour, C286). Scope: `.md`, `.csv`, `.txt` and `.json` under `docs/`. **Exempt (`VENDOR_NAME_EXEMPT`):** `docs/research/`, `docs/plans/`, `docs/PLAN.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`, `docs/adr/` (the plan's list) and **`docs/tdd/`** (contracts-11e's choice: TDD evidence quotes the developer's paths and commands as run, and one Phase 0 note uses the name, `docs/tdd/phase0-verify.tdd.md:163`; evidence is not product text and is never rewritten); the vendor-named migration page only while the switch is on. `README.md:7` names the vendor with its not-affiliated line, outside the test's `docs/` scope, left for Q8. The repository passes it.
16. **The switch file** (`src/switches.ts`): `DOCS_SWITCHES = {vendorNaming: false, publishWired: false}` (frozen), `MIGRATION_PAGES` (`neutral`: `docs/migration/from-hosted-api.md`; `vendorNamed`: `docs/migration/from-persona.md`), `migrationPagePath(switches)`, `PUBLISH_VARIABLE = 'OPENVERIFY_PUBLISH'`, `RELEASE_WORKFLOW = '.github/workflows/release.yml'`, `publishJobOf(workflowText)` (the `publish` job's `if:` and `environment:`, read without a YAML library) and `publishGatedByVariable(job)`. docs-catalog's mapping generator reads `vendorNaming` (and takes `--vendor-name` only through it: the CLI has no flag, so the page cannot name the vendor while the committed switch is off); docs-selfhost's `verify-artifacts.md` test reads `publishWired` (off: "no artifact of this release is signed", no `cosign verify` command). The test that holds it: `release.yml`'s `publish` job still has `if: vars.OPENVERIFY_PUBLISH == 'true'` and `environment: release` (`.github/workflows/release.yml:622-626`), and `publishWired` is false. 11G changes either switch only under the owner's grant (Q8; Q2 and Q3), as C289 says.
17. **Metric names elsewhere** (`src/metrics.ts`, `checkMetricNames`): every docs-tree document but `METRIC_DOCUMENTS` is read with `unregisteredMetricsIn` and the `metrics` registry, both imported relatively from `apps/api/src/` (11E decisions item 15).

## Workflow 11E Stage 2 amendments

Recorded by `integration-11e` (claude-opus-5-5) on 2026-11-20 from each Stage 2 agent's report (its contract deviations), one subsection per agent. Each item binds 11F and 11G and wins over the contract text it amends; a code defect is stated with the 11F fixer that owns its path (rule 1), never as fixed. Every agent's evidence is its `docs/tdd/phase11-<agent>.tdd.md`.

### docs-security (C273, C274, C275; KP11-45)

Checkpoints `7f8ece65a` (threat model), `3ec17b7a5` (ASVS), `fc8cb31ee` (review, against the tree at `7d813b436`).

1. **C273.1, the format.** "One table of rows" is one table format: the model has one table per boundary group (seven), each with C273.1's header, so it can be written and reviewed in parts (rule 15). The parser (`packages/docs-tools/src/security/threat-model.ts`) also reads a boundaries table `| boundary | what crosses it |`, "not applicable" lines `- <boundary> <letter>: not applicable because <reason>` and an accepted-risks table `| risk | rows | monitoring |`, whose monitoring is `metric: <name>` or `operator check: ...`. Row ids start with their boundary. A `gap` names one of: Phase N, Workflow 1xX, 11A-11G, KPnn-n, K7C-Rnnn, Rnnn, Ennn, Qn or "next release".
2. **C273.4 with the 11E decisions' item 15.** `apps/api/src/metrics-documents.test.ts` keeps checking the metric names in the threat model and the ASVS CSV; the MASVS CSV is outside `METRIC_DOCUMENTS`, so `securityChecklistFindings` applies the same imported `unregisteredMetricsIn` to both CSVs (rule `metric-name`).
3. **C274.3 with the 11E decisions' item 6.** "A met row cites at least one test" means a test file of any suite, matched by `isTestEvidence`: `*.test.ts(x)`, `*.spec.ts`, `test_*.py`, `*Test.kt`, `*.test.sh` and helm-unittest's `*_test.yaml`. A CI workflow or a document is never enough alone; a row whose only proof is the forced typecheck stays partial (v5.0.0-15.3.5).
4. **C274.6, staleness.** `docs/security/asvs-summary.md` renders from the two CSVs only, so it stays equal to a fresh render (rule 6). Staleness is `checkSecurityWith(repo, {history})` (`packages/docs-tools/src/security/index.ts`), adding `asvs-staleness` and `masvs-staleness` with `stale-evidence` findings of severity `owner`; the tests use a fake history and a fixture git repository. integration-11e wired it into `pnpm release:check` (Stage 3 amendments).
5. **C275.1.** The MASVS CSV keeps C274.1's eight columns; its `level` column holds the control's group (MASVS-STORAGE and so on), because MASVS v2.1.0 controls carry no level, and the evidence test checks `level` against each id list's second field.
6. **Exported names** carry a security prefix (`parseSecurityChecklist`, `SecurityChecklistRow`, `securityChecklistFindings`, `staleChecklistRows`, `parseStandardIdList`), because `src/index.ts` re-exports every area with `export *`.
7. **KP11-45's placement.** (a) the idle timeout's per-instance clock: B4-S8 (operator) and a token stolen while in use, B4-I6 (accepted); (b) the deprovisioning lag, B4-S7 (accepted), and the forced token-endpoint signature check, B4-S5 (control); (c) the audit retention function's caller-stated time, B3-R3 (control) and B3-R4 (accepted); (d) `{expires}` under another ICU answering `consent_text_mismatch`, B13-D2 (operator, with the upgrade note in `docs/self-hosting/upgrades.md`); (e) every metric written `metric: <name>`. The ASVS rows 7.3.1, 7.1.3, 6.8.2, 16.4.2, 2.4.1 and 16.3.4 carry the matching facts.
8. **C274.4's targets as evidence stands (review H15).** G-REGEX (v5.0.0-15.2.2) is partial: operator patterns are met (C252), but fairness on the shared queue missed at 26.6 times idle (11D review amendments item 1). G-DEVIMG (15.2.3) is partial (item 10's face-scan:benchmark). 6.3.3 is partial: `require-mfa` is on by default and an admin can turn it off. 8.2.2 and 8.4.1 are partial: the cross-tenant probe covers 115 of 257 server operations (KP11-10, 11F). G-HSTS (3.4.1), G-TLS (12.3.1 and 12.3.3 partial, 12.3.4 operator), G-SECRETS (13.3.1) and G-SBOM (15.1.2) are partial as targeted. G-KEYDOC (11.1.x), G-CLASS (14.1.x) and G-LOGINV's inventory (16.1.1) became met at the review, once docs-records' records and their tests existed; 16.4.3 is operator.
9. **Features absent, recorded as gaps for the next release** (not 11F defects): v5.0.0-6.2.2 and 6.2.3 (no route changes or resets a password; 11B inventory 2.4), 6.2.4 and 6.2.12 (no common or breached password check), 6.1.2 and 6.2.11 (no context-specific word list), 7.1.2 (no limit on concurrent console sessions). SAML is not applicable (6.8.3; C286).
10. **Code defects for 11F** (each recorded partial in the checklist):
    - fix-clients: `apps/dashboard/src/lib/session-cookie.ts:8`, the console session cookie `openverify_session` has neither the `__Host-` nor the `__Secure-` prefix (it is Secure in production builds; the SSO binding cookie uses `__Host-`). ASVS v5.0.0-3.3.1 and 3.3.3.
    - fix-server: `apps/api/src/app.ts` (no global `onSend` header), the API sends `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` only on the routes that serve file bytes, not on its JSON responses. ASVS v5.0.0-3.4.4.
    - fix-records: `deploy/helm/openverify/templates/_security.tpl:271`, `nginx.ingress.kubernetes.io/force-ssl-redirect` is set for every host, the API host included, where ASVS v5.0.0-4.1.2 asks that only user-facing endpoints redirect HTTP to HTTPS transparently.
    - fix-server: `apps/api/tsconfig.commands.json` and `apps/api/scripts/face-scan-benchmark.ts`, face-scan:benchmark is compiled into the production API image, writes a scratch organisation and refuses nothing about production (11C Stage 1a amendment 17). ASVS v5.0.0-15.2.3; threat row B7-T4 (operator).
    - fix-server, test gaps: v5.0.0-1.3.11 (no SMTP header-injection test of the mailer), 13.4.4 (no TRACE test), 15.3.6 (no prototype-pollution test), 15.3.7 (no repeated-query-parameter test), 16.3.1 (a refused sign-in is limited and logged as a code, never an audit entry), 16.3.2 (a refused authorisation is not an audit entry).
11. **The 11F documentation reviewer** (C274.3) samples the met rows' evidence, at least one per chapter, starting with the rows that moved to met at the review (11.1.x, 14.1.x, 15.1.1, 16.1.1, 16.2.3, 13.1.1).

### docs-records (C276)

Checkpoints `855f5262f` (classification), `ca2f3cf52` (crypto inventory), `b676b3cc4` (logging, policy, index); `c85bc540a` corrected the inventory's consent-digest line.

1. **C276.2 with Stage 1a amendment 2(f).** "Generated from `categories.ts`" is met as: the classes, share categories and field-type rows from `packages/core/src/connect/categories.ts`; the 95 column rows from `COLUMN_CLASSES` in `packages/docs-tools/src/records/classification.ts`, held equal to `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS`; the 16 redaction categories parsed as text from `apps/api/src/services/redaction.test-support.ts` (never imported: it imports `@openverify/db/testing`); the retention defaults from `DASHBOARD_SETTINGS_DEFAULTS`; the export sections from `accountExportManifestSchema`. Each column carries one class, its most sensitive content's (`files.wrapped_dek` holds document images and selfies and is classed biometric).
2. **C276.2 and C230.4, third-party list data.** `DATA_CLASSES` (`categories.ts:14`) has nine classes and no class of its own for third-party list data; the page places it as `public-and-list-data` inside the `THIRD_PARTY_LIST_PII_TABLES` redaction category, the code's own distinction, and says so. A tenth class is a `categories.ts` change: 11F's review (fix-server).
3. **C276.2 and C253.1, the scope's name.** The access export's scope is `account-access:export` in the code (`packages/core/src/permissions.ts:114`; 11B Stage 1a amendment 35), not C253.1's `accounts:export`, which is Phase 9's list CSV export; the pages use the code's names.
4. **C276.3 with Stage 1a amendment 8, logging.** Only the API and the worker configure redaction. Observations for 11F's review, not established defects: vision (`services/vision/Dockerfile:74`) runs uvicorn with its default access log (the calling API's or worker's address, method, path, status), and its unhandled-error and inference-failure handlers log `exc_info` tracebacks (`services/vision/src/openverify_vision/problems.py:98`, `inference.py:72`) whose messages are not filtered (fix-server); the console's page error boundary calls `console.error(error)` in the browser (`apps/dashboard/src/components/page-error.tsx:30`; fix-clients). `logging.md` states each process as it is.
5. **C276.1, rotation.** The blind-index key and the vision, console BFF and code-runner tokens have no previous form, so rotating any of them needs a coordinated restart (and, for the blind-index key, `blind-index:rebuild`, during which lookups miss). The inventory states it as a limit; whether previous forms are wanted is 11F's review (fix-server).
6. **C276.4 with Q1 pending.** No public reporting channel exists before the public release; `SECURITY.md` and `vulnerability-policy.md` say so, and that until then a reporter who can see the private repository tells its owner privately. A fixed dependency release younger than the 14-day release age is held by an allowlist entry until it can be adopted, within the entry's 90 days; an exception to the age rule itself stays the owner's (Q5 for toolchain binaries).
7. **Stale comments (C162.9), a code defect for 11F.** Three comments say a consent sent without digests is "recorded with text-sha256: null", but no column stores a client's consent digests or catalog version (`recordInquirySessionConsent`, `packages/db/src/repositories/inquiry-sessions.ts:100`, writes text keys, optional keys, locale, address and user agent; `packages/db/src/tables/inquiries.ts:289-303`): `apps/api/src/services/templates/consent-texts.ts:6` and `packages/contracts/src/flow-api.ts:223` (fix-server; the latter also calls today's Android SDK one that sends none, while `FlowCoordinator.kt:611` computes and sends the evidence) and `android/openverify-sdk/src/commonMain/kotlin/dev/openverify/sdk/consent/ConsentDigests.kt:22` (fix-clients). The crypto inventory states what the code does.
8. **Package.** `src/records/checks.ts` is not re-exported (its `variableFindings` collides with another area's export under `export *`, TS2308). Commit order: the GREEN code commits `3bce69dfc` and `6f24e7bb2` precede the page commits `1b10df4b1` and `094712783`, so at those two commits the pages' own record tests still failed for their RED reason.

### docs-selfhost (C277; KP11-32, KP11-43, KP11-48)

Checkpoints `14043ca9f` (configuration reference), `8a0b062b5` (the guide), `2a29621e6` (the hardening checklist, after docs-security's `3ec17b7a5`).

1. **C277.1 with the docs tree.** `sizing.md`, `monitoring.md` and `upgrades.md` are hand-written pages that each hold one generated block between `<!-- openverify:<name>:start/end -->` markers (`selfhost-load-figures` from `docs/calibration/load-phase11.md`'s summary block, validated against `contracts/fixtures/calibration/summary.schema.json`; `selfhost-metrics` from `METRIC_REGISTRY`; `selfhost-migrations` from `packages/db/drizzle/` 0025 on); `docs:selfhost` splices them and the fresh-render check holds them, though `DOCS_TREE` marks the pages `generated: false`. `docs:selfhost` must run again whenever a checklist CSV, the load report's summary block, the metric or configuration registry or the migrations change.

The pg_upgrade notes were re-read on 2026-11-20: PostgreSQL 17's pg_upgrade page (step 5: the extension's shared objects in the new cluster first; `--check`) and pgvector's README at v0.8.6, whose Upgrading section covers only `ALTER EXTENSION vector UPDATE`; `database.md` cites both with the date.
5. **Record defects for 11F (fix-records).** `docs/calibration/load-phase11.md:556` (the role-timeouts row of "Database tuning") says `incremental-key-df` is "on by default since fix-11d"; the code ships it off (`packages/db/src/repositories/watchlist-switches.ts:73-75` holds only `retrieval-reach` in `WATCHLIST_SWITCHES_ON_BY_DEFAULT`; 11D review amendments items 5 and 7). `database.md` states what the code does. `CLAUDE.md` (the orchestrator's) says the compose `app` profile runs the images with "secrets as `*_FILE`"; `infra/docker-compose.yml:45-58` and each app service pass every secret as an environment variable from `.env`, and `install-compose.md` says so.

7. **Borrowed edit** `093f5d62e`, `packages/docs-tools/src/cli.test.ts` (contracts-11e's test): the render-all test injects every other area as a not-built stub, so it holds while areas get built.

### docs-compliance (C278; KP11-46)

Checkpoints `45483162c` (the guide), `236a0a465` (recipients), `9a7c10eae` (record of processing); end gate `409516477`.

1. **C278.1 and C278.2, the layout.** Beside the docs tree's `README.md`, `roles.md` and `record-of-processing.md`, the guide adds topic pages that are not stub-checked (Stage 1a amendment 13): `biometric-data.md`, `us-state-laws.md`, `dpia.md`, `access-requests.md`, `device-and-network.md`, `lists-graph-transactions.md`, `operations.md`, `licensed-data.md`, `retention.md`, `breach-response.md`, `sharing.md`, `questions-for-counsel.md`, `sources.md` and the generated `recipients.md`. Automated decisions is a section of the README that indexes Phase 8's page. Every page opens with "This is not legal advice." and "Not reviewed by counsel." (Q7).
2. **Borrowed edit** `bc92106d0` (11E decisions item 9): one line, "Not reviewed by counsel.", under line 3 of `docs/compliance/automated-decisions.md` (Phase 8) and `docs/compliance/session-integrity.md` (Phase 10), nothing else.
3. **C278.2's "every adapter, off until bound"** does not hold for two kinds, by design: `readProviderSettings({})` binds `businessRegistry` to gleif (and companies-house and sec-edgar when their credentials are set) and `domainAge` to rdap (`apps/api/src/providers/index.ts:330-366`, C61.1). The guide says "unbound until configured, except GLEIF and RDAP", and the two generated pages state it from the registry. Plan wording, not a code defect.
4. **C278.3, the re-reads (2026-11-20).** Read: the UK GDPR's articles, DUAA 2025 ss. 76, 78 and 80, RCW 19.375, the Colorado bill page, the CPPA's announcement, AI Act Annex III, WP29 9/2014 section 7.5, PECR Sch. A1 para 4 and POCA s. 333A. Not re-read, each marked in place with its reason and listed in `docs/compliance/sources.md`: the EU GDPR (EUR-Lex returned an empty body for the CELEX and ELI pages, so the UK text was read in its place), BIPA (TLS verification failed on both hosts; not bypassed), Texas ch. 503 (no text without script), the California Civil Code sections (HTTP 403). CNIL 2020-091 paragraph 49 was read in part (Légifrance 403; the PDF's authentication item not legible in extraction); Article 12A was read through DUAA s. 76 (its own URL answered 404).
5. **Input reading.** `apps/api/src/providers/index.ts` is loaded with a top-level-await dynamic import of a computed path (`packages/docs-tools/src/compliance/provider-facts.ts`, typed by a local `ProvidersModule`); a static import made the package's `tsc` take 122 s. It costs about 3 s of module load for each test file or CLI run that imports the area index.
6. **Order.** `docs/compliance/record-of-processing.md` (C278.5) is generated from docs-records' `dataClassification` and its profile texts, so after a docs-records change run `docs:records`, then `docs:compliance`.
7. **What the tests hold, and what they do not.** `compliance-retention` holds every `-retention-days` and `-ttl-days` key of `DASHBOARD_SETTINGS_DEFAULTS` (17) with its default; the two month-based analytics settings (default 13) are named in prose only. `RECIPIENT_DETAILS` (15 entries) is hand-written from each adapter's module and a test holds it to the registry's names, not its content: 11F's reviewers check each row against `apps/api/src/providers/<adapter>/` and `apps/api/src/services/device-integrity/play-integrity.ts`.
8. **No new code defect.** The guide states two known gaps as open: KP11-27 (an access export can outlive a retention step that is not a redaction cascade by at most `account-export-ttl-days`; 11F, fix-server) and Stage 1a amendment 8 (no log redaction list in the console, the flow server, vision or the code runner; 11F, fix-server and fix-clients), in `breach-response.md`.

### docs-catalog (C279, C280, C281; KP11-22)

Checkpoints `66ff5f17c` (catalog), `8fe56c01c` (API reference), `7d813b436` (mapping), `377cf724f` (the sharing guide).

1. **C279.3 with Stage 1a amendment 6, scopes.** contracts-11e's in-memory build works; the area's frozen render is synchronous and the build is not, so it runs in a child process (`src/catalog/route-table-collector.ts`, called by `spawnSync` from `src/catalog/route-table.ts`, once per process, under a 50 s guard, about 8 to 10 s at load 3 to 4), with the environment that writes `docs/api/openapi.json` and no database. A memory repository never builds the API. Scopes are rendered for all 501 operations; 32 declare no `config.scopes` and check their scope in the handler, labelled "in the handler".
2. **C279.3, rate policies: a code defect for 11F (fix-server).** No table maps a route to its rate policy: `apps/api/src/plugins/rate-limit.ts:46-79` binds only `api`, `flow-ip`, `flow-session`, `one-time-link-redeem`, `flow-signals`, `flow-handoffs`, `flow-handoffs-ip`, `flow-integrity` and `flow-sandbox-chip`; the other 32 of `RATE_LIMIT_POLICIES` are calls in handlers or services. `docs/api/README.md` names the plugin's policies per route and lists the other 32 with their limit and window and no route. Problem codes per route are not rendered either (routes declare none; review F18, C286): the page shows the `ProblemInput` shape and links each tag to a family page through `TAG_PAGES` (ours, test-held).

### docs-release (C284; KP11-47, KP11-49, KP11-50)

Checkpoints `daa2b8fbd` (checklist), `aae356079` (`pnpm release:check`), `3e9f1d7dd` (release notes).

1. **C284.1, a fourth state.** `ci-pending` joins `done`, `open` and `owner`: a line that waits only on a CI or release-workflow run (KP10-59, rule 25). `--tag` refuses it as it refuses `owner` and `open`.
2. **C284.2, what PROGRESS must hold.** PROGRESS is the orchestrator's, so a missing owner state (Q1-Q12, Phase 10 P1-P5), a deferral placement PROGRESS does not hold yet, a version disagreement and the undated changelog are `owner` findings with the exact line to add or change: the default mode exits 0 on them and `--tag` refuses them.
3. **C284.2, the verifier's table (H17).** contracts-11e froze no location; it is `docs/release/verified-findings.md` with the header `| Finding | Severity | Verdict | Fixed | Commits | Summary |` (Verdict `refuted`, `confirmed` or `plausible`; Fixed `yes` or `no`), 11F's verifier's to write. While it is absent the default mode reports "pending (11F)" and `--tag` refuses it; a CRITICAL or HIGH whose verdict is not `refuted` and whose Fixed is not `yes` is an error in both modes.
4. **C284.2, mediums past their time frame (H14).** No committed record of medium advisories exists (the scanners report mediums only in their step summaries), so the checklist's "Open medium advisories (H14)" table (`| Advisory | Package | Severity | Published | Notes |`) is the input; `release:check` lists each one published more than 90 days before the checklist's As-of date. It is empty in 11E; fix-records fills it in 11F.
5. **C284.4 and KP11-47 (b), the staged-run record.** `docs/release/staged-run.json` (`openverify/staged-run@1`: run id, commit, date, image digests, artifact SHA-256s, the `check-sbom-licences` reports) is the orchestrator's to write after `release.yml`'s first staged run. While it is absent the notes say "staged run pending" and `release:check` reports it as owner; a licence report that is not `pass` is an owner item `--tag` refuses. KP11-39 is carried on the checklist and in the notes meanwhile.
6. **C284.2, the version rule.** The target is the checklist's "Release version" fact (0.1.0, which `Chart.yaml` and the Android SDK carry; the owner confirms it at the tag); the sources are the root, `@openverify/node`, `@openverify/js` and `@openverify/react` `package.json`, `packages/node/src/transport.ts`'s `CLIENT_USER_AGENT` (KP11-47 (a)), `Chart.yaml`'s `version` and `appVersion`, `android/gradle.properties`' `openverifyVersion`, and `android/CHANGELOG.md`'s newest entry (heading `## x.y.z (YYYY-MM-DD)`, naming its minimum server, minSdk and compileSdk). No 11E agent changed a version.
7. **C284.2, the deferral rule's granularity.** An item is a top-level bullet of a Deferred section (with its sub-bullets), or its paragraph where the section has none (C88); a bullet's placement covers everything it lists. Placements are `carried` (a PROGRESS carry form or a PLAN "### Next release" section), `built` (a Phase 11 Workflow's PROGRESS entry), `handed` (a later phase's plan took it), `checklist` and `moot`, in `docs/release/deferrals.md` (89 rows).
8. **KP11-47 (c).** The chart's models-manifest copy is held in both modes: `deploy/helm/openverify/files/models-manifest.json` must equal `models/manifest.json` (identical today).
9. **A finding for the records.** `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`'s "Libraries compiled into libpdfium.so" row read "owner confirmation pending (decision 31)" and was not among the owner rows the 11E inventory listed; it became checklist line LIC-PDFIUM. integration-11e corrected the row in Stage 3 (Phase 5 decision 31 was confirmed in Phase 5's owner-delegated decisions after Workflow 5B).
10. **Borrowed edits.** `041a46e66`, the root `package.json`'s `"release:check": "pnpm --filter @openverify/docs-tools release:check"` (`--tag` passes through); `90d27c8b0`, `packages/docs-tools/src/cli.test.ts` (the built release check runs over an empty tree and fails with `checklist-missing`). Commit `041a46e66` ends with a session line no reminder gave; it is accurate to that session, and history was not rewritten (rule 2).

## Workflow 11F Stage 1 amendments (dast-11f)

Recorded by the Stage 3 verifier (claude-opus-5-5) on 2026-11-21 from `dast-11f`'s report, its evidence `docs/tdd/phase11-dast-11f.tdd.md`, its commits (`811ae2507` to `d77550af6`) and the files on disk at `d77550af6`. Each item binds the rest of 11F and 11G, and wins over the contract text it amends.

1. **The ZAP pin (decision 16 before 11F, C258.1, C258.3, C283.1).** DAST uses ZAP 2.17.0 from the project's own registry, by digest: `ghcr.io/zaproxy/zaproxy:2.17.0@sha256:9624669db72f54d86c4b46a7064518bd9bf79d8c61a4c31befa9923489f328af`, the same digest as Docker Hub's `zaproxy/zap-stable:2.17.0`, resolved by `refresh-digests.sh` on 2026-11-21 UTC. ZAP 2.17.0 was released on 2025-12-15 under Apache-2.0 (the licence read at the v2.17.0 tag). The tag's amd64 build dates from 2026-11-03, 18 days before adoption, so the 14-day rule is met. The reference lives in one place, `readonly ZAP_IMAGE` in `security/zap/run.sh`. `infra/tests/images.test.sh pins` holds it: a ZAP image named by a tag without its digest fails, while the repository's bare name, as the licence row writes it, does not. `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` section 1 has a ZAP row after kubectl, as an unmodified dev tool never shipped. `docs:release` re-rendered `docs/release/notes.md` with no change.
2. **How ZAP runs (C258.3's pin table, C283.1).** Every ZAP container runs with `-silent` and `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS=-Djava.net.preferIPv4Stack=true`, because on this Docker Desktop engine ZAP's Java cannot connect to services bound to `127.0.0.1` on the host network, although curl in the same image can. ZAP's alpha rules (`-a`) are not used, because they cannot run with `-silent`.
3. **The Validation line `bash security/zap/run.sh` does not run as one call.** With no argument, `run.sh` runs prepare, api, flow, console, report and revoke in order, with an EXIT trap that revokes the credentials. That is about 2 hours of scans and does not fit one 10-minute call (rule 8). The Validation's DAST step is therefore these calls, each one `flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock bash security/zap/run.sh <sub>`, in this order: `prepare`; `api <group>` once for each server and console group of `security/zap/api-groups.tsv`; `flow hosted noguidance`; `flow routes`; `console`; `report`; `revoke`. The no-argument form stays valid where one call may run for hours. `prepare` refuses to run while an earlier run's credentials exist. A failed subcommand revokes the credentials and removes the raw reports.
4. **The API scan is grouped (C283.1).** The API is scanned with one `zap-api-scan` per group of `security/zap/api-groups.tsv`. The file has 23 groups of at most 40 operations each: 22 server and console groups, with `tag@/prefix` entries splitting the dashboard and cases tags, and the flow tag, which `run.sh flow routes` scans as `api-flow`. Each group carries the credential its operations declare: `apiKey` (a sandbox key with all 76 scopes, two hours, loopback only), `userSession` (a seeded sandbox admin's console session) or the flow `sessionToken`. Because a config file is given (`-c rules.tsv`), `zap-api-scan.py` runs ZAP's Default Policy rather than API-Minimal, and the hook sets that policy to attack strength LOW. The scans use 2 threads 50 ms apart (70 ms for api-flow, api-templates and api-accounts, run first), to stay under the e2e stack's 1,200 requests a minute per key, with at most 6 minutes of active scan per group. Two routes are held out because they would end the run's own sessions, and the file records why: `DELETE /api/v1/dashboard/sessions/current` and `POST /api/v1/dashboard/sessions/revoke-others`.
5. **The rules file (C283.2).** `security/zap/rules.tsv` uses ZAP's packaged-scan config format: id, TAB, FAIL, WARN or IGNORE, TAB, (name), and an optional TAB and reason. Its ids and names were generated by code from the pinned image's `-g` list (114 rules), plus rule 100000, which raises alerts although `-g` does not list it: 115 rules in all. `zap.test.sh --docker` holds that every generated rule is listed. FAIL covers the active injection-class rules ZAP rates High: path traversal, file inclusion, SSI, XSS, SQL, code, command, XPath, XXE and template injection. There are **22 FAIL lines on disk**; the report's and the evidence's "27" is wrong, and fix-records corrects it to 22. An alert of a FAIL rule fails the run at Medium or High risk, and a FAIL rule's Low weakness variant (40014-2, a payload echoed in JSON) is only reported. Any High alert that is not IGNOREd fails the run, whatever its action. The 16 IGNOREs are rules that cannot apply to this stack, each with its evidence: no Java, PHP, ASP.NET, Apache, CGI or SOAP in any image, and plain HTTP on loopback. The other 77 are WARN. `security/zap/triage.tsv` records each alert's status.
6. **Credentials (C283.1, C245.1, C111.1).** `security/zap/credentials.ts` reads the stack through `readReusableStack` and `readStackInfo`, and reuses the e2e helpers (`startInquiry`, `phase5Template`, `typeConfig`, `signInToApi`, `dashboardRequest`, `withForwardedFor`). Its template is the e2e handoff spec's: consent, a QR device handoff and the database step. The run's flow calls present a random `203.0.113.x` documentation address, because the per-address handoff budget (10 an hour) that an earlier scan spent from `127.0.0.1` refused the next `prepare`. The console's 30-minute idle timeout and the key's two-hour life are both shorter than a run. So each scan first probes its credential, with the header on curl's stdin. It renews an expired key (`refresh-key`) or the admin sessions (`refresh-users`) once, and refuses to scan with a credential the API answers 401. A credential reaches ZAP only as an environment variable name, and is added by a replacer rule scoped to its target origin. MFA is not scripted.
7. **Raw reports (C283.2).** ZAP's raw reports and the per-run documents stay outside the repository, in `OPENVERIFY_ZAP_RUN_DIR` (default `/tmp/openverify-zap/run`, mode 0700, refused inside the repository), until `revoke` deletes them. The flow group's document carries the run's handoff token. After `revoke` only these are kept: `alerts.json` (rule, risk, method, route template and parameter name, with no header, evidence, attack or body), `facts.json`, `scans.tsv`, `revoke.json`, and a failed scan's log with token-shaped values removed. The committed report is generated by `security/zap/dast-zap.py report`, which removes every header and refuses any credential of the run or any token-shaped value. gitleaks found nothing in it with either config.
8. **Both flow variants (C283.6).** The images mode stages only `openverify/flow:11c-local`. `run.sh flow noguidance` starts `openverify/flow:11c-local-noguidance` beside it as `openverify-dast-flow-noguidance-<pid>`: `--network host`, `--read-only`, a loopback port, `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_API_URL` and `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_PUBLIC_URL`, and no flow-server token, so embed lookups answer "none". It scans that container and then removes it. Each variant's hosted entry `/` is checked against `FLOW_RESPONSE_HEADERS`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and the CSP `<meta>`.
9. **KP11-40 and decision 17 before 11F: rung (i).** On 2026-11-20 and 21 this host reached the images-mode stack: the driver's reachability check passed, and so did a probe of the agent's own containers on `--network host`. ZAP ran with `--network host` on the same engine. No bridge network was used, the override was not edited, and no Docker Desktop setting was changed. KP11-40 itself stays the owner's line.
10. **The builds (step 3).** The code-runner and vision images were built one after the other in one e2e-lock hold, not one build per call. Both fit easily: 45.6 s and 3.3 s (cached). The dashboard's `next build` did not stop on Next.js's notice, so decision 18's restarts were not needed.
11. **Borrowed edits and every change outside `security/zap/`, each with its path's owner.** Each file was clean first and committed alone. fix-records owns all of them for the rest of 11F:
    - `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`: the ZAP row (`f523b634f`).
    - `infra/tests/images.test.sh` pins: the ZAP reference (`811ae2507`, `a6f1477e5`).

12. **Honesty of the record.** `a6f1477e5` left `images.test.sh pins` failing on two tag literals in dast-11f's own tests, a placeholder and a grep string, until `9c8ead933` replaced them. The checkpoint subjects carry checkpoints 1, 2, 4 and 7. Steps 3 (the images and the stack), 5 (no high to fix) and 6 (the report) are recorded under their numbers in the TDD file only. The request total is **117,435** in the generated report (its Scans table sums to it), and the report and the TDD evidence's "117,770" is not supported by any table; fix-records cites 117,435. The api-webhooks group got 356 answers of 429 on the server API's key, against the report's "no 429 on the server API". These are expected rate-policy answers, never findings, but fix-records corrects the claim.
13. **Measured (the complete run, `/tmp/openverify-zap/run`).**
    - **Timing:** first scan 2026-11-21T06:37:14Z, last 09:45:37Z, revoked 09:52:46Z.
    - **Machine and stack:** Intel Xeon E5-1650 v3, 12 logical CPUs, 31.3 GiB, WSL2 with Docker Desktop 29.7.2. The e2e stack ran in images mode, at rung (i). Every e2e lock wait was 0.003 to 0.006 s.
    - **Builds** (seconds, with the load at start): api 164.9 (1.90 1.93 1.78); dashboard 141.5 (3.67 2.77 2.13); flow 38.9 (3.91 3.15 2.37); flow-noguidance 35.9 (3.21 3.07 2.37); code-runner 45.6 (3.04 3.07 2.40); vision 3.3 cached (3.48 3.21 2.48).
    - **ZAP pull and the stack:** the ZAP image pulled by digest in 111.6 s (4.15 3.19 3.45). The stack came up in 54.5 s (3.07 3.13 2.47) with ten containers healthy. `prepare` took 3.5 s (2.97 2.39 2.25).
    - **Baselines** (seconds, load, routes reached): flow-guidance 25 s (1.89 1.94 2.19), 11 routes; flow-noguidance 24 s (2.49 2.08 2.23), 11 routes; console 65 s (2.52 2.13 2.24), 258 routes.
    - **api-flow:** 395 s (5.96 3.50 2.72), 242 routes, 38 of 38 active rules; 5,413 of its 7,352 answers were 429 (the report's table; the agent's summary gives 7,282), from the flow-signals and flow-handoffs budgets.
    - **The 22 other API groups:** 133 to 399 s each, at loads from 2.00 to 2.91 (1-minute) at start, reaching 117 to 222 routes each. Twenty-one finished 38 of 38 active rules; api-webhooks was capped at 34 of 38 (the four ZAP marks informational).
    - **Totals:** 26 scans, 6,726 s of scanning (114 s of baselines and 6,612 s of API scans), 4,275 routes reached summed over the scans (not distinct), and 117,435 requests answered with 0 answers of 5xx.
    - **Alerts before and after:** the earlier calibration runs of 2026-11-21 and the complete run both had High 0 and FAIL-rule alerts at Medium or above 0, so there is no "after a fix" run. The complete run's totals are Medium 5, Low 46 and Informational 3,315.
      - The Medium 5 are rule 10202 on five console GET pages. They are not a defect: Next.js checks the Origin, held by C255.4's test.
      - Low 13 are rule 10021, nosniff missing on the API's JSON answers, open as KP11-70 for fix-server.
      - Low 4 are rule 10055, the console CSP's `report-uri` only, open for fix-clients.
      - Low 1 is rule 10044, checked: not a defect.
      - Low 28 are rule 40014-2, a payload echoed in JSON. They are not a defect.
    - **After the run:** `report` took 4.2 s and `revoke` under 10 s, after which every credential was refused (key 401, both sessions 401, flow session 401). The internal route `GET /api/v1/internal/embed-policies/{inquiryId}` answered 401 on the public listener without the flow server's token, because the e2e stack leaves `OPENVERIFY_INTERNAL_LISTEN` unset.
    - **Stack down:** the first `e2e:stack:down` took 12.4 s and its holder crashed (`RangeError: Invalid string length` while saving logs after 6.6 hours, fix-server's new item). The second, 4.3 s, removed the project.

## Workflow 11F review amendments

Recorded by `fix-records` (claude-opus-5-5) on 2026-11-21, alone and last in Workflow 11F, after `fix-server` (last checkpoint `5c144a954`) and `fix-clients` (last checkpoint `f5b289674`). The evidence is `docs/tdd/phase11-fix-server.tdd.md`, `docs/tdd/phase11-fix-clients.tdd.md` and `docs/tdd/phase11-fix-records.tdd.md`. The review's four lenses sent the verifier no CRITICAL or HIGH finding, so the verifier table (`docs/release/verified-findings.md`) holds no row. Each item below binds 11G and the next release, and wins over the contract text it amends.

### fix-server

1. **A hydrated field is a copy (F001/F002; C232.1, decision 77).** A field that an inquiry, an account or a transaction lists in `hydrated_fields` came from another organisation. `loadShareSource` leaves every such field out of a share's content, at peek, at token create and at the redemption's re-check. The object's own fields are still shared, and no `source_is_copy` refusal is added for a hydrated object.
2. **The provider's `amr` (KP11-26; C248.6).** Migration `0037_phase11f_session_amr`, the Workflow's one migration, adds `user_sessions.provider_amr` (`text[]`: null, or 1 to 10 values). An SSO session keeps the ID token's `amr` values of RFC 8176's shape. `userSessionAttributesSchema` gains the required, nullable member `provider-amr` (1 to 10 strings of 1 to 64 characters). Only a direct SSO session keeps it: a sign-in that passes a local factor after the provider (the 202 path) keeps none. `contracts/fixtures/api/user-sessions.json` carries both forms (`serverOnly` on Android), and `docs/api/openapi.json` was regenerated by `openapi:write` twice. `@openverify/node` has no dashboard session type, so nothing was regenerated there. `packages/db/src/migration-0036.test.ts` now asserts that 0037 follows 0036, not that 0036 is the newest.
3. **`face-scan:benchmark` is a development tool (KP11-71).** This reverses Workflow 11C Stage 1a amendment 17's placement. The API image does not compile it: `apps/api/src/commands-build.test.ts` lists it in `DEVELOPMENT_TOOLS`, leaving 19 operator commands, and `infra/tests/images.test.sh` holds the image to that.
4. **`nosniff` on every API answer (KP11-70; ASVS v5.0.0-3.4.4).** `buildApp`'s `onRequest` hook sends `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` on every answer, JSON and problem+json included.
5. **The export hook in two more redactions (KP11-27, part).** `redactReport` and `redactTransaction` call `accountExportHooks.subjectRedactedInTx` first in their transaction. `apps/api/src/foundation-11b-d.test.ts` counts four such calls in `services/redaction.ts`, not two. The eight other retention steps remain (carried, below).
6. **The verifier table's vocabulary (F006; C284.2).** A row's Severity must be CRITICAL, HIGH, MEDIUM or LOW, its Verdict refuted, confirmed or plausible, and its Fixed yes or no. A row with Fixed yes must name a commit. Otherwise `release:check` fails the row in both modes.
7. **The wording rule's denial (F007; C274.5, 11E Stage 1a amendment 14).** A denial counts only in the claim word's own clause. The rule no longer reads fenced code blocks, or an inline code span that holds a claim word: verbatim tool output and quoted fixtures are mentions, not claims. `text.ts`'s `sentencesOf` gains an optional third parameter `withCode`, which defaults to true. JSON under `docs/` stays out of the rule's scope (skipped, below).
8. **Comments only (KP11-73).** `apps/api/src/services/templates/consent-texts.ts` and `packages/contracts/src/flow-api.ts` now say that the digests are checked and never stored, and that a body without them is not checked. No contract member changed.
9. **The ASN seed cache (KP11-91, 7C's R010).** A missing or unexpected seed's answer is cached under the size, `mtimeNs` and `ctimeNs` of the manifest and the seeds it read. A later session start reads nothing again until one of those files changes.
10. **The summary block survives a re-render (KP11-75).** `calibration:report`'s two writers and `android_document_guidance.py --write` append the previous report's committed summary block section unchanged after the rendered report.
11. **The e2e holder streams its logs (dast-11f's new item).** `runCommand` gains `outputFile`: a command's output goes to a file, and only a 64 KiB tail is kept in memory. `saveContainerLogs` streams each container's log to `e2e/.artifacts/logs/<service>.log`, so a long-held images-mode stack always reaches `compose down`. The bucket init's log is kept and quoted the same way (KP11-68's first step).
12. **Four ASVS tests (KP11-72, part).** These exist now: 1.3.11 (a line break in a mail subject or address injects nothing), 13.4.4 (TRACE answers a problem that echoes nothing), 15.3.6 (a `__proto__` or `constructor.prototype` body is refused) and 15.3.7 (a repeated single-valued query parameter is refused). No defect was found. 16.3.1 and 16.3.2 are carried.
13. **Decisions recorded, nothing built.** KP11-30: the role change's own audit entry stands for the sessions it ends. KP10-55: binding a provider does not re-validate published versions; those versions are never asked for a token, which is safe, and re-validation at bind time is the next release's. KP11-76: there is no tenth data class, and third-party list data stays `public-and-list-data`, because a new class would change Connect's share categories (C230.4). KP11-77: no previous forms are built for the four secrets, and `docs/security/crypto-inventory.md` states the coordinated restart as a limit.

### fix-clients

1. **The console session cookie's prefix (KP11-78; ASVS v5.0.0-3.3.1, 3.3.3).** Whenever the cookie is Secure, it is named `__Host-openverify_session`: every production build without `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_INSECURE_COOKIES=true`. `next dev` and the plain-http opt-out keep `openverify_session`. Every reader reads only the name for its mode. `secureCookies` and `INSECURE_COOKIES_ENV` moved from `apps/dashboard/src/lib/config.ts` to `lib/session-cookie.ts`, and `config.ts` re-exports both. `proxy.test.ts`'s request helper and `security/console-csp.test.ts` plant `sessionCookieName()`. The e2e readers and planters were named borrowed edits of `e2e/` (`260ecb876`, `f982329bc`, `0379a2f12`). DAST's console scan sends the new name (fix-records 6).
2. **The palette follows the live trigger (KP10-15).** The draft page reads the module workflows for any writable workflow-kind draft, whatever its trigger. The palette refuses module entries while the live trigger is an account or case action (`MODULELESS_TRIGGERS`, now exported from `palette-model.ts`). The page test "offers no module to a draft whose trigger is %s" is replaced by "reads the modules ... and the palette refuses them while it is".
3. **A fixed decode-failure code (KP10-56; Phase 10 C213.3, C215.1).** `FlowApiException.InvalidResponse.causeClass` is removed. It is replaced by `decodeFailure`, one of `missing_field`, `malformed_json`, `invalid_value` or `other` (null without a cause). The class is internal, so no ABI dump and no `expected-public.txt` changed. `android/openverify-sdk/consumer-rules.pro` holds no active rule, so the SDK AAR ships none. `android/scripts/check-consumer-rules.py` no longer requires kotlinx.serialization's exception names, and requires the SDK's section of R8's merged configuration only when the SDK has rules.
4. **The provider's `amr` in the console (KP11-90; C248.6).** The sessions table gains a "Sign-in methods (identity provider)" column that shows `provider-amr` as stated, or "None stated". The dashboard's session fixture carries `'provider-amr': null`.
5. **The console's error boundary (KP11-80).** A page's error boundary logs only "The page could not be loaded" with `{error: <kind>, digest}` to the browser console, never the error object. The sign-out log, the CSP report log and the flow server's access log hold no personal data, and tests now hold that. None of the three changed.
6. **Cheaper queries (KP11-66).** `role-form.test.tsx` finds the name field by role and name, and each permission through its `<label>`'s own control. What the tests assert is unchanged.
7. **Rule 10055 (DAST).** No change: `report-to` with `Reporting-Endpoints` has stood beside `report-uri` since Phase 9 (`61ca9afa5`). `apps/dashboard/src/security/console-csp.test.ts` holds both on every console route (`6375a0549`), and the triage reads "not a defect".
8. **The SDK host-test floor** is 2,739 (ui 533, flow 427, rest 1,779), raised by fix-records in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`. The demo's 97 and 9, nfc's 116, face's 51 and the 97 screenshots are unchanged.

### fix-records

1. **`gateway.httpRoute.parentRefs` (KP11-84; C263.1; a values-schema change, fix-records' frozen file).** A new values key, `gateway.httpRoute.parentRefs`, default `[]`. It is an array of objects, each with `name` (required) and optionally `namespace`, `sectionName` and `port`; no other member is allowed. Every HTTPRoute attaches to those parents, or to the Gateway named like the release when the list is empty. `deploy/helm/openverify/tests/c263-operations_test.yaml` holds the default, a named HTTPS listener and the refusal of a parent without a name. `docs/self-hosting/install-kubernetes.md` describes the key.
2. **The API host's HTTP redirect stays on the Ingress path (KP11-81; ASVS v5.0.0-4.1.2, partial).** The NGINX controllers have no annotation that refuses plain HTTP on one host, short of a snippet annotation, which both disable by default and the chart must not require. Dropping the redirect would serve the API over plain HTTP. The Gateway route on an HTTPS-only listener (item 1) refuses plain HTTP. The API's own refusal of a plain-HTTP forwarded request is the next release's (an `apps/api` configuration and registry change).
3. **The compose `app` profile keeps one database role (KP11-83; C249.3).** Decided, not built. The compose Postgres is created by its image with one owner role. Every developer command uses that one `DATABASE_URL`. A role made by an init script would exist only on an empty data directory. The two-role install is the chart's migrate Job and `docs/self-hosting/database.md`'s. `install-compose.md` and the compose comment say why.
4. **The vision image is `pending-owner` (F004; C289, rule 20).** `services/vision/Dockerfile`'s `dev.openverify.licence-status` reads `pending-owner` while KP11-39 is open, as the API image's does while Q11 is pending. This was a named borrowed edit of fix-server's path, held by `infra/tests/images.test.sh` (static and live). The label goes back to `decided` only with the owner's KP11-39 answer.
5. **The SDK host-test floor rises to 2,739** in `ci.yml`'s android job and `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`, which also fails at 2,738 and at 11A's 2,732.
6. **DAST's console scan sends `__Host-openverify_session`** (`security/zap/run.sh`'s `do_console`). `infra/tests/zap.test.sh` reads the name from `apps/dashboard/src/lib/session-cookie.ts`, so a renamed console cookie fails the scan's contract instead of leaving the scan unauthenticated.
7. **The DAST triage.** Rule 10021 is `fixed` (KP11-70, `7f3853539`). Rule 10055 is `not a defect` (report-to beside report-uri, `6375a0549`). Rule 10202 stays `not a defect` (C255.4's origin check).
8. **dast-11f's figures, corrected** (Stage 1 amendments 5, 12 and 13), in `docs/tdd/phase11-dast-11f.tdd.md`'s new "Corrections": 22 FAIL rules, not 27; 117,435 requests answered, not 117,770; api-webhooks got 356 answers of 429 on the server API's key; api-flow got 7,352 answers, not 7,282. The generated report was already right.
9. **Borrowed edits outside fix-records' paths**, each file clean first and committed alone:
   - `packages/docs-tools/src/release/checklist.test.ts` (fix-server's; `64290e6cc`). The test "marks no line done in 11E ..." required every `KP` line to be not done. From 11F on, a known item's line is done once its fixer fixed it (decision 14 before 11F). The owner steps `O01`-`O09` and E002 are still never done, and no line dated 11E may read done.
   - `docs/tdd/phase11-fix-server.tdd.md:33` (`dd0b88d62`). F007's own example sentence is written as code, so that the wording rule reads it as a mention. The docs-tools suite failed on it.
   - `e2e/README.md` (fix-server's; `ac93ed8d4`). A "DAST on the images mode" paragraph, for dast-11f's followup.
   - `services/vision/Dockerfile` (fix-server's; item 4).
10. **The DAST Validation step** runs as Stage 1 amendment 3's calls, one `run.sh` subcommand per call under the e2e lock: `prepare`, `api <group>` for each server and console group, `flow hosted noguidance`, `flow routes`, `console`, `report` and `revoke`.
11. **Records moved with the fixes.** These changed without a contract change:
    - threat rows B4-R2 and B10-D2 are now controls;
    - B13-T4 has the hydrated-field rule;
    - BX-I1 and B3-I6 are still gaps, each with its 11F part;
