### The Validation as run (fix-records, 2026-11-21)

This amends Workflow 11E Stage 3 amendment 7's run with 11F's numbering. Every call ran in the foreground, alone on the machine. The evidence is `docs/tdd/phase11-fix-records.tdd.md` section 10.

1. **Unchanged splits.** The API's unit suite is 8 blob shards and a merge (186-265 s each), the dashboard 2 and a merge, and integration 5 calls. Vision's pytest is three parts (161, 201 and 326 s). The Android gate is twelve calls, every lock wait 0.005-0.009 s. DAST is Stage 1 amendment 3's calls (22 API groups of 139-414 s each).
2. **KP11-38 met again** in the first API run: 2 of 10,991 failed on `operator-patterns.test.ts`'s 50 ms CPU budget, at 103.1 and 120.1 ms against 100, at a shard load of 8 to 10. It was not retried for it. The step re-ran after the change in item 3: 10,992 passed. That pass does not close KP11-38, which stays the owner's (a budget-unit decision).
3. **A new failure under load, fixed test-first** (a Validation fix in fix-server's `apps/api`, a contract deviation of no route or schema). `readiness.integration.test.ts`'s afterAll met its 180 s hook guard in integration api 2/3 at load 16.8, because a `pg` pool without a timeout waits forever on a peer that never answers. The test's pool is now `apps/api/src/test-support/store-pool.ts`'s `storePool`, with a 2 s connection and query timeout. It is held by `store-pool.test.ts` against a loopback peer that accepts and never answers. No guard was raised. The API's production pools also set no connection timeout; that is carried to fix-server's successor.
4. **KP11-42 met once more**: the first e2e stack start stopped on Next.js's own notice (load 11.21), and the second start came up in 95 s. Nothing was upgraded or configured.
5. **The device suites** (step 22, `android/` changed since `f7610de7e`). The Pixel 9 was reachable (`device`), but it was dozing behind its keyguard. The results, from the instrumentation's XML:
   - openverify-nfc: 2 of 2; openverify-face: 4 of 4;
   - SDK: 7 of 14. Its 7 activity and camera tests failed, with "No compose hierarchies found" or a preview that never started. That one call ran 1,534 s, past the 10-minute cap, for that reason;
   - the demo's smoke test was not run.

   No agent unlocks the owner's phone, so the 9 tests are "device proof open". At the 11C close the same 14 SDK tests passed 14 of 14, and 11F's `android/` changes touch no activity, camera or Compose code.
6. **Skipped with their conditions:** 9b (no lockfile or verification metadata changed) and 20 (nothing 11F changed is benchmarked). **Run:** 17b, kind 1.35 and 1.37 (`deploy/helm/` changed for KP11-84), every subcommand passing.

## Orchestrator decisions before Workflow 11G (2026-11-21)

Made by the orchestrator (Claude Code, claude-opus-5-5) after Workflow 11F's close (PROGRESS "## 2026-11-21: Phase 11, Workflow 11F done", `9003bc311`, pushed), under the owner's delegation of 2026-11-21 ("take the decisions yourself and lets finish this project", recorded in that entry's `**Qn:**` lines), from read-only re-checks of the 11G script against Workflows 11A to 11F as built (read at `2002ecc05`, 2026-11-21) and from the known items 11F left. Items 1-16 are the script re-check's, 17 the known items', 18-20 the orchestrator's. The 11G script carries all of it; where this section and an earlier plan line differ, this section wins.

1. **Args.** `{base, start, prior, known, device, owner, ciFailures}`.
   - `base` is the commit that adds "## 2026-11-21: Phase 11, Workflow 11F done (…)" (`9003bc311`). `start` is this section's commit, and it is required.
   - A `base` equal to `4db86122d` is refused. contracts-11g stops with "11F NOT DONE" unless the heading is at base and start, the ancestry `4db86122d` → base → start holds, and this section is at start.

## Workflow 11G Stage 2 amendments

Recorded by `integration-11g` on 2026-11-21 from the three Stage 2 parts' reports and commits (`4b3a06a0f..db2cd3e61`); each item is a binding decision for this release and wins over an earlier contract line where they differ. The evidence is each part's TDD file. Nothing in Stage 2 was pushed, published, signed, attested or dispatched, and no repository setting changed.

### publish-11g (Q1-Q4; C262, C267.1's publish job, C285, C277.1)

1. **The dispatch input.** `release.yml` takes `dry-run` (boolean, default true) beside `version`. Every publishing command is guarded by a comparison with an explicit `false`, so a missing input is a dry run; the stage job carries the value as `DRY_RUN` and names it in its notice.
2. **The publish job's order.** It still needs `stage` (which needs `verify`, which needs `build` on main), runs only when `vars.OPENVERIFY_PUBLISH == 'true'`, in environment `release`, in its own concurrency group `release-publish`, with a 60-minute timeout. Its steps, before any registry login: a main check, `sha256sum --check --strict` of every staged file, the unchanged pending-owner refusal (whole run, dry run included), the npm manifest checks and `pnpm publish --dry-run` with the OIDC request variables removed, and a check that each loaded image's id equals the build's `IMAGE-IDS.txt`. The GitHub token is set only on the two login steps; no `secrets.*` is referenced anywhere.
3. **What a real run publishes.** Each image is pushed with its version tag and signed keyless (cosign 3.1.3) and its CycloneDX SBOM attested, both by the digest the push returned; the chart is pushed as an OCI artifact; `actions/attest-build-provenance` v4.2.2 writes provenance twice, over `staged/SHA256SUMS` and over `staged/PUBLISHED-DIGESTS` (the six image digests and the chart digest), and `push-to-registry` is not used; the three npm tarballs are published with `pnpm publish <tarball> --provenance --access public --no-git-checks` through npm's trusted publishing, with no stored token. `--no-git-checks` is needed because pnpm 12.14.0 refuses the runner's tree once the staged files are downloaded; the branch is held by the main check and the bytes by `SHA256SUMS`. The chart is not signed with cosign in this release (provenance only); signing its digest is the next release's choice.
4. **The packages' repository.** `packages/{node,sdk-js,sdk-react}/package.json` carry `repository` with the GitHub URL and the package directory, which `pack-release.ts` keeps and npm's provenance checks; the publish job checks each packed manifest's `repository.url` against `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` before anything is pushed.
5. **The switch and the page.** `DOCS_SWITCHES.publishWired` is true (a named borrowed edit of records-11g's `switches.ts`). `checkPublishSwitch` holds, in both switch states, the workflow's wiring, one repository across the published manifests and `verify-artifacts.md`'s four checks with the certificate identity of `release.yml` on main and GitHub's issuer, and the page's statements that nothing is signed until the first approved publication. The release notes' `publish-wired` disclosure replaces `unsigned` while the switch is on and says what the first approved dispatch with `dry-run` false will do, never that it happened. `npm audit signatures` is named as the reader's own tool; the project adopts no npm CLI.
6. **Tests changed** (rule 12; Q1-Q4 change the behaviour they checked): `release-workflow.test.sh`'s "npm waits for Q4" check is replaced by the npm, dry-run and simulated-run checks (103 to 141 checks; the job's run steps execute with stub tools, offline), and `selfhost/checks.test.ts`'s single wired-mode case by five and a real-tree case.
7. **The job-level main check** that publish-11g proposed is integration-11g's (Stage 3 amendments item 2).

### codeql-11g (Q1 granted, Q9 declined; C260.2)

1. **C260.2 amended.** CodeQL (`github/codeql-action` v4.39.3 by commit, bundle 2.27.4) runs informational in `.github/workflows/codeql.yml` for javascript-typescript and python (build-mode none) and java-kotlin (build-mode manual: a traced `./gradlew --no-daemon --no-build-cache assembleDebug`, a subset of the android job's own Gradle line, so the existing `verification-metadata.xml` covers it with verification on). Both jobs run only when the repository is public (`github.event.repository.private == false && github.event.repository.visibility == 'public'`), carry `continue-on-error: true`, `contents: read` and `security-events: write` on the job only, a push to main or a dispatch as their only triggers, and no schedule. The SAST baseline of C260.1 stays the gate. Its config `.github/codeql/codeql-config.yml` runs `security-extended` and ignores fixtures, the docs, vendored notices and one generated data file.
2. **C258.2 amended.** No third-party scanner action is used; GitHub's own `github/codeql-action` is the one exception, allowed in `codeql.yml` only (`ci-workflow.test.sh`'s `SCANNER_ALLOWED_IN`). Scanner actions are matched by owner and repository across every workflow and composite action, so a sub-action such as `github/codeql-action/init` is caught (the earlier whole-path match missed it).
3. **The contract test** `infra/tests/codeql-workflow.test.sh` (95 checks) runs in ci.yml's supply-chain job as the step "The CodeQL workflow's contract" (a named borrowed edit of `ci.yml`; still 16 job keys and 19 runs).
4. **Decision 69's bar** (the push's CI jobs and one staged `release.yml` run green) does not include `codeql.yml`, which is informational and never a required gate. On the private repository every CodeQL job is skipped and bills nothing; the first run after the owner's release step records each job's minutes against the estimate (about 40-75 runner minutes a push, not billed on a public repository's standard runners), confirms Kotlin files were extracted and that dependency verification passed.
5. **C286 amended:** "CodeQL (Q9)" under Hardening becomes "CodeQL wired in 11G (informational, public repository only); its first run is measured when the repository is public and minutes allow (KP10-59)".

### records-11g (Q1, Q5, Q8, Q10, Q11, Q12 granted; P2 record check; DEVICE-PROOFS)

1. **Q1.** `SECURITY.md` and the vulnerability policy say the repository stays private until the release and that private reporting opens when the owner makes it public; `q1Findings` (docs-tools' records) reads Q1's state from PROGRESS and fails a page that contradicts it.
2. **Q5.** uv 0.13.7 is in ci.yml's three setup-uv steps, each with the ledger's asset SHA-256 as `checksum`, and in the vision Dockerfile's `UV_IMAGE` by tag and digest; `ci-workflow.test.sh` holds all three to the Dockerfile and the `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` row, and `images.test.sh`'s `UV_PIN` to the digest. Every `uv.lock` was re-checked with the binary from the pinned uv image inside the pinned Python image (the uv image has no interpreter): all unchanged. ADR 0010 records the rule. uv 0.13.8 becomes the newest eligible on 2026-11-26.
3. **Q8.** `vendorNaming` is true; `docs:catalog` renders the vendor-named migration page and the catalog's source-name column; the generator no longer writes the neutral page, which was removed, and `DOCS_TREE`'s migration entry follows the switch. While the switch is on, the vendor-name rule treats a link to the vendor-named page as a path. 11E's switch-off tests were changed with the decision (rule 12).
